## I The Vanishing Man / 一 失踪的人

===Original===
The school of St. Margaret’s Hospital was fortunate in its lecturer on Medical Jurisprudence, or Forensic Medicine, as it is sometimes described. At some schools the lecturer on this subject is appointed apparently for the reason that he lacks the qualifications to lecture on any other. But with us it was very different: John Thorndyke was not only an enthusiast, a man of profound learning and great reputation, but he was an exceptional teacher, lively and fascinating in style and of endless resources. Every remarkable case that had ever been reported he appeared to have at his fingers’ ends; every fact﻿—chemical, physical, biological, or even historical﻿—that could in any way be twisted into a medico-legal significance, was pressed into his service; and his own varied and curious experiences seemed as inexhaustible as the widow’s curse. One of his favorite devices for giving life and interest to a rather dry subject was that of analyzing and commenting upon contemporary cases as reported in the papers (always, of course, with a due regard to the legal and social proprieties); and it was in this way that I first became introduced to the astonishing series of events that was destined to exercise so great an influence on my own life.

===Chinese===
圣玛格丽特医院（St. Margaret's Hospital）的医学校有幸拥有一位出色的法医学（Medical Jurisprudence，有时亦称 Forensic Medicine）讲师。在某些学校，这门课的讲师之所以得到任命，理由看来仅仅是：他已不具备讲授其他任何课程的资格。但在我们这里，情况却大不相同：约翰·桑代克（John Thorndyke）不仅对此道满怀热忱、学识渊博、声名卓著，更是一位出类拔萃的教师，讲风生动迷人，材料层出不穷。凡曾有报道的著名案例，他似乎无一不烂熟于心；凡是能以任何方式引申出法医学意义的种种事实——化学的、物理的、生物的、乃至历史的——都被他征调来为己所用；而他自己那一段段光怪陆离的亲身经历，更如寡妇的诅咒一般没完没了，仿佛永不枯竭。他惯用的招数之一，是拿报纸上报道的时事案例来剖析评点，给这门不免枯燥的学科注入生气与趣味（当然，始终对法律与社交上的种种规矩给予应有的尊重）；而我最初得以知晓那一连串惊人的事件——它们注定要对我自己的人生产生极为深远的影响——恰恰正是通过这个途径。

===Original===
The lecture which had just been concluded had dealt with the rather unsatisfactory subject of survivorship. Most of the students had left the theater, and the remainder had gathered round the lecturer’s table to listen to the informal comments that Dr. Thorndyke was wont to deliver on these occasions in an easy, conversational manner, leaning against the edge of the table and apparently addressing his remarks to a stick of blackboard chalk that he held in his fingers.

===Chinese===
刚刚结束的那堂课，讲的是“生存者问题”（survivorship）这个颇不能令人满意的题目。大多数学生已经离开阶梯教室，余下的围拢到讲台四周，听桑代克博士发表他惯常于此类场合所作的即兴评论。他斜倚着桌沿，神态闲适，语气如谈天一般，那一番话仿佛是说给指间捏着的一截黑板粉笔听的。

===Original===
“The problem of survivorship,” he was saying, in reply to a question put by one of the students, “ordinarily occurs in cases where the bodies of the parties are producible, or where, at any rate, the occurrence of death and its approximate time are actually known. But an analogous difficulty may arise in a case where the body of one of the parties is not forthcoming, and the fact of death may have to be assumed on collateral evidence.

===Chinese===
“生存者问题，”他正在回答一个学生提出的问题，“通常出现在各方当事人的遗体均可出示的案件中，或者至少，死亡的发生及其大致时间确属已知的案件中。但相仿的难题也可能出现在另一种情形里：一方当事人的遗体下落不明，死亡事实只能依据间接证据加以推定。

===Original===
“Here, of course, the vital question to be settled is, what is the latest instant at which it is certain that this person was alive? And the settlement of that question may turn on some circumstance of the most trivial and insignificant kind. There is a case in this morning’s paper which illustrates this. A gentleman has disappeared rather mysteriously. He was last seen by the servant of a relative at whose house he had called. Now, if this gentleman should never reappear, dead or alive, the question as to what was the latest moment at which he was certainly alive will turn upon the further question: ‘Was he or was he not wearing a particular article of jewelry when he called at the relative’s house?’ ”

===Chinese===
“在这类案件里，需要解决的关键问题当然是：能够确定此人尚在人世的最后一刻究竟在何时？而这一问题的解决，可能系于某个最为琐碎、最不起眼的情节。今天早晨的报纸上正好有一个案子可以说明这一点。一位绅士相当神秘地失踪了。最后见到他的，是他登门拜访的一位亲戚家的仆人。现在，倘若这位绅士从此不再露面——无论死活——那么，他确知尚在人世的最后时刻究竟为何时，就将取决于另一个问题：‘他到那位亲戚家拜访时，究竟有没有佩戴那件特殊的珠宝饰物？’”

===Original===
He paused with a reflective eye bent upon the stump of chalk he still held; then, noting the expectant interest with which we were regarding him, he resumed:

===Chinese===
他停了下来，若有所思的目光落在手中仍捏着的那截粉笔头上；随即，察觉到我们正满怀期待地注视着他，便又接了下去：

===Original===
“The circumstances in this case are very curious; in fact, they are highly mysterious; and if any legal issues should arise in respect of them, they are likely to yield some very remarkable complications. The gentleman who has disappeared, Mr. John Bellingham, is a man well known in archeological circles. He recently returned from Egypt, bringing with him a very fine collection of antiquities﻿—some of which, by the way, he has presented to the British Museum, where they are now on view﻿—and having made this presentation, he appears to have gone to Paris on business. I may mention that the gift consisted of a very fine mummy and a complete set of tomb-furniture. The latter, however, had not arrived from Egypt at the time when the missing man left for Paris, but the mummy was inspected on the fourteenth of October at Mr. Bellingham’s house by Dr. Norbury of the British Museum, in the presence of the donor and his solicitor, and the latter was authorized to hand over the complete collection to the British Museum authorities when the tomb-furniture arrived; which he has since done.

===Chinese===
“本案的情节十分离奇；说实在的，堪称高度神秘；倘若日后围绕它产生任何法律争端，多半会引出一些非常罕见的复杂情况。失踪的这位绅士，约翰·贝林厄姆先生（Mr. John Bellingham），是考古学界的一位知名人士。他不久前刚从埃及（Egypt）归来，随身带回一大批精美的古物——顺便一提，其中一部分他已捐赠给大英博物馆（British Museum），目前正在那里展出——办完这桩捐赠之后，他似乎因事前往巴黎（Paris）去了。可以说明的是，那件赠品包括一具十分精美的木乃伊和一整套墓葬随葬品。不过，在失踪者动身赴巴黎时，后者尚未从埃及运抵；木乃伊则已于十月十四日在贝林厄姆先生府上由大英博物馆的诺伯里博士（Dr. Norbury）查验，捐赠人及其律师均在场，并授权后者待随葬品运到后，将全部藏品移交大英博物馆方面；此事他后来已经办妥。

===Original===
“From Paris he seems to have returned on the twenty-third of November, and to have gone direct to Charing Cross to the house of a relative, a Mr. Hurst, who is a bachelor and lives at Eltham. He appeared at the house at twenty minutes past five, and as Mr. Hurst had not yet come down from town and was not expected until a quarter to six, he explained who he was and said he would wait in the study and write some letters. The housemaid accordingly showed him into the study, furnished him with writing materials, and left him.

===Chinese===
“他似乎是在十一月二十三日从巴黎返回的，下了车便径直经查令十字（Charing Cross）前往一位亲戚家中——这位亲戚就是赫斯特先生（Mr. Hurst），一个单身汉，住在埃尔瑟姆（Eltham）。他于五点二十分来到那所宅子；由于赫斯特先生还未从城里回来，而且要到五点三刻才到家，他便说明了自己的身份，说要在书房里等一等，顺便写几封信。女仆于是把他领进书房，备好书写用具，便退了出去。

===Original===
“At a quarter to six Mr. Hurst let himself in with his latchkey, and before the housemaid had time to speak to him he had passed through into the study and shut the door.

===Chinese===
“五点三刻，赫斯特先生用自己的弹簧锁钥匙开门进屋；女仆还来不及跟他说话，他已经径直穿过门厅走进书房，随手关上了门。

===Original===
“At six o’clock, when the dinner bell was rung, Mr. Hurst entered the dining-room alone, and observing that the table was laid for two, asked the reason.

===Chinese===
“六点钟，晚饭铃响过之后，赫斯特先生独自走进餐室，见桌上摆着两个人的餐具，便问是何缘故。

===Original===
“ ‘I thought Mr. Bellingham was staying to dinner, sir,’ was the housemaid’s reply.

===Chinese===
“‘我还以为贝林厄姆先生要留下用晚餐呢，先生。’女仆这样回答。

===Original===
“ ‘Mr. Bellingham!’ exclaimed the astonished host. ‘I didn’t know he was here. Why was I not told?’

===Chinese===
“‘贝林厄姆先生！’愕然的主人惊呼道，‘我不知道他在这儿。怎么没有人告诉我？’

===Original===
“ ‘I thought he was in the study with you, sir,’ said the housemaid.

===Chinese===
“‘我以为他在书房里同您在一起呢，先生。’女仆说。

===Original===
“On this a search was made for the visitor, with the result that he was nowhere to be found. He had disappeared without leaving a trace, and what made the incident more odd was that the housemaid was certain that he had not gone out by the front door. For since neither she nor the cook was acquainted with Mr. John Bellingham, she had remained the whole time either in the kitchen, which commanded a view of the front gate, or in the dining-room, which opened into the hall opposite the study door. The study itself has a French window opening on a narrow grass plot, across which is a side-gate that opens into an alley; and it appears that Mr. Bellingham must have made his exit by this rather eccentric route. At any rate﻿—and this is the important fact﻿—he was not in the house, and no one had seen him leave it.

===Chinese===
“于是众人分头寻找这位来客，结果却是遍寻不获。他消失得无影无踪，而使这件事愈发古怪的是：女仆确信他绝没有从前门出去。因为她和厨娘都不认识约翰·贝林厄姆先生，所以她自始至终不是待在可以望见前门的厨房里，就是待在开向门厅、正对书房门的餐室里。书房本身有一扇落地长窗，通向一块窄窄的草地；草地对面有一道边门，通往一条小巷。看来贝林厄姆先生必定是由这条颇为古怪的路线离开的。无论如何——这才是关键的事实——他不在宅子里，而且谁也没有见他离开。

===Original===
“After a hasty meal Mr. Hurst returned to town and called at the office of Mr. Bellingham’s solicitor and confidential agent, a Mr. Jellicoe, and mentioned the matter to him. Mr. Jellicoe knew nothing of his client’s return from Paris, and the two men at once took the train down to Woodford, where the missing man’s brother, Mr. Godfrey Bellingham, lives. The servant who admitted them said that Mr. Godfrey was not at home, but that his daughter was in the library, which is a detached building situated in a shrubbery beyond the garden at the back of the house. Here the two men found, not only Miss Bellingham, but also her father, who had come in by the back gate.

===Chinese===
“匆匆吃过一顿便饭，赫斯特先生便赶回城里，到贝林厄姆先生的律师兼机密代理人——杰利科先生（Mr. Jellicoe）——的事务所登门拜访，向他提起了这件事。杰利科先生对自己当事人从巴黎返回一事一无所知，两人当即乘火车前往伍德福德（Woodford），失踪者的弟弟戈弗雷·贝林厄姆先生（Mr. Godfrey Bellingham）就住在那里。开门迎客的仆人说戈弗雷先生不在家，但他的女儿在藏书室里。那是一座独立的小屋，坐落在宅子后面花园再往外的灌木丛中。两位来客在那里不仅找到了贝林厄姆小姐（Miss Bellingham），还找到了她的父亲——他是从后门进来的。

===Original===
“Mr. Godfrey and his daughter listened to Mr. Hurst’s story with the greatest surprise, and assured him that they had neither seen nor heard anything of John Bellingham.

===Chinese===
“戈弗雷先生和他的女儿听赫斯特先生讲完事情的经过，惊讶不已，并向他保证，他们既没有见过约翰·贝林厄姆，也没有听到关于他的任何音讯。

===Original===
“Presently the party left the library to walk up to the house; but only a few feet from the library door Mr. Jellicoe noticed an object lying in the grass and pointed it out to Mr. Godfrey.

===Chinese===
“不多时，一行人离开藏书室往宅子走去；可是离藏书室的门才几英尺远，杰利科先生就注意到草地上躺着一件东西，便指给戈弗雷先生看。

===Original===
“The latter picked it up, and they all recognized it as a scarab which Mr. John Bellingham had been accustomed to wear suspended from his watch-chain. There was no mistaking it. It was a very fine scarab of the eighteenth dynasty fashioned of lapis lazuli and engraved with the cartouche of Amenhotep III. It had been suspended by a gold ring fastened to a wire which passed through the suspension hole, and the ring, though broken, was still in position.

===Chinese===
“后者把它拾了起来，众人一眼认出，这正是约翰·贝林厄姆先生素来拴在表链上佩戴的那枚圣甲虫（scarab）。绝对不会弄错。那是一枚十分精美的十八王朝圣甲虫，以青金石（lapis lazuli）制成，上面镌刻着阿蒙霍特普三世（Amenhotep III）的王名圈（cartouche）。它原先由一枚金环悬吊，金环系在一根穿过悬孔的细金属丝上；金环虽已断裂，却仍留在原处。

===Original===
“This discovery of course only added to the mystery, which was still further increased when, on inquiry, a suitcase bearing the initials J. B. was found to be unclaimed in the cloakroom at Charing Cross. Reference to the counterfoil of the ticket-book showed that it had been deposited about the time of the arrival of the Continental express on the twenty-third of November, so that its owner must have gone straight on to Eltham.

===Chinese===
“这一发现自然只是令谜团愈加扑朔迷离；随后经过查访，又在查令十字的行李寄存处发现一只无人认领的手提箱，箱上标着 J. B. 两个姓名首字母，谜团由此更进一步加深。查阅票据簿存根可知，这口箱子是在十一月二十三日大陆快车到站前后存入的，可见它的主人必定是一下车便径直去了埃尔瑟姆。

===Original===
“That is how the affair stands at present, and, should the missing man never reappear or should his body never be found, the question, as you see, which will be required to be settled is, ‘What is the exact time and place, when and where, he was last known to be alive!’ As to the place, the importance of the issues involved in that question is obvious and we need not consider it. But the question of time has another kind of significance. Cases have occurred, as I pointed out in the lecture, in which proof of survivorship by less than a minute has secured succession to property. Now, the missing man was last seen alive at Mr. Hurst’s house at twenty minutes past five on the twenty-third of November. But he appears to have visited his brother’s house at Woodford, and, since nobody saw him at that house, it is at present uncertain whether he went there before calling on Mr. Hurst. If he went there first, then twenty minutes past five on the evening of the twenty-third is the latest moment at which he is known to have been alive; but if he went there after, there would have to be added to this time the shortest time possible in which he could travel from the one house to the other.

===Chinese===
“事情目前就是这样。你们可以看到，倘若失踪者永远不再露面，或者遗体永远无从寻获，那么届时必须裁定的问题便是：‘确知他尚在人世的确切时间与地点——何时、何地——究竟是什么！’至于地点，这个问题所关涉的利益之重大显而易见，我们不必细论。但时间问题却另有意义。正如我在讲课中指出的，曾有这样的案例：仅凭证明一方比另一方多活了不足一分钟，便确立了财产继承的归属。现在，失踪者最后一次被人看到活着，是在十一月二十三日五点二十分，于赫斯特先生家中。但他似乎也去过他弟弟在伍德福德的宅子；既然没有人在那所宅子里见到过他，他究竟是先拜访赫斯特先生还是先去弟弟家，目前尚难确定。如果他先去了伍德福德，那么二十三日傍晚五点二十分便是确知他活着的最后时刻；但如果他是在那之后去的，那么除了这个时刻之外，还得再加上他从一处宅子赶到另一处宅子所需的最短时间。

===Original===
“But the question as to which house he visited first hinges on the scarab. If he was wearing the scarab when he arrived at Mr. Hurst’s house, it would be certain that he went there first; but if it was not then on his watch-chain, a probability would be established that he went first to Woodford. Thus, you see, a question which may conceivably become of the most vital moment in determining the succession of property turns on the observation or non-observation by this housemaid of an apparently trivial and insignificant fact.”

===Chinese===
“然而，他究竟先去了哪一所宅子，这个问题就系在那枚圣甲虫上。如果他到达赫斯特先生家时圣甲虫正戴在身上，那么可以断定他是先去那里的；但如果它当时并不在他的表链上，那么便可确立一种可能性：他是先去伍德福德的。你们看，一个在决定财产继承时有可能变得至关紧要的问题，竟系于这位女仆是否注意到一个看似琐碎、毫不起眼的事实。”

===Original===
“Has the servant made any statement on this subject, sir?” I ventured to inquire.

===Chinese===
“关于这一点，那位仆人可曾作过什么陈述，先生？”我壮着胆子问道。

===Original===
“Apparently not,” replied Dr. Thorndyke; “at any rate, there is no reference to any such statement in the newspaper report, though otherwise, the case is reported in great detail; indeed, the wealth of detail, including plans of the two houses, is quite remarkable and well worth noting as being in itself a fact of considerable interest.”

===Chinese===
“显然没有，”桑代克博士答道，“至少报纸的报道中没有提到任何此类陈述，尽管在其他方面，这桩案子的报道可谓详尽之至；的确，其细节之丰富——连两所宅子的平面图都包括在内——相当不同寻常，值得留意，因为这本身便是一个饶有趣味的事实。”

===Original===
“In what respect, sir, is it of interest?” one of the students asked.

===Chinese===
“先生，这在哪一点上有趣呢？”一个学生问道。

===Original===
“Ah,” replied Dr. Thorndyke, “I think I must leave you to consider that question yourself. This is an untried case, and we mustn’t make free with the actions and motives of individuals.”

===Chinese===
“啊，”桑代克博士答道，“这个问题，我想还是留给你们自己去考虑吧。这是一桩尚未开审的案子，我们不可以对个人的行为和动机妄加议论。”

===Original===
“Does the paper give any description of the missing man, sir?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“报纸上对失踪者有没有相貌描述，先生？”我问道。

===Original===
“Yes; quite an exhaustive description. Indeed, it is exhaustive to the verge of impropriety, considering that the man may turn up alive and well at any moment. It seems that he has an old Pott’s fracture of the left ankle, a linear, longitudinal scar on each knee﻿—origin not stated, but easily guessed at﻿—and that he has tattooed on his chest in vermilion a very finely and distinctly executed representation of the symbolical Eye of Osiris﻿—or Horus or Ra, as the different authorities have it. There certainly ought to be no difficulty in identifying the body. But we hope that it will not come to that.

===Chinese===
“有；一份相当详尽的描述。说实在的，考虑到此人随时可能安然无恙地重新露面，这份描述详尽得简直近乎失当。据说他的左踝有波特氏骨折（Pott's fracture）的旧伤，两侧膝上各有一道线状纵疤——成因未加说明，但也不难猜到——胸前还以朱砂刺着一幅构图精细、线条清晰的象征图案：俄塞里斯之眼（the Eye of Osiris）——各家说法不一，或称荷鲁斯（Horus）之眼，或称拉（Ra）之眼。辨认遗体想必绝无困难。不过，我们希望不至于走到那一步。

===Original===
“And now I must really be running away, and so must you; but I would advise you all to get copies of the paper and file them when you have read the remarkably full details. It is a most curious case, and it is highly probable that we shall hear of it again. Good afternoon, gentlemen.”

===Chinese===
“好了，如今我真的得赶紧走了，你们也该散了；不过我奉劝各位都去买一份这份报纸，读完那份详尽得出奇的报道之后好好归档。这是一桩极其离奇的案子，我们极有可能还会再听到它的消息。各位，下午好。”

===Original===
Dr. Thorndyke’s advice appealed to all who heard it, for medical jurisprudence was a live subject at St. Margaret’s, and all of us were keenly interested in it. As a result, we sallied forth in a body to the nearest news-vendor’s, and, having each provided himself with a copy of the *Daily Telegraph*, adjourned together to the Common room to devour the report and thereafter to discuss the bearings of the case, unhampered by those considerations of delicacy that afflicted our more squeamish and scrupulous teacher.

===Chinese===
桑代克博士的这番建议得到了在场众人的一致响应，因为法医学在圣玛格丽特是一门生气勃勃的学科，我们人人都对它怀有浓厚的兴趣。于是我们全体出动，结伴前往最近的报摊，每人买了一份《每日电讯报》（*Daily Telegraph*），然后一同转移到公共休息室，狼吞虎咽地读完那篇报道，随即就本案的种种关窍展开讨论，全然不受那类细谨顾忌的束缚——而正是那些顾忌，折磨着我们那位过于神经过敏、过于一丝不苟的老师。

## II The Eavesdropper / 二 偷听的人

===Original===
It is one of the canons of correct conduct, scrupulously adhered to (when convenient) by all well-bred persons, that an acquaintance should be initiated by a proper introduction. To this salutary rule, which I have disregarded to the extent of an entire chapter, I now hasten to conform; and the more so inasmuch as nearly two years have passed since my first informal appearance.

===Chinese===
得体行为诸准则之中，有一条是凡有教养之士无不恪守的（在方便的时候）：与人结识，须先经正式介绍。这条有益的规矩，我已整整一个章节置之不理，如今赶紧补行如仪；何况距离我上一回不合规矩的亮相，已经过去将近两年了。

===Original===
Permit me then, to introduce Paul Berkeley, M.B., etc., recently﻿—very recently﻿—qualified, faultlessly attired in the professional frock-coat and tall hat, and, at the moment of introduction, navigating with anxious care a perilous strait between a row of well-filled coal-sacks and a colossal tray piled high with kidney potatoes.

===Chinese===
那么，请允许我介绍：保罗·伯克利（Paul Berkeley），医学学士（M.B.）等等，新近——非常新近——取得行医资格，一身执业医师的礼服大衣与大礼帽穿得无可挑剔；在介绍之际，正小心翼翼地航行于一段险要的海峡之间：一侧是一排塞得满满的煤袋，另一侧则是一大托盘堆得冒尖的肾形土豆。

===Original===
The passage of this strait landed me on the terra firma of Fleur-de-Lys Court, where I halted for a moment to consult my visiting list. There was only one more patient for me to see this morning, and he lived at 49, Nevill’s Court, wherever that might be. I turned for information to the presiding deity of the coal shop.

===Chinese===
驶过这段海峡，我便在百合花院（Fleur-de-Lys Court）的坚实陆地上靠了岸，在此驻足片刻，查阅我的出诊名单。今天上午我只剩一位病人要看了，他住在内维尔巷（Nevill's Court）49 号——管它在什么地方。于是我转身向煤店的主事神明问路。

===Original===
“Can you direct me, Mrs. Jablett, to Nevill’s Court?”

===Chinese===
“劳驾，贾布利特太太（Mrs. Jablett），到内维尔巷去，能给我指个路吗？”

===Original===
She could and she did, grasping me confidentially by the arm (the mark remained on my sleeve for weeks) and pointing a shaking forefinger at the dead wall ahead. “Nevill’s Court,” said Mrs. Jablett, “is a alley, and you goes into it through a archway. It turns out on Fetter Lane on the right ’and as you goes up, oppersight Bream’s Buildings.”

===Chinese===
她能，她也就指了。她一副知交密友的架势一把攥住我的胳膊（那印记在我袖子上留了好几个星期），颤巍巍的食指直指前方那堵死墙。“内维尔巷啊，”贾布利特太太说，“是条小胡同儿，你得打一个门洞儿里钻进去。出了口子就是菲特巷（Fetter Lane），在你往上走的那只右手边儿，正对着布里姆大楼（Bream's Buildings）。”

===Original===
I thanked Mrs. Jablett and went on my way, glad that the morning round was nearly finished, and vaguely conscious of a growing appetite and of a desire to wash in hot water.

===Chinese===
我谢过贾布利特太太，继续赶路，心里高兴上午的巡回出诊眼看就要收尾，又隐隐觉得胃口渐开，还想用热水洗个澡。

===Original===
The practice which I was conducting was not my own. It belonged to poor Dick Barnard, an old St. Margaret’s man of irrepressible spirits and indifferent physique, who had started only the day before for a trip down the Mediterranean on board a tramp engaged in the currant trade; and this, my second morning’s round, was in some sort a voyage of geographical discovery.

===Chinese===
我眼下主持的这份诊务并非我自己的。它属于可怜的迪克·巴纳德（Dick Barnard）——圣玛格丽特医院的老毕业生，兴致旺盛得按都按不住，体格却平平——就在昨天，他登上一艘跑葡萄干买卖的不定期货船，启程游地中海去了；于是这第二个上午的巡回出诊，多少算得上一次地理发现之旅。

===Original===
I walked on briskly up Fetter Lane until a narrow arched opening, bearing the superscription “Nevill’s Court,” arrested my steps, and here I turned to encounter one of those surprises that lie in wait for the traveler in London byways. Expecting to find the gray squalor of the ordinary London court, I looked out from under the shadow of the arch past a row of decent little shops through a vista full of light and color﻿—a vista of ancient, warm-toned roofs and walls relieved by sunlit foliage. In the heart of London a tree is always a delightful surprise; but here were not only trees, but bushes and even flowers. The narrow footway was bordered by little gardens, which, with their wooden palings and well-kept shrubs, gave to the place an air of quaint and sober rusticity; and even as I entered, a bevy of workgirls, with gaily-colored blouses and hair aflame in the sunlight, brightened up the quiet background like the wild flowers that spangle a summer hedgerow.

===Chinese===
我迈开轻快的步子沿菲特巷上行，直到一处窄窄的拱形门洞令我止步，门上题着“内维尔巷”的名目。就在这里，我遭遇了那种专在伦敦僻巷中守候行人的意外之喜。原以为会见到伦敦寻常小院那灰扑扑的寒碜景象，可从拱门的阴影下望出去，掠过一排体面的小铺面，眼前豁然展开一片流光溢彩的景致——一派古旧的、色调温暖的屋顶与墙壁，在阳光照拂的绿荫掩映下错落生辉。在伦敦腹地，一棵树总归是桩令人心喜的意外；而此地非但有树，还有灌木，甚至还有花卉。窄窄的人行道两侧镶着一方方小花园，配上木条栅栏和修剪齐整的灌木，给这地方平添一股古雅而恬淡的村野之气；恰在我进巷的当口，一群女工迎面走来，色彩鲜丽的罩衫、阳光下发如飞焰，把这道安静的背景装点得灿然生色，宛如缀满夏日树篱的点点野花。

===Original===
In one of the gardens I noticed that the little paths were paved with what looked like circular tiles, but which, on inspection, I found to be old-fashioned stone ink-bottles, buried bottom upwards; and I was meditating upon the quaint conceit of the forgotten scrivener who had thus adorned his habitation﻿—a law-writer perhaps or an author, or perchance even a poet﻿—when I perceived the number that I was seeking inscribed on a shabby door in a high wall. There was no bell or knocker, so, lifting the latch, I pushed the door open and entered.

===Chinese===
在其中一座花园里，我注意到园中小径铺着一种看似圆形陶片的东西，细看之下才发现竟是老式的石质墨水瓶，瓶底朝上倒埋土中；我正自玩味那位已被遗忘的文书先生的这番别致巧思——他就这样装点了自己的居所；他也许是位代写讼状的，也许是位作家，说不定还是位诗人——忽见我所寻的门牌号正写在高墙上一扇寒酸的门上。门上既无门铃也无门环，于是我掀起门闩，推门而入。

===Original===
But if the court itself had been a surprise, this was a positive wonder, a dream. Here, within earshot of the rumble of Fleet Street, I was in an old-fashioned garden enclosed by high walls and, now that the gate was shut, cut off from all sight and knowledge of the urban world that seethed without. I stood and gazed in delighted astonishment. Sun-gilded trees and flower beds gay with blossom; lupins, snapdragons, nasturtiums, spiry foxgloves, and mighty hollyhocks formed the foreground; over which a pair of sulphur-tinted butterflies flitted, unmindful of a buxom and miraculously clean white cat which pursued them, dancing across the borders and clapping her snowy paws fruitlessly in midair. And the background was no less wonderful; a grand old house, dark-eaved and venerable, that must have looked down on this garden when ruffled dandies were borne in sedan chairs through the court, and gentle Izaak Walton, stealing forth from his shop in Fleet Street, strolled up Fetter Lane to “go a-angling” at Temple Mills.

===Chinese===
可是，如果说方才那巷子已是一重意外，这里便简直是奇观，是一场梦了。就在舰队街（Fleet Street）车马轰隆声隐约可闻的地方，我竟置身于一座高墙环绕的老式花园之中；此刻园门既已关上，外面那个沸沸扬扬的尘世便被隔绝于视听与心知之外。我伫立原地，惊喜交加地凝望。阳光镀金的树木与繁花争艳的花坛：羽扇豆、金鱼草、旱金莲、尖塔似的毛地黄和高大的蜀葵构成前景；一对硫磺色的蝴蝶在花间翻飞，全不理会一只体态丰腴、干净得不可思议的白猫正追逐着它们，蹦跳着掠过花畦，雪白的爪子在半空一次次徒劳地拍合。而背景也毫不逊色：一座气派的老宅，檐影深黑、古意庄严，当鬈发插翎的纨绔子弟坐着轿子被人抬过这条巷子、当温文的伊萨克·沃尔顿（Izaak Walton）从他在舰队街的铺子里悄悄溜出来、踱上菲特巷到坦普尔磨坊（Temple Mills）“钓一番鱼”的年代，它想必就已俯瞰着这座花园了。

===Original===
So overpowered was I by this unexpected vision that my hand was on the bottom knob of a row of bellpulls before I recollected myself; and it was not until a most infernal jangling from within recalled me to my business that I observed underneath it a small brass plate inscribed “Miss Oman.”

===Chinese===
这意外的景象令我如此神魂颠倒，待回过神来，我的手已经搭在一排门铃拉手中最低的那只上；直到屋内爆出一阵要命至极的刺耳铃响，把我唤回正事，我才看见它下面钉着一块小小的铜牌，上刻“奥曼小姐（Miss Oman）”。

===Original===
The door opened with some suddenness and a short, middle-aged woman surveyed me hungrily.

===Chinese===
房门猛地开了，一个矮小的中年妇人带着一副如饥似渴的神气打量我。

===Original===
“Have I rung the wrong bell?” I asked﻿—foolishly enough, I must admit.

===Chinese===
“我是不是拉错铃了？”我问——我得承认，问得实在够蠢。

===Original===
“How can I tell?” she demanded. “I expect you have. It’s the sort of thing a man would do﻿—ring the wrong bell and then say he’s sorry.”

===Chinese===
“我怎么知道？”她质问道，“我看八成是拉错了。男人干的就是这种事——拉错了铃，然后再说声抱歉。”

===Original===
“I didn’t go as far as that,” I retorted. “It seems to have had the desired effect, and I’ve made your acquaintance into the bargain.”

===Chinese===
“我还没走到那一步呢，”我回敬道，“这铃看样子起了预期的效果，而且顺便还结识了您。”

===Original===
“Whom do you want to see?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“你要见谁？”她问。

===Original===
“Mr. Bellingham.”

===Chinese===
“贝林厄姆先生。”

===Original===
“Are you the doctor?”

===Chinese===
“你就是那位大夫吗？”

===Original===
“I’m *a* doctor.”

===Chinese===
“我是*一个*大夫。”

===Original===
“Follow me upstairs,” said Miss Oman, “and don’t tread on the paint.”

===Chinese===
“跟我上楼来，”奥曼小姐说，“别踩着了油漆。”

===Original===
I crossed the spacious hall, and preceded by my conductress, ascended a noble oak staircase, treading carefully on a ribbon of matting that ran up the middle. On the first-floor landing Miss Oman opened a door and, pointing to the room, said, “Go in there and wait; I’ll tell her you’re here.”

===Chinese===
我穿过宽敞的门厅，由这位引路人前头带路，登上一座气派的橡木楼梯，小心翼翼踩着楼梯正中一条窄长的草垫拾级而上。到了二楼平台，奥曼小姐打开一扇门，朝屋里一指：“进去等着；我去告诉她你来了。”

===Original===
“I said *Mr.* Bellingham﻿—” I began; but the door slammed on me, and Miss Oman’s footsteps retreated rapidly down the stairs.

===Chinese===
“我说的是贝林厄姆*先生*——”我刚开了个头；门已砰地冲我关上，奥曼小姐的脚步声急匆匆地退下楼梯去了。

===Original===
It was at once obvious to me that I was in a very awkward position. The room into which I had been shown communicated with another, and though the door of communication was shut, I was unpleasantly aware of a conversation that was taking place in the adjoining room. At first, indeed, only a vague mutter, with a few disjointed phrases, came through the door, but suddenly an angry voice rang out clear and painfully distinct.

===Chinese===
我当即明白，自己的处境十分尴尬。我被引进的这间屋子与另一间相通；虽然隔门关着，隔壁屋里正在进行的谈话仍隐隐可闻，令我很不受用。起初，透过门传来的确实只有一片含混的低语，夹着几个不相连贯的短语，可突然间，一个怒气冲冲的声音陡然响起，清晰得刺耳扎心。

===Original===
“Yes, I did! And I say it again. Bribery! Collusion! That’s what it amounts to. You want to square me!”

===Chinese===
“不错，我就是说了！我还要再说一遍。贿赂！勾结！说穿了就是这么回事。你是想收买我！”

===Original===
“Nothing of the kind, Godfrey,” was the reply in a lower tone; but at this point I coughed emphatically and moved a chair, and the voices subsided once more into an indistinct murmur.

===Chinese===
“绝无此意，戈弗雷，”回答的声调压低了些；但这时我重重咳了一声，又挪了挪椅子，那两个声音便重新低伏下去，化作一片听不真切的咕哝。

===Original===
To distract my attention from my unseen neighbors I glanced curiously about the room and speculated upon the personalities of its occupants. A very curious room it was, with its pathetic suggestion of decayed splendor and old-world dignity; a room full of interest and character and of contrasts and perplexing contradictions. For the most part it spoke of unmistakable though decent poverty. It was nearly bare of furniture, and what little there was was of the cheapest﻿—a small kitchen table and three Windsor chairs (two of them with arms); a threadbare string carpet on the floor, and a cheap cotton cloth on the table; these, with a set of bookshelves, frankly constructed of grocer’s boxes, formed the entire suite. And yet, despite its poverty, the place exhaled an air of homely if rather ascetic comfort, and the taste was irreproachable. The quiet russet of the tablecloth struck a pleasant harmony with the subdued bluish green of the worn carpet; the Windsor chairs and the legs of the table had been carefully denuded of their glaring varnish and stained a sober brown: and the austerity of the whole was relieved by a ginger jar filled with fresh-cut flowers and set in the middle of the table.

===Chinese===
为了把注意力从那素未谋面的邻人身上引开，我好奇地环顾这间屋子，揣想居于其间的是何等人物。这实在是一间古怪的屋子，隐隐透着繁华销歇、旧日尊荣式微的可怜意味；一间充满趣味与个性、也充满反差与费解之矛盾的屋子。大体而言，它道出的是一种明白无误却尚属体面的清贫。屋里家具几近于无，仅有的几件也最是廉价——一张小厨房桌，三把温莎木椅（其中两把带扶手）；地板上铺一块磨秃了的绳绒地毯，桌上罩一块廉价的棉布台布；这些，再加上一套毫不掩饰用杂货铺木箱钉成的书架，便是全部的家具陈设了。然而，尽管清贫，这地方却散发着一股家常的、甚至可说近乎苦行的舒适气息，而且品味无可挑剔。桌布沉静的红褐色与旧地毯黯淡的青绿色相映成趣，令人悦目；几把温莎椅和桌子腿上那层刺眼的亮漆都被细心磨去，改染成素净的棕色；屋子中央桌上供着一只姜罐，插满新剪的鲜花，为满室的简素平添了一分生气。

===Original===
But the contrasts of which I have spoken were most singular and puzzling. There were the bookshelves, for instance, home made and stained at the cost of a few pence, but filled with recent and costly new works on archeology and ancient art. There were the objects on the mantelpiece: a facsimile in bronze﻿—not bronze plaster﻿—of the beautiful head of Hypnos and a pair of fine Ushabti figures. There were the decorations of the walls, a number of etchings﻿—signed proofs, every one of them﻿—of Oriental subjects, and a splendid facsimile reproduction of an Egyptian papyrus. It was incongruous in the extreme, this mingling of costly refinements with the barest and shabbiest necessaries of life, of fastidious culture with manifest poverty. I could make nothing of it. What manner of man, I wondered, was this new patient of mine? Was he a miser, hiding himself and his wealth in this obscure court? An eccentric savant? A philosopher? Or﻿—more probably﻿—a crank? But at this point my meditations were interrupted by the voice from the adjoining room, once more raised in anger.

===Chinese===
然而，我方才所说的种种反差，实在奇特得令人费解。比如那几架书，自家打造、染色不过花了几便士，架上摆的却尽是考古学与古代艺术方面新近出版的昂贵新作。又如壁炉台上的陈设：一尊许普诺斯（Hypnos）美丽头像的青铜复制品——可不是仿铜石膏的——以及一对精美的巫沙布提俑（Ushabti figures）。再看墙上的装饰：一批取材东方风物的蚀刻版画——张张都是签名试印——和一幅埃及纸草文书（Egyptian papyrus）的精美复制卷。这情形不协调到了极点：昂贵的雅致与最粗陋寒酸的生活必需品混作一处，考究的教养与昭然的贫穷凑成一堆。我全然摸不着头脑。我心想，我这位新病人究竟是个怎样的人物？是个守财奴，连人带钱财躲进这条无名僻巷？是个古怪的学者？一位哲人？还是——多半如此——个偏执的怪人？但这时我的沉思被隔壁的声音打断了，那嗓门又怒气冲冲地拔高了。

===Original===
“But I say that you *are* making an accusation! You are implying that I made away with him.”

===Chinese===
“可我说，你这就*是*在指控！你这是在暗示，是我把他干掉了。”

===Original===
“Not at all,” was the reply; “but I repeat that it is your business to ascertain what has become of him. The responsibility rests upon you.”

===Chinese===
“绝无此意，”对方答道，“但我再说一遍：查明他如今下落，是你分内之事。这责任落在你身上。”

===Original===
“Upon me!” rejoined the first voice. “And what about you? Your position is a pretty fishy one if it comes to that.”

===Chinese===
“落在我身上！”头一个声音驳道，“那你自己呢？真要这么论起来，你的立场才着实可疑呢。”

===Original===
“What!” roared the other. “Do you insinuate that I murdered my own brother?”

===Chinese===
“什么！”另一个吼道，“你是想含沙射影，说我谋害了自己的亲兄弟？”

===Original===
During this amazing colloquy I had stood gaping with sheer astonishment. Suddenly I recollected myself, and dropping into a chair, set my elbows on my knees and clapped my hands over my ears; and thus I must have remained for a full minute when I became aware of the closing of a door behind me.

===Chinese===
这场惊人的对谈进行之际，我一直张着嘴呆立原地。猛然间我回过神来，一屁股坐进椅子里，手肘撑膝，双手捂耳；想必就这副模样足足待了一分钟，才察觉身后有一扇门开了又关。

===Original===
I sprang to my feet and turned in some embarrassment (for I must have looked unspeakably ridiculous) to confront the somber figure of a rather tall and strikingly handsome girl, who, as she stood with her hand on the knob of the door, saluted me with a formal bow. In an instantaneous glance I noted how perfectly she matched her strange surroundings. Black-robed, black-haired, with black-gray eyes and a grave sad face of ivory pallor, she stood, like one of old Terborch’s portraits, a harmony in tones so low as to be but one step removed from monochrome. Obviously a lady in spite of the worn and rusty dress, and something in the poise of the head and the set of the straight brows hinted at a spirit that adversity had hardened rather than broken.

===Chinese===
我一跃而起，颇为窘迫地转过身去（我那副模样必定说不出的滑稽），迎面是一位身量颇高、容貌出众的姑娘一身素黑的身影。她一手按着门钮立在那里，向我端端正正行了个礼。电光石火的一瞥间，我便注意到她与这奇特的环境何其相称。黑衣、黑发，黑灰色的眼睛，一张庄重而忧郁的、象牙般苍白的脸——她伫立在那里，宛如老特博赫（Terborch）笔下的一幅肖像，一部色调低沉的和声，低到只差一步便是单色。尽管衣着陈旧泛乌，她显然是位淑女；头颅的仪态与平直双眉的走势间自有某种东西，隐隐透出一种被逆境淬硬而非折断的精神。

===Original===
“I must ask you to forgive me for keeping you waiting,” she said; and as she spoke a certain softening at the corners of the austere mouth reminded me of the absurd position in which she had found me.

===Chinese===
“让您久等，务请见谅。”她说；说话时，那张严冷的嘴角浮起一丝柔和，倒提醒我想起自己方才被她撞见的可笑处境。

===Original===
I murmured that the trifling delay was of no consequence whatever; that I had, in fact, been rather glad of the rest; and I was beginning somewhat vaguely to approach the subject of the invalid when the voice from the adjoining room again broke forth with hideous distinctness.

===Chinese===
我含糊应道，这点区区耽搁实在算不得什么；还说我其实巴不得借此歇上一歇；我正不着边际地想引到病人的正题，隔壁屋里的声音又爆发出来，清晰得可怖。

===Original===
“I tell you I’ll do nothing of the kind! Why, confound you, it’s nothing less than a conspiracy that you’re proposing!”

===Chinese===
“告诉你，这种事我才不干！哼，你这该死的，你提的根本就是一场阴谋！”

===Original===
Miss Bellingham﻿—as I assumed her to be﻿—stepped quickly across the floor, flushing angrily, as well she might; but, as she reached the door, it flew open and a small, spruce, middle-aged man burst into the room.

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆小姐——我猜想必是她——快步穿过房间，气得满脸通红，这也难怪；可她刚走到门边，门猛地开了，一个矮小利落的中年男子一头闯进屋来。

===Original===
“Your father is mad, Ruth!” he exclaimed; “absolutely stark mad! And I refuse to hold any further communication with him.”

===Chinese===
“你父亲疯了，露丝（Ruth）！”他嚷道，“彻头彻尾地疯了！我拒绝再与他有任何来往。”

===Original===
“The present interview was not of his seeking,” Miss Bellingham replied coldly.

===Chinese===
“眼下这次会面可不是他要来的。”贝林厄姆小姐冷冷答道。

===Original===
“No, it was not,” was the wrathful rejoinder; “it was my mistaken generosity. But there﻿—what is the use of talking? I’ve done my best for you and I’ll do no more. Don’t trouble to let me out; I can find my way. Good morning.” With a stiff bow and a quick glance at me, the speaker strode out of the room, banging the door after him.

===Chinese===
“不错，不是，”他怒气冲冲地回敬，“是我发错了善心。不过算了——还有什么可说的？我已为你们竭尽绵力，往后也不再管了。不必费心送我；路我自己认得。早安。”说话人僵硬地一躬身，又朝我飞快瞥了一眼，大步走出屋去，随手把门砰地摔上。

===Original===
“I must apologize for this extraordinary reception,” said Miss Bellingham; “but I believe medical men are not easily astonished. I will introduce you to your patient now.” She opened the door and, as I followed her into the adjoining room, she said: “Here is another visitor for you, dear. Doctor﻿—”

===Chinese===
“这般非同寻常的迎接，我必须向您致歉，”贝林厄姆小姐说，“不过我相信，行医之人是不容易大惊小怪的。我这就领您去见您的病人。”她推开门，当我随她走进隔壁房间时，她说道：“亲爱的，又来了一位客人看你。大夫是——”

===Original===
“Berkeley,” said I. “I am acting for my friend Doctor Barnard.”

===Chinese===
“伯克利，”我说，“我正替我朋友巴纳德大夫代诊。”

===Original===
The invalid, a fine-looking man of about fifty-five, who sat propped up in bed with a pile of pillows, held out an excessively shaky hand, which I grasped cordially, making a mental note of the tremor.

===Chinese===
病人是个相貌堂堂的男人，约莫五十五岁，靠一堆枕头支着半坐在床上，向我伸出一只抖得厉害的手；我热诚地一握，心里暗暗记下这震颤。

===Original===
“How do you do, sir?” said Mr. Bellingham. “I hope Doctor Barnard is not ill.”

===Chinese===
“您好，先生。”贝林厄姆先生说，“巴纳德大夫没病着吧？”

===Original===
“Oh, no,” I answered; “he has gone for a trip down the Mediterranean on a currant ship. The chance occurred rather suddenly, and I bustled him off before he had time to change his mind. Hence my rather unceremonious appearance, which I hope you will forgive.”

===Chinese===
“哦，没有，”我答道，“他乘葡萄干货船游地中海去了。机会来得颇突然，我趁他还没来得及回心转意，就赶紧把他打发上了船。所以我这回登门未免唐突了些，还望您宽宥。”

===Original===
“Not at all,” was the hearty response. “I’m delighted to hear that you sent him off; he wanted a holiday, poor man. And I am delighted to make your acquaintance, too.”

===Chinese===
“哪儿的话，”回应热诚恳切，“听说你把他送走了，我很高兴；他早该歇歇了，可怜人。能结识你，我同样高兴。”

===Original===
“It is very good of you,” I said; whereupon he bowed as gracefully as a man may who is propped up in bed with a heap of pillows; and having thus exchanged broadsides of civility, so to speak, we﻿—or, at least, I﻿—proceeded to business.

===Chinese===
“您太客气了。”我说；于是他尽量优雅地欠身还了一礼——一个靠一堆枕头支在床上的男人所能摆出的优雅，也不过如此；你一来我一往，如此互放了一轮客套的排炮之后，可以说，我们——至少是我——便转入正题。

===Original===
“How long have you been laid up?” I asked cautiously, not wishing to make too evident the fact that my principal had given me no information respecting his case.

===Chinese===
“您卧床多久了？”我谨慎地问，不愿过分显扬一个事实：我的委托人根本不曾向我交代过他的病情。

===Original===
“A week today,” he replied. “The *fons et origo mali* was a hansom-cab which upset me opposite the Law Courts﻿—sent me sprawling in the middle of the road. My own fault, of course﻿—at least, the cabby said so, and I suppose he knew. But that was no consolation to me.”

===Chinese===
“到今天整一个星期。”他答道，“祸源（*fons et origo mali*）是一辆双轮马车，在皇家法院（the Law Courts）对面把我掀了个跟头——摔得我四仰八叉躺在大路当中。自然是我自己的不是——至少车夫是这么说的，我想他总归清楚。可这于我算不得什么安慰。”

===Original===
“Were you hurt much?”

===Chinese===
“伤得重吗？”

===Original===
“No, not really; but the fall bruised my knee rather badly and gave me a deuce of a shake up. I’m too old for that sort of thing, you know.”

===Chinese===
“不，倒也不算重；可这一跤把膝盖磕得相当厉害，还把我结结实实震了一通。您知道，我这把年纪，禁不起这种折腾了。”

===Original===
“Most people are,” said I.

===Chinese===
“大多数人也都禁不起。”我说。

===Original===
“True; but you can take a cropper more gracefully at twenty than at fifty-five. However, the knee is getting on quite well﻿—you shall see it presently﻿—and you observe that I am giving it complete rest. But that isn’t the whole of the trouble or the worst of it. It’s my confounded nerves. I’m as irritable as the devil and as nervous as a cat. And I can’t get a decent night’s rest.”

===Chinese===
“此话不假；不过二十岁上摔个跟头，总比五十五岁上摔得体面些。好在膝盖复原得挺不错——回头让你瞧瞧——你也看得出，我正让它全然静养。可毛病并不全在这里，这也不是最糟的。是我那些该死的神经。我暴躁得像魔鬼，又惊惶得像只猫。夜里连一个囫囵觉也睡不成。”

===Original===
I recalled the tremulous hand that he had offered me. He did not look like a drinker, but still﻿—

===Chinese===
我想起他方才伸给我的那只颤巍巍的手。他看着不像贪杯之徒，不过——

===Original===
“Do you smoke much?” I inquired diplomatically.

===Chinese===
“您烟抽得凶吗？”我婉转地探问。

===Original===
He looked at me slyly and chuckled. “That’s a very delicate way to approach the subject, Doctor,” he said. “No, I don’t smoke much, and I don’t crook my little finger. I saw you look at my shaky hand just now﻿—oh, it’s all right; I’m not offended. It’s a doctor’s business to keep his eyelids lifting. But my hand is steady enough as a rule, when I’m not upset, but the least excitement sets me shaking like a jelly. And the fact is that I have just had a deucedly unpleasant interview﻿—”

===Chinese===
他狡黠地看了我一眼，嘿嘿一笑。“大夫，您这题目切得可真委婉。”他说，“不，我烟抽得不多，酒也是一滴不沾。方才我就瞧见您看我这只发抖的手了——哦，不妨事；我不见怪。当大夫的，本分就是眼皮勤抬、时刻留神。我这只手平日里倒也拿得稳，只要不心绪不宁；可稍一激动，便抖得像块果子冻。实情是，我刚经历了一场糟透了的面谈——”

===Original===
“I think,” Miss Bellingham interrupted, “Doctor Berkeley and, indeed, the neighborhood at large, are aware of the fact.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”贝林厄姆小姐插言道，“伯克利大夫——说实话，还有这一带左邻右舍——都已经知道这个事实了。”

===Original===
Mr. Bellingham laughed rather shamefacedly. “I’m afraid I did lose my temper,” he said; “but I am an impulsive old fellow, Doctor, and when I’m put out I’m apt to speak my mind﻿—a little too bluntly perhaps.”

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆先生笑得颇有些讪然。“恐怕我方才确是动了肝火，”他说，“可我是个急性子的老家伙，大夫，一被人惹恼，就管不住要直抒胸臆——兴许还太直白了些。”

===Original===
“And audibly,” his daughter added. “Do you know that Doctor Berkeley was reduced to the necessity of stopping his ears?” She glanced at me as she spoke, with something like a twinkle in her solemn gray eyes.

===Chinese===
“而且嗓门还挺大。”他女儿补了一句，“您可知道，伯克利大夫都被逼到只好捂住耳朵的地步了？”说话间她向我瞟了一眼，那双端凝的灰眼睛里隐约闪过一丝笑谑。

===Original===
“Did I shout?” Mr. Bellingham asked, not very contritely, I thought, though he added: “I’m very sorry, my dear; but it won’t happen again. I think we’ve seen the last of that good gentleman.”

===Chinese===
“我嚷嚷了？”贝林厄姆先生问，依我看，悔意颇为有限，虽然他又添了一句：“真抱歉，亲爱的；不过不会再有了。我想，那位好先生咱们已经见着他的最后一面了。”

===Original===
“I am sure I hope so,” she rejoined, adding: “And now I will leave you to your talk; I shall be in the next room if you should want me.”

===Chinese===
“但愿如此，这是肯定的。”她应道，又补充说：“现在我把你们留给正事吧；若需要我，我就在隔壁屋里。”

===Original===
I opened the door for her, and when she had passed out with a stiff little bow I seated myself by the bedside and resumed the consultation. It was evidently a case of nervous breakdown, to which the cab accident had, no doubt, contributed. As to the other antecedents, they were of no concern of mine, though Mr. Bellingham seemed to think otherwise, for he resumed: “That cab business was the last straw, you know, and it finished me off, but I have been going down the hill for a long time. I’ve had a lot of trouble during the last two years. But I suppose I oughtn’t to pester you with the details of my personal affairs.”

===Chinese===
我替她拉开门，她微微一躬、略显拘谨地出去之后，我便在床沿坐下，重新开始问诊。这显然是一桩神经衰弱的病症，那场马车事故无疑也推了一把。至于其他种种前因，本与我无干，虽然贝林厄姆先生似乎另有想法，因为他又说了下去：“你可知道，那场马车事故是最后压下来的一根稻草，把我彻底压垮了；其实我走下坡路已经很久。这两年来我烦心事太多了。不过我想，总不该拿我私事的种种细节来烦扰您。”

===Original===
“Anything that bears on your present state of health is of interest to me if you don’t mind telling it,” I said.

===Chinese===
“凡是关系到您目前健康状况的事，只要您愿意讲，我都乐意一听。”我说。

===Original===
“Mind!” he exclaimed. “Did you ever meet an invalid who didn’t enjoy talking about his own health? It’s the listener who minds, as a rule.”

===Chinese===
“愿不愿意！”他叫起来，“你可曾见过哪个病人不喜欢絮谈自己健康的？照例嫌烦的，是听的那一位。”

===Original===
“Well, the present listener doesn’t,” I said.

===Chinese===
“嗯，眼下听讲的这一位就不嫌。”我说。

===Original===
“Then,” said Mr. Bellingham, “I’ll treat myself to the luxury of telling you all my troubles; I don’t often get the chance of a confidential grumble to a responsible man of my own class. And I really have some excuses for railing at Fortune, as you will agree when I tell you that, a couple of years ago, I went to bed one night a gentleman of independent means and excellent prospects and woke up in the morning to find myself practically a beggar. Not a cheerful experience that, you know, at my time of life, eh?”

===Chinese===
“那么，”贝林厄姆先生说，“我就破费一回，享用一番向您倾诉全部烦恼的奢侈；能有机会向一位我本阶层的体面人吐一通心腹牢骚，这机会我可难得碰上。何况我也着实有理由咒骂几句命运女神——等我说完您自会同意：两年前的一个晚上，我上床时还是个不愁生计、前程似锦的绅士，一觉醒来，早上竟发现自己实际上成了个乞丐。您知道，在我这把年纪，这可不是什么愉快的经历，嗯？”

===Original===
“No,” I agreed, “nor at any other.”

===Chinese===
“可不是，”我表示同意，“搁在哪个年纪也都不是。”

===Original===
“And that was not all,” he continued; “for at the same moment I lost my brother, my dearest, kindest friend. He disappeared﻿—vanished off the face of the earth; but perhaps you have heard of the affair. The confounded papers were full of it at the time.”

===Chinese===
“还不止于此呢，”他接着说，“因为在同一刻，我还失去了我的兄弟，我最亲爱、最仁厚的挚友。他失踪了——从这世上的地面上消失得无影无踪；不过这桩案子您或许已有耳闻。当时那些该死的报纸连篇累牍，满篇都是这事。”

===Original===
He paused abruptly, noticing, no doubt, a sudden change in my face. Of course I recollected the case now. Indeed, ever since I had entered the house some chord of memory had been faintly vibrating, and now his last words had struck out the full note.

===Chinese===
他蓦地顿住，无疑是察觉到我脸上骤然一变。当然，我此刻想起这案子了。说真的，打从踏进这座宅子起，记忆中的某根弦便一直在隐隐颤动，而今他末了这几句话，终于把全音敲响了。

===Original===
“Yes,” I said, “I remember the incident, though I don’t suppose I should but for the fact that our lecturer on medical jurisprudence drew my attention to it.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”我说，“我记得这件事，不过，若不是我们那位法医学讲师曾引我留意此案，我怕是想不起来。”

===Original===
“Indeed,” said Mr. Bellingham, rather uneasily, as I fancied. “What did he say about it?”

===Chinese===
“哦？”贝林厄姆先生说，在我看去颇有些不安，“他对此是怎么说的？”

===Original===
“He referred to it as a case that was calculated to give rise to some very pretty legal complications.”

===Chinese===
“他把它说成是一个势必引出种种漂亮法律纠纷的案子。”

===Original===
“By Jove!” exclaimed Bellingham, “that man was a prophet! Legal complications, indeed! But I’ll be bound he never guessed at the sort of infernal tangle that has actually gathered round the affair. By the way, what was his name?”

===Chinese===
“天哪！”贝林厄姆失声叫道，“那人是个先知！可不，法律纠纷！可我敢打包票，他绝猜不到这桩案子周围实际纠结起了一团怎样地狱般的乱麻。对了，他叫什么名字？”

===Original===
“Thorndyke,” I replied. “Doctor John Thorndyke.”

===Chinese===
“桑代克，”我答道，“约翰·桑代克博士。”

===Original===
“Thorndyke,” Mr. Bellingham repeated in a musing, retrospective tone. “I seem to remember the name. Yes, of course. I have heard a legal friend of mine, a Mr. Marchmont, speak of him in reference to the case of a man whom I knew slightly years ago﻿—a certain Jeffrey Blackmore, who also disappeared very mysteriously. I remember now that Dr. Thorndyke unraveled that case with most remarkable ingenuity.”

===Chinese===
“桑代克，”贝林厄姆先生用一种沉吟追怀的口吻重复道，“这名字我似乎记得。啊，对了。我听我一位法律界的朋友——一位马奇蒙特先生（Mr. Marchmont）——提起过他，说的是一桩案子，案子里那人我多年前略有往来——某个杰弗里·布莱克莫尔（Jeffrey Blackmore），他也是失踪得极其神秘。我现在想起来了，桑代克博士解开那桩案子，手法之精妙令人叹服。”

===Original===
“I daresay he would be very much interested to hear about your case,” I suggested.

===Chinese===
“我想，他若听说您的案子，准会极感兴趣。”我进言道。

===Original===
“I daresay he would,” was the reply; “but one can’t take up a professional man’s time for nothing, and I couldn’t afford to pay him. And that reminds me that I’m taking up your time by gossiping about purely personal affairs.”

===Chinese===
“我想也是，”他答道，“可谁也不能白白占用一位专业人士的时间，而我出不起他的酬金。这么一说倒提醒我了——我正拿纯粹的家务私事闲扯，占着您的时间呢。”

===Original===
“My morning round is finished,” said I, “and, moreover, your personal affairs are highly interesting. I suppose I mustn’t ask what is the nature of the legal entanglement?”

===Chinese===
“我上午的巡回出诊已经完了，”我说，“再说，您的家务私事实在引人入胜。那桩法律纠葛究竟是什么性质，我大概不便动问吧？”

===Original===
“Not unless you are prepared to stay here for the rest of the day and go home a raving lunatic. But I’ll tell you this much: the trouble is about my poor brother’s will. In the first place it can’t be administered because there is not sufficient evidence that my brother is dead; and in the second place, if it could, all the property would go to people who were never intended to benefit. The will itself is the most diabolically exasperating document that was ever produced by the perverted ingenuity of a wrongheaded man. That’s all. Will you have a look at my knee?”

===Chinese===
“除非您预备在这里从早坐到晚，回家时变成个满口疯话的躁狂病人。不过这么多总是可以奉告的：麻烦出在我那可怜兄弟的遗嘱上。第一，遗嘱无法执行，因为没有足够的证据证明我兄弟已死；第二，退一步说纵然能够执行，全部产业也将尽数落入从来无人打算使之受益的人手中。这份遗嘱本身，乃是一个乖张人的偏执聪明所能炮制出的最最气煞人的文件。要说的就是这些。您来瞧瞧我的膝盖如何？”

===Original===
As Mr. Bellingham’s explanation (delivered in a rapid crescendo and ending almost in a shout) had left him purple-faced and trembling, I thought it best to bring our talk to an end. Accordingly I proceeded to inspect the injured knee, which was now nearly well, and to overhaul my patient generally; and having given him detailed instructions as to his general conduct, I rose and took my leave.

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆先生这一番解说——语声渐急渐高，末了几乎成了吼叫——已说得他面皮发紫、周身发颤，我想还是就此收束为妙。于是我着手查验那受伤的膝盖——它现已近乎痊愈——又给我的病人做了一番总体检视；就日常起居向他详细嘱咐一通之后，我便起身告辞。

===Original===
“And remember,” I said as I shook his hand, “No tobacco, no coffee, no excitement of any kind. Lead a quiet, bovine life.”

===Chinese===
“还有，记住，”我握着他的手说，“不吸烟，不喝咖啡，不沾任何刺激。过一种恬静如牛的日子。”

===Original===
“That’s all very well,” he grumbled, “but supposing people come here and excite me?”

===Chinese===
“说起来容易，”他咕哝道，“可要是有人偏上这儿来刺激我呢？”

===Original===
“Disregard them,” said I, “and read *Whitaker’s Almanac*.” And with this parting advice I passed out into the other room.

===Chinese===
“只当没看见，”我说，“只管读《惠特克年鉴》（*Whitaker's Almanac*）。”留下这句临别赠言，我便穿出到外间去了。

===Original===
Miss Bellingham was seated at the table with a pile of blue-covered notebooks before her, two of which were open, displaying pages closely written in a small, neat handwriting. She rose as I entered and looked at me inquiringly.

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆小姐正坐在桌前，面前摊着一摞蓝皮笔记本，其中两本敞开着，露出写满小巧工整字迹的密密书页。我进去时她起身相迎，用探询的目光望着我。

===Original===
“I heard you advising my father to read *Whitaker’s Almanac*,” she said. “Was that a curative measure?”

===Chinese===
“我听见您劝我父亲读《惠特克年鉴》了。”她说，“这也是一项治疗措施吗？”

===Original===
“Entirely,” I replied. “I recommended it for its medicinal virtues, as an antidote to mental excitement.”

===Chinese===
“纯粹是治疗。”我答道，“我推荐它是取其药性，用作精神亢奋的解药。”

===Original===
She smiled faintly. “It certainly is not a highly emotional book,” she said, and then asked: “Have you any other instructions to give?”

===Chinese===
她浅浅一笑。“那确实算不上一本叫人情绪激动的书。”她说，随即问道：“您可还有别的嘱咐？”

===Original===
“Well, I might give the conventional advice﻿—to maintain a cheerful outlook and avoid worry; but I don’t suppose you would find it very helpful.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，我本可以送上一句俗套的忠告——保持乐观心境，避免忧烦；不过我想，您听了怕也不会觉得有多大用处。”

===Original===
“No,” she answered bitterly; “it is a counsel of perfection. People in our position are not a very cheerful class, I’m afraid; but still they don’t seek out worries from sheer perverseness. The worries come unsought. But, of course, you can’t enter into that.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”她苦涩地答道，“那是一条只有圣人做得到的忠告。只怕处在我们这种境地的人，算不得乐观的一类；不过话说回来，他们倒也并非纯粹出于别扭才自寻烦恼。烦恼总是不请自来。当然，这层滋味，您是无从体会的。”

===Original===
“I can’t give any practical help, I fear, though I do sincerely hope that you father’s affairs will straighten themselves out soon.”

===Chinese===
“实际的帮助，我怕是给不上了，”我说，“不过我确实诚心希望，令尊的事务不久便能理顺。”

===Original===
She thanked me for my good wishes and accompanied me down to the street door, where, with a bow and a rather stiff handshake, she gave me my *congé*.

===Chinese===
她谢过我的好意，陪我一路下楼送到临街大门；在那里，她一躬身，与我略嫌生硬地握了握手，便对我下了逐客令（*congé*）。

===Original===
Very ungratefully the noise of Fetter Lane smote on my ears as I came out through the archway, and very squalid and unrestful the little street looked when contrasted with the dignity and monastic quiet of the old garden. As to the surgery, with its oilcloth floor and walls made hideous with gaudy insurance show-cards in sham gilt frames, its aspect was so revolting that I flew to the daybook for distraction, and was still busily entering the morning’s visits when the bottle-boy, Adolphus, entered stealthily to announce lunch.

===Chinese===
从拱门下走出来，菲特巷的喧嚣很不识相地扑击着我的耳膜；与那座老花园的庄重和修道院般的岑寂两相对照，这条小巷显得何等寒碜而不得安宁。至于那间诊室——油布铺的地板，墙上挂满俗艳的保险招贴画，配着冒牌镀金的框子，丑得不堪入目——那副景象实在倒人胃口，我只得逃到日志簿里去寻解脱；正当我还忙着登记上午的出诊记录时，配药小厮阿道弗斯（Adolphus）蹑手蹑脚地走进来，传报午饭已好。

## III John Thorndyke / 三 约翰·桑代克

===Original===
That the character of an individual tends to be reflected in his dress is a fact familiar to the least observant. That the observation is equally applicable to aggregates of men is less familiar, but equally true. Do not the members of fighting professions, even to this day, deck themselves in feathers, in gaudy colors and gilded ornaments, after the manner of the African war-chief or the Redskin “brave,” and thereby indicate the place of war in modern civilization? Does not the Church of Rome send her priests to the altar in habiliments that were fashionable before the fall of the Roman Empire, in token of her immovable conservatism? And, lastly, does not the Law, lumbering on in the wake of progress, symbolize its subjection to precedent by headgear reminiscent of the good days of Queen Anne?

===Chinese===
一个人的品性往往会反映在他的衣着上，这是连最不留意的人也熟悉的事实。这条观察同样适用于人的群体，便不那么为人熟知了，却同样成立。时至今日，行伍中人不还是以非洲战酋或红种印第安人“勇士”的做派，拿羽毛、艳色和镀金饰物装点自己，从而标明战争在现代文明中的地位么？罗马教会不还是让她的祭司穿着罗马帝国倾覆之前便已时兴的祭服走上圣坛，以示其不可动摇的保守么？最后，法律界蹒跚地跟在进步的后面，不也用令人追忆安妮女王盛世的那号头饰，来象征它对先例的臣服么？

===Original===
I should apologize for intruding upon the reader these somewhat trite reflections; which were set going by the quaint stock-in-trade of the wig-maker’s shop in the cloisters of the Inner Temple, whither I strayed on a sultry afternoon in quest of shade and quiet. I had halted opposite the little shop window, and, with my eyes bent dreamily on the row of wigs, was pursuing the above train of thought when I was startled by a deep voice saying softly in my ear: “I’d have the full-bottomed one if I were you.”

===Chinese===
把这些略显老生常谈的感想强加于读者，我本该致歉；引动这番感想的，是内殿（Inner Temple）回廊里那家假发店中一排古怪的货色——一个闷热的下午，我为了觅些荫凉与清静，信步走到了那里。我在那小小的橱窗前站住，双眼梦幻似的凝视着一排假发，正顺着上述思绪想下去，忽听得一个低沉的嗓音在我耳边轻轻说道：“换作是我，就买那顶大垂肩的。”

===Original===
I turned swiftly and rather fiercely, and looked into the face of my old friend and fellow student, Jervis; behind whom, regarding us with a sedate smile, stood my former teacher, Dr. John Thorndyke. Both men greeted me with a warmth that I felt to be very flattering, for Thorndyke was quite a great personage, and even Jervis was several years my academic senior.

===Chinese===
我猛地、且颇带几分凶相地转过身去，正对上我的老友兼同窗杰维斯（Jervis）的脸；他身后立着我从前的老师约翰·桑代克博士，正带着沉稳的微笑望着我们两位。两人向我致意时的热忱令我颇觉受宠若惊，因为桑代克已是一位相当了不起的人物，就连杰维斯，在学龄上也比我高了好几级。

===Original===
“You are coming in to have a cup of tea with us, I hope,” said Thorndyke; and as I assented gladly, he took my arm and led me across the court in the direction of the Treasury.

===Chinese===
“我想，你这就进来跟我们喝杯茶吧。”桑代克说；我欣然应允，他便挽起我的手臂，领我穿过庭院，朝财务楼（the Treasury）的方向走去。

===Original===
“But why that hungry gaze at those forensic vanities, Berkeley?” he asked. “Are you thinking of following my example and Jervis’s﻿—deserting the bedside for the Bar?”

===Chinese===
“可是伯克利，你为何那样眼馋地盯着这些法庭上的虚荣行头？”他问，“莫非你想效仿我和杰维斯的榜样——抛下病榻，改投律师席？”

===Original===
“What! Has Jervis gone in for the law?” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“什么！杰维斯改行学法律了？”我失声叫道。

===Original===
“Bless you, yes!” replied Jervis. “I have become parasitical on Thorndyke! ‘The big fleas have little fleas,’ you know. I am the additional fraction trailing after the whole number in the rear of a decimal point.”

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛！”杰维斯答道，“我如今寄生在桑代克身上啦！你知道那句老话——‘大跳蚤身上还有小跳蚤’。我就是小数点后面缀在整数后头的那点零头。”

===Original===
“Don’t you believe him, Berkeley,” interposed Thorndyke. “He is the brains of the firm. I supply the respectability and moral worth. But you haven’t answered my question. What are you doing here on a summer afternoon staring into a wig-maker’s window?”

===Chinese===
“别信他的，伯克利。”桑代克插话道，“他才是本事务所的智囊。体面与道德声望由我来充数。可你还没回答我的问题呢：一个大夏天的下午，你站在假发店橱窗前盯着看什么？”

===Original===
“I am Barnard’s locum; he is in practise in Fetter Lane.”

===Chinese===
“我是替巴纳德代诊的；他就在菲特巷开业。”

===Original===
“I know,” said Thorndyke; “we meet him occasionally, and very pale and peaky he has been looking of late. Is he taking a holiday?”

===Chinese===
“我知道，”桑代克说，“我们偶尔碰见他，近来他看上去脸色苍白、形容憔悴。他这是去休假了么？”

===Original===
“Yes. He has gone for a trip to the Isles of Greece in a currant ship.”

===Chinese===
“是啊。他乘运葡萄干的货船去希腊诸岛旅行了。”

===Original===
“Then,” said Jervis, “you are actually a local G.P. I thought you were looking beastly respectable.”

===Chinese===
“那么，”杰维斯说，“你当真是个坐地行医的全科大夫了。我还只觉得你看上去体面得要命呢。”

===Original===
“And judging from your leisured manner when we encountered you,” added Thorndyke, “the practise is not a strenuous one. I suppose it is entirely local?”

===Chinese===
“再说，从我们撞见你时那副闲散劲儿来看，”桑代克又道，“这诊务也繁忙不到哪儿去。我想病人全都在左近一带吧？”

===Original===
“Yes,” I replied. “The patients mostly live in the small streets and courts within a half-mile radius of the surgery, and the abodes of some of them are pretty squalid. Oh! and that reminds me of a very strange coincidence. It will interest you, I think.”

===Chinese===
“正是，”我答道，“病人大半住在诊所以半英里为半径的小街陋巷之内，有几户的住处实在寒碜得很。哦！这倒提醒了我，有一桩极其奇怪的巧合。我想，你们听了准有兴趣。”

===Original===
“Life is made up of strange coincidences,” said Thorndyke. “Nobody but a reviewer of novels is ever really surprised at a coincidence. But what is yours?”

===Chinese===
“人生本就是由离奇的巧合拼成的，”桑代克说，“除了给小说挑错的评论家，谁也不会真叫巧合吓一大跳。你那桩是怎样的？”

===Original===
“It is connected with a case that you mentioned to us at the hospital about two years ago, the case of a man who disappeared under rather mysterious circumstances. Do you remember it? The man’s name was Bellingham.”

===Chinese===
“它牵涉到大约两年前您在医院里向我们提起过的一桩案子——一个人在相当神秘的情形下失踪了。您还记得么？那人姓贝林厄姆。”

===Original===
“The Egyptologist? Yes, I remember the case quite well. What about it?”

===Chinese===
“那位埃及学家？记得，这案子我记得清清楚楚。它怎么了？”

===Original===
“The brother is a patient of mine. He is living in Nevill’s Court with his daughter, and they seem to be as poor as church mice.”

===Chinese===
“他弟弟如今是我的病人，带着女儿住在内维尔巷，父女俩看样子穷得像教堂里的老鼠。”

===Original===
“Really,” said Thorndyke, “this is quite interesting. They must have come down in the world rather suddenly. If I remember rightly, the brother was living in a house of some pretentions standing in its own grounds.”

===Chinese===
“真的么，”桑代克说，“这可很有意思。他们必定是相当突然地家道中落的。如果我没记错，那位弟弟当时住在一所自带庭园、颇为气派的宅子里。”

===Original===
“Yes, that is so. I see you recollect all about the case.”

===Chinese===
“不错，正是这样。看来这案子的一切您都记得。”

===Original===
“My dear fellow,” said Jervis, “Thorndyke never forgets a likely case. He is a sort of medico-legal camel. He gulps down the raw facts from the newspapers or elsewhere, and then, in his leisure moments, he calmly regurgitates them and has a quiet chew at them. It is a quaint habit. A case crops up in the papers or in one of the courts, and Thorndyke swallows it whole. Then it lapses and everyone forgets it. A year or two later it crops up in a new form, and, to your astonishment, you find that Thorndyke has got it all cut and dried. He has been ruminating on it periodically in the interval.

===Chinese===
“亲爱的伙计，”杰维斯说，“凡是日后可能有点苗头的案子，桑代克从来不忘。他算得上一头法医学的骆驼。他把报纸上或别处的生料事实囫囵吞下，然后到了闲暇时分，再从容不迫地反刍上来，慢慢咀嚼一番。这是个古怪的习惯。一件案子在报上或某个法院里冒了头，桑代克便把它整个儿吞了下去。随后案子沉寂下去，人人都把它忘了。一两年后它换了个面目重新冒头，你会惊讶地发现，桑代克早已把一切料理得妥妥帖帖。原来这段空当里，他一直在周期性地对它反刍呢。

===Original===
“You notice,” said Thorndyke, “that my learned friend is pleased to indulge in mixed metaphors. But his statement is substantially true, though obscurely worded. You must tell us more about the Bellinghams when we have fortified you with a cup of tea.”

===Chinese===
“你注意到没有，”桑代克说，“我这位博学的朋友很喜欢玩弄混搭的比喻。不过他的话虽措辞晦涩，实质却不错。等一杯茶给你补充了气力，你必须多给我们讲讲贝林厄姆家的事。”

===Original===
Our talk had brought us to Thorndyke’s chambers, which were on the first floor of No. 5A, King’s Bench Walk, and as we entered the fine, spacious, paneled room we found a small, elderly man, neatly dressed in black, setting out the tea-service on the table. I glanced at him with some curiosity. He hardly looked like a servant, in spite of his neat, black clothes; in fact, his appearance was rather puzzling, for while his quiet dignity and his serious intelligent face suggested some kind of professional man, his neat, capable hands were those of a skilled mechanic.

===Chinese===
一路谈说着，我们已到了桑代克的寓所——就在王座步道五号A（5A King's Bench Walk）的二楼；走进那间雅致宽敞、镶着壁板的屋子，只见一个矮小的上了年纪的男人一身整洁的黑衣裳，正在桌上摆放茶具。我带着几分好奇打量他。尽管一身利落的黑衣，他实在不大像个仆役；说实在的，他的模样颇令人费解：他那沉静的气度、那张严肃而聪慧的脸，像是一位什么专业的先生，可他那双干净利落、十分能干的手，却又是熟练技工的手。

===Original===
Thorndyke surveyed the tea-tray thoughtfully and then looked at his retainer. “I see you have put three teacups, Polton,” he said. “Now, how did you know I was bringing someone in to tea?”

===Chinese===
桑代克若有所思地打量了一下茶盘，随后看向他的老仆。“我看你摆了三只茶杯，波尔顿（Polton），”他说，“你是怎么知道我要带个人回来喝茶的？”

===Original===
The little man smiled a quaint, crinkly smile of gratification as he explained:

===Chinese===
那小个子绽开一个古里古怪、起了满面皱纹的得意微笑，解释道：

===Original===
“I happened to look out of the laboratory window as you turned the corner, sir.”

===Chinese===
“二位转过街角的时候，我恰好从实验室的窗口望了出去，先生。”

===Original===
“How disappointingly simple,” said Jervis. “We were hoping for something abstruse and telepathic.”

===Chinese===
“简单得叫人失望，”杰维斯说，“我们还巴望着是什么玄奥的心灵感应呢。”

===Original===
“Simplicity is the soul of efficiency, sir,” replied Polton as he checked the tea-service to make sure that nothing was forgotten, and with this remarkable aphorism he silently evaporated.

===Chinese===
“简单是效率之魂，先生。”波尔顿答道，一面把茶具清点一遍，确保样样齐备；撂下这句不同凡响的格言，他便无声无息地蒸发了。

===Original===
“To return to the Bellingham case,” said Thorndyke, when he had poured out the tea. “Have you picked up any facts relating to the parties﻿—and facts, I mean, of course, that it would be proper for you to mention?”

===Chinese===
“咱们回到贝林厄姆的案子上来吧，”桑代克倒好了茶，说道，“你可曾拾得什么与当事各方有关的事实——当然，我指的是你方便明说的那种事实？”

===Original===
“I have learned one or two things that there is no harm in repeating. For instance, I gather that Godfrey Bellingham﻿—my patient﻿—lost all his property quite suddenly about the time of the disappearance.”

===Chinese===
“我听说了一两件转述无妨的事。比如，我得知戈弗雷·贝林厄姆——我的病人——大约就在失踪案发生的当口，突然之间丧失了全部产业。”

===Original===
“That is really odd,” said Thorndyke. “The opposite condition would be quite understandable, but one doesn’t see exactly how this can have happened, unless there was an allowance of some sort.”

===Chinese===
“这就着实奇怪了，”桑代克说，“若是情形相反，倒完全说得通；可除非其中有什么津贴之类的安排，否则实在看不出这事怎么可能发生。”

===Original===
“No, that was what struck me. But there seem to be some queer features in the case, and the legal position is evidently getting complicated. There is a will, for example, which is giving trouble.”

===Chinese===
“可不是，令我惊奇的也正是这一点。不过这案子里似乎有些蹊跷的地方，法律上的处境显然正变得复杂起来。比如有一份遗嘱，正在惹麻烦。”

===Original===
“They will hardly be able to administer the will without either proof or presumption of death,” Thorndyke remarked.

===Chinese===
“若没有死亡的证明或推定，这遗嘱怕是难以执行。”桑代克评道。

===Original===
“Exactly. That’s one of the difficulties. Another is that there seems to be some fatal defect in the drafting of the will itself. I don’t know what it is, but I expect I shall hear sooner or later. By the way, I mentioned the interest that you have taken in the case, and I think Bellingham would have liked to consult you, but, of course, the poor devil has no money.”

===Chinese===
“正是。这是难处之一。另一个难处是，遗嘱本身的拟稿似乎有某种致命缺陷。我不知道缺陷何在，不过我想迟早总会听说。顺便说一句，我提起了您对此案的关切，我想贝林厄姆本是很乐意向您请教的，只是——这可怜鬼自然是没有钱的。”

===Original===
“That is awkward for him if the other interested parties have. There will probably be legal proceedings of some kind, and as the law takes no account of poverty, he is likely to go to the wall. He ought to have advice of some sort.”

===Chinese===
“如果其他的利害当事人手头有钱，那他就被动了。这案子多半会闹出某种法律程序，而法律是不管穷人死活的，他多半要吃败仗。他总该得到某种出主意的行家才是。”

===Original===
“I don’t see how he is to get it,” said I.

===Chinese===
“我看他上哪儿去弄这个呢。”我说。

===Original===
“Neither do I,” Thorndyke admitted. “There are no hospitals for impecunious litigants; it is assumed that only persons of means have a right to go to law. Of course, if we knew the man and the circumstances we might be able to help him; but for all we know to the contrary, he may be an arrant scoundrel.”

===Chinese===
“我也看不出，”桑代克承认道，“世上没有专为打不起官司的人开设的医院；人们认定，只有有钱人才有资格打官司。当然，倘若我们认识这个人、了解这些情形，或许还能帮上他；可就我们所知，也排除不了他是个彻头彻尾的恶棍的可能。”

===Original===
I had recalled the strange conversation that I had overheard, and wondered what Thorndyke would have thought of it if it had been allowable for me to repeat it. Obviously it was not, however, and I could only give my own impressions.

===Chinese===
我这时想起了自己偶然偷听到的那场奇怪的谈话，心中盘算，倘若允许我把它复述出来，桑代克会作何感想。可是这显然是不被允许的，我只能说说自己的印象。

===Original===
“He doesn’t strike me as that,” I said; “but of course, one never knows. Personally, he impressed me rather favorably, which is more than the other man did.”

===Chinese===
“他给我的可不是那种印象，”我说，“不过当然，谁也说不准。就我个人观感而言，他给我的印象还算不错——这可比另一位强多了。”

===Original===
“What other man?” asked Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“哪一位？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“There was another man in the case, wasn’t there? I forget his name. I saw him at the house and didn’t much like the look of him. I suspect he’s putting some sort of pressure on Bellingham.”

===Chinese===
“这案子里不是还有另一个男人么？我忘了他的名字。我在那宅子里见过他，不大喜欢他那副长相。我疑心他正在对贝林厄姆施加某种压力。”

===Original===
“Berkeley knows more about this than he’s telling us,” said Jervis. “Let us look up the report and see who this stranger is.” He took down from a shelf a large volume of newspaper cuttings and laid it on the table.

===Chinese===
“伯克利肚里知道的，可比他肯对我们说的多。”杰维斯说，“咱们把那份报道查出来，看看这位生人是谁。”他从书架上取下一大本剪报集，摊在桌上。

===Original===
“You see,” said he, as he ran his finger down the index. “Thorndyke files all the cases that are likely to come to something, and I know he had expectations regarding this one. I fancy he had some ghoulish hope that the missing gentleman’s head might turn up in somebody’s dustbin. Here we are; the other man’s name is Hurst. He is apparently a cousin, and it was at his house the missing man was last seen alive.”

===Chinese===
“你瞧，”他一面用手指顺着索引往下捋，一面说，“凡是日后可能闹出名堂的案子，桑代克全都存了档；我知道他对这一件是抱有指望的。我猜他还起过什么阴惨的念头，巴望那位失踪先生的脑袋会在哪家的垃圾箱里冒出来。找到了；另一位叫赫斯特。他显然是位表亲，失踪者最后一次被人见到活着，就是在他的宅子里。”

===Original===
“So you think Mr. Hurst is moving in the matter?” said Thorndyke, when he had glanced over the report.

===Chinese===
“这么说，你认为赫斯特先生正在这案子里活动？”桑代克浏览过剪报后说。

===Original===
“That is my impression,” I replied, “though I really know nothing about it.”

===Chinese===
“这是我的印象，”我答道，“不过说实在的，其中内情我一概不知。”

===Original===
“Well,” said Thorndyke, “if you should learn what is being done and should have permission to speak of it, I shall be very interested to hear how the case progresses and if an unofficial opinion on any point would be of service, I think there would be no harm in giving it.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，”桑代克说，“倘若你日后得知了案中的动向，又获准谈论，我会非常有兴趣听听这案子如何进展；而如果在某个问题上，一份非正式的意见能派上用场，我想提供出来也无妨。”

===Original===
“It would certainly be of great value if the other parties are taking professional advice,” I said; and then, after a pause, I asked: “Have you given this case much consideration?”

===Chinese===
“如果对方各造正在请教专业人士，那这份意见自然价值重大。”我说；停了片刻，我又问：“这案子您细细推敲过么？”

===Original===
Thorndyke reflected. “No,” he said, “I can’t say that I have. I turned it over rather carefully when the report first appeared, and I have speculated on it occasionally since. It is my habit, as Jervis was telling you, to utilize odd moments of leisure (such as a railway journey, for instance) by constructing theories to account for the facts of such obscure cases as have come to my notice. It is a useful habit, I think, for, apart from the mental exercise and experience that one gains from it, an appreciable portion of these cases ultimately comes into my hands, and then the previous consideration of them is so much time gained.”

===Chinese===
桑代克沉吟了一下。“没有，”他说，“谈不上细细推敲。报道初次刊出时，我相当仔细地琢磨过它，此后也不时推想一番。正如杰维斯方才告诉你的，我的习惯是利用零星的闲暇时刻——比如坐火车的时候——为那些引起我注意的疑案的种种事实构建理论，以求解释它们。我认为这是个有益的习惯，因为撇开从中获得的思维锻炼与经验不谈，这些案件里有相当可观的一部分最终会落到我手上，到那时，先前的思考便都是赚来的时间。”

===Original===
“Have you formed any theory to account for the facts in this case?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“对本案的种种事实，您形成过什么解释理论么？”我问。

===Original===
“Yes, I have several theories, one of which I especially favor, and I am awaiting with great interest such new facts as may indicate to me which of these theories is probably the correct one.”

===Chinese===
“形成过。我有好几种理论，其中一种我尤其偏爱；此刻我正怀着极大的兴趣等待新的事实，好向我指明这几种理论中哪一种多半是正确的。”

===Original===
“It’s no use your trying to pump him, Berkeley,” said Jervis. “He is fitted with an information valve that opens inward. You can pour in as much as you like, but you can’t get any out.”

===Chinese===
“你想从他嘴里套话是白搭，伯克利。”杰维斯说，“他身上装着一只只进不出的情报阀门。随你往里倒多少，一点也倒不出来。”

===Original===
Thorndyke chuckled. “My learned friend is, in the main, correct,” he said. “You see, I may be called upon any day to advise on this case, in which event I should feel remarkably foolish if I had already expounded my views in detail. But I should like to hear what you and Jervis make of the case as reported in the newspapers.”

===Chinese===
桑代克扑哧一笑。“我这位博学的朋友大体不差，”他说，“你想，说不定哪一天就会有人来就这案子征询我的意见，到那时，我若是早已把自己的见解详详细细抖搂出来，岂不显得傻气透顶。不过我倒很乐意听听，你和杰维斯对报纸所载的这桩案子作何见解。”

===Original===
“There now,” exclaimed Jervis, “what did I tell you? He wants to suck our brains.”

===Chinese===
“瞧见没有，”杰维斯嚷道，“我刚才说什么来着？他要吸咱们的脑髓呢。”

===Original===
“As far as my brain is concerned,” I said, “the process of suction isn’t likely to yield much except a vacuum, so I will resign in favor of you. You are a full-blown lawyer, whereas I am only a simple G.P.”

===Chinese===
“至于我这点脑髓，”我说，“吸是吸不出什么来的，只能吸出一片真空，所以我就告退让贤。你是位羽毛丰满的律师，我不过是个普普通通的全科大夫。”

===Original===
Jervis filled his pipe with deliberate care and lighted it. Then, blowing a slender stream of smoke into the air, he said:

===Chinese===
杰维斯不慌不忙、仔仔细细地装满烟斗，点着了。然后，朝空中吹出一缕细细的烟，说道：

===Original===
“If you want to know what I make of the case from that report, I can tell you in one word﻿—nothing. Every road seems to end in a cul-de-sac.”

===Chinese===
“你们若想知道我从那份报道里把案子看出了什么，我一个词便能奉告——什么也没有。条条道路，似乎都到死胡同为止。”

===Original===
“Oh, come!” said Thorndyke, “this is mere laziness. Berkeley wants to witness a display of your forensic wisdom. A learned counsel may be in a fog﻿—he very often is﻿—but he doesn’t state the fact baldly; he wraps it up in a decent verbal disguise. Tell us how you arrive at your conclusion. Show us that you have really weighed the facts.”

===Chinese===
“嗳，得啦！”桑代克说，“这纯粹是偷懒。伯克利想见识见识你法庭智慧的表演。一位博学的律师大状也可能一头雾水——他也确实常常如此——但他不会把这一点说得那么直白，他会拿一套体面的言辞外衣把它包裹起来。给我们讲讲你是如何得出结论的。让我们看看，你确实掂量过这些事实。”

===Original===
“Very well,” said Jervis, “I will give you a masterly analysis of the case﻿—leading to nothing.” He continued to puff at his pipe for a time with slight embarrassment, as I thought﻿—and I fully sympathized with him. Finally he blew a little cloud and commenced:

===Chinese===
“好吧，”杰维斯说，“我就给你们来一份关于本案的精辟分析——分析到一无所有为止。”他继续抽着他的烟斗，抽了一阵子，略显局促——我看是如此，而且我完全同情他。最后他吹出一小团烟雾，开了腔：

===Original===
“The position appears to be this: Here is a man seen to enter a certain house, who is shown into a certain room, and shut in. He is not seen to come out, and yet, when the room is next entered, it is found to be empty; and that man is never seen again, alive or dead. That is a pretty tough beginning.

===Chinese===
“情势看来是这样的：有这么一个人，人们看见他走进一所宅子，被引进一个房间，关在了里面；没有人看见他出来，可是下一次再进那房间时，里面已是空空如也；而这个人从此再无人见过，不论生死。这样的开头，可够棘手的。

===Original===
“Now, it is evident that one of three things must have happened. Either he must have remained in that room, or at least in that house, alive; or he must have died, naturally or otherwise, and his body have been concealed; or he must have left the house unobserved. Let us take the first case. This affair happened nearly two years ago. Now, he couldn’t have remained alive in the house for two years. He would have been noticed. The servants, for instance, when cleaning out the rooms, would have observed him.”

===Chinese===
“那么，显而易见，所发生之事必居三者之一：要么他留在那个房间里、至少那所宅子里，一直活着；要么他死了——或自然或不然——尸体被人藏匿；要么他趁人不备离开了宅子。且看第一种。这件事发生在将近两年之前。喏，他不可能在这宅子里活上两年。那样早该被人发觉了。比如仆人们打扫房间的时候，总该瞧见他。”

===Original===
Here Thorndyke interposed with an indulgent smile at his junior: “My learned friend is treating the inquiry with unbecoming levity. We accept the conclusion that the man did not remain in the house alive.”

===Chinese===
这时桑代克朝他的晚辈投去宽容的一笑，插话道：“我这位博学的朋友是拿这项探究当儿戏了，态度轻率得有失庄重。我们接受这个结论：此人并未活着留在宅中。”

===Original===
“Very well. Then did he remain in it dead? Apparently not. The report says that as soon as the man was missed, Hurst and the servants together searched the house thoroughly. But there had been no time or opportunity to dispose of the body, whence the only possible conclusion is that the body was not there. Moreover, if we admit the possibility of his having been murdered﻿—for that is what concealment of the body would imply﻿—there is the question: ‘Who could have murdered him?’ Not the servants, obviously, and as to Hurst﻿—well, of course, we don’t know what his relations with the missing man may have been﻿—at least, I don’t.”

===Chinese===
“很好。那么，他是死了留在宅中了么？显然没有。报道里说，此人一被发现失踪，赫斯特便和仆人们一道，把宅子彻底搜了一遍。但那时既没有时间、也没有机会处置尸体，由此唯一的结论便是尸体并不在宅中。再者，倘若我们承认他有遭谋杀的可能——藏匿尸体正隐含着这层意思——那么问题便来了：‘谁能谋杀他？’显然不会是仆人们；至于赫斯特——唔，当然，他与失踪者究竟是什么关系，我们无从知晓——至少我是无从知晓。”

===Original===
“Neither do I,” said Thorndyke. “I know nothing beyond what is in the newspaper report and what Berkeley has told us.”

===Chinese===
“我也不知道。”桑代克说，“除了报纸报道和伯克利方才讲的，对其余我一概不知。”

===Original===
“Then we know nothing. He may have had a motive for murdering the man or he may not. The point is that he doesn’t seem to have had the opportunity. Even if we suppose that he managed to conceal the body temporarily, still there was the final disposal of it. He couldn’t have buried it in the garden with the servants about; neither could he have burned it. The only conceivable method by which he could have got rid of it would have been that of cutting it up into fragments and burying the dismembered parts in some secluded spots or dropping them into ponds or rivers. But no remains of the kind have been found, as some of them probably would have been by now, so that there is nothing to support this suggestion; indeed, the idea of murder, in this house at least, seems to be excluded by the search that was made the instant the man was missed.

===Chinese===
“那么我们是一无所知了。他可能有谋杀此人的动机，也可能没有。要害在于：他似乎不曾有过机会。纵使我们假设他设法把尸体暂时藏了起来，最后仍免不了处置一环。仆人们就在宅中走动，他不可能把尸体埋进花园，也不可能把它焚化。唯一想得出的销尸法子，是把它肢解成碎块，把残躯埋到几处隐蔽的地点，或抛入池塘河流。可这一类残骸一无所获——若真抛撒过，如今多半总会发现几块——足见此说并无佐证；不但如此，至少在这所宅子里，谋杀一说似乎已被那人一失踪便立即进行的那场搜查排除在外。

===Original===
“Then to take the third alternative: Did he leave the house unobserved? Well, it is not impossible, but it would be a queer thing to do. He may have been an impulsive or eccentric man. We can’t say. We know nothing about him. But two years have elapsed and he has never turned up, so that if he left the house secretly he must have gone into hiding and be hiding still. Of course, he may have been the sort of lunatic who would behave in that manner or he may not. We have no information as to his personal character.

===Chinese===
“再取第三种可能：他是趁人不备离开宅子的么？唔，这并非不可能，只是如此行事未免离奇。他也许是个冲动任性的人，或是个性情古怪的人。我们说不上来。我们对他一无所知。可两年过去，他从未露面，所以倘若他是秘密出走，那他必定躲了起来，而且至今还躲着。当然，他也许正是那一路肯如此行事的疯子，也许不是。至于他的个人品性，我们毫无资料。

===Original===
“Then there is the complication of the scarab that was picked up in the grounds of his brother’s house at Woodford. That seems to show that he visited that house at some time. But no one admits having seen him there; and it is uncertain, therefore, whether he went first to his brother’s house or to Hurst’s. If he was wearing the scarab when he arrived at the Eltham house, he must have left that house unobserved and gone to Woodford; but if he was not wearing it he probably went from Woodford to Eltham, and there finally disappeared. As to whether he was or was not wearing the scarab when he was last seen alive by Hurst’s housemaid, there is at present no evidence.

===Chinese===
“再有那枚圣甲虫引出的纠葛——它是在伍德福德他弟弟宅子的园子里拾得的。这似乎表明，他曾于某个时候到过那所宅子。可谁也不承认在那里见过他；因此，他是先到弟弟的宅子、还是先到赫斯特的宅子，便无从断定。倘若他抵达埃尔瑟姆那所宅子时圣甲虫还挂在身上，那他必定是趁人不备离开了那宅子，转往伍德福德；倘若没挂，那他多半是从伍德福德去了埃尔瑟姆，并在那里彻底失踪。至于赫斯特家的女仆最后一次见到他活着时，他究竟戴没戴那枚圣甲虫，眼下毫无证据。

===Original===
“If he went to his brother’s house after his visit to Hurst, the disappearance is more understandable if we don’t mind flinging accusations of murder about rather casually; for the disposal of the body would be much less difficult in that case. Apparently no one saw him enter the house, and, if he did enter, it was by a back gate which communicated with the library﻿—a separate building some distance from the house. In that case it would have been physically possible for the Bellinghams to have made away with him. There was plenty of time to dispose of the body unobserved﻿—temporarily, at any rate. Nobody had seen him come to the house, and nobody knew that he was there﻿—if he was there; and apparently no search was made either at the time or afterward. In fact, if it could be shown that the missing man left Hurst’s house alive, or that he was wearing the scarab when he arrived there, things would look rather fishy for the Bellinghams﻿—for, of course, the girl must have been in it if the father was. But there’s the crux: there is no proof that the man ever did leave Hurst’s house alive. And if he didn’t﻿—but there! as I said at first, whichever turning you take, you find that it ends in a blind alley.”

===Chinese===
“倘若他是在造访赫斯特之后才去的弟弟家，那么失踪一案就容易解释多了——前提是我们不介意把谋杀的指控随口乱扔；因为在那样的情形下，处置尸体就要容易得多。显然没有人看见他走进那所宅子；而且他若进去过，走的是一道与书房相通的后门——书房是一座独立的建筑，与宅子隔着一段距离。倘若如此，贝林厄姆父女在客观上完全有可能把他干掉。趁人不备处置尸体的时间绰绰有余——至少暂时藏匿是绰绰有余的。没有人看见他来过那宅子，也没有人知道他在那里——如果他真在那里的话；而且无论当时还是事后，那里显然都不曾进行过搜查。事实上，只要能证明失踪者活着离开了赫斯特的宅子，或者证明他到达那里时戴着圣甲虫，事情对贝林厄姆父女来说可就相当可疑了——因为，自然，父亲若是同谋，姑娘就必定也是。可症结正在这里：并没有任何证据，证明此人确曾活着离开赫斯特的宅子。倘若他没有——唉，罢了！正像我起初所说，任你拐进哪条岔路，走到头都是一条死胡同。”

===Original===
“A lame ending to a masterly exposition,” was Thorndyke’s comment.

===Chinese===
“一篇精辟的宏论，配了个跛脚的结局。”这是桑代克的评语。

===Original===
“I know,” said Jervis. “But what would you have? There are quite a number of possible solutions, and one of them must be the true one. But how are we to judge which it is? I maintain that until we know something of the parties and the financial and other interests involved we have no data.”

===Chinese===
“我知道，”杰维斯说，“可你们还待怎样？可能的解答数不胜数，其中之一必是真相。可我们凭什么裁定是哪一个？我坚持认为，在对当事各方、对牵涉的金钱及其他利害有所知之前，我们根本谈不上有什么资料。”

===Original===
“There,” said Thorndyke, “I disagree with you entirely. I maintain that we have ample data. You say that we have no means of judging which of the various possible solutions is the true one; but I think that if you read the report carefully and thoughtfully you will find that the facts now known point to one explanation, and one only. It may not be the true explanation, and I don’t suppose it is. But we are now dealing with the matter speculatively, academically, and I contend that our data yield a definite conclusion. What do you say, Berkeley?”

===Chinese===
“在这一点上，”桑代克说，“我与你全然相左。我主张，我们的资料绰绰有余。你说我们无从判断种种可能的解答中哪一种为真；可我认为，你若把那份报道仔细而凝神地读上一遍，就会发现现已查明的事实只指向一种解释，且唯独一种。它未必是真实的解释，我也不以为它是。但我们此刻是在思辨地、学理地对待此事，而我的主张是：我们的资料足以引出一个明确的结论。你说呢，伯克利？”

===Original===
“I say that it is time for me to be off; the evening consultations begin at half-past six.”

===Chinese===
“我说的是：我该动身了；晚间门诊六点半就开始。”

===Original===
“Well,” said Thorndyke, “don’t let us keep you from your duties, with poor Barnard currant picking in the Grecian Isles. But come in and see us again. Drop in when you like after your work is done. You won’t be in our way even if we are busy, which we very seldom are after eight o’clock.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，”桑代克说，“可怜的巴纳德还在希腊诸岛采葡萄干呢，我们总不能耽误你的正事。不过改天再来看看我们。做完了事，什么时候高兴就过来。哪怕我们正忙着，你也绝不碍事——何况八点钟以后，我们难得有什么事。”

===Original===
I thanked Dr. Thorndyke most heartily for making me free of his chambers in this hospitable fashion and took my leave, setting forth homeward by way of Middle Temple Lane and the Embankment; not a very direct route for Fetter Lane, it must be confessed; but our talk had revived my interest in the Bellingham household and put me in a reflective vein.

===Chinese===
我衷心感谢桑代克博士这般好客，让我得以随意出入他的寓所，随即告辞出门，取道中殿巷（Middle Temple Lane）和泰晤士河堤（the Embankment）踏上归途；必须承认，这条路回菲特巷并不怎么走直；但我们的一席谈话重新唤起了我对贝林厄姆一家的兴味，也令我陷入沉思。

===Original===
From the remarkable conversation that I had overheard it was evident that the plot was thickening. Not that I supposed that these two respectable gentlemen really suspected one another of having made away with the missing man; but still, their unguarded words, spoken in anger, made it clear that each had allowed the thought of sinister possibilities to enter his mind﻿—a dangerous condition that might easily grow into actual suspicion. And then the circumstances really were highly mysterious, as I realized with especial vividness now after listening to my friend’s analysis of the evidence.

===Chinese===
从我偶然偷听到的那场非同寻常的谈话看来，这出戏的情节显然正日渐错综。倒不是我以为这两位体面先生当真疑心对方害了那失踪的人；但是，他们盛怒之中吐出的那些不加提防的话，分明表明各人心里都已容凶险的种种可能钻了进去——一种危险的状况，极容易滋长成真真切切的怀疑。何况这桩案子的种种情形，也委实神秘已极；听了我朋友对证据的这番剖析之后，我此刻对此感受得格外真切。

===Original===
From the problem itself my mind traveled, not for the first time during the last few days, to the handsome girl, who had seemed in my eyes the high-priestess of this temple of mystery in the quaint little court. What a strange figure she had made against this strange background, with her quiet, chilly, self-contained manner, her pale face, so sad and worn, her black, straight brows and solemn gray eyes, so inscrutable, mysterious, Sibylline. A striking, even impressive, personality this, I reflected, with something in it somber and enigmatic that attracted and yet repelled.

===Chinese===
从这桩谜案本身，我的思绪又飘向了那位美丽的姑娘——近几日来，这已不是头一回了——在那条古雅的小巷里，她在我眼中俨然是这座神秘殿堂的女祭司。映衬着这奇异的背景，她构成了一个何等奇异的形象：沉静、清冷、矜持的仪态；苍白、忧伤而憔悴的脸；乌黑平直的双眉，端凝肃穆的灰眼睛，那般不可测度，那般神秘，那般如女巫的谶语。这样一位人物，夺目而出奇，甚至令人心折，我暗自思量；她身上有某种阴郁而费解的东西，既吸引人，又叫人却步。

===Original===
And here I recalled Jervis’s words: “The girl must have been in it if the father was.” It was a dreadful thought, even though only speculatively uttered, and my heart rejected it; rejected it with indignation that rather surprised me. And this notwithstanding that the somber black-robed figure that my memory conjured up was one that associated itself with the idea of mystery and tragedy.

===Chinese===
这时我记起了杰维斯的那句话：“父亲若是同谋，姑娘就必定也是。”这个念头令人胆寒——哪怕它只是思辨中的一句泛泛之言；我的心拒斥了它，而且是怀着一种颇令我自家吃惊的义愤拒斥了它。尽管如此，我记忆中唤起的那一身黑衣的阴郁身影，恰恰正是与神秘和悲剧的意念难分难解地连在一起的。

## IV Legal Complications and a Jackal / 四 法律纠葛与一头豺

===Original===
My meditations brought me by a circuitous route, and ten minutes late, to the end of Fetter Lane, where, exchanging my rather abstracted air for the alert manner of a busy practitioner, I strode briskly forward and darted into the surgery with knitted brows, as though just released from an anxious case.
