What I do know is that when Ruth Bellingham entered the reading-room she appeared in comparison with these like a creature of another order; even as the head of Antinous, which formerly stood (it has since been moved) amidst the portrait-busts of the Roman Emperors, seemed like the head of a god set in a portrait gallery of illustrious baboons.

===Chinese===
我常常想，倘若能把某种致命而防腐性能极强的蒸气——比如福尔马林（formaldehyde）之类——散布到这间厅堂的空气里，那么这里整套齐全的藏书连同书虫们，作为博物馆主体收藏的一篇人类学附录，实在很值得保存下来，以启迪后世。因为，可以断言，世界上再没有哪个地方，能把这么多古怪而反常的人类聚会在一处。而许多观察者必定都油然起过的一个古怪问题是：这些奇特的生灵打哪儿来，待到那面眉目分明的挂钟（专为文人视力校准）宣告闭馆的时刻，又往哪儿去？比如那位一脸悲剧色彩的先生，螺锥般的发鬈随着脚步像螺旋弹簧似的一起一伏？又比如那位穿黑法衣、戴圆顶硬礼帽的矮小老先生，忽地一转身，竟显出是个中年妇人，惊得你魂飞魄散？他们都到哪里去了？别处谁也见不着他们。莫非到了闭馆的时候，他们便悄悄溜进博物馆深处，藏身石棺或木乃伊匣中过夜到天明？还是从书架的空隙里爬将进去，在皮革和古旧纸张的亲切氛围里、在一册册书卷背后消磨长夜？谁说得上来？我确知的是：当露丝·贝林厄姆走进这间阅览室时，同这些人两相比较，她竟像是另一等第的造物；正如安提诺乌斯（Antinous）的那颗头颅——它从前（后来已挪走了）立在罗马众皇帝的半身像中间——仿佛神祇的头颅，供在一座尽是声名显赫的狒狒的肖像馆里。

===Original===
“What have we got to do?” I asked when we had found a vacant seat. “Do you want to look up the catalogue?”

===Chinese===
“咱们该做些什么？”找到空位子后我问，“要查目录么？”

===Original===
“No, I have the tickets in my bag. The books are waiting in the ‘kept books’ department.”

===Chinese===
“不查，索书单就在我包里。书都在‘留书处’里等着呢。”

===Original===
I placed my hat on the leather-covered shelf, dropped her gloves into it﻿—how delightfully intimate and companionable it seemed!﻿—altered the numbers on the tickets, and then we proceeded together to the “kept books” desk to collect the volumes that contained the material for our day’s work.

===Chinese===
我把帽子搁上蒙着皮革的搁板，又把她的手套丢进帽子里——这显得多么亲昵而投契，多么可喜！——改好索书单上的书号，然后我们便一同走向“留书处”柜台，去提取那几卷装着我们一天工作材料的书。

===Original===
It was a blissful afternoon. Two and a half hours of happiness unalloyed did I spend at that shiny, leather-clad desk, guiding my nimble pen across the pages of the notebook. It introduced me to a new world﻿—a world in which love and learning, sweet intimacy and crusted archeology, were mingled into the oddest, most whimsical and most delicious confection that the mind of man can conceive. Hitherto, these recondite histories had been far beyond my ken. Of the wonderful heretic, Amenhotep the Fourth, I had already heard﻿—at the most he had been a mere name; the Hittites a mythical race of undetermined habitat; while cuneiform tablets had presented themselves to my mind merely as an uncouth kind of fossil biscuit suited to the digestion of a prehistoric ostrich.

===Chinese===
那是个至福的下午。两个半钟头纯而不杂的幸福，我就在那张亮闪闪的皮面书桌前度过，引导着灵巧的笔在笔记本的纸页间驰行。它引我进了一个新世界——在那个世界里，爱情与学问、甜蜜的亲昵与斑驳陈古的考古学，调和成人类心智所能设想的最古怪、最别致、最美味的一剂甜点。在此之前，这些冷僻的史事远远超出我的见识。那位奇妙的异端阿蒙霍特普四世，我倒听说过——至多不过是一个空名字；赫梯人（Hittites）是一支居址不明的神话种族；至于楔形文字泥板，在我心里呈现出来的，不过是一种粗粝的化石饼干，正合史前鸵鸟的肠胃。

===Original===
Now all this was changed. As we sat with our chairs creaking together and she whispered the story of those stirring times into my receptive ear﻿—talking is strictly forbidden in the reading-room﻿—the disjointed fragments arranged themselves into a romance of supreme fascination. Egyptian, Babylonian, Aramean, Hittite, Memphis, Babylon, Hamath, Megiddo﻿—I swallowed them all thankfully, wrote them down, and asked for more. Only once did I disgrace myself. An elderly clergyman of ascetic and acidulous aspect had passed us with a glance of evident disapproval, clearly setting us down as intruding philanderers; and when I contrasted the parson’s probable conception of the whispered communications that were being poured into my ear so tenderly and confidentially with the dry reality, I chuckled aloud. But my fair taskmistress only paused, with her finger on the page, smilingly to rebuke me, and then went on with the dictation. She was certainly a Tartar for work.

===Chinese===
如今这一切全变了。当我们两人的椅子一道吱呀作响，她把那个风云时代的故事一段段悄悄讲进我这只乐于领教的耳朵——阅览室里是严格禁止说话的——那些支离的碎片便自行缀成了一篇魅力无穷的传奇。埃及、巴比伦、阿拉米（Aramean）、赫梯；孟菲斯（Memphis）、巴比伦、哈马（Hamath）、米吉多（Megiddo）——我满怀感激地把它们囫囵咽下，一一写下，还要求再来一点。只有一回我丢了丑。一位形容苦修而尖刻的老教士打我们身旁走过，投来分明非难的一瞥，显然把我们当成了闯进来调笑的闲人；待我把这位牧师对那些凑在我耳边、那样温存而机密的低语多半会有的想象，同那干燥的实际一对照，我不禁咯咯笑出了声。可是我那位美丽的女监工只是停了停，手指按在书页上，笑吟吟地申斥了我一句，便接着口授下去。干起活来，她着实是个厉害角色。

===Original===
It was a proud moment for me when, in response to my interrogative “Yes?” my companion said “That is all” and closed the book. We had extracted the pith and marrow of six considerable volumes in two and a half hours.

===Chinese===
当我用探询的一声“怎么样？”相问，同伴应一声“就这些了”，把书合上——那于我是个得意的时刻。两个半钟头，我们已从六部大书里榨出了精髓。

===Original===
“You have been better than your word,” she said. “It would have taken me two full days of really hard work to make the notes that you have written down since we commenced. I don’t know how to thank you.”

===Chinese===
“您做的比说的还好，”她说，“咱们动手以来您记下的这些笔记，若换了我，实打实拼上整整两天也做不出来。我真不知道该怎么谢您。”

===Original===
“There’s no need to. I’ve enjoyed myself and polished up my shorthand. What is the next thing? We shall want some books for tomorrow, shan’t we?”

===Chinese===
“不必谢。我自己乐在其中，还把速记温习了一遍。接下来做什么？明天咱们还得要几本书，对吧？”

===Original===
“Yes. I have made out a list, so if you will come with me to the catalogue desk I will look up the numbers and ask you to write the tickets.”

===Chinese===
“对。我已开出一张单子，那么请您陪我到目录台去，我来查编号，请您来填索书单。”

===Original===
The selection of a fresh batch of authorities occupied us for another quarter of an hour, and then, having handed in the volumes that we had squeezed dry, we took our way out of the reading-room.

===Chinese===
又选了一批新的参考书，占去我们一刻钟；随后，把那几卷已被我们榨干的书交还，我们便出了阅览室。

===Original===
“Which way shall we go?” she asked as we passed out of the gate, where stood a massive policeman, like the guardian angel at the gate of Paradise (only, thank Heaven! he bore no flaming sword forbidding reentry).

===Chinese===
“咱们打哪边走？”出了大门她问。门口立着一条魁伟的警察，好比乐园门前守卫的天使（只是，谢天谢地！他手里没有提着不许人再进去的火焰剑）。

===Original===
“We are going,” I replied, “to Museum Street, where is a milkshop in which one can get an excellent cup of tea.”

===Chinese===
“咱们要去的，”我答道，“是博物馆街（Museum Street），那儿有一家牛奶店，能喝到极好的一杯茶。”

===Original===
She looked as if she would have demurred, but eventually followed obediently, and we were soon settled side by side at the little marble-topped table, retracing the ground we had covered in the afternoon’s work and discussing various points of interest over a joint teapot.

===Chinese===
她露出想要推辞的神色，但终究乖乖地跟了来；不多时我们便并排坐在那张大理石面的小桌旁，回顾着下午工作中走过的地界，对着一壶共斟的茶，讨论种种有趣的问题。

===Original===
“Have you been doing this sort of work long?” I asked, as she handed me my second cup of tea.

===Chinese===
“这类工作您做久了么？”她递给我第二杯茶时，我问。

===Original===
“Professionally,” she answered, “only about two years; since we broke up our home, in fact. But long before that I used to come to the Museum with my Uncle John﻿—the one who disappeared, you know, in that dreadfully mysterious way﻿—and help him to look up references. We were good friends, he and I.”

===Chinese===
“作为营生，”她答道，“不过两年上下；其实就是打我们散了家宅那年起。可在那之前许久，我就常跟我约翰伯父——您知道，就是以那桩可怕而神秘的方式失踪的那一位——一道来博物馆，帮他查文献。我们俩是很好的朋友，他和我。”

===Original===
“I suppose he was a very learned man?” I suggested.

===Chinese===
“我想他是一位很有学问的人吧？”我探问道。

===Original===
“Yes, in a certain way; in the way of the better-class collector he was very learned indeed. He knew the contents of every museum in the world, in so far as they were connected with Egyptian antiquities, and had studied them specimen by specimen. Consequently, as Egyptology is largely a museum science, he was a learned Egyptologist. But his real interest was in things rather than events. Of course, he knew a great deal﻿—a very great deal﻿—about Egyptian history, but still he was, before all, a collector.”

===Chinese===
“是的，在某一种意义上；以上等收藏家的那一路学问而言，他实在极有学问。凡是同埃及古物相关的东西，世界上每一家博物馆的藏品，他无一不了然于胸，而且一件一件标本地研究过。因此，既然埃及学在很大程度上是一门博物馆的科学，他就算得一位博学的埃及学家。可是他真正的兴趣在器物，不在史事。当然，埃及史他懂得极多——极多——但归根结底，他首先是个收藏家。”

===Original===
“And what will happen to his collection if he is really dead?”

===Chinese===
“如果他果真死了，他的收藏会落个什么下场？”

===Original===
“The greater part of it goes to the British Museum by his will, and the remainder he has left to his solicitor, Mr. Jellicoe.”

===Chinese===
“大头儿照他的遗嘱归大英博物馆，其余的，他留给了他的律师杰利科先生。”

===Original===
“To Mr. Jellicoe! Why, what will Mr. Jellicoe do with Egyptian antiquities?”

===Chinese===
“给杰利科先生！咦，杰利科先生要埃及古物做什么？”

===Original===
“Oh, he is an Egyptologist too, and quite an enthusiast. He has really a fine collection of scarabs and other small objects such as it is possible to keep in a private house. I have always thought that it was his enthusiasm for everything Egyptian that brought him and my uncle together on terms of such intimacy; though I believe he is an excellent lawyer, and he is certainly a very discreet, cautious man.”

===Chinese===
“哦，他也是位埃及学家，而且还相当入迷。他着实藏有一批精美的圣甲虫和别的小件器物，都是私家宅子里收藏得下的那种。我一向认为，正是他对一切埃及事物的热心，才使他和我伯父结下了那样亲密的关系；不过我相信他是位极出色的律师，而且他肯定是个十分谨言慎行、小心仔细的人。”

===Original===
“Is he? I shouldn’t have thought so, judging by your uncle’s will.”

===Chinese===
“是么？照您伯父的遗嘱来看，我可真想不到。”

===Original===
“Oh, but that is not Mr. Jellicoe’s fault. He assures us that he entreated my uncle to let him draw up a fresh document with more reasonable provisions. But he says Uncle John was immovable; and he really was a rather obstinate man. Mr. Jellicoe repudiates any responsibility in the matter. He washes his hands of the whole affair, and says that it is the will of a lunatic. And so it is, I was glancing through it only a night or two ago, and really I cannot conceive how a sane man could have written such nonsense.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那可怪不着杰利科先生。他向我们保证，他曾恳求我伯父让他另拟一份条款更合情理的新文书。可他说，约翰伯父岿然不动；他也确实是个相当固执的人。杰利科先生对这件事上的任何责任一概不认。他对整桩事洗手不干，还说那是一个疯子立的遗嘱。也确实是——就在一两个晚上之前我还翻过一遍，我实在想不出，一个神志清明的人怎么会写下这样的胡话。”

===Original===
“You have a copy then?” I asked eagerly, remembering Thorndyke’s parting instructions.

===Chinese===
“那么说，您手头有一份副本？”我想起桑代克博士临别的叮嘱，急切地问。

===Original===
“Yes. Would you like to see it? I know my father has told you about it, and it is worth reading as a curiosity of perverseness.”

===Chinese===
“有。您愿意看看么？我知道我父亲给您讲过它；作为一件乖谬的奇物，它也值得一读。”

===Original===
“I should very much like to show it to my friend, Doctor Thorndyke,” I replied. “He said he would be interested to read it and learn the exact provisions; and it might be well to let him, and hear what he has to say about it.”

===Chinese===
“我很想把它拿给我的朋友桑代克博士看看，”我答道，“他说过，他很有兴趣读一读，了解确切的条款；让他读了、再听听他有什么说法，或许是有益的。”

===Original===
“I see no objection,” she rejoined; “but you know what my father is: his horror, I mean, of what he calls ‘cadging for advice gratis.’ ”

===Chinese===
“我看没什么不可以，”她应道，“不过您是知道我父亲的为人的：我是说，他最忌讳他自己所谓的‘白讨主意不掏钱’。”

===Original===
“Oh, but he need have no scruples on that score. Doctor Thorndyke wants to see the will because the case interests him. He is an enthusiast, you know, and he put the request as a personal favor to himself.”

===Chinese===
“哦，这一点他大可不必顾忌。桑代克博士想看这份遗嘱，是因为这个案子使他发生了兴趣。您知道，他是个痴心的人，况且他提这个请求，还是当作给他本人的一点情面呢。”

===Original===
“That is very nice and delicate of him, and I will explain the position to my father. If he is willing for Doctor Thorndyke to see the copy, I will send or bring it over this evening. Have we finished?”

===Chinese===
“他这真是又周到又体贴，我会把情形向我父亲说明。倘若他愿意让桑代克博士看这份副本，今晚我就差人送来，或者自己送来。咱们的事完了么？”

===Original===
I regretfully admitted that we had, and, when I had paid the modest reckoning, we sallied forth, turning back with one accord into Great Russell Street to avoid the noise and bustle of the larger thoroughfares.

===Chinese===
我遗憾地承认完了；付过那笔不大的茶资，我们便出门上路，又不约而同地折进大拉塞尔街（Great Russell Street），避开了通衢大道上的喧嚣。

===Original===
“What sort of man was your uncle?” I asked presently, as we walked along the quiet, dignified street. And then I added hastily: “I hope you don’t think me inquisitive, but, to my mind, he presents himself as a kind of mysterious abstraction; the unknown quantity of a legal problem.”

===Chinese===
“您伯父生前是怎样一个人？”沿着这条安静而气派的街道走了一程，我问道。随即又慌忙找补一句：“希望您别怪我好打听；只是在我心里，他始终呈现为一个神秘的抽象物；一道法律难题里的未知数。”

===Original===
“My Uncle John,” she answered reflectively, “was a very peculiar man, rather obstinate, very self-willed, what people call ‘masterful,’ and decidedly wrongheaded and unreasonable.”

===Chinese===
“我的约翰伯父，”她若有所思地答道，“是一个非常特别的人：相当固执，极有主见，就是人们所谓的‘专横’，而且分明执拗乖张，不讲道理。”

===Original===
“That is certainly the impression that the terms of his will convey,” I said.

===Chinese===
“他的遗嘱条款，给人的确实正是这个印象。”我说。

===Original===
“Yes, and not the will only. There was the absurd allowance that he made to my father. That was a ridiculous arrangement, and very unfair too. He ought to have divided the property up as my grandfather intended. And yet he was by no means ungenerous, only he would have his own way, and his own way was very commonly the wrong way.”

===Chinese===
“是的，还不止遗嘱呢。还有他给我父亲的那份可笑的津贴。那是个荒唐的安排，而且极不公道。他本该照我祖父的意思把产业分开。然而他也绝非不慷慨，只是他非得自行其是，而他那一套，十有八九是错的那一套。”

===Original===
“I remember,” she continued, after a short pause, “a very odd instance of his wrongheadedness and obstinacy. It was a small matter, but very typical of him. He had in his collection a beautiful little ring of the eighteenth dynasty. It was said to have belonged to Queen Ti, the mother of our friend Amenhotep the Fourth; but I don’t think that could have been so, because the device on it was the Eye of Osiris, and Ti, as you know, was an Aten-worshiper. However, it was a very charming ring, and Uncle John, who had a queer sort of devotion to the mystical eye of Osiris, commissioned a very clever goldsmith to make two exact copies of it, one for himself and one for me. The goldsmith naturally wanted to take the measurements of our fingers, but this Uncle John would not hear of; the rings were to be exact copies, and an exact copy must be the same size as the original. You can imagine the result; my ring was so loose that I couldn’t keep it on my finger, and Uncle John’s was so tight that though he did manage to get it on, he was never able to get it off. And it was only the circumstance that his left hand was decidedly smaller than his right that made it possible for him to wear it all.”

===Chinese===
“我还记得，”歇了片刻，她又接着说，“他乖张固执的一个非常古怪的例子。那是件小事，却极能代表他的为人。他的收藏里有一枚十八王朝的漂亮小戒指。据说它早年属于蒂王后（Queen Ti），就是我们那位朋友阿蒙霍特普四世的母亲；不过我想不致如此，因为戒面上的纹样是俄塞里斯之眼，而您知道，蒂是崇奉阿顿（Aten）的。然而那确是一枚非常可爱的戒指，而约翰伯父对神秘的俄塞里斯之眼怀着一种奇特的虔敬，便雇了一位非常高明的金匠，打造了两枚分毫不差的复制品，一枚给他自己，一枚给我。金匠自然想量一量我们手指的尺寸，可这个约翰伯父说什么也不依；戒指必须分毫不差，而分毫不差的复制品，尺寸就必定得同原件一般大。结果您可想而知；我那枚松得在手指上根本戴不住，约翰伯父那枚又紧得——他倒是硬戴了上去，却再也取不下来。若不是他的左手分明比右手小着一号，他压根儿就休想戴得上。”

===Original===
“So you never wore your copy?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，您那枚复制品从来没能戴过？”

===Original===
“No. I wanted to have it altered to make it fit, but he objected strongly; so I put it away, and have it in a box still.”

===Chinese===
“没有。我本想把它改一改，改得合适，可他坚决不依；于是我便把它收了起来，至今还收在一只盒子里。”

===Original===
“He must have been an extraordinarily pigheaded old fellow,” I remarked.

===Chinese===
“他准是个倔得异乎寻常的老家伙。”我说。

===Original===
“Yes; he was very tenacious. He annoyed my father a good deal, too, by making unnecessary alterations in the house in Queen Square when he fitted up his museum. We have a certain sentiment with regard to that house. Our people have lived in it ever since it was built, when the square was first laid out in the reign of Queen Anne, after whom it was named. It is a dear old house. Would you like to see it? We are quite near it now.”

===Chinese===
“是啊；他这人非常执拗。他在皇后广场的宅子里布置他那座博物馆时，做了好些不必要的改动，也为此把我父亲惹恼得不轻。我们对那座宅子是有一番感情的。打那房子落成起——广场初辟是在安妮女王在位年间，广场便是以她命名的——我们家的人就一直住在里面。那是一座亲切的老宅。您想看看它么？咱们现在离它很近了。”

===Original===
I assented eagerly. If it had been a coal-shed or a fried-fish shop I would still have visited it with pleasure, for the sake of prolonging our walk; but I was also really interested in this old house as a part of the background of the mystery of the vanished John Bellingham.

===Chinese===
我热切地答应了。哪怕它是一座煤棚、或是一家炸鱼铺，单为把我们的散步拉长些，我也乐意前去一游；何况这座老宅本身也当真使我感兴趣——它正是失踪的约翰·贝林厄姆那一团疑案的背景的一部分。

===Original===
We crossed into Cosmo Place, with its quaint row of the now rare, cannon-shaped iron posts, and passing through stood for a few moments looking into the peaceful, stately old square. A party of boys disported themselves noisily on the range of stone posts that form a bodyguard round the ancient lamp-surmounted pump, but otherwise the place was wrapped in dignified repose suited to its age and station. And very pleasant it looked on this summer afternoon with the sunlight gilding the foliage of its widespreading plane trees and lighting up the warm-toned brick of the house-fronts. We walked slowly down the shady west side, near the middle of which my companion halted.

===Chinese===
我们横穿过科斯莫坊（Cosmo Place），那儿有一排别致的炮形铁柱，如今已属罕见；穿过去后，便驻足片刻，望进这条安谧、庄严的老广场。一群男孩子正在那一列界石柱上喧闹嬉耍——那圈石柱环卫着顶上带灯的古水泵，像一队卫队；除此之外，这地方便笼罩在与它的年岁和身份相称的端庄静穆之中。在这夏日的午后，它看上去着实宜人：阳光给四下铺展的悬铃木的叶丛镀上金色，又照亮了一面面住宅临街的暖色调砖墙。我们沿着背阴的西侧缓缓走去，将近中段的地方，我的同伴停住了脚。

===Original===
“This is the house,” she said. “It looks gloomy and forsaken now; but it must have been a delightful house in the days when my ancestors could look out of the windows through the open end of the square across the fields of meadows to the heights of Hampstead and Highgate.”

===Chinese===
“就是这座房子，”她说，“它如今瞧着阴郁冷落；可从前，在我的先人们还能从窗口望出去、穿过广场敞开的一端、越过片片草场直望到汉普斯特德（Hampstead）和海格特（Highgate）高冈的那些日子里，它一定是一座叫人住着快活的宅子。”

===Original===
She stood at the edge of the pavement looking up with a curious wistfulness at the old house; a very pathetic figure I thought, with her handsome face and proud carriage, her threadbare dress and shabby gloves, standing at the threshold of the home that had been her family’s for generations, that should now have been hers, and that was shortly to pass away into the hands of strangers.

===Chinese===
她站在人行道边上，带着一种难以名状的怅望，仰视着那座老宅；我想，那是一个十分动人的身影——俊美的脸，矜持的仪态，磨旧的衣裙和寒碜的手套——立在一个她的家族聚居了几代的家的门槛前；这个家如今本该是她的，不久却要落入外人之手。

===Original===
I, too, looked up at it with a strange interest, impressed by something gloomy and forbidding in its aspect. The windows were shuttered from basement to attic, and no sign of life was visible. Silent, neglected, desolate, it breathed an air of tragedy. It seemed to mourn in sackcloth and ashes for its lost master. The massive door within the splendid carven portico was crusted with grime, and seemed to have passed out of use as completely as the ancient lamp-irons or the rusted extinguishers wherein the footmen were wont to quench their torches when some Bellingham dame was borne up the steps in her gilded chair, in the days of good Queen Anne.

===Chinese===
我也怀着一种奇异的兴味仰视着它，它那气象中的某种阴郁而峻拒的东西给了我深刻的印象。窗子从地窖到阁楼都上着窗板，看不见一点生命的迹象。静默，荒废，凄凉，它吐纳着一片悲剧的气息。它仿佛在为它失去的主人披麻蒙灰。雕饰华美的门廊里那扇厚重的大门积满垢污，仿佛已同古老的灯架、锈蚀的熄炬筒一样全然废而不用了——想当年好女王安妮的日子里，贝林厄姆家的哪位太太坐着镀金轿椅被抬上台阶时，跟班的仆役还惯在那筒里熄灭手中的火把呢。

===Original===
It was in a somewhat sobered frame of mind that we presently turned away and started homeward by way of Great Ormond Street. My companion was deeply thoughtful, relapsing for a while into that somberness of manner that had so impressed me when I first met her. Nor was I without a certain sympathetic pensiveness; as if, from the great, silent house, the spirit of the vanished man had issued forth to bear us company.

===Chinese===
少顷，我们怀着几分沉静下来的心绪转身离去，取道大奥蒙德街（Great Ormond Street）回家。我的同伴深深地出了神，一时又落回我初见她时令我印象至深的那种阴郁的神色里。我也并非没有一点同感的幽思；仿佛从那座沉寂的大宅里，那位失踪者的魂灵已然飘出，一路与我们相伴。

===Original===
But still it was a delightful walk, and I was sorry when at last we arrived at the entrance to Nevill’s Court, and Miss Bellingham halted and held out her hand.

===Chinese===
但这仍是一次愉快的散步；待我们终于走到内维尔巷的巷口、贝林厄姆小姐停步伸手道别时，我心里还觉着惋惜。

===Original===
“Goodbye,” she said; “and many, many thanks for your invaluable help. Shall I take the bag?”

===Chinese===
“再见，”她说，“多谢、多谢您无可估量的帮助。提包要我拿么？”

===Original===
“If you want it. But I must take out the notebooks.”

===Chinese===
“您要就归您。不过笔记本我得取出来。”

===Original===
“Why must you take them?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“为什么您非要带走不可？”她问。

===Original===
“Why, haven’t I got to copy the notes out into longhand?”

===Chinese===
“咦，这些笔记不是还得由我誊成普通文字么？”

===Original===
An expression of utter consternation spread over her face; in fact, she was so completely taken aback that she forgot to release my hand.

===Chinese===
一副全然惊愕的神色在她脸上漾开；实际上，她窘得竟忘了松开我的手。

===Original===
“Heavens!” she exclaimed. “How idiotic of me! But it is impossible, Doctor Berkeley! It will take you hours!”

===Chinese===
“天哪！”她叫道，“我多蠢呀！可这不行，伯克利大夫！这要花您好几个钟头呢！”

===Original===
“It is perfectly possible, and it is going to be done; otherwise the notes would be useless. Do you want the bag?”

===Chinese===
“行也行得，而且非做不可；不然这些笔记就全白费了。您要提包么？”

===Original===
“No, of course not. But I am positively appalled. Hadn’t you better give up the idea?”

===Chinese===
“不要，当然不要。可我实在惶恐得很。您还是打消这个念头不好么？”

===Original===
“And this is the end of our collaboration?” I exclaimed tragically, giving her hand a final squeeze (whereby she became suddenly aware of its position, and withdrew it rather hastily). “Would you throw away a whole afternoon’s work? I won’t certainly; so, goodbye until tomorrow. I shall turn up in the reading-room as early as I can. You had better take the tickets. Oh, and you won’t forget about the copy of the will for Doctor Thorndyke, will you?”

===Chinese===
“这就是咱们合作的下场么？”我悲腔悲调地嚷道，把她的手最后握了一握（这一握，她才猛然察觉那只手正落在哪里，慌忙抽了回去）。“您忍心把一下午的劳动整个儿扔掉么？反正我不肯；那么，明天见。我要一早尽快赶到阅览室。索书单您最好带上。哦，还有，给桑代克博士的遗嘱副本，您总不会忘的吧？”

===Original===
“No; if my father agrees, you shall have it this evening.”

===Chinese===
“不会忘；只要我父亲同意，今晚您就能拿到它。”

===Original===
She took the tickets from me, and, thanking me yet again, retired into the court.

===Chinese===
她从我手里接过索书单，再次向我道谢，便转身进巷去了。

## VII John Bellingham’s Will / 七 约翰·贝林厄姆的遗嘱
===Original===
The task upon which I had embarked so light-heartedly, when considered in cold blood, did certainly appear, as Miss Bellingham had said, rather appalling. The result of two and a half hours’ pretty steady work at an average speed of nearly a hundred words a minute, would take some time to transcribe into longhand; and if the notes were to be delivered punctually on the morrow, the sooner I got to work the better.

===Chinese===
我当初满心轻快地承担下来的这桩任务，冷静下来一考量，确实如贝林厄姆小姐所言，显得相当吓人。两个半钟头相当扎实的劳动成果——平均速度接近每分钟一百个词——要誊写成普通文字，颇得花些时间；何况笔记须在明天如期交出，自然是动手越早越好。

===Original===
Recognizing this truth, I lost no time, but, within five minutes of my arrival at the surgery, was seated at the writing-table with my copy before me busily converting the sprawling, inexpressive characters into good, legible round-hand.

===Chinese===
明白了这层道理，我便不敢耽搁，回到诊所不出五分钟，就已在写字台前坐定，面前摊开那份速记稿，忙着把那些张牙舞爪、毫无表情的符号，改写成端正易读的圆体字。

===Original===
The occupation was by no means unpleasant, apart from the fact that it was a labor of love; for the sentences, as I picked them up, were fragrant with the reminiscences of the gracious whisper in which they had first come to me. And then the matter itself was full of interest. I was gaining a fresh outlook on life, was crossing the threshold of a new world (which was her world); and so the occasional interruptions from the patients, while they gave me intervals of enforced rest, were far from welcome.

===Chinese===
这桩活计本身绝不算讨厌，何况它还是一桩甘之如饴的爱之劳作；因为那些句子被我逐一拾起时，还带着当初送到我耳边的那温柔耳语的余香。再者，内容本身就趣味盎然。我正在获得一种全新的人生见识，正跨进一个新世界的门槛（那正是她的世界）；因此病人们不时上门打岔，虽然给了我几段强制休息的间歇，却实在不受欢迎。

===Original===
The evening wore on without any sign from Nevill’s Court, and I began to fear that Mr. Bellingham’s scruples had proved insurmountable. Not, I am afraid, that I was so much concerned for the copy of the will as for the possibility of a visit, no matter howsoever brief, from my fair employer; and when, on the stroke of half-past seven, the surgery door flew open with startling abruptness, my fears were allayed and my hopes shattered simultaneously. For it was Miss Oman who stalked in, holding out a blue foolscap envelope with a warlike air as if it were an ultimatum.

===Chinese===
晚色渐深，内维尔巷方面始终毫无动静，我开始担心贝林厄姆先生的顾忌到底没法打消。恐怕我惦记遗嘱誊本还在其次，更惦记的是我那位美丽的雇主前来一访的可能——哪怕一趟再短也好；等到七点半钟声一响，诊所的门蓦地大敞，我的疑惧顿消，希望也随之破灭。原来大步走进来的是奥曼小姐，手里递出一封蓝色大页纸信封，那副临阵的架势，仿佛递交的是一纸最后通牒。

===Original===
“I’ve brought you this from Mr. Bellingham,” she said. “There’s a note inside.”

===Chinese===
“这是贝林厄姆先生叫我带给您的，”她说。“里面有张便条。”

===Original===
“May I read the note, Miss Oman?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“我可以看看便条么，奥曼小姐？”我问。

===Original===
“Bless the man!” she exclaimed. “What else would you do with it? Isn’t that what it’s brought for?”

===Chinese===
“我的老天爷！”她嚷道，“不然您还要它做什么？不正是为这个才带来的么？”

===Original===
I supposed it was; and, thanking her for her gracious permission, I glanced through the note﻿—a few lines authorizing me to show the copy of the will to Dr. Thorndyke. When I looked up from the paper I found her eyes fixed on me with an expression critical and rather disapproving.

===Chinese===
想必正是；我谢过她慨然恩准，便把便条浏览了一遍——寥寥数行，准许我把遗嘱誊本出示给桑代克博士。我从纸上抬起头来，发现她两眼紧盯着我，神情挑剔，颇不以为然。

===Original===
“You seem to be making yourself mighty agreeable in a certain quarter,” she remarked.

===Chinese===
“您在某一处地方，似乎把自己经营得格外讨人喜欢哪。”她说。

===Original===
“I make myself universally agreeable. It is my nature to.”

===Chinese===
“我是一贯到处讨人喜欢。天性如此。”

===Original===
“Ha!” she snorted.

===Chinese===
“哈！”她哼了一声。

===Original===
“Don’t you find me rather agreeable?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“您不觉得我相当讨人喜欢么？”我问。

===Original===
“Oily,” said Miss Oman. And then with a sour smile at the open notebooks, she remarked:

===Chinese===
“滑头。”奥曼小姐说。接着，她朝摊开的笔记本露出一个酸溜溜的微笑，说道：

===Original===
“You’ve got some work to do now; quite a change for you.”

===Chinese===
“如今您也有活儿可干了；对您倒真是件新鲜事。”

===Original===
“A delightful change, Miss Oman. ‘For Satan findeth’﻿—but no doubt you are acquainted with the philosophical works of Dr. Watts?”

===Chinese===
“可是件叫人愉快的新鲜事呢，奥曼小姐。‘撒旦总会替闲手找到——’不过，瓦茨博士（Dr. Watts）那些富于哲理的诗篇，想必您是熟读过的了？”

===Original===
“If you are referring to ‘idle hands,’ ” she replied, “I’ll give you a bit of advice. Don’t you keep that hand idle any longer than is really necessary. I have my suspicions about that splint﻿—oh, you know what I mean,” and before I had time to reply, she had taken advantage of the entrance of a couple of patients to whisk out of the surgery with the abruptness that had distinguished her arrival.

===Chinese===
“您要是暗指‘闲着的手’，”她答道，“那我就劝您一句。那只手啊，凡是实在不必闲着的时候，就别再让它闲着了。我对那副夹板自有疑心——哦，您知道我是什么意思，”不等我来得及回答，她已趁着两位病人进门，一阵风似的卷出诊所，来得突兀，去得也突兀。

===Original===
The evening consultations were considered to be over by half-past eight; at which time Adolphus was wont with exemplary punctuality to close the outer door of the surgery. Tonight he was not less prompt than usual; and having performed this, his last daily office, and turned down the surgery gas, he reported the fact and took his departure.

===Chinese===
晚间应诊照例到八点半就算结束；每到这个时辰，阿道弗斯总以堪称楷模的准时，把诊所的外门关上。今晚他也不比往常迟缓；办完这桩他每日的最后一项职务，把诊所的煤气灯捻小，他前来禀明事毕，随即告退。

===Original===
As his retreating footsteps died away and the slamming of the outer door announced his final disappearance, I sat up and stretched myself. The envelope containing the copy of the will lay on the table, and I considered it thoughtfully. It ought to be conveyed to Thorndyke with as little delay as possible, and, as it certainly could not be trusted out of my hands, it ought to be conveyed by me.

===Chinese===
他远去的脚步声渐渐消逝，外门砰的一响，宣告他彻底离去，我坐直身子，伸了个懒腰。装着遗嘱誊本的信封摊在桌上，我对着它出了会儿神。这东西应该尽快送到桑代克手里；而它既然万万不能离手托付他人，就该由我亲自送去。

===Original===
I looked at the notebooks. Nearly two hours’ work had made a considerable impression on the matter that I had to transcribe, but still, a great deal of the task yet remained to be done. However, I reflected, I could put in a couple of hours or more before going to bed and there would be an hour or two to spare in the morning. Finally I locked the notebooks, open as they were, in the writing-table drawer, and slipping the envelope into my pocket, set out for the Temple.

===Chinese===
我看了看那几本笔记。近两个钟头的功夫，已在我该誊的材料上消化掉可观的一段，但任务还剩下老大一截。不过我想，睡前还可以再做上两个钟头乃至更多，明早也还抽得出一两个钟头的富余。末了，我把笔记原样摊开，锁进写字台的抽屉，把信封揣进口袋，动身往内殿去。

===Original===
The soft chime of the Treasury clock was telling out, in confidential tones, the third quarter as I rapped with my stick on the forbidding “oak” of my friends’ chambers. There was no response, nor had I perceived any gleam of light from the windows as I approached, and I was considering the advisability of trying the laboratory on the next floor, when footsteps on the stone stairs and familiar voices gladdened my ear.

===Chinese===
我用手杖叩响我那两位朋友寓所那扇拒人千里的“橡木门”时，财务楼的钟正以近乎耳语的柔和音响，报出第三刻。门内无人回应；我一路走来，也不曾看见窗里透出些微亮光。我正在盘算要不要上隔一层楼去实验室碰碰运气，石楼梯上忽然传来脚步声和熟悉的语声，叫我心头一喜。

===Original===
“Hallo, Berkeley!” said Thorndyke, “do we find you waiting like a Peri at the gates of Paradise? Polton is upstairs, you know, tinkering at one of his inventions. If you ever find the nest empty, you had better go up and bang at the laboratory door. He’s always there in the evenings.”

===Chinese===
“喂，伯克利！”桑代克说，“瞧咱们发现的，竟是一位守在乐园门口的佩丽（Peri）仙子？波尔顿在楼上呢，你知道，正在摆弄他的一项发明。往后你要是发现这巢里空无一人，最好就上楼去擂实验室的门。晚上他总在那儿。”

===Original===
“I haven’t been waiting long,” said I, “and I was just thinking of rousing him up when you came.”

===Chinese===
“我并没等多久，”我说，“你们来的时候，我正想着要不要去把他唤起来呢。”

===Original===
“That was right,” said Thorndyke, turning up the gas. “And what news do you bring? Do I see a blue envelope sticking out of your pocket?”

===Chinese===
“这就对了，”桑代克说着，把煤气灯捻大。“你带来了什么消息？你口袋里露出来的，是不是一只蓝色信封？”

===Original===
“You do.”

===Chinese===
“正是。”

===Original===
“Is it a copy of the will?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“是遗嘱的誊本么？”他问。

===Original===
I answered “yes,” and added that I had full permission to show it to him.

===Chinese===
我回答“是”，并补充说，我已获得充分许可，可以把它拿给他看。

===Original===
“What did I tell you?” exclaimed Jervis. “Didn’t I say that he would get the copy for us if it existed?”

===Chinese===
“我怎么说来着？”杰维斯嚷道，“我不是说过，只要誊本存在，他准会给咱们弄到手么？”

===Original===
“We admit the excellence of your prognosis,” said Thorndyke, “but there is no need to be boastful. Have you read through the document, Berkeley?”

===Chinese===
“我们承认你这个预后诊断高明，”桑代克说，“可也犯不着自夸。那份文书你通读过了么，伯克利？”

===Original===
“No, I haven’t taken it out of the envelope.”

===Chinese===
“没有，我还不曾把它从信封里取出来过。”

===Original===
“Then it will be equally new to us all, and we shall see if it tallies with your description.”

===Chinese===
“那它对咱们三个就一样新鲜，咱们正好看看，它同你的描述对不对得上。”

===Original===
He placed three easy-chairs at a convenient distance from the light, and Jervis, watching him with a smile, remarked:

===Chinese===
他把三把安乐椅安放在离灯光不远不近的地方，杰维斯含笑望着他，说道：

===Original===
“Now Thorndyke is going to enjoy himself. To him, a perfectly unintelligible will is a thing of beauty and a joy forever; especially if associated with some kind of recondite knavery.”

===Chinese===
“瞧，桑代克要开始享福了。在他看来，一份完全读不懂的遗嘱，便是一件美的事物、一份永久的喜悦；倘若再牵连着某种深奥的奸计，那就更妙。”

===Original===
“I don’t know,” said I, “that this will is particularly unintelligible. The mischief seems to be that it is rather too intelligible. However, here it is,” and I handed it over to Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“我倒看不出，”我说，“这份遗嘱有什么特别难懂的地方。毛病似乎倒在于它太容易懂了。不过，喏，在这儿，”说着我把它递给了桑代克。

===Original===
“I suppose that we can depend on this copy,” said the latter, as he drew out the document and glanced at it. “Oh, yes,” he added, “I see it is copied by Godfrey Bellingham, compared with the original and certified correct. In that case I will get you to read it out slowly, Jervis, and I will make a rough copy for reference. Let us make ourselves comfortable and light our pipes before we begin.”

===Chinese===
“这份誊本想必靠得住，”后者一面抽出文书、扫它一眼，一面说道。“哦，不错，”他补充道，“我看出这是戈弗雷·贝林厄姆誊录、经与原件核对、证明无误的。既然如此，杰维斯，就请你慢慢地念，我来做一个粗略的底本备查。开始之前，咱们先坐舒服，把烟斗点起来。”

===Original===
He provided himself with a writing-pad, and, when we had seated ourselves and got our pipes well alight, Jervis opened the document, and with a premonitory “hem!” commenced the reading.

===Chinese===
他给自己备好一叠便笺，待我们各自落座、烟斗都吸得旺了，杰维斯展开文书，咳嗽一声“哼！”作个引子，便开始宣读。

===Original===
> “In the name of God Amen. This is the last will and testament of me John Bellingham of number 141 Queen Square in the parish of St. George Bloomsbury London in the county of Middlesex Gentleman made this twenty-first day of September in the year of our Lord one thousand eight hundred and ninety-two.
> “1. I give and bequeath unto Arthur Jellicoe of number 184 New Square Lincoln’s Inn London in the county of Middlesex Attorney-at-law the whole of my collection of seals and scarabs and those in my cabinets marked A, B, and D together with the contents thereof and the sum of two thousand pounds sterling free of legacy duty.
> “Unto the trustees of the British Museum the residue of my collection of antiquities.
> “Unto my cousin George Hurst of the Poplars Eltham in the county of Kent the sum of five thousand pounds free of legacy duty and unto my brother Godfrey Bellingham or if he should die before the occurrence of my death unto his daughter Ruth Bellingham the residue of my estate and effects real and personal subject to the conditions set forth hereinafter namely:
> “2. That my body shall be deposited with those of my ancestors in the churchyard appertaining to the church and parish of St. George the Martyr or if that shall not be possible in some other churchyard cemetery burial ground church or chapel or other authorized place for the reception of the bodies of the dead situate within or appertaining to the parishes of St. Andrew above the Bars and St. George the Martyr or St. George Bloomsbury and St. Giles in the Fields. But if the conditions in this clause be not carried out then
> “3. I give and devise the said residue of my estate and effects unto my cousin George Hurst aforesaid and I hereby revoke all wills and codicils made by me at any time heretofore and I appoint Arthur Jellicoe aforesaid to be the executor of this my will jointly with the principal beneficiary and residuary legatee that is to say with the aforesaid Godfrey Bellingham if the conditions set forth hereinbefore in clause 2 shall be duly carried out but with the aforesaid George Hurst if the said conditions in the said clause 2 be not carried out.
> “**John Bellingham**.
> “Signed by the said testator John Bellingham in the presence of us present at the same time who at his request and in his presence and in the presence of each other have subscribed our names as witnesses.
> “**Frederick Wilton**, 16 Medford Road, London, N., clerk.
> “**James Barber**, 32 Wadbury Crescent, London, S.W., clerk.”

===Chinese===
> “以上帝之名，阿门。本文件为我约翰·贝林厄姆之最后遗嘱及遗言，我为米德尔塞克斯郡伦敦市布卢姆斯伯里圣乔治（St. George）堂区皇后广场 141 号绅士，本遗嘱订立于我主一千八百九十二年九月二十一日。
> “1. 我将本人收藏之印章与圣甲虫全部，连同本人标有 A、B、D 字样之柜橱及其中物件，以及两千英镑之金额，免征遗产税，赠与并遗赠予米德尔塞克斯郡伦敦林肯律师学院新广场 184 号之律师阿瑟·杰利科（Arthur Jellicoe）。
> “本人古物收藏之其余部分，赠予大英博物馆之受托人。
> “本人赠予肯特郡埃尔瑟姆白杨居之本人表亲乔治·赫斯特五千英镑，免征遗产税；并将本人不动产与动产之余产，赠予本人之弟戈弗雷·贝林厄姆，倘其先于本人身故，则赠予其女露丝·贝林厄姆，惟须遵从下文所载之条件，即：
> “2. 本人遗体应安放于殉道者圣乔治（St. George the Martyr）教堂及其堂区所辖墓园，与本人列祖列宗同处；如无可能，则应安放于下列堂区之内或所辖之其他教堂墓园、坟场、墓地、教堂或礼拜堂，或收殓死者遗体之其他获准场所：即栏上圣安德鲁（St. Andrew above the Bars）与殉道者圣乔治两堂区，或布卢姆斯伯里圣乔治与田野圣吉尔斯（St. Giles in the Fields）两堂区。但若本条款所载条件未获履行，则——
> “3. 本人将上述本人产业与财物之余产，赠与并转让予前述本人表亲乔治·赫斯特；本人谨此撤销本人以往任何时刻所立之一切遗嘱及遗嘱附件；并指定前述阿瑟·杰利科与主要受益人兼余产受遗赠人共同为本遗嘱之执行人，即：若前文第二条所载条件获得正当履行，则与前述戈弗雷·贝林厄姆共同执行；若该第二条所载条件未获履行，则与前述乔治·赫斯特共同执行。
> “**约翰·贝林厄姆**。
> “本遗嘱由上述立遗嘱人约翰·贝林厄姆签署，其时我等同时在场，应其请求、当其面并当彼此之面，作为见证人签押于下。
> “**弗雷德里克·威尔顿**（Frederick Wilton），伦敦北区梅德福德路 16 号，职员。
> “**詹姆斯·巴伯**（James Barber），伦敦西南区沃德伯里新月街 32 号，职员。”

===Original===
“Well,” said Jervis, laying down the document as Thorndyke detached the last sheet from his writing-pad, “I have met with a good many idiotic wills, but this one can give them all points. I don’t see how it is ever going to be administered. One of the two executors is a mere abstraction﻿—a sort of algebraical problem with no answer.”

===Chinese===
“这个么，”杰维斯说——桑代克这时正从便笺簿上撕下最后一页——“我见过的白痴遗嘱也算多了，可这一份可以给它们全都让分。我不明白它究竟怎么个执行法。两位执行人当中有一位，纯粹是个抽象观念——好比一道没有答案的代数题。”

===Original===
“I think that difficulty could be overcome,” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“我看那个困难是有法子克服的。”桑代克说。

===Original===
“I don’t see how,” retorted Jervis. “If the body is deposited in a certain place, A is executor; if it is somewhere else, B is the executor. But as you cannot produce the body, and no one has the least idea where it is, it is impossible to prove either that it is or that it is not in any specified place.”

===Chinese===
“我看不出怎么个克服法，”杰维斯反驳道。“遗体若是安放在某个地方，甲便是执行人；若是安放在别处，乙便是执行人。可你既拿不出遗体，又没有任何人对它的下落有丝毫概念，要证明它是在、或不在任何一个指定的地方，都是不可能的。”

===Original===
“You are magnifying the difficulty, Jervis,” said Thorndyke. “The body may, of course, be anywhere in the entire world, but the place where it is lying is either inside or outside the general boundary of those two parishes. If it has been deposited within the boundary of those two parishes, the fact must be ascertainable by examining the burial certificates issued since the date when the missing man was last seen alive and by consulting the registers of those specified places of burial. I think that if no record can be found of any such interment within the boundary of those two parishes, that fact will be taken by the Court as proof that no such interment has taken place, and that therefore the body must have been deposited somewhere else. Such a decision would constitute George Hurst the co-executor and residuary legatee.”

===Chinese===
“你把困难夸大了，杰维斯，”桑代克说。“遗体固然可能在全世界的任何一个角落，但它所在的地点，总不外乎在那两个堂区的总边界之内或之外。倘若它被安放在那两个堂区的边界之内，这一事实必定查证得出来：只须查验失踪者最后一次被人见到活着之后所签发的各项安葬证明，再查阅那些指定安葬场所的登记簿。我想，倘若在那两个堂区边界之内查不到任何此类下葬的记录，法院就会把这一事实当作证据，认定未曾发生过此类下葬，从而断定遗体必定被安放在了别处。这样的判决，就会使乔治·赫斯特成为共同执行人兼余产受遗赠人。”

===Original===
“That is cheerful for your friends, Berkeley,” Jervis remarked, “for we may take it as pretty certain that the body has not been deposited in any of the places named.”

===Chinese===
“这对你的朋友们可是件喜事，伯克利，”杰维斯说，“因为咱们几乎可以断定，遗体并没有被安放在文中所列的任何一个地点。”

===Original===
“Yes,” I agreed gloomily, “I’m afraid there is very little doubt of that. But what an ass that fellow must have been to make such a to-do about his beastly carcass! What the deuce could it have mattered to him where it was dumped, when he had done with it?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我黯然附和，“恐怕这一点没什么可怀疑的了。可是这家伙也真是一头蠢驴，竟为自己那副臭皮囊这么大动干戈！人都用完它了，它被扔在什么地方，又与他何干呢？”

===Original===
Thorndyke chuckled softly. “Thus the irreverent youth of today,” said he. “But yours is hardly a fair comment, Berkeley. Our training makes us materialists, and puts us a little out of sympathy with those in whom primitive beliefs and emotions survive. A worthy priest who came to look at our dissecting-room expressed surprise to me that the students, thus constantly in the presence of relics of mortality, should be able to think of anything but the resurrection and the life hereafter. He was a bad psychologist. There is nothing so dead as a dissecting-room ‘subject’; and the contemplation of the human body in the process of being quietly taken to pieces﻿—being resolved into its structural units like a worn-out clock or an old engine in the scrapper’s yard﻿—is certainly not conducive to a vivid realization of the doctrine of the resurrection.”

===Chinese===
桑代克低声轻笑。“当今狂妄的青年就是这样，”他说。“不过你这评语有欠公允，伯克利。我们受的训练把我们造成了唯物论者，使我们对于那些身上还存留着原始信仰与原始情感的人，多少失去了同情。有一位可敬的教士来参观我们的解剖室，向我表示惊异：学生们常年置身于死亡的遗迹之间，居然还能想到复活与来生以外的任何事情。他是个不高明的心理学家。世上再没有比解剖室的‘标本’更死的东西了；静观一具人的躯体被不声不响地拆解——像废品场里一只破钟或一台旧引擎那样，被分解成一个个结构单元——实在无助于对复活教义获得活生生的体认。”

===Original===
“No; but this absurd anxiety to be buried in some particular place has nothing to do with religious belief; it is merely silly sentiment.”

===Chinese===
“不对；可是这种非要葬在某个特定地点不可的荒唐焦虑，同宗教信仰毫无关系；那不过是愚蠢的感伤罢了。”

===Original===
“It is sentiment, I admit,” said Thorndyke, “but I wouldn’t call it silly. The feeling is so widespread in time and space that we must look on it with respect as something inherent in human nature. Think﻿—as doubtless John Bellingham did﻿—of the ancient Egyptians, whose chief aspiration was that of everlasting repose for the dead. See the trouble they took to achieve it. Think of the great Pyramid, or that of Amenhotep the Fourth with its labyrinth of false passages and its sealed and hidden sepulchral chambers. Think of Jacob, borne after death all those hundreds of weary miles in order that he might sleep with his fathers and then remember Shakespeare and his solemn adjuration to posterity to let him rest undisturbed in his grave. No, Berkeley, it is not a silly sentiment. I am as indifferent as you as to what becomes of my body ‘when I have done with it,’ to use your irreverent phrase; but I recognize the solicitude that some other men display on the subject as a natural feeling that has to be taken seriously.”

===Chinese===
“这是感伤，我承认，”桑代克说，“但我不会说它愚蠢。这种情感在时间上和空间上散布得如此之广，我们必须怀着敬意把它看作人性中固有的东西。想想古埃及人吧——约翰·贝林厄姆无疑也想到过——他们最大的心愿，便是让死者得到永世的安息。瞧瞧他们为达到这个目的费了多少事。想想大金字塔，再想想阿蒙霍特普四世的那一座，带着迷宫般的假甬道和封闭隐匿的墓室。想想雅各，死后被抬着走了那好几百英里的倦程，只为能同他的列祖同眠；然后再想想莎士比亚，想想他向后世发出的庄严训诫——让他在坟墓里安息，不受搅扰。不，伯克利，这不是愚蠢的感伤。用你不敬的话说，‘等我用完了它’之后，我这副躯壳如何处置，我同你一样漠然；但我承认，另一些人在这个问题上表现出的牵挂，是一种天然的情怀，必须认真对待。”

===Original===
“But even so,” I said, “if this man had a hankering for a freehold residence in some particular bone-yard, he might have gone about the business in a more reasonable way.”

===Chinese===
“可即便是这样，”我说，“这个人既然一心想在某一座特定的埋骨之所里置办一处永久产权的居所，他也满可以用更合情理的方式来办这件事。”

===Original===
“There I am entirely with you,” Thorndyke replied. “It is the absurd way in which this provision is worded that not only creates all the trouble but also makes the whole document so curiously significant in view of the testator’s disappearance.”

===Chinese===
“在这一点上我完全同意你，”桑代克答道。“正是这一条款措辞的荒唐方式，不但惹出了全部的麻烦，而且使整份文件——考虑到立遗嘱人的失踪——显得出奇地意味深长。”

===Original===
“How significant?” Jervis demanded eagerly.

===Chinese===
“怎么个意味深长法？”杰维斯急切地追问。

===Original===
“Let us consider the provisions of the will point by point,” said Thorndyke; “and first note that the testator commanded the services of a very capable lawyer.”

===Chinese===
“咱们来逐条考量这份遗嘱的条款，”桑代克说；“首先要注意，立遗嘱人雇到的是一位非常干练的律师。”

===Original===
“But Mr. Jellicoe disapproved of the will,” said I; “in fact, he protested strongly against the form of it.”

===Chinese===
“可是杰利科先生并不赞成这份遗嘱，”我说；“事实上，他对遗嘱的形式提出过强烈抗议。”

===Original===
“We will bear that in mind too,” Thorndyke replied. “And now with reference to what we may call the contentious clauses: the first thing that strikes us is their preposterous injustice. Godfrey’s inheritance is made conditional on a particular disposal of the testator’s body. But this is a matter not necessarily under Godfrey’s control. The testator might have been lost at sea, or killed in a fire or explosion, or have died abroad and been buried where his grave could not have been identified. There are numerous probable contingencies besides the improbable one that has happened that might prevent the body from being recovered.

===Chinese===
“这一点咱们也记在心里，”桑代克答道。“现在来谈谈我们不妨称之为争议条款的部分：最先跳进我们眼里的，是它们骇人听闻的不公。戈弗雷的继承权，竟以立遗嘱人遗体之得到某种特定处置为条件。可这是一件未必归戈弗雷掌控的事。立遗嘱人可能葬身大海，可能丧命于火灾或爆炸，可能死在国外、葬在坟茔无从辨认之处。除了实际发生的这个不大可能的事件之外，还有许许多多可能发生的意外，都会使遗体无法寻回。”

===Original===
“But even if the body had been recovered, there is another difficulty. The places of burial in the parishes have all been closed for many years. It would be impossible to reopen any of them without a special faculty, and I doubt whether such a faculty would be granted. Possibly cremation might meet the difficulty, but even that is doubtful; and, in any case, the matter would not be in the control of Godfrey Bellingham. Yet, if the required interment should prove impossible, he is to be deprived of his legacy.”

===Chinese===
“但即使遗体寻回了，还有另一重困难。那几个堂区里的安葬之地，全都封禁多年了。没有一纸特别特许状，一处也重开不了；而这样的特许状会不会颁下来，我很怀疑。火葬也许能应付这个难题，但就连这一点也属可疑；况且无论如何，这件事都不归戈弗雷·贝林厄姆掌控。然而，一旦所要求的安葬被证明无法办到，他就要被剥夺遗赠。”

===Original===
“It is a monstrous and absurd injustice,” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“这是骇人听闻而荒唐的不公。”我嚷道。

===Original===
“It is,” Thorndyke agreed; “but this is nothing to the absurdity that comes to light when we consider clauses two and three in detail. Observe that the testator presumably wished to be buried in a certain place; also he wished his brother should benefit under the will. Let us take the first point and see how he has set about securing the accomplishment of what he desired. Now if we read clauses two and three carefully, we shall see that he has rendered it virtually impossible that his wishes can be carried out. He desires to be buried in a certain place and makes Godfrey responsible for his being so buried. But he gives Godfrey no power or authority to carry out the provision, and places insuperable obstacles in his way. For until Godfrey is an executor, he has no power or authority to carry out the provision; and until the provisions are carried out, he does not become an executor.”

===Chinese===
“正是，”桑代克同意道；“可这同咱们细看第二、第三两条时水落石出的荒唐相比，又算不得什么了。且看：立遗嘱人多半愿意葬在某个特定的地点；同时他也愿意让弟弟依照遗嘱受益。先取第一点，看看他是怎样着手确保自己愿望之实现的。现在，咱们若细读第二、第三两条，便会看出，他已经使自己的愿望事实上无法实现。他要求葬在某个特定地点，又让戈弗雷对这一安葬负责。可他既不给戈弗雷执行这一条款的任何权柄或权限，反倒在戈弗雷的路上设下不可逾越的障碍。因为戈弗雷不到当上执行人的一天，就没有执行条款的权柄或权限；而条款一天不执行，他就一天当不上执行人。”

===Original===
“It is a preposterous muddle,” exclaimed Jervis.

===Chinese===
“这是一团糟得离谱。”杰维斯嚷道。

===Original===
“Yes, but that is not the worst of it,” Thorndyke continued. “The moment John Bellingham dies, his dead body has come into existence; and it is ‘deposited,’ for the time being, wherever he happens to have died. But unless he should happen to have died in one of the places of burial mentioned﻿—which is in the highest degree unlikely﻿—his body will be, for the time being, ‘deposited’ in some place other than those specified. In that case clause two is﻿—for the time being﻿—not complied with, and consequently George Hurst becomes, automatically, the co-executor.

===Chinese===
“是的，可这还不是最糟的，”桑代克接着说。“约翰·贝林厄姆一死，他的遗体便已经存在；而且——就眼下而言——遗体是‘安放’在他碰巧死去的任何地方。但除非他碰巧死在文中所列的某一处安葬场所——这万分地不可能——他的遗体，就眼下而言，便是‘安放’在指定场所之外的某处。这样一来，第二条就——就眼下而言——未获履行，乔治·赫斯特于是自然而然地成了共同执行人。”

===Original===
“But will George Hurst carry out the provisions of clause two? Probably not. Why should he? The will contains no instructions to that effect. It throws the whole duty on Godfrey. On the other hand, if he should carry out clause two, what happens? He ceases to be an executor and he loses some seventy thousand pounds. We may be pretty certain that he will do nothing of the kind. So that, on considering the two clauses, we see that the wishes of the testator could only be carried out in the unlikely event of his dying in one of the burial-places mentioned, or his body being conveyed immediately after death to a public mortuary in one of the said parishes. In any other event, it is virtually certain that he will be buried in some place other than that which he desired, and that his brother will be left absolutely without provision or recognition.”

===Chinese===
“可乔治·赫斯特会去履行第二条的规定么？多半不会。他凭什么要履行？遗嘱里没有这方面的任何指示。它把全部责任都推给了戈弗雷。反过来，倘若他真履行了第二条，会怎么样呢？他就当不成执行人了，还要损失大约七万英镑。咱们可以相当有把握地说，他绝不会干这种事。所以，把这两条合起来一看，咱们就明白：立遗嘱人的愿望，只有在他碰巧死在文中所列的某一处葬地，或遗体于死后即刻被运往上述堂区之一的公共停尸房——这一不太可能的事件——之下，才有可能实现。在任何其他情形下，他几乎注定要葬在他所意愿之外的地方，而他的弟弟则将被完全置于脑后，既无遗赠，亦无名分。”

===Original===
“John Bellingham could never have intended that,” I said.

===Chinese===
“约翰·贝林厄姆绝不可能是这个用意。”我说。

===Original===
“Clearly not,” agreed Thorndyke; “the provisions of the will furnish internal evidence that he did not. You note that he bequeathed five thousand pounds to George Hurst, in the event of clause two being carried out; but he has made no bequest to his brother in the event of its not being carried out. Obviously, he had not entertained the possibility of this contingency at all. He assumed, as a matter of course, that the conditions of clause two would be fulfilled, and regarded the conditions themselves as a mere formality.”

===Chinese===
“显然不是，”桑代克同意道；“遗嘱的条款本身就提供了内在证据，证明他没有这个用意。你注意到：他遗赠给乔治·赫斯特五千英镑，是以第二条获得履行为前提；可若第二条未获履行，他却没有给弟弟留下任何遗赠。显而易见，他压根儿不曾虑及这种意外。他想当然地认定第二条的条件必定会履行，并把条件本身仅仅看作一道例行手续。”

===Original===
“But,” Jervis objected, “Jellicoe must have seen the danger of a miscarriage and pointed it out to his client.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”杰维斯反驳道，“杰利科必定看出了失手的风险，并且向他委托人指出过。”

===Original===
“Exactly,” said Thorndyke. “There is the mystery. We understand that he objected strenuously, and that John Bellingham was obdurate. Now it is perfectly understandable that a man should adhere obstinately to the most stupid and perverse disposition of his property; but that a man should persist in retaining a particular form of words after it has been proved to him that the use of such form will almost certainly result in the defeat of his own wishes; that, I say, is a mystery that calls for very careful consideration.”

===Chinese===
“正是，”桑代克说。“谜就出在这里。我们得知，他曾极力反对，而约翰·贝林厄姆顽固不化。一个人死抱着对自己财产最愚蠢、最乖张的处置安排不放，这完全可以理解；可是，当人家已经向他证明，采用这样一种措辞形式几乎必然导致他自己愿望的落空，他却仍旧坚持保留这一特定的措辞形式——这个么，我说，才是一个需要极仔细斟酌的谜。”

===Original===
“If Jellicoe had been an interested party,” said Jervis, “one would have suspected him of lying low. But the form of clause two doesn’t affect him at all.”

===Chinese===
“倘若杰利科是个有利害关系的人，”杰维斯说，“人家就会疑心他是故意蛰伏不动了。可第二条的形式对他毫无影响。”

===Original===
“No,” said Thorndyke; “the person who stands to profit by the muddle is George Hurst. But we understand that he was unacquainted with the terms of the will, and there is certainly nothing to suggest that he is in any way responsible for it.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”桑代克说；“能从这团乱麻里坐收其利的是乔治·赫斯特。可我们得知，他原先并不知悉遗嘱的条款；而且，绝没有什么迹象表明，这份遗嘱同他有任何干系。”

===Original===
“The practical question is,” said I, “what is going to happen? and what can be done for the Bellinghams?”

===Chinese===
“眼下切实的问题是，”我说，“事情会怎样发展？又能为贝林厄姆父女做些什么？”

===Original===
“The probability is,” Thorndyke replied, “that the next move will be made by Hurst. He is the party immediately interested. He will probably apply to the Court for permission to presume death and administer the will.”

===Chinese===
“多半是这样，”桑代克答道，“下一步棋要由赫斯特来走。他是直接的利害当事人。他大概会向法院申请推定死亡并执行遗嘱的许可。”

===Original===
“And what will the Court do?”

===Chinese===
“那么法院会怎么做呢？”

===Original===
Thorndyke smiled dryly. “Now you are asking a very pretty conundrum. The decisions of Courts depend on idiosyncrasies of temperament that no one can foresee. But one may say that a Court does not lightly grant permission to presume death. There will be a rigorous inquiry﻿—and a decidedly unpleasant one, I suspect﻿—and the evidence will be reviewed by the judge with a strong predisposition to regard the testator as being still alive. On the other hand, the known facts point very distinctly to the probability that he is dead; and, if the will were less complicated and all the parties interested were unanimous in supporting the application, I don’t see why it might not be granted. But it will clearly be to the interest of Godfrey to oppose the application, unless he can show that the conditions of clause two have been complied with﻿—which it is virtually certain he cannot; and he may be able to bring forward reasons for believing John to be still alive. But even if he is unable to do this, inasmuch as it is pretty clear that he was intended to be the chief beneficiary, his opposition is likely to have considerable weight with the Court.”

===Chinese===
桑代克淡淡一笑。“这你可问着了一道绝妙的难题。法院的裁决，取决于谁也预见不到的性情癖好。不过可以这么说：法院不会轻率地恩准推定死亡。将会有一场严苛的查问——我看还是一场断然不愉快的查问——而且法官覆核证据时，会带着一种强烈的成见，倾向于把立遗嘱人看作仍然在世。另一方面，已知的事实又分明指向他已死亡的可能；倘若遗嘱不这么复杂，而各利害当事人又一致支持申请，我看没有理由不批。但显然，反对此项申请符合戈弗雷的利益，除非他能证明第二条的条件业已履行——而这几乎可以断定他做不到；况且他还可能举出理由，使人相信约翰仍然活着。不过，即便这一点他做不到，既然相当清楚立遗嘱人的原意是要他做主要受益人，他的反对在法院那里也很可能颇有分量。”

===Original===
“Oh, is it?” I exclaimed eagerly. “Then that accounts for a very peculiar proceeding on the part of Hurst. I have stupidly forgotten to tell you about it. He has been trying to come to a private agreement with Godfrey Bellingham.”

===Chinese===
“哦，是么？”我急切地嚷道。“那就说得通赫斯特一桩非常古怪的举动了。我糊涂，竟忘了告诉你们这件事。他一直在设法同戈弗雷·贝林厄姆达成一项私下的协议。”

===Original===
“Indeed!” said Thorndyke. “What sort of agreement?”

===Chinese===
“哦！”桑代克说。“什么样的协议？”

===Original===
“His proposal was this: that Godfrey should support him and Jellicoe in an application to the Court for permission to presume death and to administer the will, that if it was successful, Hurst should pay him four hundred pounds a year for life: the arrangement to hold good in all eventualities.”

===Chinese===
“他的提议是这样：由戈弗雷支持他和杰利科向法院申请推定死亡的许可并执行遗嘱；事情若成功，赫斯特须付给他每年四百镑，以终身为限：此项安排*无论出现何种结局*一律有效。”

===Original===
“By which he means?”

===Chinese===
“他这话是什么意思？”

===Original===
“That if the body should be discovered at any future time, so that the conditions of clause two could be carried out, Hurst should still retain the property and continue to pay Godfrey the four hundred a year for life.”

===Chinese===
“意思是说：倘若遗体日后任何时候被寻获，从而使第二条的条件得以履行，赫斯特仍保留产业，并继续终身付给戈弗雷每年四百镑。”

===Original===
“Hey, ho!” exclaimed Thorndyke; “that is a queer proposal; a very queer proposal indeed.”

===Chinese===
“嘿，嗬！”桑代克叹道；“这是个古怪的提议；实实在在是个非常古怪的提议。”

===Original===
“Not to say fishy,” added Jervis. “I don’t fancy the Court would look with approval on that little arrangement.”

===Chinese===
“岂止古怪，简直可疑，”杰维斯补充道。“我看法院是不会嘉许那套小安排的。”

===Original===
“The law does not look with much favor on any little arrangements that aim at getting behind the provisions of a will,” Thorndyke replied; “though there would be nothing to complain of in this proposal if it were not for the reference to ‘all eventualities.’ If a will is hopelessly impracticable, it is not unreasonable or improper for the various beneficiaries to make such private arrangements among themselves as may seem necessary to avoid useless litigation and delay in administering the will. If, for instance, Hurst had proposed to pay four hundred a year to Godfrey so long as the body remained undiscovered on condition that, in the event of its discovery, Godfrey should pay him a like sum for life, there would have been nothing to comment upon. It would have been an ordinary sporting chance. But the reference to ‘all eventualities’ is an entirely different matter. Of course, it may be mere greediness, but all the same it suggests some very curious reflections.”

===Chinese===
“凡是意在绕开遗嘱条款的小安排，法律都不怎么青眼相看，”桑代克答道；“不过，若不是有‘无论出现何种结局’那一笔，这项提议原也无可指摘。一份遗嘱若是无可救药地行不通，各受益人为避免无谓的讼争、避免执行遗嘱的延误，在他们之间自行作出看来必要的私下安排，既非无理，亦非不当。譬如说，倘若赫斯特提议：在遗体未被寻获期间，他付给戈弗雷每年四百镑，条件是遗体一旦寻获，戈弗雷终身付给他同等的数目——那就毫无可议之处了。那不过是一场寻常的机会对赌。可是提到‘无论出现何种结局’，就完全是另一回事了。当然，这也许只是贪心，可它毕竟暗示出一些非常耐人寻味的思量。”

===Original===
“Yes, it does,” said Jervis. “I wonder if he has any reason to expect that the body will be found? Of course it doesn’t follow that he has. He may be merely taking the opportunity offered by the other man’s poverty to make sure of the bulk of the property whatever happens. But it is uncommonly sharp practice, to say the least.”

===Chinese===
“是耐人寻味，”杰维斯说。“我不知道他是不是有什么理由，预期遗体会被寻获？当然，这未必说明他有。他也不过是趁对方贫困之危，好无论出什么事都稳拿产业的大头。但至少可以说，这是精明得异乎寻常的手法。”

===Original===
“Do I understand that Godfrey declined the proposal?” Thorndyke asked.

===Chinese===
“我理解得对不对：戈弗雷拒绝了这个提议？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“Yes, he did, very emphatically; and I fancy the two gentlemen proceeded to exchange opinions on the circumstances of the disappearance with more frankness than delicacy.”

===Chinese===
“是的，拒绝了，拒绝得斩钉截铁；而且我猜想，两位先生接着便就失踪的原委交换起意见来，其坦率有余，而雅驯不足。”

===Original===
“Ah,” said Thorndyke, “that is a pity. If the case comes into Court, there is bound to be a good deal of unpleasant discussion and still more unpleasant comment in the newspapers. But if the parties themselves begin to express suspicions of one another there is no telling where the matter will end.”

===Chinese===
“啊，”桑代克说，“那就可惜了。这案子一旦闹上法院，势必有一大番不愉快的争辩，报上还要有更不愉快的评论。可若是当事人自己先互相猜疑起来，这事就不知要收场到哪里了。”

===Original===
“No, by Jove!” said Jervis. “If they begin flinging accusations of murder about, the fat will be in the fire with a vengeance. That way lies the Old Bailey.”

===Chinese===
“可不是，老天爷作证！”杰维斯说。“他们要是开始把谋杀的指控满天乱甩，那可真要炸了锅了。顺着这条路走下去，直通老贝利（the Old Bailey）中央刑事法院。”

===Original===
“We must try to prevent them from making an unnecessary scandal,” said Thorndyke. “It may be that an exposure will be unavoidable, and that must be ascertained in advance. But to return to your question, Berkeley, as to what is to be done. Hurst will probably make some move pretty soon. Do you know if Jellicoe will act with him?”

===Chinese===
“咱们得设法拦住他们，别闹出一场不必要的丑闻，”桑代克说。“曝光也许是免不了的，那必须预先查明。不过，回到你的问题上来，伯克利，看看眼下能做些什么。赫斯特大概不久就会有所动作。你知道杰利科会不会同他联手么？”

===Original===
“No, he won’t. He declines to take any steps without Godfrey’s assent﻿—at least, that is what he says at present. His attitude is one of correct neutrality.”

===Chinese===
“不，他不会。没有戈弗雷的同意，他拒绝采取任何步骤——至少，他目前是这么说的。他的态度是一种规规矩矩的中立。”

===Original===
“That is satisfactory so far,” said Thorndyke, “though he may alter his tone when the case comes into Court. From what you said just now I gathered that Jellicoe would prefer to have the will administered and be quit of the whole business; which is natural enough, especially as he benefits under the will to the extent of two thousand pounds and a valuable collection. Consequently, we may fairly assume that, even if he maintains an apparent neutrality, his influence will be exerted in favor of Hurst rather than of Bellingham; from which it follows that Bellingham ought certainly to be properly advised, and, when the case goes into Court, properly represented.”

===Chinese===
“到此为止，这还算差强人意，”桑代克说，“尽管案子闹上法院后，他的口风也可能变。从你刚才的话里我听出来，杰利科倒巴不得遗嘱早日执行，好把这桩差事整个儿卸掉；这也够自然的，何况他在这份遗嘱下受益达两千英镑，外加一批值钱的收藏。因此，咱们尽可以假定：即使他保持表面的中立，他的影响力也会朝着赫斯特而不是贝林厄姆那一边使；由此可见，贝林厄姆理应得到妥善的咨询，而且案件进入法院时，理应得到妥善的代理。”

===Original===
“He can’t afford either the one or the other,” said I. “He’s as poor as an insolvent church mouse and as proud as the devil. He wouldn’t accept professional aid that he couldn’t pay for.”

===Chinese===
“这两样他一样也负担不起，”我说。“他穷得像破产的教堂老鼠，傲得像魔鬼。凡是他付不起报酬的专业援助，他是不会接受的。”

===Original===
“H’m,” grunted Thorndyke, “that’s awkward. But we can’t allow the case to go ‘by default,’ so to speak﻿—to fail for the mere lack of technical assistance. Besides, it is one of the most interesting cases that I have ever met with, and I am not going to see it bungled. He couldn’t object to a little general advice in a friendly, informal way﻿—*amicus curiae*, as old Brodribb is so fond of saying; and there is nothing to prevent us from pushing forward the preliminary inquiries.”

===Chinese===
“唔，”桑代克哼了一声，“这就难办了。可咱们不能听任这案子——这么说吧——‘缺席’败诉，仅仅因为缺少技术性的协助。再说，这也是我平生所遇到的最有兴味的案子之一，我可不愿眼看它被人办砸。以友好而非正式的方式给他一点泛泛的忠告，他总不至于见怪——用老布罗德里布（Brodribb）爱挂在嘴上的话说，*amicus curiae*（“法院之友”）；而且没有什么能拦着咱们把初步的调查推向前去。”

===Original===
“Of what nature would they be?”

===Chinese===
“那会是些什么性质的调查呢？”

===Original===
“Well, to begin with, we have to satisfy ourselves that the conditions of clause two have not been complied with: that John Bellingham has not been buried within the parish boundaries mentioned. Of course he has not, but we must not take anything for granted. Then we have to satisfy ourselves that he is not still alive and accessible. It is perfectly possible that he is, after all, and it is our business to trace him, if he is still in the land of the living. Jervis and I can carry out these investigations without saying anything to Bellingham; my learned brother will look through the register of burials﻿—not forgetting the cremations﻿—in the metropolitan area, and I will take the other matter in hand.”

===Chinese===
“这个么，首先，咱们得让自己确信第二条的条件并未履行：约翰·贝林厄姆并未葬在所提的那些堂区边界之内。他当然没有，可咱们不能想当然。其次，咱们得确信他并非仍然活着、而且寻得着。他到底还活着，这完全有可能；倘若他尚在人世，寻出他的下落便是咱们的本分。杰维斯和我可以不声不响地进行这些调查，不必对贝林厄姆提一个字；我这博学的同仁去翻检都会地区的安葬登记簿——火葬的也别忘了——另一件事由我来抓。”

===Original===
“You really think that John Bellingham may still be alive?” said I.

===Chinese===
“你当真认为约翰·贝林厄姆可能还活着？”我说。

===Original===
“Since his body has not been found, it is obviously a possibility. I think it in the highest degree improbable, but the improbable has to be investigated before it can be excluded.”

===Chinese===
“他的遗体既然没有寻获，这显然是一种可能。我认为它万分地不可能；可是，‘不可能’的事必得查究过了，才能排除。”

===Original===
“It sounds a rather hopeless quest,” I remarked. “How do you propose to begin?”

===Chinese===
“这趟寻觅听起来颇有渺茫之感，”我说。“你打算从哪儿着手呢？”

===Original===
“I think of beginning at the British Museum. The people there may be able to throw some light on his movements. I know that there are some important excavations in progress at Heliopolis﻿—in fact, the Director of the Egyptian Department is out there at the present moment; and Doctor Norbury, who is taking his place temporarily, is an old friend of Bellingham’s. I shall call on him and try to discover if there is anything that might have induced Bellingham suddenly to go abroad to Heliopolis, for instance. Also he may be able to tell me what it was that took the missing man to Paris on that last, rather mysterious journey. That might turn out to be an important clue. And meanwhile, Berkeley, you must endeavor tactfully to reconcile your friend to the idea of letting us give an eye to the case. Make it clear to him that I am doing this entirely for the enlargement of my own knowledge.”

===Chinese===
“我想从大英博物馆着手。那里的人也许能对失踪者的行踪提供些线索。我知道赫利奥波利斯（Heliopolis）正在进行的几项重要发掘——事实上，埃及部主任此刻就在那边；临时代理他职位的诺伯里博士，是贝林厄姆的一位老友。我要去拜访他，设法查明有没有什么事情，可能促使贝林厄姆突然出国——比如去赫利奥波利斯。另外，他也许能告诉我：是什么使那位失踪者在最后那次颇为神秘的旅程中去了一趟巴黎。那也许会成一条重要的线索。与此同时，伯克利，你必须婉转行事，设法让你的朋友想通，让咱们在这案子上搭一只眼。要向他讲明白：我这样做，完全是为了增进我自己的学识。”

===Original===
“But won’t you have to be instructed by a solicitor?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“可是，你不就得先经一位事务律师正式委托么？”我问。

===Original===
“Yes, nominally; but only as a matter of etiquette. We shall do all the actual work. Why do you ask?”

===Chinese===
“是的，名义上如此；但那不过是照规矩行事。实际的工作全由咱们自己做。你问这个做什么？”

===Original===
“I was thinking of the solicitor’s costs, and I was going to mention that I have a little money of my own﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我是想到事务律师的费用。我正想说，我自己有一点积蓄——”

===Original===
“Then you keep it, my dear fellow. You’ll want it when you go into practice. There will be no difficulty about the solicitor; I shall ask one of my friends to act nominally as a personal favor to me﻿—Marchmont would take the case for us, Jervis, I am sure.”

===Chinese===
“那你就留着吧，亲爱的伙计。你自己开业的时候用得着。事务律师这边毫无难处；我会请我的一位朋友挂个名义，算是对我个人的情分——杰维斯，马奇蒙特准肯替咱们接这个案子，我敢担保。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Jervis. “Or old Brodribb, if we put it to him *amicus curia*.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”杰维斯说。“要不就找老布罗德里布，只要咱们拿 *amicus curia*（“法院之友”）的名义同他开个口。”

===Original===
“It is excessively kind of both of you to take this benevolent interest in the case of my friends,” I said; “and it is to be hoped that they won’t be foolishly proud and stiff-necked about it. It’s rather the way with poor gentlefolk.”

===Chinese===
“你们两位对我朋友们的案子这样古道热肠，实在客气极了，”我说；“但愿他们不至于愚蠢地逞傲、死硬地扭捏。家道式微的体面人家，倒是容易犯这个脾气。”

===Original===
“I’ll tell you what!” exclaimed Jervis. “I have a most brilliant idea. You shall give us a little supper at your rooms and invite the Bellinghams to meet us. Then you and I will attack the old gentleman, and Thorndyke shall exercise his persuasive powers on the lady. These chronic incurable old bachelors, you know, are quite irresistible.”

===Chinese===
“我有了个主意！”杰维斯嚷道。“一个妙极了的主意。你在你的寓所里请咱们吃一顿小小的晚饭，把贝林厄姆父女请来同咱们相见。然后你我二人进攻那位老先生，桑代克就对那位小姐施展他的说服功夫。要知道，这种患了慢性不治之症的老光棍，向来是叫人无法抗拒的。”

===Original===
“You observe that my respected junior condemns me to lifelong celibacy,” Thorndyke remarked. “But,” he added, “his suggestion is quite a good one. Of course, we mustn’t put any sort of pressure on Bellingham to employ us﻿—for that is what it amounts to, even if we accept no payment﻿—but a friendly talk over the supper-table would enable us to put the matter delicately and yet convincingly.”

===Chinese===
“你们瞧，我这位受敬重的后生晚辈给我判了个终身独身，”桑代克说。“不过，”他补充道，“他的建议倒是挺好。当然，咱们决不可对贝林厄姆施加任何压力、让他雇用咱们——因为即便分文不取，骨子里也就是那么回事——不过，饭桌上一次友好的交谈，可以让咱们把这件事说得婉转，然而又有说服力。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said I, “I see that, and I like the idea immensely. But it won’t be possible for several days, because I’ve got a job that takes up all my spare time﻿—and that I ought to be at work on now,” I added, with a sudden qualm at the way in which I had forgotten the passage of time in the interest of Thorndyke’s analysis.

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我说，“这我明白，而且我极其喜欢这个主意。可是这几天都办不成，因为我手头有一件活计，占去了我全部的闲暇——而且这会儿我就该去赶它了，”我补充道，心里猛然一阵愧怍，怪自己竟在桑代克分析的兴味里，忘记了光阴的流逝。

===Original===
My two friends looked at me inquiringly, and I felt it necessary to explain about the injured hand and the Tell-el-Amarna tablets; which I accordingly did rather shyly and with a nervous eye upon Jervis. The slow grin, however, for which I was watching, never came; on the contrary, he not only heard me through quite gravely, but when I had finished said with some warmth, and using my old hospital pet name:

===Chinese===
我那两位朋友询问似的望着我，我觉得有必要把受伤的手和泰尔-埃尔-阿马尔纳泥板的事解释一番；于是我照办了，只是颇为腼腆，一边还提心吊胆地拿眼瞟着杰维斯。然而，我提防着要等的那副慢慢咧开的嬉笑，始终没有出现；恰恰相反，他不但自始至终神情郑重地听我讲完，等我讲完，还带着几分热诚、用我医院里旧日的昵称说道：

===Original===
“I’ll say one thing for you, Polly; you’re a good chum, and you always were. I hope your Nevill’s Court friends appreciate the fact.”

===Chinese===
“我给你说一句公道话，波莉（Polly）：你是个好伙伴，从来都是。但愿你的内维尔巷的朋友们识得这份情义。”

===Original===
“They are far more appreciative than the occasion warrants,” I answered. “But to return to this question: how will this day week suit you?”

===Chinese===
“他们领的情，远超过这份情义所值的分量，”我答道。“不过，回到方才的问题：下星期的今天，你们合适么？”

===Original===
“It will suit me,” Thorndyke answered, with a glance at his junior.

===Chinese===
“我合适，”桑代克答道，朝他的后生晚辈望了一眼。

===Original===
“And me too,” said the latter; “so, if it will do for the Bellinghams, we will consider it settled; but if they can’t come, you must fix another night.”

===Chinese===
“我也合适，”后者说；“那么，只要贝林厄姆父女那边行得通，这事就算定下了；若是他们来不了，你就得另定一个晚上。”

===Original===
“Very well,” I said, rising and knocking out my pipe. “I will issue the invitation tomorrow. And now I must be off to have another slog at those notes.”

===Chinese===
“很好，”我说着站起身，磕空了烟斗。“明天我就发出邀请。现在我可得走了，还要再去苦赶那些笔记。”

===Original===
As I walked homeward I speculated cheerfully on the prospect of entertaining my friends under my own (or rather Barnard’s) roof, if they could be lured out of their eremitical retirement. The idea had, in fact, occurred to me already, but I had been deterred by the peculiarities of Barnard’s housekeeper. For Mrs. Gummer was one of those housewives who make up for an archaic simplicity of production by preparations on the most portentous and alarming scale. But this time I would not be deterred. If only the guests could be enticed into my humble lair it would be easy to furnish the raw materials of the feast from outside; and the consideration of ways and means occupied me pleasantly until I found myself once more at my writing-table, confronted by my voluminous notes on the incidents of the North Syrian War.

===Chinese===
一路步行回家，我兴致勃勃地盘算着款待朋友的前景：倘若能把他们从隐士式的退隐里诱出来，在我自己的——说得更确切些，巴纳德的——屋檐下招待他们。这主意其实我早已动过，只是被巴纳德那位管家婆的古怪脾气吓退了。因为古默太太（Mrs. Gummer）属于那一类主妇：出品的做法古拙简朴，预备的阵仗却铺张骇人，以后者补前者之不足。可这一回，我不打算再退缩了。只要能把客人诱进我这寒陋的巢穴，宴席的生材料尽可以从外面置办来；这一路的盘算办法与手段，使我心境舒畅，直到不知不觉又坐回自己的写字台前，面对着那几大册关于北叙利亚战争（North Syrian War）战事的浩繁笔记。

## VIII A Museum Idyll / 八 博物馆牧歌
===Original===
Whether it was that practise revived a forgotten skill on my part, or that Miss Bellingham had overestimated the amount of work to be done, I am unable to say. But whichever may have been the explanation, the fact is that the fourth afternoon saw our task so nearly completed that I was fain to plead that a small remainder might be left over to form an excuse for yet one more visit to the reading-room.

===Chinese===
我说不清，究竟是反复练习使我久已生疏的手艺重新纯熟起来，还是贝林厄姆小姐把工作量估得过大。但无论原因何在，事实是：到了第四天下午，我们的任务已接近完成，以致我心甘情愿地恳求：不如留下一小部分，好作再往阅览室跑一趟的借口。

===Original===
Short, however, as had been the period of our collaboration, it had been long enough to produce a great change in our relations to one another. For there is no friendship so intimate and satisfying as that engendered by community of work, and none﻿—between man and woman, at any rate﻿—so frank and wholesome.

===Chinese===
然而，我们协作的日子虽短，却已长得足以使我们彼此的关系大为改观。因为再没有一种友谊像共同工作所孕育的那样亲密而惬怀；也再没有一种——至少在男女之间——像它这样坦率而健全。

===Original===
Every day had arrived to find a pile of books with the places duly marked and the blue-covered quarto notebooks in readiness. Every day we had worked steadily at the allotted task, had then handed in the books and gone forth together to enjoy a most companionable tea in the milkshop; thereafter to walk home by way of Queen Square, talking over the day’s work and discussing the state of the world in the far-off days when Ahkhenaten was king and the Tell-el-Amarna tablets were a-writing.

===Chinese===
每天我到达时，总有一摞书页标记妥当的书和蓝皮四开本笔记本在那里伺候。每天我们按部就班地做分内的活计，然后交还书籍，一同出门，到牛奶店享受一壶极惬意的茶；随后取道皇后广场步行回家，一路谈论当天的工作，谈论阿肯那顿（Ahkhenaten）为王、泰尔-埃尔-阿马尔纳泥板方在书写之时，那遥远年代的世界情状。

===Original===
It had been a pleasant time, so pleasant, that as I handed in the books for the last time, I sighed to think that it was over; that not only was the task finished, but that the recovery of my fair patient’s hand, from which I had that morning removed the splint, had put an end to the need of my help.

===Chinese===
那是一段快乐的时光，快乐得使我在最后一次交还书籍时不禁叹惋：这一切就要完了；不单任务已经完成，而且我那位美丽的病人手伤痊愈——夹板我就在那天早晨替她拆了——使我无从再效绵薄。

===Original===
“What shall we do?” I asked, as we came out into the central hall. “It is too early for tea. Shall we go and look at some of the galleries?”

===Chinese===
“我们现在做点什么好呢？”我们走到中央大厅时，我问。“吃茶还太早。要不要去看看几个陈列馆？”

===Original===
“Why not?” she answered. “We might look over some of the things connected with what we have been doing. For instance, there is a relief of Ahkhenaten upstairs in the Third Egyptian Room; we might go and look at it.”

===Chinese===
“好啊。”她答道。“我们不妨看看几件与这些天手头活计相关的东西。比如楼上第三埃及展厅里有一块阿肯那顿的浮雕，我们可以去看看。”

===Original===
I fell in eagerly with the suggestion, placing myself under her experienced guidance, and we started by way of the Roman Gallery, past the long row of extremely commonplace and modern-looking Roman Emperors.

===Chinese===
我欣然赞成这个提议，把自己交到她老练的引导之下，于是取道罗马展厅出发，走过长长一列极其平庸、模样十分“现代”的罗马皇帝。

===Original===
“I don’t know,” she said, pausing for a moment opposite a bust labelled “Trajan” (but obviously a portrait of Phil May), “how I am ever even to thank you for all that you have done, to say nothing of repayment.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道，”她说，在一座标着“图拉真”（Trajan）的胸像前略停了停（但那显然是菲尔·梅（Phil May）的肖像），“你为我做的这一切，我连怎样道谢都说不上来，更谈不上报答。”

===Original===
“There is no need to do either,” I replied. “I have enjoyed working with you so I have had my reward. But still,” I added, “if you want to do me a great kindness, you have it in your power.”

===Chinese===
“道谢和报答都用不着。”我答道。“同你一起工作我很快活，报偿我已经拿过了。不过，”我补充道，“你要是真想帮个大忙，倒也力所能及。”

===Original===
“How?”

===Chinese===
“帮什么忙？”

===Original===
“In connection with my friend, Doctor Thorndyke. I told you he was an enthusiast. Now he is, for some reason, most keenly interested in everything relating to your uncle, and I happen to know that, if any legal proceedings should take place, he would very much like to keep a friendly eye on the case.”

===Chinese===
“这与我朋友桑代克博士有关。我说过，他是个热心人。如今也不知怎的，凡是与你伯父有关的事，他都怀着最强烈的兴趣；而我恰好知道，倘若日后果真发生什么法律程序，他很愿意以友善之身留意这桩案子。”

===Original===
“And what do you want me to do?”

===Chinese===
“那你要我做什么呢？”

===Original===
“I want you, if an opportunity should occur for him to give your father advice or help of any kind, to use your influence with your father in favor of, rather than in opposition to, his accepting it﻿—always assuming that you have no real feeling against his doing so.”

===Chinese===
“我要你答应：倘若有朝一日机会来临，他可以向令尊提供何种建议或帮助，你便运用你对令尊的影响，使他乐于接受而非拒之门外——当然，前提是你自己并不真心反对。”

===Original===
Miss Bellingham looked at me thoughtfully for a few moments, and then laughed softly.

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆小姐若有所思地望了我片刻，随即轻轻地笑了。

===Original===
“So the great kindness that I am to do you is to let you do me a further kindness through your friend?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，我要帮你的大忙，就是容你通过你朋友，再帮我一个忙？”

===Original===
“No,” I protested; “that is where you are mistaken. It isn’t benevolence on Doctor Thorndyke’s part; it’s professional enthusiasm.”

===Chinese===
“不，”我分辩道，“这你可就想岔了。这算不得桑代克博士的慈善心肠；这是职业热情。”

===Original===
She smiled sceptically.

===Chinese===
她将信将疑地一笑。

===Original===
“You don’t believe in it,” I said; “but consider other cases. Why does a surgeon get out of bed on a winter’s night to do an emergency operation at a hospital? He doesn’t get paid for it. Do you think it is altruism?”

===Chinese===
“你是信不过这个，”我说，“可是想想别的例子吧。外科医生为什么要在冬夜从床上爬起来，赶到医院去做一台急诊手术？他分文不取。你以为那是利他主义？”

===Original===
“Yes, of course. Isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，当然是。难道不是吗？”

===Original===
“Certainly not. He does it because it is his job, because it is his business to fight with disease﻿—and win.”

===Chinese===
“当然不是。他那么做，是因为那是他的本行，因为与疾病搏斗——并且战而胜之——正是他的职分。”

===Original===
“I don’t see much difference,” she said. “It’s work done for love instead of for payment. However, I will do as you ask if the opportunity arises; but I shan’t suppose that I am repaying your kindness to me.”

===Chinese===
“我看不出有多大分别，”她说，“无非是凭爱好而非报酬去做的工作罢了。也罢，倘若机会来了，我就照你说的做；只是我不会以为，这样便算报答了你对我的好意。”

===Original===
“I don’t mind so long as you do it,” I said, and we walked on for some time in silence.

===Chinese===
“只要你肯做，随你怎么想我都不在乎。”我说。于是我们默默走了一程。

===Original===
“Isn’t it odd,” she said presently, “how our talk always seems to come back to my uncle? Oh, and that reminds me that the things he gave to the Museum are in the same room as the Ahkhenaten relief. Would you like to see them?”

===Chinese===
“说来也怪，”过了片刻她说，“我们的话头怎么总绕回我伯父身上？哦，这倒提醒我了，他赠给博物馆的那些东西，就跟阿肯那顿浮雕同在一室。你想看看吗？”

===Original===
“Of course I should.”

===Chinese===
“当然想。”

===Original===
“Then we will go and look at them first.” She paused, and then, rather shyly and with a rising color, she continued: “And I think I should like to introduce you to a very dear friend of mine﻿—with your permission, of course.”

===Chinese===
“那就先去看它们。”她顿了顿，随即带着几分羞怯、红晕渐生地接着说：“还有，我想介绍你认识我一位极亲爱的朋友——当然，得你允许。”

===Original===
This last addition she made hastily, seeing, I suppose, that I looked rather glum at the suggestion. Inwardly I consigned her friend to the devil, especially if of the masculine gender; outwardly I expressed my felicity at making the acquaintance of any person whom she should honor with her friendship. Whereat, to my discomfiture, she laughed enigmatically; a very soft laugh, low-pitched and musical, like the cooing of a glorified pigeon.

===Chinese===
这最后一句是匆忙添上的，我猜是因为她见我闻言面有愠色。我在心里把那位朋友咒到了魔鬼那里，倘若他是个男的，尤其如此；表面上，我却表示：凡蒙她垂青引为朋友之人，我一概乐予结识。闻言她莫测高深地笑了，使我颇为狼狈；那是一种十分温柔的笑，低沉而悦耳，像一只超凡入圣的鸽子在咕咕作声。

===Original===
I strolled on by her side, speculating a little anxiously on the coming introduction. Was I being conducted to the lair of one of the savants attached to the establishment? and would he add a superfluous third to our little party of two, so complete and companionable, *solus cum sola*, in this populated wilderness? Above all, would he turn out to be a young man, and bring my aerial castles tumbling about my ears? The shy look and the blush with which she had suggested the introduction were ominous indications, upon which I mused gloomily as we ascended the stairs and passed through the wide doorway. I glanced apprehensively at my companion, and met a quiet, inscrutable smile; and at that moment she halted opposite a wall-case and faced me.

===Chinese===
我在她身旁踱着，对这番即将到来的引见颇有些忐忑地揣想。莫非我正被领去本馆某位学者的巢穴？他会不会给我们这支圆满而融洽的两人小队——在这片人烟稠密的荒野里*solus cum sola*（男子与女子单独相处）的小队——添上一个多余的第三者？尤其是，万一他是个年轻人，把我半空中的楼阁撞塌在我头顶呢？她提议引见时那羞怯的神情和脸上的红晕，都是不祥之兆；我们拾级而上、穿过宽阔门洞时，我就为这些念头郁郁出神。我惴惴地瞟了同伴一眼，迎面碰上一个沉静而莫测的微笑；便在那一刻，她在一座壁柜前停了步，向我转过身来。

===Original===
“This is my friend,” she said. “Let me present you to Artemidorus, late of the Fayyum. Oh, don’t smile!” she pleaded. “I am quite serious. Have you never heard of pious Catholics who cherish a devotion to some long-departed saint? That is my feeling toward Artemidorus, and if you only knew what comfort he has shed into the heart of a lonely woman; what a quiet, unobtrusive friend he has been to me in my solitary, friendless days, always ready with a kindly greeting on his gentle, thoughtful face, you would like him for that alone. And I want you to like him and to share our silent friendship. Am I very silly, very sentimental?”

===Chinese===
“这就是我的朋友，”她说。“请容我把你介绍给阿耳忒弥多鲁斯（Artemidorus），法尤姆（the Fayyum）故人。哦，你别笑！”她央求道。“我是很认真的。你难道没听说过，那些虔诚的天主教徒对某位久已谢世的圣者怀着一种特殊的敬爱？我对阿耳忒弥多鲁斯的感情正是如此。你若知道他曾经往一个孤单女子的心里倾注过多少慰藉；在我形单影只、举目无亲的日子里，他又是怎样一位安静而不惊人的朋友，总在他那温文恳挚的脸上备着一声亲切的问候——单为这个，你也该喜欢他。而我要你喜欢他，与我们共享这份无言的友谊。我是不是很傻、很多愁善感？”

===Original===
A wave of relief swept over me, and the mercury of my emotional thermometer, which had shrunk almost into the bulb, leaped up to summer heat. How charming it was of her and how sweetly intimate, to wish to share this mystical friendship with me! And what a pretty conceit it was, too, and how like this strange, inscrutable maiden, to come here and hold silent converse with this long-departed Greek. And the pathos of it all touched me deeply amidst the joy of this newborn intimacy.

===Chinese===
一阵如释重负之感涌遍全身，我那情感温度计里的水银本来几乎缩回了球泡，此刻一跃而升至盛夏的热度。她多么动人，又何等亲昵甜蜜，竟愿与我分享这份神秘的友谊！这又是何等精致的奇想，何其酷肖这位奇特而莫测的少女——跑到这里来，与一位久已作古的希腊人默默交谈。而这一切的哀婉动人之处，在这份新萌的亲密的欢愉之中，深深打动了我。

===Original===
“Are you scornful?” she asked, with a shade of disappointment, as I made no reply.

===Chinese===
见我不作声，她带着一丝失望问：“你是不是心里瞧不起？”

===Original===
“No, indeed I am not,” I answered earnestly. “I want to make you aware of my sympathy and my appreciation without offending you by seeming to exaggerate, and I don’t know how to express it.”

===Chinese===
“不，真的没有。”我恳切地答道。“我想让你察觉我的同情与领会，却又不愿显得夸张而冒犯你，我实在不知如何表达才好。”

===Original===
“Oh, never mind about the expression, so long as you feel it. I thought you would understand,” and she gave me a smile that made me tingle to my fingertips.

===Chinese===
“哦，措辞不打紧，只要你心里有那个意思。我就知道你会懂。”她冲我嫣然一笑，笑得我麻到了指尖。

===Original===
We stood awhile gazing in silence at the mummy﻿—for such, indeed, was her friend Artemidorus. But not an ordinary mummy. Egyptian in form, it was entirely Greek in feeling; and brightly colored as it was, in accordance with the racial love of color, the tasteful refinement with which the decoration of the case was treated made those around look garish and barbaric. But the most striking feature was a charming panel picture which occupied the place of the usual mask. This painting was a revelation to me. Except that it was executed in tempera instead of oil, it differed in no respect from modern work. There was nothing archaic or ancient about it. With its freedom of handling and its correct rendering of light and shade, it might have been painted yesterday; indeed, enclosed in an ordinary gilt frame, it might have passed without remark in an exhibition of modern portraits.

===Chinese===
我们默默伫立了片刻，凝望那具木乃伊——因为她的朋友阿耳忒弥多鲁斯，正是此物。但这可不是一具寻常的木乃伊。形制是埃及的，精神却全然是希腊的；它固然色彩浓丽，正合那个民族爱彩的天性，可棺匣装饰的那种雅致精纯，使四周那些展品相形之下都显得俗艳而野蛮。而最惹眼的是一幅迷人的嵌板画像，正占了通常覆面具的位置。这幅画于我是一次开眼。除了以丹培拉（tempera）而非油彩绘成之外，它与近代作品毫无二致。它毫无古奥陈旧之气。笔致的洒脱、明暗的准确，直似昨日方才画就；说实在的，配上一个寻常的镀金画框，它混进近代肖像画展里也不会引人一声议论。

===Original===
Miss Bellingham observed my admiration and smiled approvingly.

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆小姐看出我的倾慕，赞许地笑了。

===Original===
“It is a charming little portrait, isn’t it?” she said; “and such a sweet face too; so thoughtful and human, with just a shade of melancholy. But the whole thing is full of charm. I fell in love with it the first time I saw it. And it is so Greek!”

===Chinese===
“一幅多迷人的小肖像，是不是？”她说，“而且那张脸多甜美；那么沉静、那么富于人情，只带一丝淡淡的哀愁。而整件东西处处见韵致。我头一回见着就爱上它了。而且它是那样地希腊！”

===Original===
“Yes, it is, in spite of the Egyptian gods and symbols.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，尽管有那些埃及神祇和符记，它仍是希腊的。”

===Original===
“Rather because of them, I think,” said she. “There we have the typical Greek attitude, the genial, cultivated eclecticism that appreciated the fitness of even the most alien forms of art. There is Anubis standing beside the bier; there are Isis and Nephthys, and there below Horus and Tahuti. But we can’t suppose Artemidorus worshiped or believed in those gods. They are there because they are splendid decoration and perfectly appropriate in character. The real feeling of those who loved the dead man breaks out in the inscription.” She pointed to a band below the pectoral, where, in gilt capital letters, was written the two words, “*Αρτεμιδορε ευψυχι.*”

===Chinese===
“我想，倒是因为有它们才如此，”她说。“这里我们看到典型的希腊态度，那种和蔼的、有教养的折衷主义，连最异邦的艺术形式，它也赏识其相宜之处。那边是守在灵床旁的阿努比斯（Anubis）；那边是伊西斯（Isis）和奈芙蒂斯（Nephthys），下方还有荷鲁斯与塔胡提（Tahuti）。但我们不能设想阿耳忒弥多鲁斯当真崇拜或信奉那些神祇。它们出现在那里，是因为它们是华丽的装饰，品性上也恰恰相称。爱着死者的人们的真情，是在铭文里迸发出来的。”她指着胸饰下方的一条饰带，那里以镀金大写字母写着两个词：“*Αρτεμιδορε ευψυχι.*”

===Original===
“Yes,” I said, “it is very dignified and very human.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我说，“非常庄重，也非常富于人性。”

===Original===
