“And so sincere and full of real emotion,” she added. “I find it unspeakably touching. ‘O Artemidorus, farewell!’ There is the real note of human grief, the sorrow of eternal parting. How much finer it is than the vulgar boastfulness of the Semitic epitaphs, or our own miserable, insincere make-believe of the ‘Not lost but gone before’ type. He was gone from them forever; they would look on his face and hear his voice no more; they realized that this was their last farewell. Oh, there is a world of love and sorrow in those two simple words!”

===Chinese===
“又是那样真挚，充满真实的感情，”她补充道。“我觉得它动人得无以言表。‘啊，阿耳忒弥多鲁斯，永别了！’这才是人类悲恸的真正调子，是永诀的哀伤。比起闪米特碑铭的庸俗自夸，比起我们自己那套‘未尝逝去，先行一步’（Not lost but gone before）式的可怜而虚伪的自欺，它何止高明多少倍！他从他们身边永远去了；他们再不能看他的脸、听他的声音；他们明白，这是最后的诀别。啊，那简单的两个词里，有着多少爱、多少悲伤！”

===Original===
For some time neither of us spoke. The glamour of this touching memorial of a long-buried grief had stolen over me, and I was content to stand silent by my beloved companion and revive, with a certain pensive pleasure, the ghosts of human emotions over which so many centuries had rolled. Presently she turned to me with a frank smile. “You have been weighed in the balance of friendship,” she said, “and not found wanting. You have the gift of sympathy, even with a woman’s sentimental fancies.”

===Chinese===
有一阵子我们谁也没有说话。这座久埋悲恸的动人纪念物，其魅力悄悄笼上了我，我甘愿默默立在我心爱的同伴身旁，带着几分怅惘的愉悦，重新唤醒那些被千百个世纪碾过的人类情感的幽魂。过了片刻，她带着坦率的微笑向我转过身来。“你已经在友谊的天平上称过了，”她说，“并无亏缺。你有同情的禀赋，连一个女子的多愁善感也能体谅。”

===Original===
I suspected that a good many men would have developed this precious quality under the circumstances, but I refrained from saying so. There is no use in crying down one’s own wares. I was glad enough to have earned her good opinion so easily, and when she at length turned away from the case and passed through into the adjoining room, it was a very complacent young man who bore her company.

===Chinese===
我暗想，换了这个情境，多半男人都会养成这种可贵品质的，但我忍住没说。贬低自家的货物毫无益处。能这样轻易博得她的好评，我已喜出望外；待她终于从柜前转过身、走进隔壁展室时，陪在她身边的，是一个心满意足得可以的年轻人。

===Original===
“Here is Ahkhenaten﻿—or Khu-en-aten, as the authorities here render the hieroglyphics. She indicated a fragment of a colored relief labeled: ‘Portion of a painted stone tablet with a portrait figure of Amenhotep IV,’ and we stopped to look at the frail, effeminate figure of the great king, with his large cranium, his queer, pointed chin, and the Aten rays stretching out their weird hands as if caressing him.

===Chinese===
“这里就是阿肯那顿——或者按本馆诸公对圣书体的译读，叫库恩阿顿（Khu-en-aten）。她指着一帧彩绘浮雕的残片，标牌写着：‘彩绘石碑残段，饰阿蒙霍特普四世（Amenhotep IV）立像，’我们便停下来，端详这位大王的身形：纤弱而脂粉气，颅骨硕大，下巴尖得出奇，阿顿（Aten）的光芒伸出一双双向怪异的手，仿佛正爱抚着他。

===Original===
“We mustn’t stay here if you want to see my uncle’s gift, because this room closes at four today.” With this admonition she moved on to the other end of the room, where she halted before a large floor-case containing a mummy and a large number of other objects. A black label with white lettering set forth the various contents with a brief explanation as follows:

===Chinese===
“你要是想看我伯父的赠品，咱们就不能在这儿耽搁，因为这间展室今天四点闭馆。”她提出这一警告，便走向展室另一头，在一只大型落地展柜前停下；柜里是一具木乃伊和为数众多的其他物件。一块白字黑牌简要列明柜中各项内容如下：

===Original===
> Mummy of Sebek-hotep, a scribe of the twenty-second dynasty, together with the objects found in the tomb. These include the four Canopic jars, in which the internal organs were deposited, the Ushabti figures, tomb provisions and various articles that had belonged to the deceased; his favorite chair, his headrest, his ink-palette, inscribed with his name and the name of the king, Osorkon I, in whose reign he lived, and other smaller articles. Presented by John Bellingham, Esq.

===Chinese===
> 塞贝克霍特普（Sebek-hotep）木乃伊，第二十二王朝书吏，连同墓中所出各物。包括盛殓内脏的四只卡诺皮克罐、巫沙布提俑、墓中饮食及死者生前用过的诸般物件：他心爱的座椅、头枕、调墨板——板上刻有他的名字，以及他在位时的法老奥索尔孔一世（Osorkon I）的王名——及其他零星小件。由约翰·贝林厄姆先生（John Bellingham, Esq.）捐赠。

===Original===
“They have put all the objects together in one case,” Miss Bellingham explained, “to show the contents of an ordinary tomb of the better class. You see that the dead man was provided with all his ordinary comforts; provisions, furniture, the ink-palette that he had been accustomed to use in writing on papyri, and a staff of servants to wait on him.”

===Chinese===
“他们把所有物件都归进一只柜里，”贝林厄姆小姐解释道，“好展示一座上等阶层普通墓葬的内部陈设。你瞧，死者的日用舒适一应俱全：饮食、家具、他惯常在纸草上书写所用的调墨板，还有一班仆役侍候他。”

===Original===
“Where are the servants?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“仆役在哪里？”我问。

===Original===
“The little Ushabti figures,” she answered; “they were the attendants of the dead, you know, his servants in the underworld. It was a quaint idea, wasn’t it? But it was all very complete and consistent, and quite reasonable, too, if one once accepts the belief in the persistence of the individual apart from the body.”

===Chinese===
“就是那些小小的巫沙布提俑，”她答道；“你可知道，它们是死者的随侍，是他在冥间的仆役。这念头古雅得很，是不是？但整套东西非常完备、非常自洽，而且，一旦接受了灵魂离体独存的信仰，它也相当合理。”

===Original===
“Yes,” I agreed, “and that is the only fair way to judge a religious system, by taking the main beliefs for granted. But what a business it must have been, bringing all these things from Egypt to London.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我附和道，“而且这才是评判一种宗教体系的唯一公道办法：把它的主要信奉先当作前提接受下来。不过，把这一切从埃及运到伦敦，想必是好大一桩工程。”

===Original===
“It is worth the trouble, though, for it is a fine and instructive collection. And the work is all very good of its kind. You notice that the Ushabti figures and the heads that form the stoppers of the Canopic jars are quite finely modeled. The mummy itself, too, is rather handsome, though that coat of bitumen on the back doesn’t improve it. But Sebek-hotep must have been a fine-looking man.”

===Chinese===
“不过这番辛苦是值得的，因为这是一批精美而有教益的藏品。而且件件做工都是同类中的上乘。你注意到没有，那些巫沙布提俑，还有充作卡诺皮克罐塞头的几个人头，塑造得相当精细。木乃伊本身也挺俊美，虽然背面那层沥青有损观瞻。但塞贝克霍特普生前想必是位美男子。”

===Original===
“The mask on the face is a portrait, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
“脸上的面具是件肖像吧，我猜？”

===Original===
“Yes; in fact, it’s rather more. To some extent it is the actual face of the man himself. This mummy is enclosed in what is called a cartonnage, that is a case molded on the figure. The cartonnage was formed of a number of layers of linen or papyrus united by glue or cement, and when the case had been fitted to a mummy it was molded to the body, so that the general form of the features and limbs was often apparent. After the cement was dry the case was covered with a thin layer of stucco and the face modeled more completely, and then decorations and inscriptions were painted on. So that, you see, in a cartonnage, the body was sealed up like a nut in its shell, unlike the more ancient forms in which the mummy was merely rolled up and enclosed in a wooden coffin.”

===Chinese===
“是的；说实在的，还不止是肖像。在某种程度上，它就是他本人的真面目。这具木乃伊封在所谓棺匣（cartonnage）里，也就是一具依着人形模制的棺匣。棺匣由数层亚麻布或纸草以胶浆黏合而成，待套到木乃伊身上，便就着躯体捏塑，所以五官四肢的大致轮廓往往隐约可辨。胶浆干后，棺匣外面敷上一层薄薄的灰泥，把面部塑造得更加完备，再彩绘上纹饰和铭文。于是你瞧见，在棺匣里，遗体就像果仁封在壳里一般封得严严实实，不像更古老的形制，木乃伊只须裹起来、装进一口木棺了事。”

===Original===
At this moment there smote upon our ears a politely protesting voice announcing in singsong tones that it was closing time; and simultaneously a desire for tea suggested the hospitable milkshop. With leisurely dignity that ignored the official who shepherded us along the galleries, we made our way to the entrance, still immersed in conversation on matters sepulchral.

===Chinese===
就在这时，一嗓子客客气气的通牒，用抑扬的声腔灌进我们耳中，宣告闭馆时间已到；与此同时，对茶的渴望想起了那家好客的牛奶店。我们以一种从容的尊严，全然不顾那位沿路“押解”我们的馆员，径向大门走去，嘴里还沉浸在有关墓葬的谈话里。

===Original===
It was rather earlier than our usual hour for leaving the Museum and, moreover, it was our last day﻿—for the present. Wherefore we lingered over our tea to an extent that caused the milkshop lady to view us with some disfavor, and when at length we started homeward, we took so many shortcuts that six o’clock found us no nearer our destination than Lincoln’s Inn Fields; whither we had journeyed by a slightly indirect route that traversed (among other places) Russell Square, Red Lion Square, with the quaint passage of the same name, Bedford Row, Jockey’s Fields, Hand Court, and Great Turnstile.

===Chinese===
这天离馆比我们平素的钟点略早，何况又是我们的最后一天——暂时说来。于是我们把茶拖了又拖，拖得牛奶店女主人都有些侧目了；待到终于动身回家，我们又专拣近道走，结果到了六点钟，离目的地还只走到林肯律师学院广场（Lincoln’s Inn Fields）；为抵达那里，我们绕了一条稍稍迂回的路线，途中（除别处外）穿过罗素广场（Russell Square）、红狮广场（Red Lion Square）及与之同名的那条古雅小巷、贝德福德巷（Bedford Row）、乔基操场（Jockey’s Fields）、汉德院（Hand Court）和大转栅（Great Turnstile）。

===Original===
It was in the last thoroughfare that our attention was attracted by a flaring poster outside a newsvendor’s bearing the startling inscription:

===Chinese===
正是在最后那条街上，一份报摊外炫目的海报吸引了我们的注意，上面印着耸人听闻的字句：

===Original===
> **More Mementoes of Murdered Man**

===Chinese===
> **遇害者遗物又有新发现**

===Original===
Miss Bellingham glanced at the poster and shuddered.

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆小姐瞥了一眼海报，打了个寒噤。

===Original===
“Horrible, isn’t it?” she said. “Have you read about them?”

===Chinese===
“可怕，是不是？”她说。“报上说的那些，你读了没有？”

===Original===
“I haven’t been noticing the papers the last few days,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“这几天我没大留意报纸。”我答道。

===Original===
“No, of course you haven’t. You’ve been slaving at those wretched notes. We don’t very often see the papers, at least we don’t take them in, but Miss Oman has kept us supplied during the last day or two. She is a perfect little ghoul; she delights in horrors of every kind, and the more horrible the better.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，你当然没留意。你一直在那些要命的笔记里埋头苦干。我们自己也不常见报，至少不订报；不过这一两天，奥曼小姐一直给我们供应报纸。她是十足的一头小食尸鬼；种种惨事她都津津乐道，而且越惨越好。”

===Original===
“But,” I asked, “what is it they have found?”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我问，“他们究竟发现了什么？”

===Original===
“Oh, they are the remains of some poor creature who seems to have been murdered and cut into pieces. It is dreadful. It made me shudder to read of it, for I couldn’t help thinking of poor Uncle John, and, as for my father, he was really quite upset.”

===Chinese===
“哦，是哪个可怜人的遗骸，看样子是遭了谋杀、被剁成了碎块。可怕极了。读着报道我直打哆嗦，因为我不由得想到可怜的伯父约翰；至于我父亲，他真是心烦意乱得很。”

===Original===
“Are these the bones that were found in a watercress-bed at Sidcup?”

===Chinese===
“就是在锡德卡普水田芥田里找到的那些骨头吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, but they have found several more. The police have been most energetic. They seem to have been making a systematic search, and the result has been that they have discovered several portions of the body, scattered about in very widely separated places﻿—Sidcup, Lee, St. Mary Cray; and yesterday it was reported that an arm had been found in one of the ponds called ‘the Cuckoo Pits,’ close to our old home.”

===Chinese===
“是的，可他们又找到了好几处。警察一直非常卖力。他们似乎进行了一场有系统的搜索，结果发现了尸体的好几部分，散落在彼此相距极远的地方——锡德卡普、李（Lee）、圣玛丽克雷（St. Mary Cray）；昨天还据报道，在我们旧居近旁一个叫‘布谷鸟坑’（the Cuckoo Pits）的池塘里，发现了一条手臂。”

===Original===
“What! in Essex?” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“什么！在埃塞克斯（Essex）？”我叫出声来。

===Original===
“Yes, in Epping Forest, quite near Woodford. Isn’t it dreadful to think of it? They were probably hidden when we were living there. I think it was that that horrified my father so much. When he read it he was so upset that he gathered up the whole bundle of newspapers and tossed them out of the window; and they blew over the wall, and poor Miss Oman had to rush and pursue them up the court.”

===Chinese===
“是的，在埃平森林（Epping Forest）里，离伍德福德很近。想想都觉得可怕，是不是？多半还是我们住在那儿的时候藏下去的。我想，我父亲吓就吓在这里。他读到那条新闻时心里一乱，把整捆报纸统统抓起来扔出了窗外；风把报纸刮过了墙头，可怜的奥曼小姐只好冲出去，一路顺着院子追那些报纸。”

===Original===
“Do you think he suspects that these remains may be those of your uncle?”

===Chinese===
“你觉得他疑心，这些遗骸可能就是你伯父的吗？”

===Original===
“I think so, though he has said nothing to that effect and, of course, I have not made any suggestion to him. We always preserve the fiction between ourselves of believing that Uncle John is still alive.”

===Chinese===
“我想是的，虽然他这方面一个字也没提，我当然也没有向他作过任何暗示。我们彼此之间始终维持着一个虚构：相信伯父约翰还活着。”

===Original===
“But you don’t think he is, do you?”

===Chinese===
“可你自己并不这么想，对吧？”

===Original===
“No, I’m afraid I don’t; and I feel pretty sure that my father doesn’t think so either, but he doesn’t like to admit it to me.”

===Chinese===
“不，恐怕我不这么想了；而且我敢肯定，我父亲也早不这么想，只是他不肯对我承认罢了。”

===Original===
“Do you happen to remember what bones have been found?”

===Chinese===
“你还记得发现的是哪些骨头吗？”

===Original===
“No, I don’t. I know that an arm was found in the Cuckoo Pits, and I think a thighbone was dredged up out of a pond near St. Mary Cray. But Miss Oman will be able to tell you all about it, if you are interested. She will be delighted to meet a kindred spirit,” Miss Bellingham added, with a smile.

===Chinese===
“不记得了。我知道布谷鸟坑里发现了一条手臂，还有一根大腿骨，我想是从圣玛丽克雷附近一个池塘里捞上来的。不过你要是感兴趣，奥曼小姐能把一切都告诉你。她会非常高兴结识一位同道中人。”贝林厄姆小姐含笑补充道。

===Original===
“I don’t know that I claim spiritual kinship with a ghoul,” said I; “especially such a very sharp-tempered ghoul.”

===Chinese===
“我倒不晓得，我还认过食尸鬼做精神上的亲眷，”我说；“尤其是这么一位火气忒大的食尸鬼。”

===Original===
“Oh, don’t disparage her, Doctor Berkeley!” Miss Bellingham pleaded. “She isn’t really bad-tempered; only a little prickly on the surface. I oughtn’t to have called her a ghoul; she is just the sweetest, most affectionate, most unselfish little angelic human hedgehog that you could find if you traveled the wide world through. Do you know that she has been working her fingers to the bone making an old dress of mine presentable because she is so anxious that I shall look nice at your little supper party.”

===Chinese===
“哦，可别贬损她，伯克利医生！”贝林厄姆小姐央求道。“她脾气其实不坏；只是表面上有几分扎人。我不该管她叫食尸鬼；她是顶甜蜜、顶有情、顶无私的小小天使般的人形刺猬，你走遍天涯海角也找不出第二个。你知道吗，为了让我在你的小小晚饭会上显得体面些，她一直在拼死拼活地拾掇我一件旧衣裳，好叫它拿得出手。”

===Original===
“You are sure to do that, in any case,” I said; “but I withdraw my remark as to her temper unreservedly. And I really didn’t mean it, you know; I have always liked the little lady.”

===Chinese===
“无论如何，你肯定会体面的。”我说；“不过，我把方才关于她脾气的话无条件收回。而且说真的，你知道，我本不是有意的；我一直很喜欢这位小个子女士。”

===Original===
“That’s right; and now won’t you come in and have a few minutes’ chat with my father? We are quite early in spite of the shortcuts.”

===Chinese===
“这就对了；现在，要不要进来跟我父亲聊上几分钟？别看净抄了近道，我们到底还很早呢。”

===Original===
I accepted readily, and the more so inasmuch as I wanted a few words with Miss Oman on the subject of catering and did not want to discuss it before my friends. Accordingly I went in and gossiped with Mr. Bellingham, chiefly about the work we had done at the Museum, until it was time for me to return to the surgery.

===Chinese===
我欣然应允，尤其是因为我正想就备办晚饭的事跟奥曼小姐说几句，又不愿当着朋友们的面谈。于是我便进屋去，同贝林厄姆先生闲话了一番，多半谈的是我们在博物馆做的工作，直到我该回诊所的时候。

===Original===
Having taken my leave, I walked down the stairs with reflective slowness and as much creaking of my boots as I could manage; with the result, hopefully anticipated, that as I approached the door of Miss Oman’s room it opened and the lady’s head protruded.

===Chinese===
告辞之后，我若有所思地缓步下楼，还尽力让靴子弄出最大的吱嘎声；结果不出所望——我快到奥曼小姐房门口时，门开了，探出女主人的头来。

===Original===
“I’d change my cobbler if I were you,” she said.

===Chinese===
“我要是你，就换个鞋匠。”她说。

===Original===
I thought of the “angelic human hedgehog,” and nearly sniggered in her face.

===Chinese===
我想起那句“天使般的人形刺猬”，险些当面嗤笑出来。

===Original===
“I am sure you would, Miss Oman, instantly; though, mind you, the poor fellow can’t help his looks.”

===Chinese===
“我担保你会换的，奥曼小姐，而且立刻就换；不过话说回来，那可怜人的长相可由不得他自己。”

===Original===
“You are a very flippant young man,” she said severely. Whereat I grinned, and she regarded me silently with a baleful glare. Suddenly I remembered my mission and became serious and sober.

===Chinese===
“你这个年轻人，嘴皮子轻薄得很。”她正色道。我闻言咧嘴一笑，她便默默地瞪着我，目光凶狠。我突然记起自己此行的使命，登时端正严肃起来。

===Original===
“Miss Oman,” I said. “I very much want to take your advice on a matter of some importance﻿—to me, at least.” (That ought to fetch her, I thought. The “advice fly”﻿—strangely neglected by Izaak Walton﻿—is guaranteed to kill in any weather.) And it did fetch her. She rose in a flash and gorged it, cock’s feathers, worsted body and all.

===Chinese===
“奥曼小姐，”我说，“有一件事——至少对我——非同小可，我很想听听你的高见。”（这一钓准能让她上钩，我心想。“求教蝇”这味饵——伊萨克·沃尔顿奇怪地未曾著录——保准任何天气都有杀伤力。）果然钓着了她。她一跃而起，连钩带饵吞了个干净：公鸡毛的翅子、绒线的身躯，一概不剩。

===Original===
“What is it about?” she asked eagerly. “But don’t stand out there where everybody can hear but me. Come in and sit down.”

===Chinese===
“是关于什么事？”她急切地问。“不过别站在外头，那儿人人听得见，偏偏漏了我。进来坐下。”

===Original===
Now I didn’t want to discuss the matter here, and, besides, there was not time. I therefore assumed an air of mystery.

===Chinese===
可我并不想在这里谈这件事，再说也没工夫。我便装出一副神秘兮兮的神气。

===Original===
“I can’t, Miss Oman. I’m due at the surgery now. But if you should be passing and should have a few minutes to spare, I should be greatly obliged if you would look in. I really don’t quite know how to act.”

===Chinese===
“不成，奥曼小姐。我这就要回诊所了。可你要是路过，又有几分钟空，肯进来坐坐，我将感激不尽。我实在有点儿不知如何是好。”

===Original===
“No, I expect not. Men very seldom do. But you’re better than most, for you know when you are in difficulties and have the sense to consult a woman. But what is it about? Perhaps I might be thinking it over.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，我看也是。男人家很少有知道的。不过你比大多数人强，因为你知道自己为难，还有这份脑子，晓得去请教一个女人。可到底是什么事？说不定我可以先替你琢磨琢磨。”

===Original===
“Well, you know,” I began evasively, “it’s a simple matter, but I can’t very well﻿—no, by Jove!” I added, looking at my watch, “I must run, or I shall keep the multitude waiting.” And with this I bustled away, leaving her literally dancing with curiosity.

===Chinese===
“呃，是这样，”我含糊其辞地开口，“事情本身很简单，可我不大好——不，天哪！”我看了看表，补充道，“我得跑了，不然要让大众久等了。”说罢我便匆匆而去，撇下她直被好奇心撩得手舞足蹈。

## IX The Sphynx of Lincoln’s Inn / 九 林肯律师学院的斯芬克斯
===Original===
At the age of twenty-six one cannot claim to have attained to the position of a person of experience. Nevertheless, the knowledge of human nature accumulated in that brief period sufficed to make me feel confident that, at some time during the evening, I should receive a visit from Miss Oman. And circumstances justified my confidence; for the clock yet stood at two minutes to seven when a premonitory tap at the surgery door heralded her arrival.

===Chinese===
二十六岁的年纪，自然还称不上是个有阅历的人。然而，短短这一段岁月里积攒下的人情世故，已足以使我心下笃定：晚间总有一个时候，奥曼小姐要登门来看我。事实也证明我的自信不误；时钟还差两分才到七点，诊室的门上便先响起了通报来客的轻叩。

===Original===
“I happened to be passing,” she explained, and I forbore to smile at the coincidence, “so I thought I might as well drop in and hear what you wanted to ask me about.”

===Chinese===
“我正好路过，”她解释道，我忍住没有为这份巧合失笑，“就想着不妨进来坐坐，听听你要问我什么。”

===Original===
She seated herself in the patients’ chair and laying a bundle of newspapers on the table, glared at me expectantly.

===Chinese===
她在病人坐的那把椅子上落了座，把一卷报纸往桌上一放，瞪起眼睛等我开口。

===Original===
“Thank you, Miss Oman,” said I. “It is very good of you to look in on me. I am ashamed to give you all this trouble about such a trifling matter.”

===Chinese===
“多谢你了，奥曼小姐，”我说，“肯顺路来看我。为这么一桩鸡毛蒜皮的小事劳你的驾，实在叫我过意不去。”

===Original===
She rapped her knuckles impatiently on the table.

===Chinese===
她不耐烦地用指节敲了敲桌子。

===Original===
“Never mind about the trouble,” she exclaimed tartly. “What﻿—is﻿—it﻿—that﻿—you﻿—want﻿—to﻿—*ask*﻿—me about?”

===Chinese===
“麻烦不麻烦的且不去说它，”她尖刻地嚷道，“你——到——底——想——要——问——我——*什么*？”

===Original===
I stated my difficulties in respect of the supper-party, and, as I proceeded, an expression of disgust and disappointment spread over her countenance.

===Chinese===
我把晚宴一节的种种难处细细说了一遍；随着话头展开，她脸上渐渐布满一层又嫌恶又失望的神色。

===Original===
“I don’t see why you need have been so mysterious about it,” she said glumly.

===Chinese===
“我不明白，这么点事，有什么好弄得这样神神秘秘的。”她怏怏地说。

===Original===
“I didn’t mean to be mysterious; I was only anxious not to make a mess of the affair. It’s all very fine to assume a lofty scorn of the pleasures of the table, but there is great virtue in a really good feed, especially when low-living and high-thinking have been the order of the day.”

===Chinese===
“我并非存心神秘，只是一心不想把这事办砸了。把口腹之乐摆出一副高姿态来鄙薄，话说得自然漂亮，可一顿真正的好饭自有天大的好处，尤其是当粗茶淡饭、高情远思已成了一日之常的时候。”

===Original===
“Coarsely put,” said Miss Oman, “but perfectly true.”

===Chinese===
“话说得糙，”奥曼小姐道，“理却不差。”

===Original===
“Very well. Now, if I leave the management to Mrs. Gummer, she will probably provide a tepid Irish stew with flakes of congealed fat on it, and a plastic suet-pudding or something of that kind, and turn the house upside down in getting it ready. So I thought of having a cold spread and getting the things from outside. But I don’t want it to look as if I had been making enormous preparations.”

===Chinese===
“很好。这么说吧：这事若交由古默太太去张罗，她多半会端上一盆温吞吞的爱尔兰炖肉，面上浮着一层层凝住的白油，外加一块死板板的板油布丁之类的吃食，还得把整座宅子闹个天翻地覆才算备齐。所以我想索性摆一桌冷餐，东西全从外面置办。只是我又不愿弄得像是我做了多大准备似的。”

===Original===
“They won’t think the things came down from heaven,” said Miss Oman.

===Chinese===
“人家又不会当这些东西是天上掉下来的。”奥曼小姐说。

===Original===
“No, I suppose they won’t. But you know what I mean. Now, where do you advise me to go for the raw materials of conviviality?”

===Chinese===
“是，想必不会。可你懂我的意思。那么依你看，我这席上宾主尽欢的生材料，该往哪儿去置办？”

===Original===
Miss Oman reflected. “You had better let me do your shopping and manage the whole business,” was her final verdict.

===Chinese===
奥曼小姐沉吟片刻。“你最好把采买交给我，整桩事一概归我料理。”这便是她的终审判决。

===Original===
This was precisely what I wanted, and I accepted thankfully, regardless of the feelings of Mrs. Gummer. I handed her two pounds, and, after some protests at my extravagance, she bestowed them in her purse; a process that occupied time, since that receptacle, besides being a sort of miniature Record Office of frayed and time-stained bills, already bulged with a lading of draper’s samples, ends of tape, a card of linen buttons, another of hooks and eyes, a lump of beeswax, a rat-eaten stump of leadpencil, and other trifles that I have forgotten. As she closed the purse at the imminent risk of wrenching off its fastenings she looked at me severely and pursed her lips.

===Chinese===
这正中我的下怀，我便感激地应承下来，全不顾古默太太心里作何感想。我递给她两镑钱；她先是嗔怪我铺张、推让了几句，随后便把钱收进了钱夹。这道工序颇费了些时光，因为那件容器，除了堪称一座收着磨损发黄的账单的小型档案馆之外，早已塞得满满当当：一叠布商的布样、几段断头衣带、一板亚麻纽扣、又一板领钩领扣、一块蜂蜡、半截叫老鼠啃过的铅笔头，还有旁的些我已记不清的零碎。她冒着扯脱搭扣的危险把钱夹扣拢，末了狠狠瞪了我一眼，抿紧了嘴唇。

===Original===
“You’re a very plausible young man,” she remarked.

===Chinese===
“你这个年轻人，嘴上倒怪能说的。”她说道。

===Original===
“What makes you say that?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“这话从何说起？”我问。

===Original===
“Philandering about museums,” she continued, “with handsome young ladies on the pretense of work. Work, indeed! Oh, I heard her telling her father about it. She thinks you were perfectly enthralled by the mummies and dried cats and chunks of stone and all the other trash. She doesn’t know what humbugs men are.”

===Chinese===
“仗着做学问的名头，”她接着说，“陪着漂亮姑娘逛博物馆。做学问，亏你说得出口！哼，我可听见她讲给她的父亲听了。她还当你真叫那些木乃伊、干猫、石头块子和其余那些劳什子迷得神魂颠倒呢。她就是不知道男人里头有多少伪君子。”

===Original===
“Really, Miss Oman,” I began.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，奥曼小姐，”我刚开始分辩。

===Original===
“Oh, don’t talk to me!” she snapped. “I can see it all. You can’t impose upon me. I can see you staring into those glass cases, egging her on to talk and listening open-mouthed and bulging-eyed and sitting at her feet﻿—now, didn’t you?”

===Chinese===
“得了，少跟我来这套！”她厉声道。“这一套我全看得分明，你蒙不了我。我看得见你盯着那些玻璃柜的模样，一句句引她说话，听得张大了嘴、瞪圆了眼，一路坐到她的脚边去——嗯，是不是这样？”

===Original===
“I don’t know about sitting at her feet,” I said, “though it might easily have come to that with those infernal slippery floors; but I had a very jolly time, and I mean to go again if I can. Miss Bellingham is the cleverest and most accomplished woman I have ever spoken to.”

===Chinese===
“坐到脚边倒说不上，”我说，“虽说照那要命的光滑地板看来，滑到那步田地也大有可能；不过我确实过了一个极快活的下午，只要去得成，我还打算再去。贝林厄姆小姐是我平生交谈过的女士里最聪明、最有学问的一位。”

===Original===
This was a poser for Miss Oman, whose admiration and loyalty, I knew, were only equaled by my own. She would have liked to contradict me, but the thing was impossible. To cover her defeat she snatched up the bundle of newspapers and began to open them out.

===Chinese===
这一军将得奥曼小姐不轻；我知道，她对小姐的敬慕与忠心，唯有我自己的可与之匹敌。她本想驳我，无奈这话实在驳不动。为遮掩败阵，她一把抓起那卷报纸，抖了开来。

===Original===
“What sort of stuff is ‘hibernation’?” she demanded suddenly.

===Chinese===
“‘冬眠’（hibernation）是样什么东西？”她突然发问。

===Original===
“Hibernation!” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“冬眠！”我失声道。

===Original===
“Yes. They found a patch of it on a bone that was discovered in a pond at St. Mary Cray, and a similar patch on one that was found at some other place in Essex. Now, I want to know what ‘hibernation’ is.”

===Chinese===
“是啊。他们在圣玛丽克雷一个池塘里捞出的一根骨头上发现了一块，在埃塞克斯另外一处找到的一根上也有相似的一块。好了，我要知道，这‘冬眠’到底是什么。”

===Original===
“You must mean ‘eburnation,’ ” I said, after a moment’s reflection.

===Chinese===
“你说的怕是‘骨象牙化’（eburnation）吧。”我略一思索后说。

===Original===
“The newspapers say ‘hibernation,’ and I suppose they know what they are talking about. If you don’t know what it is, don’t be ashamed to say so.”

===Chinese===
“报纸上印的是‘冬眠’，想必人家明白自己印的是什么。你要是不知道它是什么，也不妨直说，不必难为情。”

===Original===
“Well, then, I don’t.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，那我就直说，我不知道。”

===Original===
“In that case you had better read the papers and find out,” she said, a little illogically. And then: “Are you fond of murders? I am, awfully.”

===Chinese===
“既是这样，你最好去读读报纸，弄个明白。”她说，逻辑有点欠通。接着又问：“你喜欢凶杀案吗？我可是喜欢得不得了。”

===Original===
“What a shocking little ghoul you must be!” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“你这个小食尸鬼，可真够吓人的！”我失声道。

===Original===
She stuck out her chin at me. “I’ll trouble you,” she said, “to be a little more respectful in your language. Do you realize that I am old enough to be your mother?”

===Chinese===
她朝我扬起下巴。“有劳你，”她说，“说话放客气些。你可晓得，我如今的年纪，都够做你的母亲了？”

===Original===
“Impossible!” I ejaculated.

===Chinese===
“不可能！”我冲口而出。

===Original===
“Fact,” said Miss Oman.

===Chinese===
“千真万确。”奥曼小姐说。

===Original===
“Well, anyhow,” said I, “age is not the only qualification. And besides, you are too late for the billet. The vacancy’s filled.”

===Chinese===
“呃，好吧，”我说，“单凭年纪可算不得唯一的资格。再说，这个缺你来晚了一步——已经有人补上了。”

===Original===
Miss Oman slapped the papers down on the table and rose abruptly.

===Chinese===
奥曼小姐把那卷报纸往桌上一摔，霍然起身。

===Original===
“You had better read the papers and see if you can learn a little sense,” she said severely as she turned to go. “Oh, and don’t forget the finger!” she added eagerly. “That is really thrilling.”

===Chinese===
“你最好把报纸读一读，看看能不能学得明白些。”她转身要走时正色说道。“哦，还有，别忘了那根手指！”她又热切地添上一句。“那才真是够味儿。”

===Original===
“The finger?” I repeated.

===Chinese===
“手指？”我重复道。

===Original===
“Yes. They found a hand with one missing. The police think it is an important clue. I don’t know what they mean; but you read the account and tell me what you think.”

===Chinese===
“是啊。他们找到一只手，短了一根手指。警察当它是条重要的线索。我不懂他们是什么意思；不过你去把那篇报道读了，再告诉我你是怎么个看法。”

===Original===
With this parting injunction she bustled out through the surgery, and I followed to bid her a ceremonious adieu on the doorstep. I watched her little figure tripping with quick, birdlike steps down Fetter Lane, and was about to turn back into the surgery when my attention was attracted by the evolutions of an elderly gentleman on the opposite side of the street. He was a somewhat peculiar-looking man, tall, gaunt, and bony, and the way in which he carried his head suggested to the medical mind a pronounced degree of near sight and a pair of “deep” spectacle glasses. Suddenly he espied me and crossed the road with his chin thrust forward and a pair of keen blue eyes directed at me through the centers of his spectacles.

===Chinese===
丢下这句临别叮嘱，她便风风火火地穿过诊室出去了，我随后跟上，在门阶上与她郑重作别。我望着她那小小的身影迈着轻快如鸟的碎步沿菲特巷远去，正要转身回诊室，街对面一位老先生那番东寻西看的走动引起了我的注意。那是个模样颇为特异的人，身量高，瘦骨嶙峋；他那探着脑袋的姿势，落在懂医的人眼里，便提示出相当深的近视和一副“深”度眼镜。忽然他一眼望见了我，下巴朝前一挺，穿过马路走来，一双锐利的蓝眼睛从镜片正中直射着我。

===Original===
“I wonder if you can and will help me,” said he, with a courteous salute. “I wish to call on an acquaintance, and I have forgotten his address. It is in some court, but the name of that court has escaped me for the moment. My friend’s name is Bellingham. I suppose you don’t chance to know it? Doctors know a great many people, as a rule.”

===Chinese===
“不知您能不能、也肯不肯帮我一个忙，”他说着，恭而有礼地欠身致意。“我想去拜访一位熟人，却把他的地址忘了。只记得是在哪个院落里，可那院子的名字一时想不起来了。我那位朋友姓贝林厄姆。您不会碰巧知道吧？医生这一行，照例是三教九流认得最全的。”

===Original===
“Do you mean Mr. Godfrey Bellingham?”

===Chinese===
“您是说戈弗雷·贝林厄姆先生么？”

===Original===
“Ah! Then you do know him. I have not consulted the oracle in vain. He is a patient of yours, no doubt?”

===Chinese===
“啊！这么说您果然认得他。我这尊神谕总算没有白求。他自然是您的病人了？”

===Original===
“A patient and a personal friend. His address is Forty-nine Nevill’s Court.”

===Chinese===
“是病人，也是私交。他的地址是内维尔巷四十九号。”

===Original===
“Thank you, thank you. Oh, and as you are a friend, perhaps you can inform me as to the customs of the household. I am not expected, and I do not wish to make an untimely visit. What are Mr. Bellingham’s habits as to his evening meal? Would this be a convenient time to call?”

===Chinese===
“谢谢，谢谢。哦，您既是朋友，或许还能给我指点指点那府上的规矩。他们并不知道我要来，我也不想来得不是时候。贝林厄姆先生用晚饭习惯在什么钟点？眼下这时候登门方便么？”

===Original===
“I generally make my evening visits a little later than this﻿—say about half-past eight; they have finished their meal by then.”

===Chinese===
“我晚间出诊总比这稍晚些——大约八点半吧；到那时他们已用罢晚饭了。”

===Original===
“Ah! Half-past eight, then? Then I suppose I had better take a walk until that time. I don’t want to disturb them.”

===Chinese===
“啊！那就是八点半喽？那么我想，我最好还是先散散步，挨到那个钟点。我不想搅了他们。”

===Original===
“Would you care to come in and smoke a cigar until it is time to make your call? If you would, I could walk over with you and show you the house.”

===Chinese===
“您愿不愿进来抽支雪茄，等时候到了再去？您若肯赏光，我可以陪您走一趟，把那宅子指给您看。”

===Original===
“That is very kind of you,” said my new acquaintance, with an inquisitive glance at me through his spectacles. “I think I should like to sit down. It’s a dull affair, mooning about the streets, and there isn’t time to go back to my chambers﻿—in Lincoln’s Inn.”

===Chinese===
“那就叨光了，”我这位新交说，隔着镜片把我探究地打量了一眼。“我想我还是坐一坐的好。在街上没头没脑地闲荡是桩乏味的事，而要回我的事务房去——在林肯律师学院——时间又不够。”

===Original===
“I wonder,” said I, as I ushered him into the room lately vacated by Miss Oman, “if you happen to be Mr. Jellicoe.”

===Chinese===
“恕我冒昧，”我一面把他让进奥曼小姐方才腾出的那间屋子，一面说，“您不会恰好就是杰利科先生吧？”

===Original===
He turned his spectacles full on me with a keen, suspicious glance. “What makes you think I am Mr. Jellicoe?” he asked.

===Chinese===
他把镜片整个转过来对准我，目光锐利而多疑。“您凭什么认为我是杰利科先生？”他问。

===Original===
“Oh, only that you live in Lincoln’s Inn.”

===Chinese===
“哦，不过是因为您住在林肯律师学院。”

===Original===
“Ha! I see. I live in Lincoln’s Inn; Mr. Jellicoe lives in Lincoln’s Inn; therefore I am Mr. Jellicoe. Ha! ha! Bad logic, but a correct conclusion. Yes, I am Mr. Jellicoe. What do you know about me?”

===Chinese===
“哈！我明白了。我住在林肯律师学院；杰利科先生住在林肯律师学院；所以我就是杰利科先生。哈！哈！逻辑不通，结论倒对。不错，我正是杰利科先生。您知道我些什么？”

===Original===
“Mighty little, excepting that you were the late John Bellingham’s man of business.”

===Chinese===
“微乎其微，只知道您是已故的约翰·贝林厄姆的事务代理人。”

===Original===
“The ‘*late* John Bellingham,’ hey! How do you know he is the late John Bellingham?”

===Chinese===
“‘*已故的*约翰·贝林厄姆’，嘿！您怎么知道他就是已故的约翰·贝林厄姆？”

===Original===
“As a matter of fact, I don’t; only I rather understood that that was your own belief.”

===Chinese===
“说实在的，我并不知道；只是我多少听出一点意思，仿佛那是您自己的看法。”

===Original===
“You understood! Now from whom did you ‘understand’ that? From Godfrey Bellingham? H’m! And how did he know what I believe? I never told him. It is a very unsafe thing, my dear sir, to expound another man’s beliefs.”

===Chinese===
“你听出了！那么您这是打谁那里‘听出来’的？打戈弗雷·贝林厄姆那里？哼！他又怎么会知道我相信什么？我可从来没有告诉过他。亲爱的先生，阐发别人的信念，是件很不稳妥的事。”

===Original===
“Then you think that John Bellingham is alive?”

===Chinese===
“那么您认为约翰·贝林厄姆还活着？”

===Original===
“Do I? Who said so? I did not, you know.”

===Chinese===
“我这么认为么？谁说的？您知道，我可没这么说。”

===Original===
“But he must be either dead or alive.”

===Chinese===
“可他不是死了，便是活着，二者必居其一。”

===Original===
“There,” said Mr. Jellicoe, “I am entirely with you. You have stated an undeniable truth.”

===Chinese===
“在这一点上，”杰利科先生说，“我完全与您一致。您说的是一条无可否认的真理。”

===Original===
“It is not a very illuminating one, however,” I replied, laughing.

===Chinese===
“不过这条真理可照不出多少光亮。”我笑着答道。

===Original===
“Undeniable truths often are not,” he retorted. “They are apt to be extremely general. In fact, I would affirm that the certainty of the truth of a given proposition is directly proportional to its generality.”

===Chinese===
“无可否认的真理往往如此，”他反驳道，“它们十有八九空泛得很。事实上我敢断言：一个给定命题为真的确定性，与其空泛的程度成正比。”

===Original===
“I suppose that is so,” said I.

===Chinese===
“想来是这样的。”我说。

===Original===
“Undoubtedly. Take an instance from your own profession. Given a million normal human beings under twenty, and you can say with certainty that a majority of them will die before reaching a certain age, that they will die in certain circumstances and of certain diseases. Then take a single unit from that million, and what can you predict concerning him? Nothing. He may die tomorrow; he may live to be a couple of hundred. He may die of a cold in the head or a cut finger, or from falling off the cross of St. Paul’s. In a particular case you can predict nothing.”

===Chinese===
“毫无疑问。且从贵行举一个例子。给一百万个二十岁以下的正常人，你便能有把握地说：其中大多数人不到某个年龄便会死，会在某种情形下、死于某种疾病。再从这一百万里单挑出一个来，你能对他预言些什么？什么也不能。他也许明天就死，也许活上两百来岁；他也许死于一场伤风、一道割破的指头，或是从圣保罗教堂的十字架上跌下来。在个别的个案里，你什么也预言不了。”

===Original===
“That is perfectly true,” said I. And then realizing that I had been led away from the topic of John Bellingham, I ventured to return to it.

===Chinese===
“千真万确。”我说。随即发觉话头已从约翰·贝林厄姆身上引开，便大着胆子又拉了回来。

===Original===
“That was a very mysterious affair﻿—the disappearance of John Bellingham, I mean.”

===Chinese===
“那真是一桩神秘莫测的事——我是说约翰·贝林厄姆的失踪。”

===Original===
“Why mysterious?” asked Mr. Jellicoe. “Men disappear from time to time, and when they reappear, the explanations that they give (when they give any) seem more or less adequate.”

===Chinese===
“神秘在何处？”杰利科先生问。“人时不时总要失踪几个；待他们再露面时，给出的解释（如果给的话）倒也多半说得过去。”

===Original===
“But the circumstances were surely rather mysterious.”

===Chinese===
“可当时的情形，总归相当蹊跷吧。”

===Original===
“What circumstances?” asked Mr. Jellicoe.

===Chinese===
“什么情形？”杰利科先生问。

===Original===
“I mean the way in which he vanished from Mr. Hurst’s house.”

===Chinese===
“我是指他从赫斯特先生府上消失的那个法子。”

===Original===
“In what way did he vanish from it?”

===Chinese===
“他是用个什么法子消失的？”

===Original===
“Well, of course, I don’t know.”

===Chinese===
“呃，这个我自然不知道。”

===Original===
“Precisely. Neither do I. Therefore I can’t say whether that way was a mysterious one or not.”

===Chinese===
“正是。我也不知道。所以我说不上那个法子究竟神秘不神秘。”

===Original===
“It is not even certain that he did leave it,” I remarked, rather recklessly.

===Chinese===
“甚至他到底离没离开那宅子，都还没有个定论呢。”我说得相当孟浪。

===Original===
“Exactly,” said Mr. Jellicoe. “And if he did not, he is there still. And if he is there still, he has not disappeared﻿—in the sense understood. And if he has not disappeared, there is no mystery.”

===Chinese===
“不错，”杰利科先生说。“倘若他没有离开，那他便还在那里。倘若他还在那里，那他便不曾失踪——按通常所理解的字义说。倘若他不曾失踪，那便无所谓神秘。”

===Original===
I laughed heartily, but Mr. Jellicoe preserved a wooden solemnity and continued to examine me through his spectacles (which I, in my turn, inspected and estimated at about minus five dioptres). There was something highly diverting about this grim lawyer, with his dry contentiousness and almost farcical caution. His ostentatious reserve encouraged me to ply him with fresh questions, the more indiscreet the better.

===Chinese===
我放声大笑，杰利科先生却始终板着一副木然的面孔，继续透过镜片审视我（那副镜片呢，我也回敬了一番端详，估计约有负五屈光度）。这位冷面律师身上有种极其逗趣的东西：那副干巴巴的好辩，加上那份近乎滑稽的谨慎。他那摆给人看的缄默反倒怂恿我再三向他发问，越发不知轻重才好。

===Original===
“I suppose,” said I, “that, under these circumstances, you would hardly favor Mr. Hurst’s proposal to apply for permission to presume death?”

===Chinese===
“我想，”我说，“既是这样，您想必不大赞成赫斯特先生那项申请推定死亡的提议吧？”

===Original===
“Under what circumstances?” he inquired.

===Chinese===
“既是怎么样？”他问。

===Original===
“I was referring to the doubt you have expressed as to whether John Bellingham is, after all, really dead.”

===Chinese===
“我是指您方才流露的疑虑——约翰·贝林厄姆究竟是不是真的死了。”

===Original===
“My dear sir,” said he, “I fail to see your point. If it were certain that the man was alive, it would be impossible to presume that he was dead; and if it were certain that he was dead, presumption of death would still be impossible. You do not presume a certainty. The uncertainty is of the essence of the transaction.”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的先生，”他说，“我不明白您的意思。倘若确知这人活着，便不可能推定他已死；倘若确知他死了，推定死亡仍旧不可能。确定之事，无所谓推定。不确定，才是这桩交易的精髓所在。”

===Original===
“But,” I persisted, “if you really believe that he may be alive, I should hardly have thought that you would take the responsibility of presuming his death and dispersing his property.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我不肯罢休，“倘若您当真相信他可能还活着，我倒想不到您竟肯担起这副担子——推定他死亡，再把他的财产散分掉。”

===Original===
“I don’t,” said Mr. Jellicoe. “I take no responsibility. I act in accordance with the decision of the Court and have no choice in the matter.”

===Chinese===
“我没有担，”杰利科先生说，“我什么责任也不担。我依照法院的裁定行事，在这件事上无从选择。”

===Original===
“But the Court may decide that he is dead and he may nevertheless be alive.”

===Chinese===
“可法院也许裁定他已死，而他却仍旧活着呢。”

===Original===
“Not at all. If the Court decides that he is presumably dead, then he is presumably dead. As a mere irrelevant, physical circumstance he may, it is true, be alive. But legally speaking, and for testamentary purposes, he is dead. You fail to perceive the distinction, no doubt?”

===Chinese===
“绝非如此。倘若法院裁定他推定死亡，那他便是推定死亡。不错，单就无关宏旨的肉身情形而论，他或许活着。但从法律上说，就遗嘱事务而言，他是死了。您看不出这中间的分别吧？”

===Original===
“I am afraid I do,” I admitted.

===Chinese===
“恐怕我确实看不出。”我承认道。

===Original===
“Yes; the members of your profession usually do. That is what makes them such bad witnesses in a court of law. The scientific outlook is radically different from the legal. The man of science relies on his own knowledge and observation and judgment, and disregards testimony. A man comes to you and tells you he is blind in one eye. Do you accept his statement? Not in the least. You proceed to test his eyesight with some infernal apparatus of colored glasses, and you find that he can see perfectly well with both eyes. Then you decide that he is not blind in one eye; that is to say, you reject his testimony in favor of facts of your own ascertaining.”

===Chinese===
“是的；贵行同人多半看不出。这正是他们在法庭上沦为糟糕证人的缘故。科学的视野与法律的视野，根本是两回事。科学人倚仗的是自己的知识、观察与判断，而不把证词放在眼里。一个人来见您，自称一只眼瞎了。您接受他的说法么？丝毫不。您接着便用某种有色玻璃的鬼器械去测他的视力，结果发现他两只眼都看得好好儿的。于是您断定他并非一只眼瞎；这就是说，您摒弃他的证词，而采信您亲身查明的事实。”

===Original===
“But surely that is the rational method of coming to a conclusion?”

===Chinese===
“可这总归是求得结论的合理方法吧？”

===Original===
“In science, no doubt. Not in law. A court of law must decide according to the evidence which is before it; and that evidence is of the nature of sworn testimony. If a witness is prepared to swear that black is white and no evidence to the contrary is offered, the evidence before the Court is that black is white, and the Court must decide accordingly. The judge and the jury may think otherwise﻿—they may even have private knowledge to the contrary﻿—but they have to decide according to the evidence.”

===Chinese===
“在科学里无疑如此。在法律里则不然。法庭必须依摆在其面前的证据裁断；而那证据，性质上属于宣誓证词。倘若一名证人肯起誓说黑即是白，又无人提出相反的证据，那么摆在法庭面前的证据便是黑即是白，法庭便须照此裁断。法官与陪审员尽可以另作想头——他们甚至可以私下知情，恰与此相反——但他们必须依证据裁断。”

===Original===
“Do you mean to say that a judge would be justified in giving a decision which he knew to be contrary to the facts? Or that he might sentence a man whom he knew to be innocent?”

===Chinese===
“您是说，一名法官明知判决与事实相悖，判下去也算名正言顺？或者说，他明知某人无辜，也可以给那人定罪？”

===Original===
“Certainly. It has been done. There is a case of a judge who sentenced a man to death and allowed the execution to take place, notwithstanding that he﻿—the judge﻿—had actually seen the murder committed by another man. But that was carrying correctness of procedure to the verge of pedantry.”

===Chinese===
“当然。这种事是有过的。有这么一桩案子：一名法官判处一人死刑，并听任行刑，尽管他——这位法官——曾亲眼目睹凶案系另一人所为。不过，那就是把程序的正确推到迂腐的边缘了。”

===Original===
“It was, with a vengeance,” I agreed. “But to return to the case of John Bellingham. Supposing that after the Court has decided that he is dead he should return alive? What then?”

===Chinese===
“可不，而且还变本加厉。”我附和道。“不过还是回到约翰·贝林厄姆的案子上来吧。假定法院裁定他死亡之后，他却又活着回来了，那可怎么办？”

===Original===
“Ah! It would then be his turn to make an application, and the Court, having fresh evidence laid before it, would probably decide that he was alive.”

===Chinese===
“啊！那时便轮到他来递呈申请了；法院面前既摆上了新的证据，多半会裁定他活着。”

===Original===
“And meantime his property would have been dispersed?”

===Chinese===
“而在此期间，他的财产已经散分掉了？”

===Original===
“Probably. But you will observe that the presumption of death would have arisen out of his own proceedings. If a man acts in such a way as to create a belief that he is dead, he must put up with the consequences.”

===Chinese===
“多半如此。但您会注意到，那推定死亡本是由他自己的行为招致的。一个人行事若到了使人相信他已死的地步，便必须自承其后果。”

===Original===
“Yes, that is reasonable enough,” said I. And then, after a pause, I asked: “Is there any immediate likelihood of proceedings of the kind being commenced?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，这也够公道的。”我说。顿了一顿，我又问：“这一类的程序，眼下有立刻启动的指望么？”

===Original===
“I understood from what you said just now that Mr. Hurst was contemplating some action of the kind. No doubt you had your information from a reliable quarter.” This answer Mr. Jellicoe delivered without moving a muscle, regarding me with the fixity of a spectacled figurehead.

===Chinese===
“从您方才的话里，我倒是听出赫斯特先生正在筹算这一类的举动。您的消息，想必得自可靠的来路。”这一句答复，杰利科先生说时脸上纹丝不动，只拿一尊戴眼镜的船首像般定定的眼光盯住我。

===Original===
I smiled feebly. The operation of pumping Mr. Jellicoe was rather like the sport of boxing with a porcupine, being chiefly remarkable as a demonstration of the power of passive resistance. I determined, however, to make one more effort, rather, I think, for the pleasure of witnessing his defensive maneuvers than with the expectation of getting anything out of him. I accordingly “opened out” on the subject of the “remains.”

===Chinese===
我勉强笑了笑。想从杰利科先生嘴里套话，颇像同豪猪打拳的运动，其主要可观之处，在于演示消极抵抗的力量。然而我决定再试一回——与其说指望从他身上套出什么，不如说更是为了赏鉴他那套防御身法的乐趣。于是我就在“遗骸”这个题目上“展开了攻势”。

===Original===
“Have you been following these remarkable discoveries of human bones that have been appearing in the papers?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“报上连日登载的那些人骨的重大发现，您一直在留意么？”我问。

===Original===
He looked at me stonily for some moments, and then replied:

===Chinese===
他石像似的看了我半晌，然后答道：

===Original===
“Human bones are rather more within your province than mine, but, now that you mention it, I think I recall having read of some such discoveries. They were disconnected bones, I believe.”

===Chinese===
“人骨一事，与其说归我管，不如说更归贵行管。不过您既提起了，我想我记起确曾读到过这一类的发现。若我没记错，都是些零散的骨头。”

===Original===
“Yes; evidently parts of a dismembered body.”

===Chinese===
“是的；显然是一具被肢解的尸身的若干部件。”

===Original===
“So I should suppose. No, I have not followed the accounts. As we get on in life our interests tend to settle into grooves, and my groove is chiefly connected with conveyancing. These discoveries would be of more interest to a criminal lawyer.”

===Chinese===
“我料想也是。没有，那些报道我并未追着看。人上了年纪，兴趣便渐渐落定成几条辙，而我的那条辙，主要与产权转让事务相连。这些发现，该是刑事律师更感兴趣的东西。”

===Original===
“I thought you might, perhaps, have connected them with the disappearance of your client?”

===Chinese===
“我还当您或许会把它们同您那位当事人的失踪联系到一处呢？”

===Original===
“Why should I? What could be the nature of the connection?”

===Chinese===
“我为什么要联系？这联系又能是何种性质？”

===Original===
“Well,” I said, “these are the bones of a man﻿—”

===Chinese===
“呃，”我说，“这些骨头是一个人的——”

===Original===
“Yes; and my client was a man with bones. That is a connection, certainly, though not a very specific or distinctive one. But perhaps you had something more particular in your mind?”

===Chinese===
“是啊；而我那位当事人也是个长着骨头的男人。这确是一种联系，虽然算不得怎么特定，也算不得怎么独特。不过您心里想的，或许比这个更要具体些？”

===Original===
“I had,” I replied. “The fact that some of the bones were actually found on land belonging to your client seemed to me rather significant.”

===Chinese===
“是要具体些。”我答道。“有几根骨头恰恰是在您当事人名下的土地上找到的，这一层在我看来便颇有意味。”

===Original===
“Did it, indeed?” said Mr. Jellicoe. He reflected for a few moments, gazing steadily at me the while, and then continued: “In that I am unable to follow you. It would have seemed to me that the finding of human remains upon a certain piece of land might conceivably throw a prima facie suspicion upon the owner or occupant of the land as being the person who deposited them. But the case that you suggest is the one case in which this would be impossible. A man cannot deposit his own dismembered remains.”

===Chinese===
“是么，果然如此？”杰利科先生说。他凝神想了片刻，一面目光定定地望住我，然后续道：“在这一点上我跟不上您了。依我想来，某一块土地上发现了人体遗骸，原可能对那土地的主人或占用人投下一层初步嫌疑，疑心弃置遗骸者正是其人。可您设想的情形，恰恰是唯一不可能如此的例外。一个人总不能弃置他自己那被肢解的遗骸。”

===Original===
“No, of course not. I was not suggesting that he deposited them himself, but merely that the fact of their being deposited on his land, in a way, connected these remains with him.”

===Chinese===
“自然不能。我并不是说遗骸是他亲手弃置的，只是说，它们既弃置在他的土地上，这一事实便在某种意义上把这些遗骸同他联系了起来。”

===Original===
“Again,” said Mr. Jellicoe, “I fail to follow you, unless you are suggesting that it is customary for murderers who mutilate bodies to be punctilious in depositing the dismembered remains upon land belonging to their victims. In which case I am skeptical as to your facts. I am not aware of the existence of any such custom. Moreover, it appears that only a portion of the body was deposited on Mr. Bellingham’s land, the remaining portions having been scattered broadcast over a wide area. How does that agree with your suggestion?”

===Chinese===
“我又跟不上您了，”杰利科先生说，“除非您是想说，肢解尸身的凶手们素来讲究，必把碎骸恭而敬之地弃置在被害人名下的土地上。倘使如此，您说的事实我可要存疑。我不曾听说世上存在这样的习俗。再者，弃置在贝林厄姆先生土地上的似乎只是尸体的一部分，其余各部都四散抛洒在一片广大的地域之内。这与您的设想如何吻合？”

===Original===
“It doesn’t, of course,” I admitted. “But there is another fact that I think you will admit to be more significant. The first remains that were discovered were found at Sidcup. Now, Sidcup is close to Eltham; and Eltham is the place where Mr. Bellingham was last seen alive.”

===Chinese===
“自然是吻合不上的。”我承认。“可是另有一层事实，我想您也会承认它更有意味。最早发现的那批遗骸，是在锡德卡普找到的。而锡德卡普紧挨着埃尔瑟姆；埃尔瑟姆，正是贝林厄姆先生最后一次被人见到活着的地方。”

===Original===
“And what is the significance of this? Why do you connect the remains with one locality rather than the various other localities in which other portions of the body were found?”

===Chinese===
“这又有何意味？您为什么单把遗骸与这一处相联，而不与寻获尸体其他各部的另外几处相联？”

===Original===
“Well,” I replied, rather graveled by this very pertinent question, “the appearances seem to suggest that the person who deposited these remains started from the neighborhood of Eltham, where the missing man was last seen.”

===Chinese===
“呃，”我答道，被这个正中要害的问话难得有点狼狈，“种种迹象似乎表明，弃置这些遗骸的人是从埃尔瑟姆一带动身的，而那正是失踪者最后一次被人见到的地方。”

===Original===
Mr. Jellicoe shook his head. “You appear,” said he, “to be confusing the order of deposition with the order of discovery. What evidence is there that the remains found at Sidcup were deposited before those found elsewhere?”

===Chinese===
杰利科先生摇了摇头。“您似乎，”他说，“把弃置的先后与发现的先后混作一谈了。有什么证据，能证明锡德卡普寻获的遗骸比别处寻获的弃置在先？”

===Original===
“I don’t know that there is any,” I admitted.

===Chinese===
“我不知道有什么证据。”我承认。

===Original===
“Then,” said he, “I don’t see how you support your suggestion that the person started from the neighborhood of Eltham.”

===Chinese===
“那么，”他说，“我看不出您凭什么支持您那‘此人从埃尔瑟姆一带动身’的设想。”

===Original===
On consideration, I had to admit that I had nothing to offer in support of my theory; and having thus shot my last arrow in this very unequal contest, I thought it time to change the subject.

===Chinese===
细想之下，我只得承认自己拿不出半点支撑这个理论的东西；在这场兵力悬殊的较量里射出了最后一箭之后，我想，该换个题目了。

===Original===
“I called in at the British Museum the other day,” said I, “and had a look at Mr. Bellingham’s last gift to the nation. The things are very well shown in that central case.”

===Chinese===
“前几天我到大英博物馆去了一趟，”我说，“看了看贝林厄姆先生献给国家的最后一件礼物。那些东西陈设在中央那座柜里，布置得很好。”

===Original===
“Yes. I was very pleased with the position they have given to the exhibit, and so would my poor old friend have been. I wished, as I looked at the case, that he could have seen it. But perhaps he may, after all.”

===Chinese===
“是的。他们给这批展品安顿的位置令我很满意，我那位可怜的老朋友若在，也会满意的。我望着那座陈列柜时不禁想，但愿他能亲眼见到。不过，说不定他终究还是见得着的。”

===Original===
“I am sure I hope he will,” said I, with more sincerity, perhaps, than the lawyer gave me credit for. For the return of John Bellingham would most effectually have cut the Gordian knot of my friend Godfrey’s difficulties. “You are a good deal interested in Egyptology yourself, aren’t you?” I added.

===Chinese===
“我诚心诚意盼他见得着。”我说，这一份诚意，或许比这位律师肯相信的还要多些。因为约翰·贝林厄姆一旦归来，我朋友戈弗雷种种难题的那个戈尔迪之结（Gordian knot）便可一挥而断。“您自己不也对埃及学大有兴趣么？”我又添了一句。

===Original===
“Greatly interested,” replied Mr. Jellicoe, with more animation than I had thought possible in his wooden face. “It is a fascinating subject, the study of this venerable civilization, extending back to the childhood of the human race, preserved forever for our instruction in its own unchanging monuments like a fly in a block of amber. Everything connected with Egypt is full of an impressive solemnity. A feeling of permanence, of stability, defying time and change, pervades it. The place, the people, and the monuments alike breathe of eternity.”

===Chinese===
“大有兴趣。”杰利科先生答道，那张木然的脸上竟浮起一股我原以为绝无可能的生气。“这是一门迷人的学问——研究这个可敬的文明：它上溯到人类的童年，像琥珀中的一只苍蝇，在它自己那些亘古不变的古迹里永远封存下来，供我们受教。凡与埃及相连的事物，无不充满一种动人的庄严。一种恒久之感、一种稳固之感，蔑视着时间与变迁，弥漫于其间。那地方、那人民、那些古迹，无不吐纳着永恒的气息。”

===Original===
I was mightily surprised at this rhetorical outburst on the part of this dry, taciturn lawyer. But I liked him the better for the touch of enthusiasm that made him human, and determined to keep him astride of his hobby.

===Chinese===
这位干燥少言的律师竟发出这样一番辞采飞扬的宏论，我不胜诧异。然而正是这一星热情使他有了人气，倒教我愈发喜欢他，于是打定主意，要让他稳稳骑在他那匹爱好的木马上跑下去。

===Original===
“Yet,” said I, “the people must have changed in the course of centuries.”

===Chinese===
“不过，”我说，“那人民历经千百年的岁月，总归是变了的吧。”

===Original===
“Yes, that is so. The people who fought against Cambyses were not the race who marched into Egypt five thousand years before﻿—the dynastic people whose portraits we see on the early monuments. In those fifty centuries the blood of Hyksos and Syrians and Ethiopians and Hittites, and who can say how many more races, must have mingled with that of the old Egyptians. But still the national life went on without a break; the old culture leavened the new peoples, and the immigrant strangers ended by becoming Egyptians. It is a wonderful phenomenon. Looking back on it from our own time, it seems more like a geological period than the life history of a single nation. Are you at all interested in the subject?”

===Chinese===
“是的，是变了。与冈比西斯（Cambyses）交战的人民，已不是五千年前开进埃及的那一族——不是我们在早期古迹上见到其肖像的、王朝时代的民族。在那五十个世纪里，希克索斯人（Hyksos）、叙利亚人、埃塞俄比亚人、赫梯人（Hittites），还有谁能说清多少旁的血统，必然已混进了古埃及人的血里。然而国民的生机仍旧一脉不断；古老的文化酵素发酵了新来的人民，外来的客族到头来都成了埃及人。这是一个了不起的现象。从我们自己的时代回望过去，它与其说是一个单一民族的生平史，不如说更像一段地质纪元。您对这门学问可有些兴趣么？”

===Original===
“Yes, decidedly, though I am completely ignorant of it. The fact is that my interest is of quite recent growth. It is only of late that I have been sensible of the glamor of things Egyptian.”

===Chinese===
“有，而且很大，虽然我对它一窍不通。实情是，我这兴趣滋长得极近。直到近来，我才感知到埃及事物的魅力。”

===Original===
“Since you made Miss Bellingham’s acquaintance, perhaps?” suggested Mr. Jellicoe, himself as unchanging in aspect as an Egyptian effigy.

===Chinese===
“大约是自您结识贝林厄姆小姐之后吧？”杰利科先生提示道，他自己那副容色，恰似一尊埃及雕像般亘古不变。

===Original===
I suppose I must have reddened﻿—I certainly resented the remark﻿—for he continued in the same even tone: “I made the suggestion because I know that she takes an intelligent interest in the subject and is, in fact, quite well informed on it.”

===Chinese===
我想我准是红了脸——那句话着实叫我恼——因为他续用同样平匀的声调说了下去：“我这样提示，是因为我知道她在这门学问上有一种富有智性的兴味，而且，事实上，于此道相当娴熟。”

===Original===
“Yes; she seems to know a great deal about the antiquities of Egypt, and I may as well admit that your surmise was correct. It was she who showed me her uncle’s collection.”

===Chinese===
“是啊；她似乎对埃及的古物所知极多，我不妨承认，您的猜测没有错。正是她领我看了她叔父的收藏。”

===Original===
“So I had supposed,” said Mr. Jellicoe. “And a very instructive collection it is, in a popular sense; very suitable for exhibition in a public museum, though there is nothing in it of unusual interest to the expert. The tomb furniture is excellent of its kind and the cartonnage case of the mummy is well made and rather finely decorated.”

===Chinese===
“我本就这样猜想。”杰利科先生说。“而且就通俗的意义上说，那是一批很能启人见识的收藏；拿到公共博物馆去陈列再合适不过，虽说其中没有什么叫专家格外瞩目的东西。那批随葬陈设是同类中的精品，那具木乃伊的棺匣也做工精良，装饰得颇为考究。”

===Original===
“Yes, I thought it quite handsome. But can you explain to me why, after taking all that trouble to decorate it, they should have disfigured it with those great smears of bitumen?”

===Chinese===
“是的，我也觉得它相当美观。可您能给我解释一下么：他们既下那么大功夫去装点它，为什么又用那一大片一大片的沥青涂抹，把它弄得面目全非？”

===Original===
“Ah!” said Mr. Jellicoe, “that is quite an interesting question. It is not unusual to find mummy cases smeared with bitumen; there is a mummy of a priestess in the next gallery which is completely coated with bitumen except the gilded face. Now, this bitumen was put on for a purpose﻿—for the purpose of obliterating the inscriptions and thus concealing the identity of the deceased from the robbers and desecrators of tombs. And there is the oddity of this mummy of Sebek-hotep. Evidently there was an intention of obliterating the inscriptions. The whole of the back is covered thickly with bitumen, and so are the feet. Then the workers seem to have changed their minds and left the inscriptions and decoration untouched. Why they intended to cover it, and why, having commenced, they left it partially covered only, is a mystery. The mummy was found in its original tomb and quite undisturbed, so far as tomb-robbers are concerned. Poor Bellingham was greatly puzzled as to what the explanation could be.”

===Chinese===
“啊！”杰利科先生说，“这倒是个很有意思的问题。木乃伊棺匣上涂着沥青的并不罕见；隔壁展厅里就有一具女祭司的木乃伊，除了鎏金的脸面，通体覆着沥青。而这种沥青，涂上去是有所为的——为的是消灭铭文，从而瞒过盗墓贼和渎墓者，不叫他们认出死者的身份。而塞贝克霍特普这具木乃伊的怪处也正在这里。显然，当初曾有抹去铭文的打算：整个背面厚厚地覆着沥青，两脚也是。随后，工匠们似乎又改了主意，铭文和纹饰便原封未动。他们当初为何打算涂盖，动了手之后又为何只涂了一半便住手，这是一个谜。这具木乃伊是在它原葬的墓里寻获的，就盗墓贼而论，全然未曾扰动。可怜的贝林厄姆为这该作何解释伤透了脑筋。”

===Original===
“Speaking of bitumen,” said I, “reminds me of a question that has occurred to me. You know that this substance has been used a good deal by modern painters and that it has a very dangerous peculiarity; I mean its tendency to liquefy, without any obvious reason, long after it has dried.”

===Chinese===
“说起沥青，”我说，“倒提醒了我一个纳闷已久的问题。您知道，这东西近代画家用得很多，而它有一种很危险的癖性；我是说，它干透之后许久许久，会毫无明显缘由地重新液化。”

===Original===
“Yes, I know. Isn’t there some story about a picture of Reynolds’s in which bitumen had been used? A portrait of a lady, I think. The bitumen softened, and one of the lady’s eyes slipped down on to her cheek; and they had to hang the portrait upside down and keep it warm until the eye slipped back again into its place. But what was your question?”

===Chinese===
“是的，我知道。雷诺兹（Reynolds）不是有一幅用过沥青的画么，不是有这么一段掌故？我记得是位女士的肖像。沥青软了，那位女士的一只眼睛便滑到了脸颊上；他们只好把肖像倒挂起来，给它烘着保暖，直到那只眼睛重新滑回原处。不过，您要问的是什么呢？”

===Original===
“I was wondering whether the bitumen used by the Egyptian artists has ever been known to soften after this great lapse of time.”

===Chinese===
“我想知道的是，埃及的艺人所用的沥青，隔了这样漫长的岁月之后，可曾听说有软化过的事例。”

===Original===
“Yes, I think it has. I have heard of instances in which the bitumen coatings have softened under certain circumstances and become quite ‘tacky.’ But, bless my soul! here am I gossiping with you and wasting your time, and it is nearly a quarter to nine!”

===Chinese===
“有，我想是有的。我听说过一些实例，沥青涂层在某些情形之下软化了，变得相当‘发黏’。可是，哎呀我的天！瞧我在这儿同你闲扯，白糟蹋你的工夫——都快差一刻九点了！”

===Original===
My guest rose hastily, and I, with many apologies for having detained him, proceeded to fulfil my promise to guide him to his destination. As we sallied forth together the glamour of Egypt faded by degrees, and when he shook my hand stiffly at the gate of the Bellinghams’ house, all his vivacity and enthusiasm had vanished, leaving the taciturn lawyer, dry, uncommunicative, and not a little suspicious.

===Chinese===
我的客人急忙起身，我连声为羁留他而告罪，随即履行前约，引路送他前往。我们一同出门上路，埃及的魅力渐渐淡去；待他在贝林厄姆家的大门口同我硬邦邦地握手时，他那一团的生气与热忱已消散净尽，剩下的仍是那个寡言的律师：干燥、缄口，而且不无猜疑之心。

## X The New Alliance / 十 新同盟
===Original===
The “Great Lexicographer”﻿—tutelary deity of my adopted habitat﻿—has handed down to shuddering posterity a definition of the act of eating which might have been framed by a dyspeptic ghoul. “Eat: to devour with the mouth.” It is a shocking view to take of so genial a function: cynical, indelicate, and finally unforgivable by reason of its very accuracy. For, after all, that is what eating amounts to, if one must needs express it with such crude brutality. But if “the ingestion of alimentary substances”﻿—to ring a modern change upon the older formula﻿—is in itself a process material even unto carnality, it is undeniable that it forms a highly agreeable accompaniment to more psychic manifestations.

===Chinese===
“大词典家”——我客居之地的守护神——给战战兢兢的后世留下了一条关于吃的定义，那条子简直像是出自一个消化不良的食尸鬼之手：“吃：用嘴吞食。”对如此和乐的一项人体机能竟持这种看法，实在骇人：愤世，粗鄙，而最不可饶恕的是，它偏偏一语中的。因为归根结底，倘若非要用这般粗野直白的话来说，吃也确实就是这么一回事。不过，“食物性物质的摄入”——在旧公式上翻个新花样——本身纵然是一种直逼肉欲层面的物质过程，无可否认，它却也足以为更偏于心灵的活动，充当一件极惬意的伴奏。

===Original===
And so, as the lamplight, reinforced by accessory candles, falls on the little table in the first-floor room looking on Fetter Lane﻿—only now the curtains are drawn﻿—the conversation is not the less friendly and bright for a running accompaniment executed with knives and forks, for clink of goblet, and jovial gurgle of wine-flask. On the contrary, to one of us, at least﻿—to wit, Godfrey Bellingham﻿—the occasion is one of uncommon festivity, and his boyish enjoyment of the simple feast makes pathetic suggestions of hard times, faced uncomplainingly, but keenly felt nevertheless.

===Chinese===
于是，当灯光——又有几支蜡烛增援——洒落在临菲特巷那间二楼小屋的小餐桌上时——只是如今窗帘已经拉上——席间的谈话，并不因刀叉的轮番伴奏、酒杯的轻碰和酒壶欢快的汩汩声而减少半分友爱与明快。恰恰相反，至少对我们中间的一位——也就是戈弗雷·贝林厄姆——这是一场难得的欢宴，他对这顿家常便饭孩子气般的欢享，隐隐透出一段苦日子的况味：那样的日子他毫无怨言地熬了过来，却并非不觉痛切。

===Original===
The talk flitted from topic to topic, mainly concerning itself with matters artistic, and never for one moment approaching the critical subject of John Bellingham’s will. From the stepped pyramid of Sakkara with its encaustic tiles to medieval church floors; from Elizabethan woodwork to Mycenean pottery, and thence to the industrial arts of the Stone Age and the civilization of the Aztecs. I began to suspect that my two legal friends were so carried away by the interest of the conversation that they had forgotten the secret purpose of the meeting, for the dessert had been placed on the table (by Mrs. Gummer with the manner of a bereaved dependent dispensing funeral bakemeats), and still no reference had been made to the “case.” But it seemed that Thorndyke was but playing a waiting game; was only allowing the intimacy to ripen while he watched for the opportunity. And that opportunity came, even as Mrs. Gummer vanished spectrally with a tray of plates and glasses.

===Chinese===
话题翩翩地从一个掠到另一个，大抵在艺术范围里打转，一刻也不曾逼近约翰·贝林厄姆的遗嘱这个紧要关头。从镶着蜡彩砖的萨卡拉（Sakkara）阶式金字塔，说到中世纪教堂的地面；从伊丽莎白时代的木雕说到迈锡尼陶器，再说到石器时代的工艺和阿兹特克人的文明。我开始疑心，我那两位法律界的朋友谈得兴发，竟把这次聚会的隐秘目的给忘了，因为甜食已经端上了桌（端点心的是古默太太，那副神气，活像一名居丧的仆人在分发葬礼上的糕饼），却仍旧无人只字提起那个“案子”。但看样子，桑代克不过是在打一场静候的仗；他只是听任情谊慢慢酿熟，一面守候时机。而时机终于来了，恰在古默太太托着一盘杯盘碗盏、幽灵似的退出门外的当儿。

===Original===
“So you had a visitor last night, Doctor,” said Mr. Bellingham. “I mean my friend Jellicoe. He told us he had seen you, and mighty curious he was about you. I have never known Jellicoe to be so inquisitive before. What did you think of him?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，昨晚您有位客人来过了，博士，”贝林厄姆先生说，“我指的是我那位朋友杰利科。他告诉我们说见过您，还对您分外好奇。我以前从没见杰利科这么爱打听过。您对他的观感如何？”

===Original===
“A quaint old cock. I found him highly amusing. We entertained one another for quite a long time with cross-questions and crooked answers; I affecting eager curiosity, he replying with a defensive attitude of universal ignorance. It was a most diverting encounter.”

===Chinese===
“一个古怪的老家伙。我觉得他非常有意思。我们彼此消遣了好一阵子，尽是些盘问与歪答：我这边装出热切的好奇，他那边摆出一副一问三不知的守势来应付。那真是一场妙趣横生的交锋。”

===Original===
“He needn’t have been so close,” Miss Bellingham remarked, “seeing that all the world will be regaled with our affairs before long.”

===Chinese===
“他其实用不着那么守口如瓶，”贝林厄姆小姐说道，“反正要不了多久，全世界都要拿我们家的事当谈资消遣了。”

===Original===
“They are proposing to take the case into Court, then?” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“这么说，他们要把案子提到法庭上去了？”桑代克说。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Mr. Bellingham. “Jellicoe came to tell me that my cousin, Hurst, has instructed his solicitors to make the application and to invite me to join him. Actually he came to deliver an ultimatum from Hurst﻿—but I mustn’t disturb the harmony of this festive gathering with litigious discords.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”贝林厄姆先生说。“杰利科来告诉我，我的表亲赫斯特已委托他的律师们提出申请，并邀我一同办理。其实，他是来向我转达赫斯特的一份最后通牒——不过，我总不好拿诉讼的不谐之音，来搅了这场喜庆聚会的和气吧。”

===Original===
“Now, why mustn’t you?” asked Thorndyke. “Why is a subject in which we are all keenly interested to be taboo? You don’t mind telling us about it, do you?”

===Chinese===
“哦，为什么不好？”桑代克问。“一个我们人人都极感兴趣的话题，凭什么成了禁忌？您并不介意讲给我们听，对吧？”

===Original===
“No, of course not. But what do you think of a man who buttonholes a doctor at a dinner-party to retail a list of ailments?”

===Chinese===
“不，当然不介意。可是，一个人在宴会上死死拉住医生不放，絮絮叨叨数说一串病痛，您对这种人作何感想？”

===Original===
“It depends on what his ailments are,” replied Thorndyke. “If he is a chronic dyspeptic and wishes to expound the virtues of Doctor Snaffler’s Purple Pills for Pimply People, he is merely a bore. But if he chances to suffer from some rare and choice disease such as Trypanosomiasis or Acromegaly, the doctor will be delighted to listen.”

===Chinese===
“那要看他生的是什么病，”桑代克答道。“倘若他是个老胃病，一心要宣讲斯纳夫勒博士（Doctor Snaffler）那‘疙瘩脸人士专用紫药丸’的种种神效，那他不过是个讨厌鬼。可倘若他碰巧害着什么罕见而名贵的病，比如锥虫病（Trypanosomiasis）或者肢端肥大症（Acromegaly），那医生可就要听得眉飞色舞了。”

===Original===
“Then are we to understand,” Miss Bellingham asked, “that we are rare and choice products, in a legal sense?”

===Chinese===
“那么我们可不可以这样理解，”贝林厄姆小姐问道，“从法律意义上说，我们是罕见而名贵的珍品喽？”

===Original===
“Undoubtedly,” replied Thorndyke. “The case of John Bellingham is, in many respects, unique. It will be followed with the deepest interest by the profession at large, and especially by medical jurists.”

===Chinese===
“毫无疑问，”桑代克答道，“约翰·贝林厄姆一案在许多方面都是独一无二的。整个法律界都会怀着最深厚的兴趣注视它，法医学家们尤其如此。”

===Original===
“How gratifying that should be to us!” said Miss Bellingham. “We may even attain undying fame in textbooks and treatises; and yet we are not so very much puffed up with our importance.”

===Chinese===
“这对我们可真是莫大的荣幸！”贝林厄姆小姐说。“说不定还能在教科书和专门论著里博得不朽之名呢；尽管如此，我们倒也不曾因此多么飘飘然。”

===Original===
“No,” said her father; “we could do without the fame quite well, and so, I think, could Hurst. Did Berkeley tell you of the proposal that he made?”

===Chinese===
“那倒没有，”她父亲说，“这名声，没有也全无妨碍，而且我想，赫斯特也一样。伯克利跟您说过他提的那项提议没有？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Thorndyke; “and I gather from what you say that he has repeated it.”

===Chinese===
“说过，”桑代克说，“而且听您这话的意思，他又提了一次。”

===Original===
“Yes. He sent Jellicoe to give me another chance, and I was tempted to take it; but my daughter was strongly against any compromise, and probably she is right. At any rate, she is more concerned than I am.”

===Chinese===
“是的。他派杰利科来再给我一次机会，我未尝不曾动心；可我女儿坚决反对任何妥协，而她多半是对的。无论如何，她在这件事上比我更上心。”

===Original===
“What view did Mr. Jellicoe take?” Thorndyke asked.

===Chinese===
“杰利科先生是什么态度？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“Oh, he was very cautious and reserved, but he didn’t disguise his feeling that I should be wise to take a certainty in lieu of a very problematical fortune. He would certainly like me to agree, for he naturally wishes to get the affair settled and pocket his legacy.”

===Chinese===
“哦，他十分谨慎，也十分矜持，但他并不掩饰自己的看法：拿一笔稳到手的实惠，去顶替一份渺茫难凭的家产，才是明智之举。他当然巴不得我答应，因为他自然盼着这桩事了结，好把他的遗赠揣进腰包。”

===Original===
“And have you definitely refused?”

===Chinese===
“那么，您是正式回绝了？”

===Original===
“Yes; quite definitely. So Hurst will apply for permission to presume death and prove the will, and Jellicoe will support him; he says he has no choice.”

===Chinese===
“是的；回绝得斩钉截铁。所以赫斯特将申请推定死亡并执行遗嘱的许可，杰利科会支持他；他说他别无选择。”

===Original===
“And you?”

===Chinese===
“那您呢？”

===Original===
“I suppose I shall oppose the application, though I don’t quite know on what grounds.”

===Chinese===
“我想我会反对这项申请，虽然我不大清楚该依据什么理由。”

===Original===
“Before you take definite steps,” said Thorndyke, “you ought to give the matter very careful consideration. I take it that you have very little doubt that your brother is dead. And if he is dead, any benefit that you may receive under the will must be conditional on the previous presumption or proof of death. But perhaps you have taken advice?”

===Chinese===
“在迈出确定的一步之前，”桑代克说，“您应当把这事掂量得极其仔细。我想，令兄已不在人世，这一点您心里是没有多少怀疑的。而倘若他已亡故，那么您按遗嘱所能取得的任何利益，都必须以先行推定或证明死亡为先决条件。不过，您或许已经请教过法律意见了？”

===Original===
“No, I have not. As our friend the Doctor has probably told you, my means﻿—or rather, the lack of them﻿—do not admit of my getting professional advice. Hence my delicacy about discussing the case with you.”

===Chinese===
“没有。咱们这位博士朋友多半已经告诉过您，我的财力——或者说，财力的匮乏——不容我请教专业人士。所以我才一直有所顾忌，不便同您讨论这个案子。”

===Original===
“Then do you propose to conduct your case in person?”

===Chinese===
“那么您打算亲自出庭，打理这个案子？”

===Original===
“Yes; if it is necessary for me to appear in Court, as I suppose it will be, if I oppose the application.”

===Chinese===
“是的；倘若我必须到庭的话——我想，我一反对这项申请，多半就非到庭不可。”

===Original===
Thorndyke reflected for a few moments and then said gravely:

===Chinese===
桑代克沉吟片刻，然后正色说道：

===Original===
“You had much better not appear in person to conduct your case, Mr. Bellingham, for several reasons. To begin with, Mr. Hurst is sure to be represented by a capable counsel, and you will find yourself quite unable to meet the sudden exigencies of a contest in Court. You will be outmaneuvered. Then there is the judge to be considered.”

===Chinese===
“贝林厄姆先生，有好几层缘故，您千万别亲自出庭办案。头一层，赫斯特先生必定有一位干练的出庭律师代他出庭，而您会发觉，自己全然应付不了法庭交锋中种种猝发的变局。您会处处受制于人。再者，还有法官这一层要考虑。”

===Original===
“But surely one can rely on the judge dealing fairly with a man who is unable to afford a solicitor and counsel?”

===Chinese===
“可是，对一个延请不起事务律师和出庭律师的人，总该可以指望法官公平相待吧？”

===Original===
“Undoubtedly, as a rule, a judge will give an unrepresented litigant every assistance and consideration. English judges in general are high-minded men with a deep sense of their great responsibilities. But you cannot afford to take any chances. You must consider the exceptions. A judge has been a counsel, and he may carry to the bench some of the professional prejudices of the bar. Indeed, if you consider the absurd license permitted to counsel in their treatment of witnesses, and the hostile attitude adopted by some judges toward medical and other scientific men who have to give their evidence, you will see that the judicial mind is not always quite as judicial as one would wish, especially when the privileges and immunities of the profession are concerned. Now, your appearance in person to conduct your case must, unavoidably, cause some inconvenience to the Court. Your ignorance of procedure and legal details must occasion some delay; and if the judge should happen to be an irritable man he might resent the inconvenience and delay. I don’t say that would affect his decision﻿—I don’t think it would﻿—but I am sure it would be wise to avoid giving offense to the judge. And, above all, it is most desirable to be able to detect and reply to any maneuvers on the part of the opposing counsel, which you certainly would not be able to do.”

===Chinese===
“毫无疑问，按通例，法官对于没有律师代理的诉讼当事人，会给予种种协助与体谅。英国的法官大都品格高尚，对自己肩负的重大责任有深切的自觉。但您经不起碰运气，您必须考虑到例外情形。法官本人做过出庭律师，可能把律师界的某些职业成见带上审判席。说真的，您只要想想出庭律师对待证人时被容许的那种荒唐的放肆，再想想某些法官对必须出庭作证的医生及其他科学界人士所抱的敌视态度，就会明白，司法的头脑并不总像人们期望的那样司法，尤其当事涉本界的特权和豁免时，更是如此。而您亲自出庭办案，势必给法庭带来某些不便。您对程序和法律细节的生疏，必致耽延时日；倘若法官碰巧是个火气旺的人，他可能就会怨怪这些麻烦与延误。我不是说这会影响他的裁断——我想不至于——但我敢肯定，开罪法官总归不是上策。而最要紧的是，要能识破对方律师的种种手段并即时回击，这一层，您必定是办不到的。”

===Original===
“This is excellent advice, Doctor Thorndyke,” said Bellingham, with a grim smile; “but I’m afraid I shall have to take my chance.”

===Chinese===
“这忠告真是无可挑剔，桑代克博士，”贝林厄姆带着一丝冷笑说，“可惜我怕只有听天由命的份儿。”

===Original===
“Not necessarily,” said Thorndyke. “I am going to make a little proposal, which I will ask you to consider without prejudice as a mutual accommodation. You see, your case is one of exceptional interest﻿—it will become a textbook case, as Miss Bellingham prophesied; and, since it lies within my specialty, it will be necessary for me to follow it in the closest detail. Now, it would be much more satisfactory to me to study it from within than from without, to say nothing of the credit which would accrue to me if I should be able to conduct it to a successful issue. I am therefore going to ask you to put your case in my hands and let me see what can be done with it. I know this is an unusual course for a professional man to take, but I think it is not improper under the circumstances.”

===Chinese===
“那倒未必，”桑代克说。“我有个小小的提议，想请您不带成见地斟酌一番，权当彼此行个方便。您瞧，您这案子旨趣非凡——正如贝林厄姆小姐所预言，它将成为一桩教科书式的案例；而它既落在我的专业范围之内，我就有必要把它的细节巨细无遗地追索到底。那么，从局内而非局外去研究它，于我岂不称心得多，更不必说，倘若我能把它办出个圆满的结果，随之而来的声誉了。因此，我想请您把案子交到我手里，让我看看能有什么作为。我知道，以一个执业之士的身份来说，这是非常之举，但我想，在眼下这种情形之下，未必失当。”

===Original===
Mr. Bellingham pondered in silence for a few moments, and then, after a glance at his daughter, began rather hesitatingly: “It’s very generous of you, Doctor Thorndyke﻿—”

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆先生默然思量了片刻，然后瞥了女儿一眼，颇有些迟疑地开口道：“您真是太慷慨了，桑代克博士——”

===Original===
“Pardon me,” interrupted Thorndyke, “it is not. My motives, as I have explained, are purely egoistic.”

===Chinese===
“对不起，”桑代克打断他，“并非如此。我已经说明，我的动机纯粹是利己的。”

===Original===
Mr. Bellingham laughed uneasily and again glanced at his daughter, who, however, pursued her occupation of peeling a pear with calm deliberation and without lifting her eyes. Getting no help from her he asked: “Do you think that there is any possibility whatever of a successful issue?”

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆先生局促地笑了笑，又看了女儿一眼，可她只顾从容不迫地削她的梨，眼皮也不曾抬一下。从她那里讨不到救兵，他便问道：“您认为这案子究竟还有没有一线成功的指望？”

===Original===
“Yes, a remote possibility﻿—very remote, I fear, as things look at present; but if I thought the case absolutely hopeless I should advise you to stand aside and let events take their course.”

===Chinese===
“有，一线渺茫的可能——照眼下的光景看，我怕是极其渺茫；但倘若我认为这案子全然无望，我就会劝您靠边站开，听凭事态自行发展了。”

===Original===
“Supposing the case should come to a favorable termination, would you allow me to settle your fees in the ordinary way?”

===Chinese===
“倘若案子得了个圆满的结局，您能容我按寻常的规矩奉上酬金么？”

===Original===
“If the choice lay with me,” replied Thorndyke, “I should say ‘yes’ with pleasure. But it does not. The attitude of the profession is very definitely unfavorable to ‘speculative’ practise. You may remember the well-known firm of Dodson and Fogg, who gained thereby much profit, but little credit. But why discuss contingencies of this kind? If I bring your case to a successful issue I shall have done very well for myself. We shall have benefited one another mutually. Come now, Miss Bellingham, I appeal to you. We have eaten salt together, to say nothing of pigeon pie and other cakes. Won’t you back me up, and at the same time do a kindness to Doctor Berkeley?”

===Chinese===
“倘若由得我挑，”桑代克答道，“我会欣然说一声‘愿意’。可惜由不得我。法律界对‘投机性’执业的态度是断然不利的。您也许记得那家大名鼎鼎的事务所道森和福格（Dodson and Fogg），他们借此博得了大笔进项，却不曾博得多少令名。不过，何必去讨论这类悬想之数呢？倘若我把您的案子办成了，我自己已大有好处，我们彼此彼此，两受其益。好了，贝林厄姆小姐，我向您求助。我们一同吃过盐——更不必说还分食过鸽肉派和各色糕点。您就不愿替我说句话，顺便也对伯克利博士行个方便么？”

===Original===
“Why, is Doctor Berkeley interested in our decision?”

===Chinese===
“咦，难道伯克利博士还把我们的决定放在心上不成？”

===Original===
“Certainly he is, as you will appreciate when I tell you that he actually tried to bribe me secretly out of his own pocket.”

===Chinese===
“他当然放在心上。等您听说他竟试图私下里自掏腰包来贿赂我，您就明白了。”

===Original===
“Did you?” she asked, looking at me with an expression that rather alarmed me.

===Chinese===
“真有这事么？”她问，一面望着我，那神情叫我不免心头发慌。

===Original===
“Well, not exactly,” I replied, mighty hot and uncomfortable, and wishing Thorndyke at the devil with his confidences. “I merely mentioned that the﻿—the﻿—solicitor’s costs, you know, and that sort of thing﻿—but you needn’t jump on me, Miss Bellingham; Doctor Thorndyke did all that was necessary in that way.”

===Chinese===
“呃，也算不上什么，”我答道，脸上滚烫，浑身不自在，一心只盼桑代克连同他的体己话一齐见鬼去。“我不过提了一句那个——那个——事务律师的费用，您知道的，还有那一类的事——可您也不必揪住我不放呀，贝林厄姆小姐；这方面该说的话，桑代克博士都已说尽了。”

===Original===
She continued to look at me thoughtfully as I stammered out my excuses, and then said: “I wasn’t going to. I was only thinking that poverty has its compensations. You are all so very good to us; and, for my part, I should accept Doctor Thorndyke’s generous offer most gratefully, and thank him for making it so easy for us.”

===Chinese===
我结结巴巴辩白之际，她仍若有所思地望着我，然后说道：“我原也没想揪住您。我只是在想，贫穷也有它的补偿。你们诸位待我们实在太好了；要依我说，我就满怀感激地领受桑代克博士的美意，还要谢谢他把话说得这样周到，令我们全不为难。”

===Original===
“Very well, my dear,” said Mr. Bellingham; “we will enjoy the sweets of poverty, as you say﻿—we have sampled the other kind of thing pretty freely﻿—and do ourselves the pleasure of accepting a great kindness, most delicately offered.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，亲爱的，”贝林厄姆先生说，“正像你说的，我们就享一享贫穷的甜头吧——另一种滋味，我们已领教得够充分的了——同时欣然领受这份厚意，它献得如此体贴入微。”

===Original===
“Thank you,” said Thorndyke. “You have justified my faith in you, Miss Bellingham, and in the power of Dr. Berkeley’s salt. I understand that you place your affairs in my hands?”

===Chinese===
“谢谢你们，”桑代克说。“贝林厄姆小姐，你们没有辜负我对你们的信赖，也没有辜负伯克利博士那一撮盐的效力。这么说，你们是把诸事都交到我手里了？”

===Original===
“Entirely and thankfully,” replied Mr. Bellingham. “Whatever you think best to be done we agree to beforehand.”

===Chinese===
“毫无保留，感激不尽，”贝林厄姆先生答道。“您认为怎样最妥当，我们都预先应承。”

===Original===
“Then,” said I, “let us drink success to the cause. Port, if you please, Miss Bellingham; the vintage is not recorded, but it is quite wholesome, and a suitable medium for the sodium chloride of friendship.” I filled her glass, and when the bottle had made its circuit, we stood up and solemnly pledged the new alliance.

===Chinese===
“那么，”我说，“让我们为这桩事的成功干一杯。请赏光用波特酒，贝林厄姆小姐；年份虽无从查考，倒还纯正，正好做友谊中那一份氯化钠的溶媒。”我给她斟满了酒，酒瓶巡行一周之后，我们全体起立，郑重地举杯，缔结了这一新的同盟。

===Original===
“There is just one thing I would say before we dismiss the subject for the present,” said Thorndyke. “It is a good thing to keep one’s own counsel. When you get formal notice from Mr. Hurst’s solicitors that proceedings are being commenced, you may refer them to Mr. Marchmont of Gray’s Inn, who will nominally act for you. He will actually have nothing to do, but we must preserve the fiction that I am instructed by a solicitor. Meanwhile, and until the case goes into court, I think it very necessary that neither Mr. Jellicoe nor anyone else should know that I am connected with it. We must keep the other side in the dark, if we can.”

===Chinese===
“在这个题目暂且搁下之前，我还有一句话要说，”桑代克道。“守口如瓶，永远是件好事。等您接到赫斯特先生的律师发来的正式通知，说诉讼即将开始，您可以把他们引荐给格雷律师学院（Gray's Inn）的马奇蒙特先生，由他挂名代理您。他其实无事可办，但我们必须保全那层虚构，就是说，我是受一位事务律师之托。在此期间——直到案子开庭之前——我认为极有必要做到：无论杰利科先生还是其他任何人，都不得知道我与这案子有牵连。只要办得到，就得叫对面蒙在鼓里。”

===Original===
“We will be as secret as the grave,” said Mr. Bellingham; “and, as a matter of fact, it will be quite easy, since it happens, by a curious coincidence, that I am already acquainted with Mr. Marchmont. He acted for Stephen Blackmore, you remember, in that case that you unraveled so wonderfully. I knew the Blackmores.”

===Chinese===
“我们一定守口如瓶，密得像坟墓，”贝林厄姆先生说，“而且，说来也巧，这事再容易不过，因为我偏偏本来就与马奇蒙特先生相熟。您总记得，您那桩神妙破解的案子里，他正是斯蒂芬·布莱克莫尔（Stephen Blackmore）的代理律师。布莱克莫尔一家我都认识。”

===Original===
“Did you?” said Thorndyke. “What a small world it is. And what a remarkable affair that was! The intricacies and cross-issues made it quite absorbingly interesting; and it is noteworthy for me in another respect, for it was one of the first cases in which I was associated with Doctor Jervis.”

===Chinese===
“是么？”桑代克说。“这世界可真小。那也是一桩多么了不得的奇案！千头万绪，问题交错，迷人到了极处；于我，它另有一层可记之处，因为那是我与杰维斯博士共事的最早几桩案子之一。”

===Original===
“Yes, and a mighty useful associate I was,” remarked Jervis, “though I did pick up one or two facts by accident. And, by the way, the Blackmore case had certain points in common with your case, Mr. Bellingham. There was a disappearance and a disputed will, and the man who vanished was a scholar and an antiquarian.”

===Chinese===
“可不是，我这位搭档可真有大用处，”杰维斯说道，“虽说那一两条要紧的事实，是我不小心撞见的。对了，说起布莱克莫尔那案子，它跟您这案子倒有几处相通，贝林厄姆先生。同样是一场失踪，一纸有争议的遗嘱，失踪的那一位又是个学者、好古之士。”

===Original===
“Cases in our specialty are apt to have certain general resemblances,” Thorndyke said; and as he spoke he directed a keen glance at his junior, the significance of which I partly understood when he abruptly changed the subject.

===Chinese===
“我们这一行的案子，往往会有某些大面上的相似，”桑代克说；说话间，他向这位晚辈射去锐利的一瞥，那一瞥的意味，等他突然把话头一转，我才算明白了几分。

===Original===
“The newspaper reports of your brother’s disappearance, Mr. Bellingham, were remarkably full of detail. There were even plans of your house and that of Mr. Hurst. Do you know who supplied the information?”

===Chinese===
“报上关于令兄失踪的报道，细节详尽得出奇，贝林厄姆先生。甚至还有您宅子和赫斯特先生宅子的平面图。您可知道，这些消息是谁提供的？”

===Original===
“No, I don’t,” replied Mr. Bellingham. “I know that I didn’t. Some newspaper men came to me for information, but I sent them packing. So, I understand, did Hurst; and as for Jellicoe, you might as well cross-examine an oyster.”

===Chinese===
“不知道，”贝林厄姆先生答道，“我只知道不是我。有几个报馆的人来找我打听，叫我打发走了。据我所知，赫斯特也一样；至于杰利科嘛，你还不如去盘问一只牡蛎。”

===Original===
“Well,” said Thorndyke, “the pressmen have queer methods of getting ‘copy’; but still, someone must have given them that description of your brother and those plans. It would be interesting to know who it was. However, we don’t know; and now let us dismiss these legal topics, with suitable apologies for having introduced them.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”桑代克说，“报人们弄‘稿件’的路数本来就出奇；不过，关于令兄相貌的那番描述、还有那些平面图，总归是有人供给他们的。若知道那人是谁，倒是一桩趣事。然而我们无从知晓；现在，让我们把这些法律话题打发掉吧——为方才提起它们，谨致适当的歉意。”

===Original===
“And perhaps,” said I, “we may as well adjourn to what we call the drawing-room﻿—it is really Barnard’s den﻿—and leave the housekeeper to wrestle with the debris.”

===Chinese===
“再不然，”我说，“我们不妨挪步到我们称之为客厅的那间屋里去——其实那是巴纳德的窝——也好让管家去同这一桌残席搏斗。”

===Original===
We migrated to the cheerfully shabby little apartment, and, when Mrs. Gummer had served coffee, with gloomy resignation (as who should say: “If you will drink this sort of stuff I suppose you must, but don’t blame me for the consequences”), I settled Mr. Bellingham in Barnard’s favorite lopsided easy chair﻿—the depressed seat of which suggested its customary use by an elephant of sedentary habits﻿—and opened the diminutive piano.

===Chinese===
我们迁到那间虽破旧却暖意融融的小屋里，等古默太太端上咖啡——那神情郁郁而听天由命（仿佛是说：“你们既要喝这种玩意儿，那也只好由你们，只是往后有什么后果，可别怪我”）——我把贝林厄姆先生安置进巴纳德心爱的那把歪斜的安乐椅——那凹陷的座面，足叫人想见惯常坐它的是一头惯于久坐的大象——然后掀开了那架小小的钢琴。

===Original===
“I wonder if Miss Bellingham would give us a little music?” I said.

===Chinese===
“不知道贝林厄姆小姐肯不肯赏我们一点音乐？”我说。

===Original===
“I wonder if she could?” was the smiling response. “Do you know,” she continued, “I have not touched a piano for nearly two years? It will be quite an interesting experiment﻿—to me; but if it fails, you will be the sufferers. So you must choose.”

===Chinese===
“不知道她还会不会弹呢？”回答是莞尔一笑。“您可知道，”她接着说，“我快两年没碰过钢琴了么？这可要成为一场很有意思的实验了——于我而言；但若是砸了锅，遭殃的可是你们。所以，还是请你们来定夺吧。”

===Original===
“My verdict,” said Mr. Bellingham, “is *fiat experimentum*, though I won’t complete the quotation, as that would seem to disparage Doctor Barnard’s piano. But before you begin, Ruth, there is one rather disagreeable matter that I want to dispose of, so that I may not disturb the harmony with it later.”

===Chinese===
“我的裁断，”贝林厄姆先生说，“是 *fiat experimentum*（尽管实验）——不过后半句我就不引了，免得似乎贬低了巴纳德博士的钢琴。但在你开场之前，露丝，还有一件颇为不痛快的事，我想先料理清楚，免得待会儿又拿它来搅了和气。”

===Original===
He paused and we all looked at him expectantly.

===Chinese===
他顿了一顿，我们都期待地望着他。

===Original===
“I suppose, Doctor Thorndyke,” he said, “you read the newspapers?”

===Chinese===
“我猜，桑代克博士，”他说，“您是看报纸的吧？”

===Original===
“I don’t,” replied Dr. Thorndyke. “But I ascertain, for purely business purposes, what they contain.”

===Chinese===
“我不看，”桑代克博士答道，“不过，出于纯粹业务上的目的，它们登了些什么，我会查个明白。”

===Original===
“Then,” said Mr. Bellingham, “you have probably met with some accounts of the finding of certain human remains, apparently portions of a mutilated body.”

===Chinese===
“那么，”贝林厄姆先生说，“您多半见到过一些报道，说发现了若干人的遗骸，看得出是一具遭肢解的尸身的若干部位。”

===Original===
“Yes, I have seen those reports and filed them for future reference.”

===Chinese===
“是的，那些报道我都看过，还一一归档，以备日后查考。”

===Original===
“Exactly. Well, now, it can hardly be necessary for me to tell you that those remains﻿—the mutilated remains of some poor murdered creature, as there can be no doubt they are﻿—have seemed to have a very dreadful significance for me. You will understand what I mean; and I want to ask you if﻿—if they have made a similar suggestion to you?”

===Chinese===
“正是。嗯，如今也毋庸我对您说：那些遗骸——那分明是某个可怜的被谋害者的残骸，这一点毫无疑问——在我看来，似乎有着极其可怕的意味。您明白我的意思；我只想请问您一句——它们可曾也向您提示过同样的联想？”

===Original===
