Thorndyke paused before replying, with his eyes bent thoughtfully on the floor, and we all looked at him anxiously.

===Chinese===
桑代克答话之前先停了一停，双眼若有所思地俯视着地板，我们全都焦灼地望着他。

===Original===
“It’s very natural,” he said at length, “that you should associate these remains with the mystery of your brother’s disappearance. I should like to say that you are wrong in doing so, but if I did I should be uncandid. There are certain facts that do, undoubtedly, seem to suggest a connection, and, up to the present, there are no definite facts of a contrary significance.”

===Chinese===
“您把这些遗骸同令兄失踪之谜联系起来，本是极自然的事，”他终于说道，“我很想告诉您，您这样想是想错了，可那样说便是不诚实。确实有某些事实，无可置疑地像是在暗示一种关联，而直到如今，还没有任何明确的事实带着相反的意味。”

===Original===
Mr. Bellingham sighed deeply and shifted uncomfortably in his chair.

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆先生深深叹了口气，在椅子里不安地挪动身子。

===Original===
“It is a horrible affair!” he said huskily; “horrible! Would you mind, Doctor Thorndyke, telling us just how the matter stands in your opinion﻿—what the probabilities are, for and against?”

===Chinese===
“这是一桩骇人的事！”他声音沙哑地说；“骇人啊！桑代克博士，可否劳您告诉我们，依您的看法，这事眼下究竟到了什么地步——有利的可能有多大，不利的又有多大？”

===Original===
Again Thorndyke reflected awhile, and it seemed to me that he was not very willing to discuss the subject. However, the question had been asked pointedly, and eventually he answered:

===Chinese===
桑代克又沉吟了一阵，依我看，他并不十分愿意谈论这个题目。然而，问题已径直摆在那里，他终于回答了：

===Original===
“At the present stage of the investigation it is not very easy to state the balance of probabilities. The matter is still quite speculative. The bones which have been found hitherto (for we are dealing with a skeleton, not with a body) have been exclusively those which are useless for personal identification; which is, in itself, a rather curious and striking fact. The general character and dimensions of the bones seem to suggest a middle-aged man of about your brother’s height, and the date of deposition appears to be in agreement with the date of his disappearance.”

===Chinese===
“在调查的现阶段，还不大容易断言可能性的天平倾向何方。事情仍全属悬测。迄今为止发现的骨殖（因为我们面对的是一副骸骨，不是一具尸身），清一色是对个人识别毫无用处的那些；这一点本身，就是一桩相当古怪而扎眼的事实。骨殖的总体性状和尺寸，似乎指向一名与令兄年纪相仿、身高相仿的中年男子，而沉放的日期，看来也与失踪的日期相合。”

===Original===
“Is it known, then, when they were deposited?” asked Mr. Bellingham.

===Chinese===
“那么，它们是何时被放下去的，已有眉目了么？”贝林厄姆先生问。

===Original===
“In the case of those found at Sidcup it seems possible to deduce an approximate date. The watercress-bed was cleaned out about two years ago, so they could not have been lying there longer than that; and their condition suggests that they could not have been there much less than two years, as there is apparently no vestige of the soft structures left. Of course, I am speaking from the newspaper reports only; I have no direct knowledge of the matter.”

===Chinese===
“就锡德卡普发现的那批而论，似可推出一个大致的日期。那片水田芥田约莫两年前清淤过一次，所以它们在那里躺卧的时间不可能更长；而它们的状况又表明，也不大可能短于两年，因为软组织显然已荡然无存。当然，我只是照报上的报道说话；这事我没有直接的了解。”

===Original===
“Have they found any considerable part of the body yet? I haven’t been reading the papers myself. My little friend, Miss Oman, brought a great bundle of ’em for me to read, but I couldn’t stand it; I pitched the whole boiling of ’em out of the window.”

===Chinese===
“他们可曾找到过尸身上什么像样的部位么？我自己一直没看报。我的小朋友奥曼小姐给我抱来一大捆叫我读，可我实在受不了，把那一整堆一股脑儿全从窗户扔了出去。”

===Original===
I thought I detected a slight twinkle in Thorndyke’s eye, but he answered quite gravely:

===Chinese===
我想我察觉到桑代克眼里闪过一丝笑意，但他回答得十分郑重：

===Original===
“I think I can give you the particulars from memory, though I won’t guarantee the dates. The original discovery was made, apparently quite accidentally, at Sidcup on the fifteenth of July. It consisted of a complete left arm, minus the third finger and including the bones of the shoulder﻿—the shoulder-blade and collarbone. This discovery seems to have set the local population, especially the juvenile part of it, searching all the ponds and streams of the neighborhood﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我想凭记忆就能把各项细节说给您听，只是日期我不敢担保。最初的发现是在七月十五日，地点在锡德卡普，显然纯属偶然。那是一条完整的左臂，独缺第三指，连肩部的骨头也包括在内——肩胛骨和锁骨。这一发现似乎搅得当地居民、尤其是其中的少年份子，把左近的池塘溪流搜了个遍——”

===Original===
“Cannibals!” interjected Mr. Bellingham.

===Chinese===
“一群食人生番！”贝林厄姆先生插嘴道。

===Original===
“With the result that there was dredged up out of a pond near St. Mary Cray, in Kent, a right thighbone. There is a slight clue to identity in respect of this bone, since the head of it has a small patch of ‘eburnation’﻿—that is a sort of porcelain-like polish that occurs on the parts of bones that form a joint when the natural covering of cartilage is destroyed by disease. It is produced by the unprotected surface of the bone grinding against the similarly unprotected surface of another.”

===Chinese===
“其结果是，在肯特郡的圣玛丽克雷（St. Mary Cray）附近，从一个池塘里捞出了一根右侧股骨。这根骨头倒带来一点有关身份的线索：骨头的一端有一小块‘骨象牙化’（eburnation）——那是骨中构成关节的部位，当软骨的天然覆盖被疾病蚀毁之后，生出的一种瓷器般的抛光。那是失了护面的骨面，同另一块同样失了护面的骨面相互研磨出来的。”

===Original===
“And how,” Mr. Bellingham asked, “would that help the identification?”

===Chinese===
“可这，”贝林厄姆先生问道，“对辨认身份又能帮上什么忙呢？”

===Original===
“It would indicate,” Thorndyke replied, “that the deceased had probably suffered from rheumatoid arthritis﻿—what is commonly known as rheumatic gout﻿—and he would probably have limped slightly and complained of some pain in the right hip.”

===Chinese===
“它可以表明，”桑代克答道，“死者生前多半害过类风湿性关节炎——也就是通称的风湿痛风——那样，他走路多半有些跛，还多半抱怨过右髋疼痛。”

===Original===
“I’m afraid that doesn’t help us very much,” said Mr. Bellingham; “for, you see, John had a pretty pronounced limp from another cause, an old injury to his left ankle; and as to complaining of pain﻿—well, he was a hardy old fellow and not much given to making complaints of any kind. But don’t let me interrupt you.”

===Chinese===
“只怕这帮不上我们多少忙，”贝林厄姆先生说；“因为您知道，约翰本就有相当明显的跛脚，另有缘故——左踝上的一处旧伤；至于抱怨疼痛嘛——唉，他是个硬朗的老家伙，向来不惯叫苦，什么苦都不叫。不过，别让我打断了您。”

===Original===
“The next discovery,” continued Thorndyke, “was made near Lee, by the police this time. They seem to have developed sudden activity in the matter, and in searching the neighborhood of West Kent they dragged out of a pond near Lee the bones of a right foot. Now, if it had been the left instead of the right we might have a clue, as I understand your brother had fractured his left ankle, and there might have been some traces of the injury on the foot itself.”

===Chinese===
“下一桩发现，”桑代克接着说，“是在李（Lee）近旁，这一回是警方的手笔。他们在这桩事上似乎忽然来了劲头，搜查肯特西部一带时，在李附近的一个池塘里捞出了一只右脚的骨头。唉，倘若捞上来的是左脚而非右脚，我们倒许有一条线索，因为据我了解，令兄折断过左踝，那只脚本身或许还留着伤的痕迹。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Mr. Bellingham. “I suppose there might. The injury was described as a Pott’s fracture.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”贝林厄姆先生说，“我想或许会有的。那处伤，据说是波特氏骨折。”

===Original===
“Exactly. Well, now, after this discovery at Lee it seems that the police set on foot a systematic search of all the ponds and small pieces of water around London, and, on the twenty-third, they found in the Cuckoo Pits in Epping Forest, not far from Woodford, the bones of a right arm (including those of the shoulder, as before), which seem to be part of the same body.”

===Chinese===
“正是。嗯，接着说。李的那桩发现之后，警方似乎着手把伦敦四郊所有的池塘和小片积水系统地搜了一遍，到二十三日，他们在埃平森林（Epping Forest）里的‘杜鹃坑’（Cuckoo Pits）——离伍德福德不远——找到了一条右臂的骨头（同先前一样，连肩部的骨头在内），看去同属一具躯体。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Mr. Bellingham, “I heard of that. Quite close to my old house. Horrible! horrible! It gave me the shudders to think of it﻿—to think that poor old John may have been waylaid and murdered when he was actually coming to see me. He may even have got into the grounds by the back gate, if it was left unfastened, and been followed in there and murdered. You remember that a scarab from his watch-chain was found there? But is it clear that this arm was the fellow of the arm that was found at Sidcup?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”贝林厄姆先生说，“这事我听说了。就在我老宅近旁。骇人！骇人啊！一想到它我就浑身发抖——想到可怜的老约翰，竟是在专程来看我的路上遭人截杀，丢了性命。他甚至可能是从后门进的园子——倘若那门没闩上——然后被人跟进去害了的。您可记得，他那表链上的一枚圣甲虫，就是在那儿找到的？只是，这条手臂是否确凿与锡德卡普找到的那条成对，这一点弄清楚了没有？”

===Original===
“It seems to agree in character and dimensions,” said Thorndyke, “and the agreement is strongly supported by a discovery made two days later.”

===Chinese===
“就性状和尺寸而论，是吻合的，”桑代克说，“而两天之后的又一桩发现，更有力地印证了这层吻合。”

===Original===
“What is that?” Mr. Bellingham demanded.

===Chinese===
“什么发现？”贝林厄姆先生追问。

===Original===
“It is the lower half of a trunk which the police dragged out of a rather deep pond on the skirts of the forest at Loughton﻿—Staple’s Pond, it is called. The bones found were the pelvis﻿—that is, the two hipbones﻿—and six vertebrae, or joints of the backbone. Having discovered these, the police dammed the stream and pumped the pond dry, but no other bones were found; which is rather odd, as there should have been a pair of ribs belonging to the upper vertebra﻿—the twelfth dorsal vertebra. It suggests some curious questions as to the method of dismemberment; but I mustn’t go into unpleasant details. The point is that the cavity of the right hip-joint showed a patch of eburnation corresponding to that on the head of the right thighbone that was found at St. Mary Cray. So there can be very little doubt that these bones are all part of the same body.”

===Chinese===
“是躯干的下半截，警方在洛顿（Loughton）一带森林边缘一个相当深的池塘里拖出来的——名叫斯特普尔池（Staple's Pond）。找到的骨头是骨盆——也就是两块髋骨——加六节椎骨，即脊梁上的六个骨节。警方有了这些发现，便拦溪筑坝，把池水抽干，却再没有捞出别的骨头；这就相当古怪了，因为连着最上那一节椎骨——第十二节胸椎——本该还有一对肋骨。这给肢解的手法提示了一些古怪的问题；但那些不愉快的细节，我不便细说。要紧的一点是：右侧髋关节的凹窝里，显出一块骨象牙化，恰与圣玛丽克雷捞到的那根右股骨头上的一块相对应。所以，这些骨头全都同属一具躯体，几乎没什么可怀疑的了。”

===Original===
“I see,” grunted Mr. Bellingham; and he added, after a moment’s thought: “Now, the question is, Are these bones the remains of my brother John? What do you say, Doctor Thorndyke?”

===Chinese===
“我明白了，”贝林厄姆先生咕哝了一声；略一思索，他又补上一句：“那么，问题就是：这些骨头，是不是我哥哥约翰的遗骸？桑代克博士，您怎么说？”

===Original===
“I say that the question cannot be answered on the facts at present known to us. It can only be said that they may be, and that some of the circumstances suggest that they are. But we can only wait for further discoveries. At any moment the police may light upon some portion of the skeleton which will settle the question definitely one way or the other.”

===Chinese===
“我的说法是：就我们目前知晓的事实而论，这个问题无法回答。只能说，它们有可能是，而且有几种情形暗示它们正是。但我们也只能静候进一步的发现。警方随时可能撞上骸骨的另外某个部位，到那时，这个问题便非此即彼，一锤定音了。”

===Original===
“I suppose,” said Mr. Bellingham, “I can’t be of any service to you in the matter of identification?”

===Chinese===
“我猜，”贝林厄姆先生说，“在辨认这件事上，我是帮不上您什么忙的吧？”

===Original===
“Indeed you can,” said Thorndyke, “and I was going to ask you to assist me. What I want you to do is this: Write down a full description of your brother, including every detail known to you, together with an account of every illness or injury from which you know him to have suffered; also the names and, if possible, the addresses of any doctors, surgeons, or dentists who may have attended him at any time. The dentists are particularly important, as their information would be invaluable if the skull belonging to these bones should be discovered.”

===Chinese===
“您恰恰帮得上，”桑代克说，“我正想请您助我一臂之力。我想请您办的是这件事：把令兄写成一份详尽的描述，凡您知道的细节一概不遗，再把他得过的每一场病、受过的每一次伤都写明白；还有，凡在任何时候为他诊治过的内科医生、外科医生或牙医，把他们的姓名——可能的话连同地址——也一并写上。牙医尤其紧要，因为倘若这些骨头所属的头颅有朝一日被发现，他们提供的信息便无价可宝。”

===Original===
Mr. Bellingham shuddered.

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆先生打了个寒噤。

===Original===
“It’s a shocking idea,” he said, “but, of course you are right. You must have the facts if you are to form an opinion. I will write out what you want and send it to you without delay. And now, for God’s sake, let us throw off this nightmare, for a little while, at least! What is there, Ruth, among Doctor Barnard’s music that you can manage?”

===Chinese===
“这念头真叫人心惊，”他说，“不过，您当然是对的。要形成见解，必先占有事实。您要的东西，我一经写出，立刻给您送去。而现在，看在上帝份上，让我们把这噩梦甩脱片刻，哪怕一小会儿也好！露丝，巴纳德博士的乐谱里，有哪些是你弹得来的？”

===Original===
Barnard’s collection in general inclined to the severely classical, but we disinterred from the heap a few lighter works of an old-fashioned kind, including a volume of Mendelssohn’s *Lieder ohne Worte*, and with one of these Miss Bellingham made trial of her skill, playing it with excellent taste and quite adequate execution. That, at least, was her father’s verdict; for, as to me, I found it the perfection of happiness merely to sit and look at her﻿—a state of mind that would have been in no wise disturbed even by “Silvery Waves” or “The Maiden’s Prayer.”

===Chinese===
巴纳德的收藏大体偏向严正的古典一路，但我们从那堆谱册里掘出了几件较为轻快的老式曲子，其中有一册门德尔松的《无词歌》（*Lieder ohne Worte*）。贝林厄姆小姐便取其中一首试了试身手，弹得趣味高雅，功夫也绰绰有余——至少，她父亲是这样裁断的；至于我，只消坐在那里望着她，便已是圆满的极乐——这种心境，纵然奏响的是《银色波浪》或《少女的祈祷》，也断不会受到半点搅扰。

===Original===
Thus, with simple, homely music, and conversation always cheerful and sometimes brilliant, slipped away one of the pleasantest evenings of my life, and slipped away all too soon. St. Dunstan’s clock was the fly in the ointment, for it boomed out intrusively the hour of eleven just as my guests were beginning thoroughly to appreciate one another, and thereby carried the sun (with a minor paternal satellite) out of the firmament of my heaven. For I had, in my professional capacity, given strict injunctions that Mr. Bellingham should on no account sit up late; and now, in my social capacity, I had smilingly to hear “the doctor’s orders” quoted. It was a scurvy return for all my care.

===Chinese===
就这样，伴着简单家常的音乐，和始终欢畅、时而妙语如珠的谈话，我平生最愉快的夜晚之一悄然逝去，而且逝去得太快。唯一的煞风景之物，是圣邓斯坦（St. Dunstan's）的时钟：正当我的客人们开始彼此深相欣赏之际，它冒失地轰鸣报出十一点，就此把太阳（连同一颗小小的父辈卫星）撵出了我的天宇。因为，我以行医的身份，曾严令贝林厄姆先生千万不得晚睡；而此刻，以社交的身份，我只好笑吟吟地听人引述“医嘱”。这便是我一片照料应得的刻薄回报。

===Original===
When Mr. and Miss Bellingham departed, Thorndyke and Jervis would have gone too; but noting my bereaved condition, and being withal compassionate and tender of heart, they were persuaded to stay awhile and bear me company in a consolatory pipe.

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆先生父女告辞之后，桑代克和杰维斯本也要走；但见我这副如丧考妣的凄凉光景，又兼两人心地慈悲柔软，经我一劝，便留了下来，陪我抽一斗宽慰的烟。

## XI The Evidence Reviewed / 十一 证据之检讨
===Original===
“So the game has opened,” observed Thorndyke, as he struck a match. “The play has begun with a cautious lead off by the other side. Very cautious and not very confident.”

===Chinese===
“这么一来，牌局开盘了，”桑代克一面划火柴，一面说道。“开场是对方一记谨慎的领出。谨慎得很，却不大有信心。”
===Original===
“Why do you say ‘not very confident’?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“您凭什么说‘不大有信心’呢？”我问道。
===Original===
“Well, it is evident that Hurst﻿—and, I fancy, Jellicoe too﻿—is anxious to buy off Bellingham’s opposition, and at a pretty long price, under the circumstances. And when we consider how very little Bellingham has to offer against the presumption of his brother’s death, it looks as if Hurst hadn’t much to say on his side.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，很显然，赫斯特——我想杰利科也一样——急于收买贝林厄姆，要他不再作对，而且就眼下的情形而论，这价钱出得也真算阔绰。再想想，要驳倒对他哥哥死亡的推定，贝林厄姆手里能拿出的东西实在少得可怜；如此看来，赫斯特自己那一边，怕也没有多少话好讲。”
===Original===
“No,” said Jervis, “he can’t hold many trumps or he wouldn’t be willing to pay four hundred a year for his opponent’s chance; and that is just as well, for it seems to me that our own hand is a pretty poor one.”

===Chinese===
“可不是，”杰维斯说，“他手里的王牌定然不多，不然他也不会肯出每年四百英镑，去买对手的那份机会；而这倒也好，因为依我看，我们自己这手牌也实在寒酸。”
===Original===
“We must look through our hand and see what we do hold,” said Thorndyke. “Our trump card at present﻿—a rather small one, I’m afraid﻿—is the obvious intention of the testator that the bulk of the property should go to his brother.”

===Chinese===
“我们得把手里的牌摊开来细看一遍，看看究竟握着些什么，”桑代克说。“我们眼下的王牌——只怕是张小牌——便是立遗嘱人那显而易见的用意：家产的大头，应当归他的弟弟。”
===Original===
“I suppose you will begin your inquiries now?” I said.

===Chinese===
“我想，您这就要着手查访了吧？”我说。
===Original===
“We began them some time ago﻿—the day after you brought us the will, in fact. Jervis has been through the registers and has ascertained that no interment under the name of John Bellingham has taken place since the disappearance; which was just what we expected. He has also discovered that some other person has been making similar inquiries; which, again, is what we expected.”

===Chinese===
“我们早已着手了——事实上，就在你把遗嘱送来的第二天。杰维斯把登记册查了个遍，查明自失踪以来，不曾有过以约翰·贝林厄姆之名下葬的葬事；这正是我们预料中的。他还发现，另有旁人也在打听同样的问题；这，同样在我们意料之中。”
===Original===
“And your own investigations?”

===Chinese===
“那您自己的调查呢？”
===Original===
“Have given negative results for the most part. I found Doctor Norbury, at the British Museum, very friendly and helpful; so friendly, in fact, that I am thinking whether I may not be able to enlist his help in certain private researches of my own, with reference to the change effected by time in the physical properties of certain substances.”

===Chinese===
“大半一无所获。大英博物馆的诺伯里博士，我发现十分友善，也肯帮忙；友善到这般地步，以至我在盘算，我那几项私人的研究——关于时间对某些物质的物理性质所造成的变化——是否也能拉他来助一臂之力。”
===Original===
“Oh; you haven’t told me about that,” said Jervis.

===Chinese===
“哦，这事你还没跟我讲过呢，”杰维斯说。
===Original===
“No; I haven’t really commenced to plan my experiments yet, and they will probably lead to nothing when I do. It occurred to me that, possibly, in the course of time, certain molecular changes might take place in substances such as wood, bone, pottery, stucco, and other common materials, and that these changes might alter their power of conducting or transmitting molecular vibrations. Now, if this should turn out to be the case, it would be a fact of considerable importance, medico-legally and otherwise; for it would be possible to determine approximately the age of any object of known composition by testing its reactions to electricity, heat, light and other molecular vibrations. I thought of seeking Doctor Norbury’s assistance because he can furnish me with materials for experiment of such great age that the reactions, if any, should be extremely easy to demonstrate. But to return to our case. I learned from him that John Bellingham had certain friends in Paris﻿—collectors and museum officials﻿—whom he was in the habit of visiting for the purpose of study and exchange of specimens. I have made inquiries of all these, and none of them had seen him during his last visit. In fact, I have not yet discovered anyone who had seen Bellingham in Paris on this occasion. So his visit there remains a mystery for the present.”

===Chinese===
“是的；我实际上连实验都还没有着手筹划，真筹划起来，多半也是一场空。我想到的是：天长日久，某些物质——诸如木头、骨头、陶器、灰泥以及其他寻常材料——内部或许会发生某种分子变化，而这些变化或许会改变它们传导或传递分子振动的性能。倘若事实果真如此，那便是一个在法医学上以及其他方面都相当重要的事实；因为，只要测知一件成分已知的物件对电、热、光及其他分子振动的反应，便可以大致断定它的年岁。我之所以想求诺伯里博士帮忙，是因为他能供给年代极其久远的实验材料，以致那些反应——倘若当真存在——必定极容易显现出来。不过，还是回到我们的案子上来吧。我从他那里得知，约翰·贝林厄姆在巴黎有几位朋友——都是些收藏家和博物馆的人员——他惯于拜访他们，借以研治学问、交换标本。这些人我一一查问过了，上一回他访巴黎期间，没有一个见过他。事实上，那一趟在巴黎见过贝林厄姆的人，我至今一个也不曾发现。所以，他的巴黎之行，眼下仍是一个谜。”
===Original===
“It doesn’t seem to be of much importance, since he undoubtedly came back,” I remarked; but to this Thorndyke demurred.

===Chinese===
“这好像算不得什么要紧事，反正他后来确凿是回来了的，”我议论道；桑代克对此却不以为然。
===Original===
“It is impossible to estimate the importance of the unknown,” said he.

===Chinese===
“未知之事，其轻重无从估量，”他说。
===Original===
“Well, how does the matter stand,” asked Jervis, “on the evidence that we have? John Bellingham disappeared on a certain date. Is there anything to show what was the manner of his disappearance?”

===Chinese===
“那么，就我们掌握的证据而论，”杰维斯问道，“事情眼下究竟是个什么局面？约翰·贝林厄姆在某个日子失踪了。可有什么迹象表明，他究竟是以何种方式失踪的么？”
===Original===
“The facts in our possession,” said Thorndyke, “which are mainly those set forth in the newspaper report, suggest several alternative possibilities; and in view of the coming inquiry﻿—for they will, no doubt, have to be gone into in Court, to some extent﻿—it may be worth while to consider them. There are five conceivable hypotheses”﻿—here Thorndyke checked them on his fingers as he proceeded﻿—“First, he may still be alive. Second, he may have died and been buried without identification. Third, he may have been murdered by some unknown person. Fourth, he may have been murdered by Hurst and his body concealed. Fifth, he may have been murdered by his brother. Let us examine these possibilities seriatim.

===Chinese===
“我们所掌握的事实，”桑代克说，“主要就是报上报道的那些；它们提示出几种可以并立的可能，而鉴于调查即将来临——因为这些事实无疑多少要在法庭上摊开来过一遍——把它们推敲一番，或许不无价值。可以设想的假说有五种”——说到这里，桑代克扳着手指，一一数下去——“第一，他可能还活着。第二，他可能已经死去，未辨身份便被下了葬。第三，他可能被某个不明身份的人谋害。第四，他可能被赫斯特谋害，尸身遭了藏匿。第五，他可能被他的弟弟谋害。让我们把这几种可能性逐条（seriatim）加以检视。
===Original===
“First, he may still be alive. If he is, he must either have disappeared voluntarily, have lost his memory suddenly and not been identified, or have been imprisoned﻿—on a false charge or otherwise. Let us take the first case﻿—that of voluntary disappearance. Obviously, its improbability is extreme.”

===Chinese===
“第一，他可能还活着。倘若如此，那么他要么是自愿出走，要么是突然失忆而未被认出，要么就是身陷囹圄——或因诬告，或因别的什么。且说第一种情形——自愿出走。显然，其可能性微乎其微，几近于无。”
===Original===
“Jellicoe doesn’t think so,” said I. “He thinks it quite on the cards that John Bellingham is alive. He says that it is not a very unusual thing for a man to disappear for a time.”

===Chinese===
“杰利科可不这么想，”我说。“依他看，约翰·贝林厄姆还活着，这事大有可能。他说，一个人销声匿迹个一段时日，并不是多么稀罕的事。”
===Original===
“Then why is he applying for a presumption of death?”

===Chinese===
“那他为什么还要申请推定死亡呢？”
===Original===
“Just what I asked him. He says that it is the correct thing to do; that the entire responsibility rests on the Court.”

===Chinese===
“我正是这么问他的。他说，这是合乎规矩的做法；全部责任，都在法庭身上。”
===Original===
“That is all nonsense,” said Thorndyke. “Jellicoe is the trustee for his absent client, and, if he thinks that client is alive, it is his duty to keep the estate intact; and he knows that perfectly well. We may take it that Jellicoe is of the same opinion as I am: that John Bellingham is dead.”

===Chinese===
“这全是胡说，”桑代克说。“杰利科是他那位缺席委托人的受托人，倘若他认为那位委托人还活着，他的本分便是把产业保全完好；这个道理，他一清二楚。我们不妨认定，杰利科的见解与我相同：约翰·贝林厄姆已经死了。”
===Original===
“Still,” I urged, “men do disappear from time to time, and turn up again after years of absence.”

===Chinese===
“话虽如此，”我仍力争，“人也确实时有失踪，过了许多年又重新露面的。”
===Original===
“Yes, but for a definite reason. Either they are irresponsible vagabonds who take this way of shuffling off their responsibilities, or they are men who have been caught in a net of distasteful circumstances. For instance, a civil servant or a solicitor or a tradesman finds himself bound for life to a locality and an occupation of intolerable monotony. Perhaps he has an ill-tempered wife, who after the amiable fashion of a certain type of woman, thinking that her husband is pinned down without a chance of escape, gives a free rein to her temper. The man puts up with it for years, but at last it becomes unbearable. Then he suddenly disappears; and small blame to him. But this was not Bellingham’s case. He was a wealthy bachelor with an engrossing interest in life, free to go whither he would and to do whatsoever he wished. Why should he disappear? The thing is incredible.

===Chinese===
“是的，但总有确定的缘故。他们要么是些没心没肺的浪子，借这条路把身上的责任一甩了之；要么是些落进不堪境遇之网里的人。比如，一个文官、一个事务律师，或者一个买卖人，发觉自己一辈子拴死在一个地方、一门单调得无法忍受的营生上。或许他家里还有个坏脾气的老婆，按照某一类女人和和气气的做派，心想丈夫已被钉死，再无逃脱的指望，便由着性子恣意发作。这男人忍了一年又一年，终于忍无可忍。于是他突然失踪了；这也实在怪不得他。但贝林厄姆的情形并非如此。他是个富有的单身汉，平生有一门叫他痴迷的学问，爱去哪儿便去哪儿，爱干什么便干什么。他为什么要失踪？这种事，叫人无法相信。
===Original===
“As to his having lost his memory and remained unidentified, that, also, is incredible in the case of a man who had visiting-cards and letters in his pocket, whose linen was marked, and who was being inquired for everywhere by the police. As to his being in prison, we may dismiss that possibility, inasmuch as a prisoner, both before and after conviction, would have full opportunity of communicating with his friends.

===Chinese===
“至于说他失了记忆而始终无人认出，就他这样一个口袋里装着名片和信件、衬衣上绣着名号、又正被警察四处寻访的人而论，这同样叫人无法相信。至于说他身陷囹圄，这个可能性也不妨勾销，因为一个囚犯，无论定罪之前还是之后，都不乏与亲友通音问的机会。
===Original===
“The second possibility, that he may have died suddenly and been buried without identification, is highly improbable; but, as it is conceivable that the body might have been robbed and the means of identification thus lost, it remains as a possibility that has to be considered, remote as it is.

===Chinese===
“第二种可能——他可能猝然死去，未经辨认便被下葬——极不可能；但是，尸身也可能遭人劫掠，辨认的凭据就此失落，这倒是可以想见的；因此它作为一种可能，尽管渺茫，仍须列入考虑。
===Original===
“The third hypothesis, that he may have been murdered by some unknown person, is, under the circumstances, not wildly improbable; but, as the police were on the lookout and a detailed description of the missing man’s person was published in the papers, it would involve the complete concealment of the body. But this would exclude the most probable form of crime﻿—the casual robbery with violence. It is therefore possible, but highly improbable.

===Chinese===
“第三种假说——他可能被某个不明身份的人谋害——就眼下情形而论，并非全然无稽；可是，警察一直在留神查访，报上又登过失踪者相貌的详细描述，那就非得把尸身彻底藏匿不可。可这样一来，便排除掉了最有可能的那一类罪案——偶发的暴力劫财。所以它有可能，却极无可能。
===Original===
“The fourth hypothesis is that Bellingham was murdered by Hurst. Now the one fact which militates against this view is that Hurst apparently had no motive for committing the murder. We are assured by Jellicoe that no one but himself knew the contents of the will, and if this is so﻿—but mind, we have no evidence that it is so﻿—Hurst would have no reason to suppose that he had anything material to gain by his cousin’s death. Otherwise the hypothesis presents no inherent improbabilities. The man was last seen alive at Hurst’s house. He was seen to enter it and he was never seen to leave it﻿—we are still taking the facts as stated in the newspapers, remember﻿—and it now appears that he stands to benefit enormously by that man’s death.”

===Chinese===
“第四种假说：贝林厄姆是被赫斯特谋害的。如今同这个看法相抵触的一个事实是：赫斯特显然没有杀人的动机。杰利科向我们保证，遗嘱的内容除他本人外无人知晓；倘若真是如此——可是记住，究竟是否如此，我们并无证据——那么赫斯特便没有理由认为，他这位表亲一死，他能捞到什么实打实的好处。除此之外，这一假说本身并没有半点说不通的地方。那个人最后一次被人见到活着，是在赫斯特家里。有人看见他进去，却从没有人看见他出来——记住，我们如今仍是照报纸上的说法采信事实——而现在看来，那人一死，赫斯特能到手的好处大得惊人。”
===Original===
“But,” I objected, “you are forgetting that, directly the man was missed Hurst and the servants together searched the entire house.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我提出异议，“您忘了，那人刚一失踪，赫斯特就和仆人们一起，把整座宅子搜了个遍。”
===Original===
“Yes. What did they search for?”

===Chinese===
“是的。他们找的是什么？”
===Original===
“Why, for Mr. Bellingham, of course.”

===Chinese===
“咦，当然是找贝林厄姆先生啊。”
===Original===
“Exactly; for Mr. Bellingham. That is, for a living man. Now how do you search a house for a living man? You look in all the rooms. When you look in a room if he is there, you see him; if you do not see him, you assume that he is not there. You don’t look under the sofa or behind the piano, you don’t pull out large drawers or open cupboards. You just look into the rooms. That is what these people seem to have done. And they did not see Mr. Bellingham. Mr. Bellingham’s corpse might have been stowed away out of sight in any one of the rooms that they looked into.”

===Chinese===
“正是；找贝林厄姆先生。也就是说，找一个活人。那么，在宅子里找一个活人，是怎么个找法？你往各个房间里瞧。往一间屋里瞧的时候，他若在里面，你就看得见他；你看不见他，便认定他不在里面。你不会去看沙发底下、钢琴后头，不会去拖出大抽屉，也不会去打开立柜。你只是往各个房间里瞧一瞧。这些人大约就是这么做的。而他们没有看见贝林厄姆先生。贝林厄姆先生的尸首，说不定当时就收在什么看不见的地方，藏在他们瞧过的随便哪一间屋里。”
===Original===
“That is a grim thought,” said Jervis; “but it is perfectly true. There is no evidence that the man was not lying dead in the house at the very time of the search.”

===Chinese===
“这念头真是阴森，”杰维斯说；“然而分毫不差。丝毫没有证据表明，搜屋的那一刻，那个人不是一具躺在宅子里的死尸。”
===Original===
“But even so,” said I, “there was the body to be disposed of somehow. Now how could he possibly have got rid of the body without being observed?”

===Chinese===
“可即便这样，”我说，“尸首总得想法子处理掉呀。他究竟怎样才能不被人察觉地把尸首打发掉呢？”
===Original===
“Ah!” said Thorndyke, “now we are touching on a point of crucial importance. If anyone should ever write a treatise on the art of murder﻿—not an exhibition of literary fireworks like De Quincey’s, but a genuine working treatise﻿—he might leave all other technical details to take care of themselves if he could describe some really practicable plan for disposing of the body. That is, and always has been, the great stumbling-block to the murderer: to get rid of the body. The human body,” he continued, thoughtfully regarding his pipe, just as, in the days of my pupilage, he was wont to regard the blackboard chalk, “is a very remarkable object. It presents a combination of properties, that make it singularly difficult to conceal permanently. It is bulky and of an awkward shape, it is heavy, it is completely incombustible, it is chemically unstable, and its decomposition yields great volumes of highly odorous gases, and it nevertheless contains identifiable structures of the highest degree of permanence. It is extremely difficult to preserve unchanged, and it is still more difficult completely to destroy. The essential permanence of the human body is well shown in the classical case of Eugene Aram; but a still more striking instance is that of Sekenen-Ra the Third, one of the last kings of the seventeenth Egyptian dynasty. Here, after a lapse of four thousand years, it has been possible to determine not only the cause of death and the manner of its occurrence, but the way in which the king fell, the nature of the weapon with which the fatal wound was inflicted, and even the position of the assailant. And the permanence of the body under other conditions is admirably shown in the case of Doctor Parkman, of Boston, U.S.A., in which identification was actually effected by means of remains collected from the ashes of a furnace.”

===Chinese===
“啊！”桑代克说，“这就触及一个至关紧要的关节了。倘若有人要写一部论谋杀技艺的专论——不是德·昆西（De Quincey）那种焰火纷飞的文学表演，而是一部真正切实可用的专著——那么，只要他能写出一套真正行得通的毁尸方案，其余的技术细节尽可以听其自便。这一点，向来正是凶手头一块、也是最大的一块绊脚石：怎样脱手这具尸首。”他接着说，一面若有所思地端详着他的烟斗，正如我做学生的年月里，他惯于端详黑板上的粉笔一般，“人体是一件非常独特的物件。它集种种性质于一身，而这些性质使它格外难以永久藏匿。它体积庞大，形状别扭，分量沉重，完全不可焚烧，化学性质又不稳定，腐烂起来要产生大量气味浓烈的气体，然而它又含有一些恒久性极高、可供辨认的结构。要使它原封不动地保全，难乎其难；要把它彻头彻尾地毁掉，更是难上加难。人体这种本质上的恒久，在尤金·艾拉姆（Eugene Aram）那桩经典案件中已表现得一清二楚；而更为触目的一例，是塞格年末拉三世（Sekenen-Ra the Third），埃及第十七王朝末年诸王之一。在他身上，时隔四千年，人们不仅能够断定死因和死亡发生时的情形，还能查明国王倒下的姿态、施加致命伤的那件凶器的性质，甚至凶手当时所处的位置。至于人体在别的条件下如何恒久，美国波士顿的帕克曼医生（Doctor Parkman）一案展示得再精采不过：在那桩案子里，凭从炉膛灰烬中收集起来的残骸，居然当真完成了身份的认定。”
===Original===
“Then we may take it,” said Jervis, “that the world has not yet seen the last of John Bellingham.”

===Chinese===
“那么我们可以认定，”杰维斯说，“约翰·贝林厄姆这出戏，世上还没有看到最后一幕呢。”
===Original===
“I think we may regard that as almost a certainty,” replied Thorndyke. “The only question﻿—and a very important one﻿—is as to when the reappearance may take place. It may be tomorrow or it may be centuries hence, when all the issues involved have been forgotten.”

===Chinese===
“我想，这一点我们几乎可以视作定局，”桑代克答道。“唯一的问题——而且是个非常要紧的问题——是重新的露面可能在何时发生。可能在明天，也可能在几百年之后，到那时，牵涉其中的种种纠葛，早已尽归遗忘。”
===Original===
“Assuming,” said I, “for the sake of argument, that Hurst did murder him and that the body was concealed in the study at the time the search was made. How could it have been disposed of? If you had been in Hurst’s place, how would you have gone to work?”

===Chinese===
“假设——只是为了便于争论——谋害他的确实是赫斯特，而且搜屋的时候，尸身就藏在书房里。它会怎样被处理掉呢？倘若您处在赫斯特的地位，会怎么着手？”
===Original===
Thorndyke smiled at the bluntness of my question.

===Chinese===
我这问题问得单刀直入，桑代克不禁莞尔。
===Original===
“You are asking me for an incriminating statement,” said he, “delivered in the presence of a witness too. But, as a matter of fact, there is no use in speculating *a priori*; we should have to reconstruct a purely imaginary situation, the circumstances of which are unknown to us, and we should almost certainly reconstruct it wrong. What we may fairly assume is that no reasonable person, no matter how immoral, would find himself in the position that you suggest. Murder is usually a crime of impulse, and the murderer a person of feeble self-control. Such persons are most unlikely to make elaborate and ingenious arrangements for the disposal of the bodies of their victims. Even the cold-blooded perpetrators of the most carefully planned murders appear as I have said, to break down at this point. The almost insuperable difficulty of getting rid of the human body is not appreciated until the murderer suddenly finds himself face to face with it.

===Chinese===
“你这可是要我招供一篇自证其罪的陈述，”他说，“而且还是当着一位证人的面。不过，说实在的，作 *a priori*（先验式）的凭空推想毫无用处；那样我们就得重建一个纯属虚构的情境，其种种实情我们又全然不知，而重建出来的几乎必然是错的。我们大可这样假定：任何一个头脑清醒的人，无论多么不讲道德，都不会落到你所设想的那种境地。谋杀通常是一桩冲动之罪，凶手多半又是自制力薄弱之辈。这类人极不可能为处置被害者的尸身，预先做出周密而巧妙的安排。即便是精心策划的冷血凶案，其凶手——一如我方才所说——似乎也都在这一关上败下阵来。脱手人体这桩几乎无法克服的难处，不到凶手猛然发觉自己正与它面面相对，是掂不出它的分量的。
===Original===
“In the case you are suggesting, the choice would seem to lie between burial on the premises or dismemberment and dispersal of the fragments; and either method would be pretty certain to lead to discovery.”

===Chinese===
“在你设想的那个情形里，选择似乎不外乎就地埋葬，或者肢解之后将残块四散抛弃；而无论哪一种法子，几乎都注定要引向败露。”
===Original===
“As illustrated by the remains of which you were speaking to Mr. Bellingham,” Jervis remarked.

===Chinese===
“正如那些残骸所昭示的——您方才还同贝林厄姆先生谈起它们呢，”杰维斯说道。
===Original===
“Exactly,” Thorndyke answered, “though we could hardly imagine a reasonably intelligent criminal adopting a watercress-bed as a hiding place.

===Chinese===
“正是，”桑代克答道，“虽说一个头脑还过得去的罪犯，我们很难想象他会挑一片水田芥田来做藏匿之所。
===Original===
“No. That was certainly an error of judgment. By the way, I thought it best to say nothing while you were talking to Bellingham, but I noticed that, in discussing the possibility of those being the bones of his brother, you made no comment on the absence of the third finger of the left hand. I am sure you didn’t overlook it, but isn’t it a point of some importance?”

===Chinese===
“不错。那一步显然是失于判断。对了，您同贝林厄姆谈话的时候，我想还是一言不发为好；不过我注意到，在讨论那些骨头是否他哥哥遗骸的可能性时，对左手第三指的缺失，您未置一词。我确信您不是看漏了；可这难道不是颇有几分紧要的一点么？”
===Original===
“As to identification? Under the present circumstances, I think not. If there were a man missing who had lost that finger it would, of course, be an important fact. But I have not heard of any such man. Or, again, if there were any evidence that the finger had been removed before death, it would be highly important. But there is no such evidence. It may have been cut off after death, and that is where the real significance of its absence lies.”

===Chinese===
“就辨认而论么？在眼下这种情形下，我看算不上。倘若正好有一名失踪的男子缺了那根手指，那自然是一桩重要的事实。可这样的人，我不曾听说。又或者，倘若有什么证据表明那根手指是生前被切去的，那也会极为紧要。可这样的证据并不存在。它可能是死后被切掉的，而这，正是它的缺失真正的意味所在。”
===Original===
“I don’t see quite what you mean,” said Jervis.

===Chinese===
“我不大明白您的意思，”杰维斯说。
===Original===
“I mean that, if there is no report of any missing man who had lost that particular finger, the probability is that the finger was removed after death. And then arises the interesting question of motive. Why should it have been removed? It could hardly have become detached accidentally. What do you suggest?”

===Chinese===
“我的意思是：倘若没有任何关于失踪者的报告提到有谁缺了那根手指，那么多半，手指是死后才被取下的。于是便引出动机这个耐人寻味的问题。它为什么被取下？总不至于是意外脱落的。您有什么猜想？”
===Original===
“Well,” said Jervis, “it might have been a peculiar finger; a finger, for instance, with some characteristic deformity such as an ankylosed joint, which would be easy to identify.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，”杰维斯说，“说不定那是一根特别的手指；比方说，带着某种独具一格的畸形，诸如关节强直（ankylosed joint）之类，一认便认得出来。”
===Original===
“Yes; but that explanation introduces the same difficulty. No person with a deformed or ankylosed finger has been reported as missing.”

===Chinese===
“不错；可这个解释带来了同样的难题。并没有任何手指畸形或强直的男子被报失踪。”
===Original===
Jervis puckered up his brows, and looked at me.

===Chinese===
杰维斯皱起了眉头，望着我。
===Original===
“I’m hanged if I see any other explanation,” he said. “Do you, Berkeley?”

===Chinese===
“我算是看不出还有什么别的解释了，”他说。“伯克利，你看得出么？”
===Original===
I shook my head.

===Chinese===
我摇了摇头。
===Original===
“Don’t forget which finger it is that is missing,” said Thorndyke. “The third finger of the left hand.”

===Chinese===
“别忘了缺的是哪一根手指，”桑代克说。“左手的第三指。”
===Original===
“Oh, I see!” said Jervis. “The ring-finger. You mean that it may have been removed for the sake of a ring that wouldn’t come off.”

===Chinese===
“哦，我明白了！”杰维斯说。“无名指。您的意思是，它可能是为了一枚褪不下来的戒指，才被人取掉的。”
===Original===
“Yes. It would not be the first instance of the kind. Fingers have been severed from dead hands﻿—and even from living ones﻿—for the sake of rings that were too tight to be drawn off. And the fact that it is the left hand supports the suggestion; for a ring that was inconveniently tight would be worn by preference on the left hand, as that is usually slightly smaller than the right. What is the matter, Berkeley?”

===Chinese===
“是的。这类事可不是头一遭。为了紧得褪不下来的戒指，手指曾从死人手上剁下——甚至从活人手上。而它偏偏是左手，这一层又印证了那个猜想；因为一枚紧得碍事的戒指，人们宁可戴在左手，左手通常比右手略小些。怎么了，伯克利？”
===Original===
A sudden light had burst upon me, and I suppose my countenance betrayed the fact.

===Chinese===
一线灵光蓦地在我心头炸亮，我猜想，我的面色已把这一层招认了出来。
===Original===
“I am a confounded fool!” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“我是个十足的大傻瓜！”我失声道。
===Original===
“Oh, don’t say that,” said Jervis. “Give your friends a chance.”

===Chinese===
“哦，别这么说，”杰维斯说。“总得给朋友们留个机会嘛。”
===Original===
“I ought to have seen this long ago and told you about it. John Bellingham did wear a ring, and it was so tight that, when once he had got it on, he could never get it off again.”

===Chinese===
“这事我早就该想到，也早就该告诉您了。约翰·贝林厄姆确实戴着一枚戒指，而且紧得很，一旦套了上去，就再也褪不下来了。”
===Original===
“Do you happen to know on which hand he wore it?” Thorndyke asked.

===Chinese===
“你可碰巧知道他戴在哪只手上？”桑代克问。
===Original===
“Yes. It was on the left hand; because Miss Bellingham, who told me about it, said that he would never have been able to get the ring on at all but for the fact that his left hand was slightly smaller than his right.”

===Chinese===
“知道。戴在左手；因为跟我说起这事的是贝林厄姆小姐，她讲，若不是他左手比右手略小些，那枚戒指根本就套不上去。”
===Original===
“There it is, then,” said Thorndyke. “With this new fact in our possession, the absence of the finger furnishes the starting-point of some very curious speculations.”

===Chinese===
“这就对上了，”桑代克说。“手里握有这个新事实，那根手指的缺失，便成了几条非常离奇的推想的起点。”
===Original===
“As, for instance,” said Jervis.

===Chinese===
“比如说呢？”杰维斯道。
===Original===
“Ah, under the circumstances, I must leave you to pursue those speculations independently. I am now acting for Mr. Bellingham.”

===Chinese===
“啊，眼下这情形，我只好请你们自己去独立追索那些推想了。我如今代理的是贝林厄姆先生。”
===Original===
Jervis grinned and was silent for awhile, refilling his pipe thoughtfully; but when he had got it alight he resumed.

===Chinese===
杰维斯咧嘴笑了笑，一时默然，只顾若有所思地给烟斗续丝；待到点着了，他又重新开口。
===Original===
“To return to the question of the disappearance; you don’t consider it highly improbable that Bellingham might have been murdered by Hurst?”

===Chinese===
“回到失踪的问题上来；您并不认为‘贝林厄姆可能死在赫斯特手上’极不可信，是么？”
===Original===
“Oh, don’t imagine I am making an accusation. I am considering the various probabilities merely in the abstract. The same reasoning applies to the Bellinghams. As to whether any of them did commit the murder, that is a question of personal character. I certainly do not suspect the Bellinghams after having seen them, and with regard to Hurst, I know nothing, or at least very little, to his disadvantage.”

===Chinese===
“哦，别以为我在提出指控。我不过是在纯然抽象地考量种种可能性。同样的道理，也适用于贝林厄姆家父女。至于他们当中究竟有谁当真下了手，那是个个人品性的问题。见过他们父女之后，我确实不疑心贝林厄姆家；至于赫斯特，有损于他的事，我一无所知——或者至多，所知极少。”
===Original===
“Do you know anything?” asked Jervis.

===Chinese===
“那就是知道一点了？”杰维斯问。
===Original===
“Well,” Thorndyke said, with some hesitation, “it seems a thought unkind to rake up the little details of a man’s past, and yet it has to be done. I have, of course, made the usual routine inquiries concerning the parties to this affair, and this is what they have brought to light:

===Chinese===
“唔，”桑代克带着几分迟疑说，“把一个人过往的细枝末节翻出来，似乎有点不厚道，然而又不得不翻。这桩案子的有关人等，我自然照例做过了常规查访，查访所得是这样：
===Original===
“Hurst, as you know, is a stockbroker﻿—a man of good position and reputation; but, about ten years ago, he seems to have committed an indiscretion, to put it mildly, which nearly got him into rather serious difficulties. He appears to have speculated rather heavily and considerably beyond his means, for when a sudden spasm of the markets upset his calculations, it turned out that he had been employing his clients’ capital and securities. For a time it looked as if there was going to be serious trouble; then, quite unexpectedly, he managed to raise the necessary amount in some way and settle all claims. Whence he got the money has never been discovered to this day, which is a curious circumstance, seeing that the deficiency was rather over five thousand pounds; but the important fact is that he did get it and that he paid up all that he owed. So that he was only a potential defaulter, so to speak; and discreditable as the affair undoubtedly was, it does not seem to have any direct bearing on this present case.”

===Chinese===
“赫斯特，你们知道，是个证券经纪人——有身份、有声誉的人；可是大约十年前，他似乎干下了一桩——说得客气些——失检的行为，险些叫他陷入相当严重的困境。他似乎投机下注颇重，远远超出了自己的财力；因为，待到市场一阵猝然的痉挛打乱了他的全盘计算，才发现他动用的原来是客户们的资金和证券。有一阵子，眼看要出大乱子；随后，全然出人意料地，他不知怎的筹足了所需的款项，把各项索偿一概付清。那笔钱他究竟从何而来，至今无人查知；鉴于亏空略逾五千英镑，这便是一桩蹊跷事；但要紧的事实是：钱他终究弄到了，而且把欠下的一概还清。所以说，他只能算一个未遂的亏空者；这桩事固然无疑很不光彩，却似乎与眼下这桩案子没有直接的干系。”
===Original===
“No,” Jervis agreed, “though it makes one consider his position with more attention than one would otherwise.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”杰维斯附和道，“不过这样一来，人们考察他的地位时，便不得不多留一分心，比原本要留的多上一分。”
===Original===
“Undoubtedly,” said Thorndyke. “A reckless gambler is a man whose conduct cannot be relied on. He is subject to vicissitudes of fortune which may force him into other kinds of wrongdoing. Many an embezzlement has been preceded by an unlucky plunge on the turf.”

===Chinese===
“毫无疑问，”桑代克说。“一个不顾死活的赌徒，是行为无从信赖的人。他受着命运浮沉的摆布，而那些浮沉可能迫使他犯下别样的罪愆。多少挪用公款的案子，前头都先有一场赛马场上的倒霉豪赌。”
===Original===
“Assuming the responsibility for this disappearance to lie between Hurst and﻿—and the Bellinghams,” said I, with an uncomfortable gulp as I mentioned the names of my friends, “to which side does the balance of probability incline?”

===Chinese===
“倘若这桩失踪案的责任，落在了赫斯特和——和贝林厄姆父女之间，”我说道，提到我朋友们的大名时，不自在地咽了一口唾沫，“那么，可能性的天平倾向哪一边？”
===Original===
“To the side of Hurst, I should say, without doubt,” replied Thorndyke. “The case stands thus﻿—on the facts presented to us: Hurst appears to have had no motive for killing the deceased (as we will call him); but the man was seen to enter the house, was never seen to leave it, and was never again seen alive. Bellingham, on the other hand, had a motive, as he had believed himself to be the principal beneficiary under the will. But the deceased was not seen at his house, and there is no evidence that he went to the house or to the neighborhood, excepting the scarab that was found there. But the evidence of the scarab is vitiated by the fact that Hurst was present when it was picked up, and that it was found on a spot over which Hurst had passed only a few minutes previously. Until Hurst is cleared, it seems to me that the presence of the scarab proves nothing against the Bellinghams.”

===Chinese===
“依我说，毫无疑问，倾向赫斯特那一边，”桑代克答道。“案情是这样——就摆在我们面前的种种事实而论：赫斯特看来没有杀害死者（我们姑且如此称呼他）的动机；可是，那人被人看见走进了那宅子，从未见有人看见他出来，此后也再没有人见过活着的他。贝林厄姆呢，换个说法，是有动机的，因为他一向认为自己是遗嘱之下的主要受益人。但是，没有任何人在贝林厄姆宅中见过死者，也没有任何证据表明他到过那宅子或左近一带——除了在那里寻见的那枚圣甲虫。然而，圣甲虫这个证据是作废了的：拾得它的时候，赫斯特在场，而它被发现的地方，几分钟之前赫斯特刚刚走过。在赫斯特洗清自己之前，依我看，圣甲虫之所在，证明不了贝林厄姆家的任何不是。”
===Original===
“Then your opinions on the case,” said I, “are based entirely on the facts that have been made public?”

===Chinese===
“那么，您对本案的见解，”我说，“就完全建立在已经公开的事实之上了？”
===Original===
“Yes, mainly. I do not necessarily accept those facts just as they are presented, and I may have certain views of my own on the case. But if I have, I do not feel in a position to discuss them. For the present, discussion has to be limited to the facts and inferences offered by the parties concerned.”

===Chinese===
“是的，大体如此。那些事实照它们摆出来的样子，我未必就照单全收；而且对这案子，我自己或许也持有几分看法。但纵然有，我也不觉得眼下的处境便于谈论。目前，讨论只能限于本案有关各方所提供的事实与推论。”
===Original===
“There!” exclaimed Jervis, rising to knock his pipe out, “that is where Thorndyke has you. He lets you think you’re in the thick of the ‘know’ until one fine morning you wake up and discover that you have only been a gaping outsider; and then you are mightily astonished﻿—and so are the other side, too, for that matter. But we must really be off now, mustn’t we, reverend senior?”

===Chinese===
“瞧见没有！”杰维斯叫道，一面起身磕出他的烟灰，“这就是桑代克拿捏你的地方。他由得你以为自己正身处‘内情’的旋涡中心，直到某个晴朗的早晨你一觉醒来，发觉自己不过是个张着嘴呆看的局外人；那时你要大吃一惊——而说来，对面那一伙也要一齐吃惊的。不过这回我们真该动身了，是不是，可敬的老前辈？”
===Original===
“I suppose we must,” replied Thorndyke; and, as he drew on his gloves, he asked: “Have you heard from Barnard lately?”

===Chinese===
“我想是不得不走了，”桑代克答道；他一面戴上手套，一面问道：“近来巴纳德有信来吗？”
===Original===
“Oh, yes,” I answered. “I wrote to him at Smyrna to say that the practise was flourishing and that I was quite happy and contented, and that he might stay away as long as he liked. He writes by return that he will prolong his holiday if an opportunity offers, but will let me know later.”

===Chinese===
“哦，有的，”我答道。“我写信到士麦那（Smyrna）给他，说诊所业务兴旺，我也过得快活惬意，他爱在外头待多久就待多久。他随即回信说，倘若有机会，他还要把假期再延长下去，日后再行奉告。”
===Original===
“Gad,” said Jervis, “it was a stroke of luck for Barnard that Bellingham happened to have such a magnificent daughter﻿—there! don’t mind me, old man. You go in and win﻿—she’s worth it, isn’t she, Thorndyke?”

===Chinese===
“好家伙，”杰维斯说，“巴纳德真是走了运，贝林厄姆偏偏有这么一位出色的女儿——得啦，别把我放在心上，老兄。放手去追、去赢吧——她值得，是不是，桑代克？”
===Original===
“Miss Bellingham’s a very charming young lady,” replied Thorndyke. “I am most favorably impressed by both the father and the daughter, and I only trust that we may be able to be of some service to them.” With this sedate little speech Thorndyke shook my hand, and I watched my two friends go on their way until their fading shapes were swallowed up in the darkness of Fetter Lane.

===Chinese===
“贝林厄姆小姐是一位非常迷人的年轻小姐，”桑代克答道。“她父亲和她本人都给我留下了极好的印象，我只盼我们能为他们效上一番劳。”说完这番庄重的小小致辞，桑代克与我握了握手；我便望着我那两位朋友上路而去，直到他们渐淡的身影，被菲特巷的黑暗吞没。

## XII A Voyage of Discovery / 十二 发现之旅
===Original===
It was two or three mornings after my little supper party that, as I stood in the consulting-room brushing my hat preparatory to starting on my morning round, Adolphus appeared at the door to announce two gentlemen waiting in the surgery. I told him to bring them in, and a moment later Thorndyke entered, accompanied by Jervis. I noted that they looked uncommonly large in that little apartment, especially Thorndyke, but I had no time to consider this phenomenon, for the latter, when he had shaken my hand, proceeded at once to explain the object of their visit.

===Chinese===
我那小小的晚餐会过后两三天的早晨，我正站在诊室里刷帽子，预备动身去作上午的巡回出诊，阿道弗斯出现在门口，通报说有两位先生在诊疗室里候着。我吩咐他把客人领进来；不多时，桑代克便进来了，由杰维斯作陪。我注意到，在那间小小的屋子里，他们两位显得出奇地魁伟，尤其是桑代克；但我无暇推究这一现象，因为他同我握过手，随即就讲明了来意。
===Original===
“We have come to ask a favor, Berkeley,” he said; “to ask you to do us a very great service in the interests of your friends the Bellinghams.”

===Chinese===
“我们是来请你帮个忙的，伯克利，”他说，“请你为了你的朋友贝林厄姆一家，帮我们效一次大劳。”
===Original===
“You know I shall be delighted,” I said warmly. “What is it?”

===Chinese===
“您知道的，我总是乐意效劳，”我热忱地说。“什么事？”
===Original===
“I will explain. You know﻿—or perhaps you don’t﻿—that the police have collected all the bones that have been discovered and deposited them in the mortuary at Woodford, where they are to be viewed by the coroner’s jury. Now, it has become imperative that I should have more definite and reliable information than I can get from the newspapers. The natural thing for me would be to go down and examine them myself, but there are circumstances that make it very desirable that my connection with the case should not leak out. Consequently, I can’t go myself, and, for the same reason, I can’t send Jervis. On the other hand, as it is now stated pretty openly that the police consider the bones to be almost certainly those of John Bellingham, it would seem perfectly natural that you, as Godfrey Bellingham’s doctor, should go down to view them on his behalf.”

===Chinese===
“我来说明。你知道——或者你并不知道——警方已经把发现的那批骨殖统统收集起来，存放在伍德福德的停尸房里，听候验尸陪审团查验。如今，我必须弄到比报纸所能供给的更确切可靠的消息，这已是刻不容缓。按常理，我该亲自下去查验一番；可是眼下的种种情形，使我不愿让自己与本案的干系泄露出去，这一点大有必要。因此，我不能亲自去；出于同样的理由，我也不能派杰维斯去。另一方面，如今报上已说得相当公开：警方认定这些骨殖几乎必然是约翰·贝林厄姆的。这么一来，你以戈弗雷·贝林厄姆医生的身份，代他下去查验一番，就显得再自然不过了。”
===Original===
“I should like to,” I said. “I would give anything to go; but how is it to be managed? It would mean a whole day off and leaving the practise to look after itself.”

===Chinese===
“我巴不得去呢，”我说。“怎么都肯去；可这事怎么安排呢？那得豁出一整天的工夫，把诊所撂下听其自便。”
===Original===
“I think it could be managed,” said Thorndyke; “and the matter is really important for two reasons. One is that the inquest opens tomorrow, and someone certainly ought to be there to watch the proceedings on Godfrey’s behalf; and the other is that our client has received notice from Hurst’s solicitors that the application will be heard in the Probate Court in a few days.”

===Chinese===
“我想总归安排得开，”桑代克说；“而且这件事确实要紧，原因有二。其一，验尸审讯明天开庭，总该有人到场，代表戈弗雷照看一番；其二，我们的当事人已接到赫斯特方面律师的通知，申请不出几日便将在遗嘱检验法院开庭听审。”
===Original===
“Isn’t that rather sudden?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“这未免有点突然吧？”我问。
===Original===
“It certainly suggests that there has been a good deal more activity than we were given to understand. But you see the importance of the affair. The inquest will be a sort of dress rehearsal for the Probate Court, and it is quite essential that we should have a chance of estimating the management.”

===Chinese===
“这确实表明，其间奔走活动的频繁，远过于人家让我们领会的那个地步。可是你瞧，这桩事情干系重大。验尸审讯将是遗嘱检验法院的一场带妆彩排，我们务必得有个机会掂一掂对方的手法，这一点至为紧要。”
===Original===
“Yes, I see that. But how are we to manage about the practise?”

===Chinese===
“是的，这个我明白。可诊所的事怎么打点？”
===Original===
“We shall find you a substitute.”

===Chinese===
“我们会给你找一位替班的。”
===Original===
“Through a medical agent?”

===Chinese===
“通过医生职业介绍所吗？”
===Original===
“Yes,” said Jervis, “Turcival will find us a man; in fact, he has done it. I saw him this morning; he has a man who is waiting up in town to negotiate for the purchase of a practise and who would do the job for a couple of guineas. Quite a reliable man. Only say the word, and I will run off to Adam Street and engage him definitely.”

===Chinese===
“正是，”杰维斯说，“特西瓦尔（Turcival）会给咱们找到人的；其实他已经找好了。我今天上午见过他；他手头有个人，正在城里候着接洽盘购一处诊所，出两个几尼就肯干这趟差。人挺可靠。只要你一句话，我就奔到亚当街（Adam Street）去，把他一锤定音地雇下来。”
===Original===
“Very well. You engage the locum tenens, and I will be prepared to start for Woodford as soon as he turns up.”

===Chinese===
“很好。你去把这位临时代理医生（locum tenens）雇定；他一到，我就准备动身去伍德福德。”
===Original===
“Excellent!” said Thorndyke. “That is a great weight off my mind. And if you could manage to drop in this evening and smoke a pipe with us we could talk over the plan of campaign and let you know what items of information we are particularly in want of.”

===Chinese===
“好极了！”桑代克说。“我心头的一块大石头这才落了地。要是你今晚能抽空过来，同我们抽一斗烟，我们就可以把作战方略细细商量一遍，也好让你知道，哪些项情报是我们眼下特别欠缺的。”
===Original===
I promised to turn up at King’s Bench Walk as soon after half-past eight as possible, and my two friends then took their departure, leaving me to set out in high spirits on my scanty round of visits.

===Chinese===
我答应八点半一过便尽快赶到王座步道；两位朋友随即起身告辞，留下我兴冲冲地出发，去作我那寥寥数家的巡回出诊。
===Original===
It is surprising what different aspects things present from different points of view; how relative are our estimates of the conditions and circumstances of life. To the urban workman﻿—the journeyman baker or tailor, for instance, laboring year in year out in a single building﻿—a holiday ramble on Hampstead Heath is a veritable voyage of discovery; whereas to the sailor the shifting panorama of the whole wide world is but the commonplace of the day’s work.

===Chinese===
事物从不同的立足点呈现出的面貌何其不同；我们对生活的境况与条件的估量又是何其相对。对城市里的工匠——比如成年累月在同一座楼房里劳作的面包师傅或裁缝——而言，到汉普斯特德荒野（Hampstead Heath）上作一次假日漫游，便不啻一场名副其实的发现之旅；而对水手来说，整个广阔世界那移步换景的画卷，不过是日常工作里平平常常的家常便饭。
===Original===
So I reflected as I took my place in the train at Liverpool Street on the following day. There had been a time when a trip by rail to the borders of Epping Forest would have been far from a thrilling experience; now, after vegetating in the little world of Fetter Lane, it was quite an adventure.

===Chinese===
第二天，我在利物浦街（Liverpool Street）登上火车、坐定下来的时候，心里正这样想着。曾几何时，乘火车到埃平森林边上一游，远算不得什么令人兴奋的经历；如今，在菲特巷这个小天地里蛰居过一场之后，这竟成了十足的一次历险。
===Original===
The enforced inactivity of a railway journey is favorable to thought, and I had much to think about. The last few weeks had witnessed momentous changes in my outlook. New interests had arisen, new friendships had grown up, and above all, there had stolen into my life that supreme influence that, for good or for evil, according to my fortune, was to color and pervade it even to its close. Those few days of companionable labor in the reading-room, with the homely hospitalities of the milkshop and the pleasant walks homeward through the friendly London streets, had called into existence a new world﻿—a world in which the gracious personality of Ruth Bellingham was the one dominating reality. And thus, as I leaned back in the corner of the railway carriage with an unlighted pipe in my hand, the events of the immediate past, together with those more problematical ones of the impending future, occupied me rather to the exclusion of the business of the moment, which was to review the remains collected in the Woodford mortuary, until, as the train approached Stratford, the odors of the soap and bone-manure factories poured in at the open window and (by a natural association of ideas) brought me back to the object of my quest.

===Chinese===
火车旅程的强制清闲正宜于思索，而我也有许多可想的。过去几个星期，见证了我人生观里的几番重大变动。新的兴趣应运而生，新的友谊滋长起来，而尤关紧要的，是有一种至高无上的影响力悄然潜入了我的生活，它为福为祸，全看我的造化，势将给我的生活敷彩浸染，直到它的终点。在阅览室里相伴用功的那几日，牛奶铺里家常的殷勤款待，取道亲切的伦敦街巷步行归家的黄昏，唤起了一个新的世界——一个由露丝·贝林厄姆温婉的人格主宰着一切现实的世界。于是，当我倚在车厢一角，手里握着一斗未点的烟斗，方才过去的种种，连同悬而未决的将来的种种，便占据了我的心怀，倒把眼下的正事——检视伍德福德停尸房中收集的遗骸——排挤到了一旁；直到火车驶近斯特拉特福德（Stratford），肥皂厂和骨粉厂的气味从敞开的车窗里涌进来，才（借着意念的自然联想）把我带回到此行的任务上来。
===Original===
As to the exact purpose of this expedition, I was not very clear; but I knew that I was acting as Thorndyke’s proxy and thrilled with pride at the thought. But what particular light my investigations were to throw upon the intricate Bellingham case I had no very definite idea. With a view to fixing the procedure in my mind, I took Thorndyke’s written instructions from my pocket and read them over carefully. They were very full and explicit, making ample allowance for my lack of experience in medico-legal matters:﻿—

===Chinese===
至于此行的确切目的何在，我心里并不十分了然；但我知道自己是桑代克的代理人，一想到这里，心中便涌起一阵自豪的悸动。至于我的查验究竟能给这桩盘根错节的贝林厄姆案投下怎样一线光明，我并没有十分确定的成算。为了把程序牢牢记在心头，我从衣袋里取出桑代克的书面指示，仔细读了一遍。指示写得周详明确，对我缺乏法医学事务经验一点也作了充分的体谅：——
===Original===
1. Do not appear to make minute investigations or in any way excite remark.
2. Ascertain if all the bones belonging to each region are present, and if not, which are missing.
3. Measure the extreme length of the principal bones and compare those of opposite sides.
4. Examine the bones with reference to age, sex, and muscular development of the deceased.
5. Note the presence or absence of signs of constitutional disease, local disease of bone or adjacent structures, old or recent injuries, and any other departures from the normal or usual.
6. Observe the presence or absence of adipocere and its position, if present.
7. Note any remains of tendons, ligaments or other soft structures.
8. Examine the Sidcup hand with reference to the question as to whether the finger was separated before or after death.
9. Estimate the probable period of submersion and note any changes (as e.g., mineral or organic staining) due to the character of the water or mud.
10. Ascertain the circumstances (immediate and remote) that led to the discovery of the bones and the names of the persons concerned in those circumstances.
11. Commit all information to writing as soon as possible, and make plans and diagrams on the spot, if circumstances permit.
12. Preserve an impassive exterior: listen attentively but without eagerness; ask as few questions as possible; pursue any inquiry that your observations on the spot may suggest.

===Chinese===
1. 切勿显得在进行细密的检查，或以任何方式惹人注目。
2. 查明属于各个部位的骨殖是否齐全；若有缺失，缺的是哪些。
3. 量取各主要骨的极限长度，并比对左右两侧的对应诸骨。
4. 就死者的年龄、性别与肌肉发育状况查验骨殖。
5. 注意有无全身性疾病的迹象、骨或邻近结构的局部病症、或新或旧的损伤，以及任何其他偏离正常或常态之处。
6. 观察有无尸蜡（adipocere）；如有，查明其部位。
7. 注意有无肌腱、韧带或其他软组织的残余。
8. 就手指系生前抑或死后分离这一问题，查验锡德卡普发现的那只手。
9. 估测浸没水中的大致时间，并注意因水的性质或淤泥的性质而起的任何变化（如矿物质或有机物的沾染）。
10. 查明导致发现骨殖的种种情况（直接与间接），以及与那些情况有关的人员姓名。
11. 一切情报务求尽快笔录；情形许可时，就地绘制平面图与示意图。
12. 保持不动声色的外表：倾听专注而不露急切；发问愈少愈好；凡就地观察所提示的任何查究，皆可放手进行。
===Original===
These were my instructions, and, considering that I was going merely to inspect a few dry bones, they appeared rather formidable; in fact, the more I read them over the greater became my misgivings as to my qualifications for the task.

===Chinese===
这便是给我的指示；想到我此行不过是去看几根枯骨，它们却显得颇为森然可畏；事实上，我越是一遍遍诵读，越对自己胜任这桩差事的才具心怀忐忑。
===Original===
As I approached the mortuary it became evident that some, at least, of Thorndyke’s admonitions were by no means unnecessary. The place was in charge of a police sergeant, who watched my approach suspiciously; and some half-dozen men, obviously newspaper reporters, hovered about the entrance like a pack of jackals. I presented the coroner’s order which Mr. Marchmont had obtained, and which the sergeant read with his back against the wall, to prevent the newspaper men from looking over his shoulder.

===Chinese===
待我走近停尸房，事情已经分明：桑代克的某些告诫，绝非无的放矢。那地方由一名警佐看管，他用怀疑的目光望着我走近；还有五六个汉子，一望便知是报记者，像一群豺狗似的在门口打转。我出示了验尸官的手令——那是马奇蒙特先生设法弄来的；警佐背抵着墙读完它，免得被报记者们从肩后偷看。
===Original===
My credentials being found satisfactory, the door was unlocked and I entered, accompanied by three enterprising reporters, whom, however, the sergeant summarily ejected and locked out, returning to usher me into the presence and to observe my proceedings with intelligent but highly embarrassing interest.

===Chinese===
我的证件被验明无误，门锁开了，我走了进去，身后跟着三位闯劲十足的记者；不过警佐当即把他们轰了出去，锁上了门，回身把我引到遗骸跟前，带着一种聪慧然而极令人窘迫的兴趣，注视我的一举一动。
===Original===
The bones were laid out on a large table and covered with a sheet, which the sergeant slowly turned back, watching my face intently as he did so to note the impression that the spectacle made upon me. I imagine that he must have been somewhat disappointed by my impassive demeanor, for the remains suggested to me nothing more than a rather shabby set of “student’s osteology.” The whole collection had been set out by the police surgeon (as the sergeant informed me) in their proper anatomical order; notwithstanding which I counted them over carefully to make sure that none were missing, checking them by the list with which Thorndyke had furnished me.

===Chinese===
骨殖摊放在一张大桌子上，盖着一条布单；警佐缓缓掀开单子，一面目不转睛地盯着我瞧，要看这场面给我留下了什么印象。我想，他对我那不动声色的举止一定有些失望，因为在我看来，这堆遗骸不过像一套颇为寒酸的“学生骨学”标本。全部骨殖已由警方医生（警佐这样告诉我）按其应有的解剖次序摆好；尽管如此，我仍旧把它们逐一清点了一遍，以确保没有缺失，并拿桑代克给我备下的那张清单逐一核对。
===Original===
“I see you have found the left thighbone,” I remarked, observing that this did not appear in the list.

===Chinese===
“我看你们又找回了左侧的股骨，”我说，因为我注意到清单上原先没有这一根。
===Original===
“Yes,” said the sergeant; “that turned up yesterday evening in a big pond called Baldwin’s Pond in the Sandpit plain, near Little Monk Wood.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”警佐说；“昨晚在沙坑坪（Sandpit plain）一个唤作鲍德温池塘（Baldwin’s Pond）的大塘里浮上来的，就在小僧林（Little Monk Wood）近旁。”
===Original===
“Is that near here?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“那地方离这儿近吗？”我问。
===Original===
“In the forest up Loughton way,” was the reply.

===Chinese===
“在森林里，往洛顿去的那一路。”他答道。
===Original===
I made a note of the fact (on which the sergeant looked as if he was sorry he had mentioned it), and then turned my attention to a general consideration of the bones before examining them in detail. Their appearance would have been improved and examination facilitated by a thorough scrubbing, for they were just as they had been taken from their respective resting-places, and it was difficult to decide whether their reddish-yellow color was an actual stain or due to a deposit on the surface. In any case, as it affected them all alike, I thought it an interesting feature and made a note of it. They bore numerous traces of their sojourn in the various ponds from which they had been recovered, but these gave me little help in determining the length of time during which they had been submerged. They were, of course, encrusted with mud, and little wisps of pondweed stuck to them in places; but these facts furnished only the vaguest measure of time.

===Chinese===
我记下了这一事实（警佐瞧那神气，倒像后悔多嘴了），然后才转而把注意力放到对骨殖的通盘考虑上，准备再作细察。若能把它们彻底擦洗一番，外观会有改善，查验也会顺手得多，因为它们都还保持着从各自安息之所取出时的原样，它们那种红黄色，究竟是浸出的真渍，还是表面附着的一层沉积，很难断定。无论如何，既然所有骨殖一概如此，我便觉得这是个有意思的特点，把它记了下来。它们带着在先后被捞起的那几口池塘里栖身的无数痕迹，但这些痕迹，对于确定它们浸在水里的时间长短，帮不上什么忙。它们自然都结着泥壳，有些地方还粘着一绺绺的池草；可这些事实，也只能给出最模糊的时间概念。
===Original===
Some of the traces were, indeed, more informing. To several of the bones, for instance, there adhered the dried egg-clusters of the common pond-snail, and in one of the hollows of the right shoulder-blade (the “infraspinous fossa”) was a group of the mud-built tubes of the red river-worm. These remains gave proof of a considerable period of submersion, and since they could not have been deposited on the bones until all the flesh had disappeared they furnished evidence that some time﻿—a month or two at any rate﻿—had elapsed since this had happened. Incidentally, too, their distribution showed the position in which the bones had lain, and though this appeared to be of no importance in the existing circumstances, I made careful notes of the situation of each adherent body, illustrating their position by rough sketches.

===Chinese===
有些痕迹，确实更能说明问题。比如，好几根骨殖上粘着普通池螺的干卵块，右肩胛骨的一个凹陷处（即“冈下窝”）里，还有一簇红色水丝蚓（red river-worm）筑的泥管。这些残留证明浸没的时间相当长久，而且既然非要等到血肉烂尽它们才可能附着到骨殖上，它们便提供了证据，表明自那以后又过去了若干时日——至少一两个月。附带一提，它们的分布还显示出骨殖当时躺卧的姿势；虽然这于眼下的情形似乎无关紧要，我还是把每一处附着物的位置仔细记了下来，并用草图加以标示。
===Original===
The sergeant watched my proceedings with an indulgent smile.

===Chinese===
警佐面带宽容的微笑，看着我的一举一动。
===Original===
“You’re making a regular inventory, sir,” he remarked, “as if you were going to put ’em up for auction. I shouldn’t think those snails’ eggs would be much help in identification. And all that has been done already,” he added as I produced my measuring-tape.

===Chinese===
“先生，您这是在开一份正规的清单哪，”他说，“倒像要把它们拿去拍卖似的。依我看，那些螺卵对验明身份帮不了什么忙。再说，该做的都已经做过了，”等我掏出卷尺，他又添了一句。
===Original===
“No doubt,” I replied; “but my business is to make independent observations, to check the others, if necessary.” And I proceeded to measure each of the principal bones separately and to compare those of the opposite sides. The agreement in dimensions and general characteristics of the pairs of bones left little doubt that all were parts of one skeleton, a conclusion that was confirmed by the eburnated patch on the head of the right thighbone and the corresponding patch in the socket of the right hipbone. When I had finished my measurements I went over the entire series of bones in detail, examining each with the closest attention for any of those signs which Thorndyke had indicated, and eliciting nothing but a monotonously reiterated negative. They were distressingly and disappointingly normal.

===Chinese===
“想必如此，”我答道；“不过我的职责是作独立的观察，必要时好核对旁人的结果。”说罢，我便把各主要骨一一量过，并比对左右两侧的对应骨。各对骨在尺寸和总体特征上的一致，几乎不容置疑地表明它们同属一具骨骼；这一结论，又由右股骨头上的象牙质样变斑块和右髋骨髋臼里相应的斑块所证实。量罢尺寸，我又把整副骨殖从头到尾细查了一遍，按照桑代克提示的种种迹象，逐根以最缜密的注意加以检视，得到的却只是一连串单调重复的否定。它们正常得叫人苦恼，又正常得叫人失望。
===Original===
“Well, sir, what do you make of ’em?” the sergeant asked cheerfully as I shut up my notebook and straightened my back. “Whose bones are they? Are they Mr. Bellingham’s, think ye?”

===Chinese===
“嗳，先生，您瞧出什么门道了没有？”我合上笔记本、直起腰来时，警佐乐呵呵地问。“这是谁的骨头？您看是不是贝林厄姆先生的？”
===Original===
“I should be very sorry to say whose bones they are,” I replied. “One bone is very much like another, you know.”

===Chinese===
“是谁的骨头，我可不情愿随便断言，”我答道。“您也知道，骨头跟骨头，本来就长得差不多。”
===Original===
“I suppose it is,” he agreed; “but I thought that, with all that measuring and all those notes, you might have arrived at something definite.” Evidently he was disappointed in me; and I was somewhat disappointed in myself when I contrasted Thorndyke’s elaborate instructions with the meager result of my investigations. For what did my discoveries amount to? And how much was the inquiry advanced by the few entries in my notebook?

===Chinese===
“这话不假，”他表示同意；“可我原以为，量也量了，记也记了，您总该琢磨出点确定的东西来了。”显然，他对我不无失望；而当我把桑代克那份头绪繁多的指示，同我查访所得的微末成绩两相对照，我自己也有些失望。因为我那些发现究竟顶得了什么呢？笔记本里寥寥几笔，又把这项查究推进了几分？
===Original===
The bones were apparently those of a man of fair though not remarkable muscular development; over thirty years of age, but how much older I was unable to say. His height I judged roughly to be five feet eight inches, but my measurements would furnish data for a more exact estimate by Thorndyke. Beyond this the bones were quite uncharacteristic. There were no signs of disease either local or general, no indications of injuries either old or recent, no departures of any kind from the normal or usual; and the dismemberment had been effected with such care that there was not a single scratch on any of the separate surfaces. Of adipocere (the peculiar waxy or soapy substance that is commonly found in bodies that have slowly decayed in damp situations) there was not a trace; and the only remnant of the soft structures was a faint indication, like a spot of dried glue, of the tendon on the tip of the right elbow.

===Chinese===
这些骨殖显然属于一个肌肉发育尚可、却并不出众的男子；年纪在三十开外，但大出多少，我说不上。他的身高，我粗略估计为五英尺八英寸，不过我的尺寸数据，可供桑代克作更精确的推算。除此之外，骨殖毫无特征可言。没有局部或全身疾病的迹象，没有或新或旧的损伤痕迹，没有任何偏离正常或常态之处；肢解又做得极其小心，各个断离的骨面上竟没有一道刮痕。尸蜡（即在潮湿环境中缓慢腐朽的尸身上常见的那种蜡质或皂状物质）则不见一丝痕迹；软组织仅存的残余，是右肘尖端肌腱上一个淡淡的印记，像一斑干了的胶水。
===Original===
The sergeant was in the act of replacing the sheet, with the air of a showman who has just given an exhibition, when there came a sharp rapping on the mortuary door. The officer finished spreading the sheet with official precision, and having ushered me out into the lobby turned the key and admitted three persons, holding the door open after they had entered for me to go out. But the appearance of the newcomers inclined me to linger. One of them was a local constable, evidently in official charge; a second was a laboring man, very wet and muddy, who carried a small sack; while in the third I thought I scented a professional brother.

===Chinese===
警佐正要重新把布单盖上，那派头活像刚演完一场把戏的耍把式者，忽听得停尸房门上响起一阵急促的敲击。这位警官以公务程序的精准把单子铺陈停当，先把我让出门厅，然后转过钥匙，放进三个人来；待他们进去之后，他仍旧拉着门，好让我出去。可是那几个新来者的形貌，使我不由得流连不去。其中一个是当地的警士，显系奉公而来；第二个是个做工的汉子，浑身又湿又泥，提着一只小口袋；至于第三个，我自以为嗅出了一位同行兄弟的气味。
===Original===
The sergeant continued to hold the door open.

===Chinese===
警佐仍旧拉着门。
===Original===
“Nothing more I can do for you, sir?” he asked genially.

===Chinese===
“先生，还有什么要小的效劳的吗？”他和气地问。
===Original===
“Is that the divisional surgeon?” I inquired.

===Chinese===
“那位是警区医生吗？”我问。
===Original===
“Yes. I am the divisional surgeon,” the newcomer answered. “Did you want anything of me?”

===Chinese===
“正是。我就是警区医生，”新来的人答道。“您找我有事？”
===Original===
“This,” said the sergeant, “is a medical gentleman who has got permission from the coroner to inspect the remains. He is acting for the family of the deceased﻿—I mean, for the family of Mr. Bellingham,” he added in answer to an inquiring glance from the surgeon.

===Chinese===
“这一位，”警佐说，“是位医生先生，得了验尸官的许可来查验遗骸的。他代表死者的家属——我是说，代表贝林厄姆先生的家属，”他见医生投来询问的一瞥，又补了一句。
===Original===
“I see,” said the latter. “Well, they have found the rest of the trunk, including, I understand, the ribs that were missing from the other part. Isn’t that so, Davis?”

===Chinese===
“原来如此，”后者说。“唔，他们把躯干的其余部分找到了，据我所知，先前那一半里缺的肋骨也在这批里头。是这样吗，戴维斯？”
===Original===
“Yes, sir,” replied the constable. “Inspector Badger says all the ribs is here, and all the bones of the neck as well.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生，”警士答道。“巴杰探长说，肋骨全都在这儿了，脖子的骨头也一根不少。”
===Original===
“The inspector seems to be an anatomist,” I remarked.

===Chinese===
“这位探长看来是位解剖学家呢，”我说。
===Original===
The sergeant grinned. “He is a very knowing gentleman, is Mr. Badger. He came down here this morning quite early and spent a long time looking over the bones and checking them by some notes in his pocketbook. I fancy he’s got something on, but he was precious close about it.”

===Chinese===
警佐咧嘴一笑。“巴杰先生是个精明透顶的人物。今天一大早他就下来了，把那些骨殖端详了好半天，还拿皮夹子里的一些单子逐项核对。我瞧他准是有了什么名堂，只是他嘴严得很。”
===Original===
Here the sergeant shut up rather suddenly﻿—perhaps contrasting his own conduct with that of his superior.

===Chinese===
说到这里，警佐蓦地住了口——多半是把自己方才的行径同上司的对照了一下。
===Original===
“Let us have these new bones out on the table,” said the police surgeon. “Take the sheet off, and don’t shoot them out as if they were coals. Hand them out carefully.”

===Chinese===
“把这几根新骨头摆到桌上来吧，”警方医生说。“把单子掀开，别像倒煤似的把骨头哗啦倒出来。一根一根小心递过来。”
===Original===
The laborer fished out the wet and muddy bones one by one from the sack, and as he laid them on the table the surgeon arranged them in their proper relative positions.

===Chinese===
那做工的汉子从口袋里把湿漉漉、泥糊糊的骨头一根根摸出来；他往桌上放一根，医生便按应有的相对位置摆好一根。
===Original===
“This has been a neatly executed job,” he remarked; “none of your clumsy hacking with a chopper or a saw. The bones have been cleanly separated at the joints. The fellow who did this must have had some anatomical knowledge, unless he was a butcher, which by the way, is not impossible. He has used his knife uncommonly skilfully, and you notice that each arm was taken off with the scapula attached, just as a butcher takes off a shoulder of mutton. Are there any more bones in that bag?”

===Chinese===
“这活儿干得干净利落，”他说；“可不是拿柴斧或锯子乱劈乱剁的那一路。骨头都是在关节处干干净净脱开的。干这事的人，若不是屠夫，就必定懂些解剖学——顺带说一句，是屠夫也并非不可能。他那把刀使得出奇地纯熟；而且你们注意到没有，两条胳膊都是连着肩胛骨整个儿卸下来的，正如屠夫卸一只羊肩。袋子里没有别的骨头了吧？”
===Original===
“No, sir,” replied the laborer, wiping his hands with an air of finality on the posterior aspect of his trousers; “that’s the lot.”

===Chinese===
“没有了，先生，”做工的汉子答道，一面带着大功告成的神气在裤子的后侧擦了擦手；“统共就这些了。”
===Original===
The surgeon looked thoughtfully at the bones as he gave a final touch to their arrangement, and remarked:

===Chinese===
医生若有所思地望着骨殖，给它们的排列作最后一番整理，随后说道：
===Original===
“The inspector is right. All the bones of the neck are there. Very odd. Don’t you think so?”

===Chinese===
“探长说得对。颈部的骨头都在。怪得很。您不觉得吗？”
===Original===
“You mean﻿—”

===Chinese===
“您是说——”
===Original===
“I mean that this very eccentric murderer seems to have given himself such an extraordinary amount of trouble for no reason that one can see. There are these neck vertebrae, for instance. He must have carefully separated the skull from the atlas instead of just cutting through the neck. Then there is the way he divided the trunk; the twelfth ribs have just come in with this lot, but the twelfth dorsal vertebra to which they belong was attached to the lower half. Imagine the trouble he must have taken to do that, and without cutting or hacking the bones about, either. It is extraordinary. This is rather interesting, by the way. Handle it carefully.”

===Chinese===
“我是说，这位怪僻得出奇的凶手，给自己惹了这么大一番说不出来由的麻烦。就拿这些颈椎来说吧。他必定是把颅骨从寰椎上细心分离了下来，而不是干脆一刀切断脖颈。再看他分解躯干的手法：第十二对肋骨是方才随这一批送来的，可它们所连属的第十二胸椎，却连在躯干的下半截上。想想他为做到这一步该费多大的事，况且还不曾把骨头切着剁着分毫。真是非同小可。对了，这一件倒有点意思。拿的时候当心。”
===Original===
He picked up the breastbone daintily﻿—for it was covered with wet mud﻿—and handed it to me with the remark:

===Chinese===
他斯斯文文地拈起那块胸骨——上面糊满了湿泥——一面递到我手里，一面说道：
===Original===
“That is the most definite piece of evidence we have.”

===Chinese===
“这是我们手头最确凿的一件证据。”
===Original===
“You mean,” I said, “that the union of the two parts into a single mass fixes this as the skeleton of an elderly man?”

===Chinese===
“您的意思是，”我说，“这两个部分既然连合成了一整块，便就此确定这是一副老年人的骨骼了？”
===Original===
“Yes, that is the obvious suggestion, which is confirmed by the deposit of bone in the rib-cartilages. You can tell the inspector, Davis, that I have checked this lot of bones and that they are all here.”

===Chinese===
“是的，这是显而易见的推论，肋软骨里的骨质沉着又提供了佐证。你可以告诉探长，戴维斯：这批骨头我已经点验过了，一根不缺。”
===Original===
“Would you mind writing it down, sir?” said the constable. “Inspector Badger said I was to have everything in writing.”

===Chinese===
“麻烦您写下来行吗，先生？”警士说。“巴杰探长吩咐过，什么都要有书面为凭。”
===Original===
The surgeon took out his pocketbook, and, while he was selecting a suitable piece of paper, he asked: “Did you form any opinion as to the height of the deceased?”

===Chinese===
医生掏出皮夹，一面挑一张合用的纸，一面问道：“您对死者的身高得出什么看法了吗？”
===Original===
“Yes, I thought he would be about five feet eight” (here I caught the sergeant’s eyes, fixed on me with a knowing leer).

===Chinese===
“得出了，我想他大约在五英尺八英寸上下”（说到这里，我撞上了警佐的目光，他正心照不宣地斜睨着我）。
===Original===
“I made it five eight and a half,” said the police surgeon; “but we shall know better when we have seen the lower leg-bones. Where was this lot found, Davis?”

===Chinese===
“我量出来是五英尺八英寸半，”警方医生说；“不过等见到小腿的骨头，心里就更有数了。这批是在哪儿找到的，戴维斯？”
===Original===
“In the pond just off the road in Lord’s Bushes, sir, and the inspector has gone off now to﻿—”

===Chinese===
“就在洛德灌木林（Lord’s Bushes）大路旁边的那个塘里，先生。探长这会儿已经动身到——”
===Original===
“Never mind where he’s gone,” interrupted the sergeant. “You just answer questions and attend to your business.”

===Chinese===
“他上哪儿去不与你相干，”警佐打断道。“你只管回答问话，办你的差事。”
===Original===
The sergeant’s reproof conveyed a hint to me on which I was not slow to act. Friendly as my professional colleague was, it was clear that the police were disposed to treat me as an interloper who was to be kept out of the “know” as far as possible. Accordingly I thanked my colleague and the sergeant for their courtesy, and bidding them adieu until we should meet at the inquest, took my departure and walked away quickly until I found an inconspicuous position from which I could keep the door of the mortuary in view. A few moments later I saw Constable Davis emerge and stride away up the road.

===Chinese===
警佐这一番训诫，给了我一个暗示，我当即照办不误。我这位同行虽然友善，但显而易见，警方是打定主意把我当作一个闯入者，能不让我知道的内情，尽量不让我知道。于是我谢过同行和警佐的礼数，同他们作别，约定验尸审讯上再会，便动身走开，快步走了一段，寻了个不惹眼的位置，从那里可以望住停尸房的大门。不多时，我看见戴维斯警士走出来，顺着大路大步而去。
===Original===
I watched his rapidly diminishing figure until he had gone as far as I considered desirable, and then I set forth in his wake. The road led straight away from the village, and in less than half a mile entered the outskirts of the forest. Here I quickened my pace to close up somewhat, and it was well that I did so, for suddenly he diverged from the road into a green lane, where for a while I lost sight of him. Still hurrying forward, I again caught sight of him just as he turned off into a narrow path that entered a beech wood with a thickish undergrowth of holly, along which I followed him for several minutes, gradually decreasing the distance between us, until suddenly there fell on my ear a rhythmical sound like the clank of a pump. Soon after I caught the sound of men’s voices, and then the constable struck off the path into the wood.

===Chinese===
我望着他那迅速缩小的身影，直到他走到我认为合宜的距离，方才动身缀了上去。大路笔直地通离村子，不到半英里便没入了森林的边缘。到这里我加快脚步，稍稍逼近了些；幸亏如此，因为他蓦地拐离大路，转进一条绿荫夹道的巷路，有一阵子我望不见他的踪影。我仍旧疾步向前，重新瞥见他时，他正折上一条窄径，小径通入一片山毛榉林，林下覆着相当密的冬青；我沿径跟了他好几分钟，渐渐缩短两人间的距离，直到一阵节奏均匀的声响蓦地落到我耳中，像是水泵的铿锵。不多时我又听到有人语声，随后警士便离开小径，一头折进了树林。
===Original===
I now advanced more cautiously, endeavoring to locate the search party by the sound of the pump, and when I had done this I made a little detour so that I might approach from the opposite direction to that from which the constable had appeared.

===Chinese===
此刻我愈加谨慎地前进，设法凭水泵的声响判定搜索队的方位；判定之后，我略略绕了个弯，好从与警士现身相反的方向接近他们。
===Original===
Still guided by the noise of the pump, I at length came out into a small opening among the trees and halted to survey the scene. The center of the opening was occupied by a small pond, not more than a dozen yards across, by the side of which stood a builder’s handcart. The little two-wheeled vehicle had evidently been used to convey the appliances which were deposited on the ground near it, and which consisted of a large tub﻿—now filled with water﻿—a shovel, a rake, a sieve, and a portable pump, the latter being fitted with a long delivery hose. There were three men besides the constable, one of whom was working the handle of the pump, while another was glancing at a paper that the constable had just delivered to him. He looked up sharply as I appeared, and viewed me with unconcealed disfavor.

===Chinese===
仍旧循着水泵的声响，我终于走出树林，来到一小片林中空地跟前，站定下来打量这场景。空地中央是一口小塘，方圆不过十来码，塘边停着一辆营造匠的手推车。那辆小小的双轮车，显然是用来运送器械的；器械就卸在车旁的地上，计有一只大桶——如今满盛着水——一把铁锹、一把耙子、一面筛子，还有一台便携水泵，泵上接了一根长长的出水软管。警士之外还有三个人：一个正摇着水泵的把手，另一个正浏览警士方才递给他的一张纸。我一露面，他霍地抬起头来，毫不掩饰地露出不豫之色。
===Original===
“Hallo, sir!” said he. “You can’t come here.”

===Chinese===
“喂，先生！”他说。“您不能到这儿来。”
===Original===
Now, seeing that I was actually here, this was clearly a mistake, and I ventured to point out the fallacy.

===Chinese===
可是，既然我分明已经到了这儿，这话显然说岔了，我便斗胆向他指出这一谬误。
===Original===
“Well, I can’t allow you to stay here. Our business is of a private nature.”

===Chinese===
“咳，我可不能让你留在这儿。我们办的是私事。”
===Original===
“I know exactly what your business is, Inspector Badger.”

===Chinese===
“你们办的是什么事，我一清二楚，巴杰探长。”
===Original===
“Oh, do you?” said he, surveying me with a foxy smile. “And I expect I know what yours is, too. But we can’t have any of you newspaper gentry spying on us just at present, so you just be off.”

===Chinese===
“哦，是吗？”他说，带着一丝狡黠的笑意把我打量了一番。“那么我瞧，你干的是哪一行，我多半也猜得着。只是眼下，可容不得你们报馆的爷们刺探我们，所以你还是趁早请吧。”
===Original===
I thought it best to undeceive him at once, and accordingly, having explained who I was, I showed him the coroner’s permit, which he read with manifest annoyance.

===Chinese===
我想还是当即打消他的误会为妙，于是说明了自己的身份，又出示了验尸官的许可；他读的时候，满脸的愠色。
===Original===
“This is all very well, sir,” said he as he handed me back the paper, “but it doesn’t authorize you to come spying on the proceedings of the police. Any remains that we discover will be deposited in the mortuary, where you can inspect them to your heart’s content; but you can’t stay here and watch us.”

===Chinese===
“这张纸么，先生，倒也罢了，”他把纸递还给我时说，“可它并没有授权你来刺探警方的行动。我们发现的任何遗骸，都会送进停尸房，您在那儿尽管看个尽兴就是；可您不能待在这儿盯着我们。”
===Original===
I had no defined object in keeping a watch on the inspector’s proceedings; but the sergeant’s indiscreet hint had aroused my curiosity, which was further excited by Mr. Badger’s evident desire to get rid of me. Moreover, while we had been talking, the pump had stopped (the muddy floor of the pond being now pretty fully exposed), and the inspector’s assistant was handling the shovel impatiently.

===Chinese===
我盯着探长的行动，本没有什么明确的用意；但那警佐不知轻重漏出的一句口风勾起了我的好奇心，而巴杰先生那副分明想甩脱我的架势，又把这好奇心拨得更旺了。何况，在我们说话的当儿，水泵已经停了（塘底的泥面如今已暴露得七七八八），探长的那名助手正拿铁锹等得不耐烦。
===Original===
“Now I put it to you, Inspector,” said I, persuasively, “is it politic of you to allow it to be said that you refused an authorized representative of the family facilities for verifying any statements that you may make hereafter?”

===Chinese===
“我且向您请教一句，探长，”我婉转地说，“日后您在这里凡有所陈述，人家若说起来，家属正式授权的代表要求到场核实，您竟加以拒绝——这话传扬出去，于您划算吗？”
===Original===
“What do you mean?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“您这是什么意思？”他问。
===Original===
“I mean that if you should happen to find some bone which could be identified as part of the body of Mr. Bellingham, that fact would be of more importance to his family than to anyone else. You know that there is a very valuable estate and a rather difficult will.”

===Chinese===
“我的意思是，倘使您碰巧找到某根骨头，能够认定是贝林厄姆先生遗体的一部分，那么这一事实，对他的家属比对任何旁人都要紧。您知道，这里头有一份很值钱的产业，还有一份颇为棘手的遗嘱。”
===Original===
“I didn’t know it, and I don’t see the bearing of it now” (neither did I for that matter); “but if you make such a point of being present at the search, I can’t very well refuse. Only you mustn’t get in our way, that’s all.”

===Chinese===
“这个我不知道，就是此刻也看不出它有什么干系”（说实在的，我也看不出）；“可你既然这样一口咬定要在场看搜查，我也犯不上硬回绝。只是别碍我们的事，如此而已。”
===Original===
On hearing this conclusion, his assistant, who looked like a plainclothes officer, took up his shovel and stepped into the mud that formed the bottom of the pond, stooping as he went and peering among the masses of weed that had been left stranded by the withdrawal of the water. The inspector watched him anxiously, cautioning him from time to time to “look out where he was treading”; the laborer left the pump and craned forward from the margin of the mud, and the constable and I looked on from our respective points of vantage. For some time the search was fruitless. Once the searcher stooped and picked up what turned out to be a fragment of decayed wood; then the remains of a long-deceased jay were discovered, examined, and rejected. Suddenly the man bent down by the side of a small pool that had been left in one of the deeper hollows, stared intently into the mud, and stood up.

===Chinese===
一听这话有了结果，他那助手——瞧模样像个便衣警员——便操起铁锹，跨进塘底的烂泥，一路俯着身子，在退水后搁浅的一团团水草间逡巡细看。探长焦灼地望着他，不时告诫他“留神脚下踩的什么地方”；做工的汉子撇下水泵，从泥塘边上探身张望；警士和我则各据有利的位置观望。有好一阵子，搜查一无所获。有一次搜索者俯下身去，拾起来的原来是一块朽木；随后又发现了一只死去已久的松鸦，验看一番，弃在一边。蓦地，那人在一个深洼留下的小水洼旁边弯下腰去，死死盯住泥里看了看，直起身来。
===Original===
“There’s something here that looks like a bone, sir,” he sang out.

===Chinese===
“先生，这儿有个东西，瞧着像根骨头。”他扬声喊道。
===Original===
“Don’t grub about then,” said the inspector. “Drive your shovel right into the mud where you saw it and bring it to the sieve.”

===Chinese===
“那就别瞎扒拉，”探长说。“把铁锹对准你瞧见它的那点，直插进泥里，端上筛子这儿来。”
===Original===
