The man followed out these instructions, and as he came shoreward with a great pile of the slimy mud on his shovel we all converged on the sieve, which the inspector took up and held over the tub, directing the constable and laborer to “lend a hand,” meaning thereby that they were to crowd round the tub and exclude me as completely as possible. This, in fact, they did very effectively with his assistance, for, when the shovelful of mud had been deposited on the sieve, the four men leaned over it and so nearly hid it from view that it was only by craning over, first on one side and then on the other, that I was able to catch an occasional glimpse of it and to observe it gradually melting away as the sieve, immersed in the water, was shaken to and fro.

===Chinese===
那人依言而行，当他端着满满一锹稀泥往岸边走来时，我们全都围向筛子；探长提起筛子，架在桶上，吩咐警士和那做工的汉子“搭把手”，意思是叫他们围拢到桶边，把我尽可能挡在外面。这一招有他从旁撮合，果然奏效之至：一锹泥倒上筛子之后，四条汉子便一齐俯身围住了它，遮得几乎密不透风；我只得先从这边、再从那边探过身子去，才偶尔窥得一眼，看那筛子浸在水里来回簸荡，泥团渐渐消融净尽。
===Original===
Presently the inspector raised the sieve from the water and stooped over it more closely to examine its contents. Apparently the examination yielded no very conclusive results, for it was accompanied by a series of rather dubious grunts.

===Chinese===
少顷，探长把筛子从水里提起，俯身凑近了检视筛中之物。这一番检视显然没有得出十分确凿的结果，因为伴随它的，是一连串颇含疑惑的哼哧。
===Original===
At length the officer stood up, and turning to me with a genial but foxy smile, held out the sieve for my inspection.

===Chinese===
末了，那位警官直起身来，转向我，堆下一副和气而狡黠的笑脸，把筛子递过来请我过目。
===Original===
“Like to see what we have found, doctor?” said he.

===Chinese===
“想瞧瞧我们找着了什么吗，大夫？”他说。
===Original===
I thanked him and stood over the sieve. It contained the sort of litter of twigs, skeleton leaves, weed, pond-snails, dead shells, and freshwater mussels that one would expect to strain out from the mud of an ancient pond; but in addition to these there were three small bones which at first glance gave me quite a start until I saw what they were.

===Chinese===
我道了谢，俯身向着筛子。里头是那种从一口古塘的淤泥里筛出来、意料之中的一堆杂屑：细枝、只剩叶脉的枯叶、水草、池螺、空壳，还有河蚌；可除此之外，另有三块小骨，乍见之下着实叫我吃了一惊，待看清是什么，才定下神来。
===Original===
The inspector looked at me inquiringly. “H’m?” said he.

===Chinese===
探长带着询问的神气望着我。“嗯？”他说。
===Original===
“Yes,” I replied. “Very interesting.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”我答道。“很有意思。”
===Original===
“Those will be human bones, I fancy; h’m?”

===Chinese===
“这几块想来是人骨吧，嗯？”
===Original===
“I should say so undoubtedly,” I answered.

===Chinese===
“我看毫无疑问是。”我回答。
===Original===
“Now,” said the inspector, “could you say, offhand, which finger those bones belong to?”

===Chinese===
“那么，”探长说，“您能不能脱口而出地说说，这几块骨头属于哪根手指？”
===Original===
I smothered a grin (for I had been expecting this question), and answered:

===Chinese===
我憋住了笑（这一问题我原就料到了），答道：
===Original===
“I can say offhand that they don’t belong to any finger. They are the bones of the left great toe.”

===Chinese===
“我脱口而出就能说：它们不属于任何一根手指。它们是左脚大拇趾的骨头。”
===Original===
The inspector’s jaw dropped.

===Chinese===
探长的下巴耷拉了下来。
===Original===
“The deuce they are!” he muttered. “H’m. I thought they looked a bit stout.”

===Chinese===
“见鬼，竟是大拇趾！”他喃喃道。“嗯。我原就说它们瞧着有点粗。”
===Original===
“I expect,” said I, “that if you go through the mud close to where this came from you’ll find the rest of the foot.”

===Chinese===
“我料想，”我说，“您若把紧挨着这东西出土处的泥再筛一遍，就会找到那只脚的其余部分。”
===Original===
The plainclothes man proceeded at once to act on my suggestion, taking the sieve with him to save time. And sure enough, after filling it twice with the mud from the bottom of the pool, the entire skeleton of the foot was brought to light.

===Chinese===
那便衣当即依我的建议动手干了起来，为了省事，把筛子也随身带了去。果然不爽：从洼底又装满两筛泥之后，整只脚的骨架便重见了天日。
===Original===
“Now you’re happy, I suppose,” said the inspector when I had checked the bones and found them all present.

===Chinese===
“这会儿您称心如意了吧，我猜，”等我核点过那些骨头、查明确已一根不缺，探长说道。
===Original===
“I should be more happy,” I replied, “if I knew what you were searching for in this pond. You weren’t looking for the foot, were you?”

===Chinese===
“若能知道您在这口塘里找的是什么，我还要更称心呢，”我答道。“您要找的原不是这只脚吧？”
===Original===
“I was looking for anything that I might find,” he answered. “I shall go on searching until we have the whole body. I shall go through all the streams and ponds around here, excepting Connaught Water. That I shall leave to the last, as it will be a case of dredging from a boat and isn’t so likely as the smaller ponds. Perhaps the head will be there; it’s deeper than any of the others.”

===Chinese===
“我找的是不论碰到什么都行，”他答道。“我要一直搜下去，直到搜齐整具尸身。这一带所有的溪流池塘我都要筛个遍，只有康诺特湖（Connaught Water）除外。那一个我留到最后，因为那得驾船用拖网打捞，比起那些小塘来，希望也未必更大。说不定脑袋就在那儿；它比别的哪一口塘都深。”
===Original===
It now occurred to me that as I had learned all that I was likely to learn, which was little enough, I might as well leave the inspector to pursue his researches unembarrassed by my presence. Accordingly I thanked him for his assistance and departed by the way I had come.

===Chinese===
此刻我转念想到，我能打听到的已经差不多都打听到了——本来也有限得很——不如就此抽身，让探长不再因我在场而放不开手脚，径自去进行他的勘察。于是我谢过他的协助，由来路离开了。
===Original===
But as I retraced my steps along the shady path I speculated profoundly on the officer’s proceedings. My examinations of the mutilated hand had yielded the conclusion that the finger had been removed after death or shortly before, but more probably after. Someone else had evidently arrived at the same conclusion, and had communicated his opinion to Inspector Badger; for it was clear that that gentleman was in full cry after the missing finger. But why was he searching for it here when the hand had been found at Sidcup? And what did he expect to learn from it when he found it? There is nothing particularly characteristic about a finger, or, at least, the bones of one; and the object of the present researches was to determine the identity of the person of whom these bones were the remains. There was something mysterious about the affair, something suggesting that Inspector Badger was in possession of private information of some kind. But what information could he have? And whence could he have obtained it? These were questions to which I could find no answer, and I was still fruitlessly revolving them when I arrived at the modest inn where the inquest was to be held, and where I proposed to fortify myself with a correspondingly modest lunch as a preparation for my attendance at the inquiry.

===Chinese===
可是，当我沿着那条浓荫小径信步折返时，我对这位警官的行止作了一番深深的推想。我对那只残手的查验，得出的结论是：手指是死后、或至多临死前不久被截去的，而更可能在死后。显然另有一个人得出了同样的结论，并且把他的见解告知了巴杰探长；因为事情很明白，那位先生正穷追猛赶地觅那根失踪的手指。可是，那只手既是在锡德卡普发现的，他却为何偏偏到这里来搜寻？找到了它，他又指望从它身上问出些什么？一根手指本没有什么特别可供辨认之处——至少，指骨是没有的；而眼下这番勘查的目的，正是要判定这些遗骨所属的那个人的身份。这件事里透着神秘，透着一种意味，仿佛巴杰探长手里握着某种私下的消息。可他能有什么消息呢？又可能从哪里得来呢？这些问题我寻不出答案；直到我抵达那家就要开庭验尸审讯的简朴小客栈——我打算在那里用一顿同样简朴的午饭，好提起精神出席这场审讯——我还在徒然绕着圈子琢磨它们。

## XIII The Coroner’s Quest / 十三 验尸官的追查
===Original===
The proceedings of that fine old institution, the coroner’s court, are apt to have their dignity impaired by the somewhat unjudicial surroundings amidst which they are conducted. The present inquiry was to be held in a long room attached to the inn, ordinarily devoted, as its various appurtenances testified, to gatherings of a more convivial character.

===Chinese===
验尸法庭这一古老而体面的机构，其开庭程序每每因场所环境的几分不类司法，而使庄严为之减损。眼下的这场审讯，便设在客栈附设的一间长屋里；屋里种种陈设物件俱可作证：这地方平素本是供性质更为欢畅的聚会之用的。

===Original===
Hither I betook myself after a protracted lunch and a meditative pipe, and being the first to arrive﻿—the jury having already been sworn and conducted to the mortuary to view the remains﻿—whiled away the time by considering the habits of the customary occupants of the room by the light of the objects contained in it. A wooden target with one or two darts sticking in it hung on the end wall and invited the Robin Hoods of the village to try their skill; a system of incised marks on the oaken table made sinister suggestions of shove-halfpenny; and a large open box filled with white wigs, gaudily colored robes and wooden spears, swords and regalia, crudely coated with gilded paper, obviously appertained to the puerile ceremonials of the Order of Druids.

===Chinese===
一顿从容的长饭、一斗沉思的板烟之后，我便移步到此；我是到得最早的一个——陪审团已经宣誓完毕，被领去停尸房验看遗骸了——于是我便借着屋里器物所提供的线索，揣摩起这屋子惯常那些光顾者的习性来，以此消磨光阴。尽头的墙上挂着一个木靶，上头插着一两支飞镖，招引村里的罗宾汉们前来一试身手；橡木桌上一套刻划的道道，隐隐透出“推半便士”赌局的可疑痕迹；还有一口敞开的大箱，盛着白色假发、彩袍和木制的矛、剑及仪仗，都粗糙地包着金纸，一望而知是德鲁伊教团（Order of Druids）那些幼稚仪式的行头。

===Original===
I had exhausted the interest of these relics and had transferred my attentions to the picture gallery when the other spectators and the witnesses began to arrive. Hastily I seated myself in the only comfortable chair beside the one placed at the head of the table, presumably for the coroner; and I had hardly done so when the latter entered accompanied by the jury. Immediately after them came the sergeant, Inspector Badger, one or two plainclothes men, and finally the divisional surgeon.

===Chinese===
这批古旧的遗物既已看乏，我又把注意力转移到这儿的“画廊”上去，这时其余的旁听者和证人们陆续到来。我急忙在那把唯一的舒适椅子上坐了——它挨着桌子顶端那把想必为验尸官而设的椅子——我刚坐定，验尸官便由陪审团陪同进来了。随后进来的是警佐、巴杰探长、一两名便衣，最后是警区医生。

===Original===
The coroner took his seat at the head of the table and opened his book, and the jury seated themselves on a couple of benches on one side of the long table.

===Chinese===
验尸官在桌子顶端的座位上落座，翻开他的簿册；陪审员们则在长桌一侧的两条长凳上坐定。

===Original===
I looked with some interest at the twelve “good men and true.” They were a representative group of British tradesmen, quiet, attentive, and rather solemn; but my attention was particularly attracted by a small man with a very large head and a shock of upstanding hair whom I had diagnosed, after a glance at his intelligent but truculent countenance and the shiny knees of his trousers, as the village cobbler. He sat between the broad-shouldered foreman, who looked like a blacksmith, and a dogged, red-faced man whose general aspect of prosperous greasiness suggested the calling of a butcher.

===Chinese===
我饶有兴味地端详这十二位“善良而正直的人”。他们是英国市井商贩中的一班代表人物，安静、专注，而且颇为肃穆；但格外引我注意的是一个小个子男人，生着一颗极大的脑袋，一头硬发根根直立——朝他那副聪明而好斗的面容和磨得发亮的裤膝望了一眼，我便“诊断”出他是村里的鞋匠。他坐在两个人中间：一边是膀阔肩圆的陪审长，看模样像个铁匠；另一边是个执拗的红脸汉子，通身那股兴旺发亮的油腻气象，暗示出他操的是屠夫这一行。

===Original===
“The inquiry, gentlemen,” the coroner commenced, “upon which we are now entering concerns itself with two questions. The first is that of identity: who was this person whose body we have just viewed? The second is: How, when, and by what means did he come by his death? We will take the identity first and begin with the circumstances under which the body was discovered.”

===Chinese===
“诸位，”验尸官开口道，“我们此刻开始的这场审讯，涉及两个问题。第一是身份问题：我们刚才验看的这个人究竟是谁？第二是：他是怎样、何时、因何而死的？我们先从身份入手，从遗骸被发现时的情形说起。”

===Original===
Here the cobbler stood up and raised an excessively dirty hand.

===Chinese===
这时那鞋匠站起身来，举起一只脏得可以的手。

===Original===
“I rise, Mr. Chairman,” said he, “to a point of order.” The other jurymen looked at him curiously and some of them, I regret to say, grinned. “You have referred, sir,” he continued, “to the body which we have just viewed. I wish to point out that we have not viewed a body; we have viewed a collection of bones.”

===Chinese===
“主席先生，”他说，“我起身是要提出一个程序问题。”其余陪审员好奇地打量他，其中有几位——说来遗憾——咧开了嘴。“先生，”他接着说，“您方才提到我们刚才验看的那个遗体。我要指出，我们验看的并不是一具遗体；我们验看的是一批骨头。”

===Original===
“We will refer to them as the remains, if you prefer it,” said the coroner.

===Chinese===
“倘若你愿意，我们可以称它们为遗骸。”验尸官说。

===Original===
“I do prefer it,” was the reply, and the objector sat down.

===Chinese===
“我正是这个意思。”对方答道，说完便坐下了。

===Original===
“Very well,” rejoined the coroner, and he proceeded to call the witnesses, of whom the first was a laborer who had discovered the bones in the watercress-bed.

===Chinese===
“很好。”验尸官应道，随即开始传唤证人；第一个是那个在水田芥田里发现骨头的劳工。

===Original===
“Do you happen to know how long it was since the watercress-beds had been cleaned out previously?” the coroner asked, when the witness had told the story of the discovery.

===Chinese===
证人把发现的经过讲完之后，验尸官问道：“你可知道那片水田芥田上回清理干净，距今有多久了？”

===Original===
“They was cleaned out by Mr. Tapper’s orders just before he gave them up. That will be a little better than two years ago. In May it were. I helped to clean ’em. I worked on this very same place and there wasn’t no bones there then.”

===Chinese===
“那是塔珀（Tapper）先生把地退掉之前，吩咐人清理的。算来是两年前还多一点。五月里的事。我帮着清理的。我干活的正是眼下这块地，那会儿这儿可没得什么骨头。”

===Original===
The coroner glanced at the jury. “Any questions, gentlemen,” he asked.

===Chinese===
验尸官朝陪审团看了一眼。“诸位有什么问题要问吗？”他问。

===Original===
The cobbler directed an intimidating scowl at the witness and demanded:

===Chinese===
鞋匠朝证人摆出一副吓人的黑脸，质问道：

===Original===
“Were you searching for bones when you came on these remains?”

===Chinese===
“你撞见这些遗骸的时候，是不是正在找骨头？”

===Original===
“Me!” exclaimed the witness. “What should I be searching for bones for?”

===Chinese===
“我！”证人叫起来，“我找骨头干什么呀？”

===Original===
“Don’t prevaricate,” said the cobbler sternly; “answer the question: Yes or no.”

===Chinese===
“别支吾其词，”鞋匠厉声说，“回答问题：是，还是不是。”

===Original===
“No, of course I wasn’t.”

===Chinese===
“不是，我当然不是。”

===Original===
The juryman shook his enormous head dubiously as though implying that he would let it pass this time but it mustn’t happen again; and the examination of the witnesses continued, without eliciting anything that was new to me or giving rise to any incident, until the sergeant had described the finding of the right arm in the Cuckoo Pits.

===Chinese===
那位陪审员似信似疑地摇了摇他的大脑袋，那意思仿佛是说：这一回姑且容你过去，下不为例。证人的讯问便如此继续下去，既没有问出什么于我为新的东西，也没有生出任何枝节，直到警佐陈述了在杜鹃坑发现右臂的经过。

===Original===
“Was this an accidental discovery?” the coroner asked.

===Chinese===
“这一次可是偶然发现的？”验尸官问。

===Original===
“No. We had instructions from Scotland Yard to search any likely ponds in this neighborhood.”

===Chinese===
“不是。我们奉苏格兰场的指示，要把这一带所有可能藏东西的池塘都搜一遍。”

===Original===
The coroner discreetly forbore to press this matter any further, but my friend the cobbler was evidently on the qui vive, and I anticipated a brisk cross-examination for Mr. Badger when his turn came. The inspector was apparently of the same opinion, for I saw him cast a glance of the deepest malevolence at the too inquiring disciple of St. Crispin. In fact, his turn came next, and the cobbler’s hair stood up with unholy joy.

===Chinese===
验尸官识趣地住口，不再深究此事；可是我那位鞋匠朋友分明已竖起了耳朵，我预料等轮到巴杰先生作证时，一场干脆利落的盘诘在所难免。探长显然所见相同，因为我看见他朝圣克里平（St. Crispin）这位过分好问的门徒投去了怨毒至深的一瞥。果然，下一个便轮到了他，鞋匠的头发也随即带着一股邪乎的狂喜根根倒竖起来。

===Original===
The finding of the lower half of the trunk in Staple’s Pond at Loughton was the inspector’s own achievement, but he was not boastful about it. The discovery, he remarked, followed naturally on the previous one in the Cuckoo Pits.

===Chinese===
在洛顿的斯特普尔池里找到躯干的下半截，是探长本人的功绩，但他并不以此自夸。他说，这一发现是杜鹃坑前一次发现之后顺理成章的结果。

===Original===
“Had you any private information that led you to search this particular neighborhood?” the cobbler asked.

===Chinese===
“你可是得了什么私下的消息，才到这个特定地方来搜查的？”鞋匠问。

===Original===
“We had no private information whatever,” replied Badger.

===Chinese===
“我们什么私下消息也没有。”巴杰答道。

===Original===
“Now I put it to you,” pursued the juryman, shaking a forensic, and very dirty, forefinger at the inspector; “here are certain remains found at Sidcup; here are certain other remains found at St. Mary Cray, and certain others at Lee. All those places are in Kent. Now isn’t it very remarkable that you should come straight down to Epping Forest, which is in Essex, and search for those bones and find ’em?”

===Chinese===
“那我把话挑明，”陪审员追问，一面朝探长摇晃着一根深具法庭风度、也脏得可以的食指；“这儿是一些遗骸，是在锡德卡普发现的；那儿是另一些，是在圣玛丽克雷发现的，还有一些在李。这些地方全在肯特郡。而你们却径直下到埃平森林——那可是在埃塞克斯——来找这些骨头，还把它们找到了，这难道不非常蹊跷么？”

===Original===
“We were making a systematic search of all likely places,” replied Badger.

===Chinese===
“我们当时正对所有可能的地方进行系统的搜查。”巴杰答道。

===Original===
“Exactly,” said the cobbler, with a ferocious grin, “that’s just my point. I say, isn’t it very funny that, after finding the remains in Kent some twenty miles from here, with the River Thames between, you should come here to look for the bones and go straight to Staple’s Pond, where they happen to be﻿—and find ’em?”

===Chinese===
“着啊，”鞋匠咧嘴狞笑，“这正是我要说的。我问你：在肯特郡——离这儿约莫二十英里，中间还隔着一条泰晤士河——发现了那些遗骸之后，你们却赶到这儿来找骨头，而且径直奔到斯特普尔池，骨头偏偏就在那儿——还把它们找到了，你说这可笑不可笑？”

===Original===
“It would have been more funny,” Badger replied sourly, “if we’d gone straight to a place where they happened *not* to be﻿—and found them.”

===Chinese===
“要是我们径直奔到一个骨头偏偏*不*在的地方——还把它们找到了，那才更可笑呢。”巴杰酸溜溜地答道。

===Original===
A gratified snigger arose from the other eleven good men and true, and the cobbler grinned savagely; but before he could think of a suitable rejoinder the coroner interposed.

===Chinese===
其余十一位善良而正直的人发出一阵称快的窃笑，鞋匠也咧嘴狞笑；但他还没想出一句得体的回敬，验尸官便插话了。

===Original===
“The question is not very material,” he said, “and we mustn’t embarrass the police by unnecessary inquiries.”

===Chinese===
“这个问题不甚切要，”他说，“我们不该用不必要的盘问使警方为难。”

===Original===
“It’s my belief,” said the cobbler, “that he knew they were there all the time.”

===Chinese===
“依我看，”鞋匠说，“他早就知道骨头一直在那儿。”

===Original===
“The witness has stated that he had no private information,” said the coroner; and he proceeded to take the rest of the inspector’s evidence, watched closely by the critical juror.

===Chinese===
“证人已经声明，他没有任何私下的消息。”验尸官说；随后他继续听取探长证词的其余部分，那位爱挑剔的陪审员则在一旁紧盯不放。

===Original===
The account of the finding of the remains having been given in full, the police surgeon was called and sworn; the jurymen straightened their backs with an air of expectancy, and I turned over a page of my notebook.

===Chinese===
发现遗骸的经过已尽数陈述完毕，警方医生被传上来宣誓作证；陪审员们一个个挺直了腰背，露出一副有所期待的神气，我也把笔记本翻到了新的一页。

===Original===
“You have examined the bones at present lying in the mortuary and forming the subject of this inquiry?” the coroner asked.

===Chinese===
“你已经查验过那些现停放在停尸房、构成本案审讯对象的骨头了吧？”验尸官问。

===Original===
“I have.”

===Chinese===
“查验过了。”

===Original===
“Will you kindly tell us what you have observed?”

===Chinese===
“请把你观察到的情况告诉我们，好吗？”

===Original===
“I find that the bones are human bones, and are, in my opinion, all parts of the same person. They form a skeleton which is complete with the exception of the skull, the third finger of the left hand, the kneecaps, and the leg-bones﻿—I mean the bones between the knees and the ankles.”

===Chinese===
“我查明这些骨头是人的骨头，而且依我之见，都属于同一个人。它们构成一副骨架，只除了头骨、左手的第三根手指、膝盖骨和小腿骨——我是说膝盖和脚踝之间的那几根骨头。”

===Original===
“Is there anything to account for the absence of the missing finger?”

===Chinese===
“有什么迹象可以解释那根手指的缺失吗？”

===Original===
“No. There is no deformity and no sign of its having been amputated during life. In my opinion it was removed after death.”

===Chinese===
“没有。没有畸形，也没有生前截除的迹象。依我看，它是死后才被取下来的。”

===Original===
“Can you give us any description of the deceased?”

===Chinese===
“你能向我们描述一下死者的形貌吗？”

===Original===
“I should say that these are the bones of an elderly man, probably over sixty years of age, about five feet eight and a half inches in height, of rather stout build, fairly muscular, and well preserved. There are no signs of disease excepting some old-standing rheumatic gout of the right hip-joint.”

===Chinese===
“依我看，这些是一个上了年纪的男人的骨头，年纪大概在六十开外，身高约五英尺八英寸半，体格颇为壮实，肌肉相当发达，保存良好。除了右髋关节有些陈年的风湿性痛风之外，没有任何疾病的迹象。”

===Original===
“Can you form any opinion as to the cause of death?”

===Chinese===
“关于死因，你能形成什么看法吗？”

===Original===
“No. There are no marks of violence or signs of injury. But it will be impossible to form any opinion as to the cause of death until we have seen the skull.”

===Chinese===
“不能。骨头上没有暴力的痕迹，也没有损伤的迹象。但是，在没有见到头骨之前，要对死因形成任何看法都是不可能的。”

===Original===
“Did you note anything else of importance?”

===Chinese===
“你还注意到别的什么要紧之处吗？”

===Original===
“Yes. I was struck by the appearance of anatomical knowledge and skill on the part of the person who dismembered the body. The knowledge of anatomy is proved by the fact that the corpse has been divided into definite anatomical regions. For instance, the bones of the neck are complete and include the top joint of the backbone known as the atlas; whereas a person without anatomical knowledge would probably take off the head by cutting through the neck. Then the arms have been separated with the scapula (or shoulder-blade) and clavicle (or collarbone) attached, just as an arm would be removed for dissection.

===Chinese===
“注意到了。肢解尸体的人所表现的解剖学知识与技巧，令我印象深刻。解剖学知识由这样一桩事实证明：尸身是按明确的解剖部位分割开来的。譬如，颈部的骨头齐全完整，还包括脊柱最顶上的那一节，即所谓寰椎（atlas）；换了没有解剖学知识的人，多半是齐颈一刀把头砍下了事。再者，两臂是被连着肩胛骨（scapula）和锁骨（clavicle）一并分离下来的，正如解剖时摘取上肢的那种做法。

===Original===
“The skill is shown by the neat way in which the dismemberment has been carried out. The parts have not been rudely hacked asunder, but have been separated at the joints so skilfully that I have not discovered a single scratch or mark of the knife on any of the bones.”

===Chinese===
“至于技巧，则见于肢解手法的干净利落。尸身的各部分并非被粗暴地乱剁乱劈，而是在关节处一一分离，其手法之精熟，竟使我在任何一块骨头上都找不出一道刀痕或刻痕。”

===Original===
“Can you suggest any class of person who would be likely to possess the knowledge and skill to which you refer?”

===Chinese===
“你能设想出哪一类人，可能具备你所说的这种知识和技巧吗？”

===Original===
“It would, of course, be possessed by a surgeon or medical student, and possibly by a butcher.”

===Chinese===
“这种本领，外科医生或医科学生自然具备，屠夫也可能具备。”

===Original===
“You think that the person who dismembered this body may have been a surgeon or a medical student?”

===Chinese===
“那么你认为，肢解这具尸体的人，可能是个外科医生或医科学生？”

===Original===
“Yes; or a butcher. Someone accustomed to the dismemberment of bodies and skilful with the knife.”

===Chinese===
“是的；或者是个屠夫。一个惯于肢解躯体、并且使刀娴熟的人。”

===Original===
Here the cobbler suddenly rose to his feet.

===Chinese===
这时鞋匠霍地站了起来。

===Original===
“I rise, Mr. Chairman,” said he, “to protest against the statement that has just been made.”

===Chinese===
“主席先生，”他说，“我站起来，是要抗议方才所做的那项陈述。”

===Original===
“What statement?” demanded the coroner.

===Chinese===
“什么陈述？”验尸官问。

===Original===
“Against the aspersion,” continued the cobbler, with an oratorical flourish, “that has been cast upon a honorable calling.”

===Chinese===
“抗议那种诽谤，”鞋匠拿出一副演说家的腔调继续说道，“对我们一个体面行当的诽谤。”

===Original===
“I don’t understand you,” said the coroner.

===Chinese===
“我不明白你的意思。”验尸官说。

===Original===
“Doctor Summers has insinuated that this murder was committed by a butcher. Now a member of that honorable calling is sitting on this jury﻿—”

===Chinese===
“萨默斯（Summers）医生影射说，这桩凶案是一个屠夫干的。而此刻，那个体面行当的一位成员，正坐在这陪审团里——”

===Original===
“You let me alone,” growled the butcher.

===Chinese===
“你少来惹我。”屠夫咕噜道。

===Original===
“I will not let you alone,” persisted the cobbler. “I desire﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我偏要管，”鞋匠不依不饶，“我要求——”

===Original===
“Oh, shut up, Pope!” This was from the foreman, who, at the same moment, reached out an enormous hairy hand with which he grabbed the cobbler’s coattails and brought him into a sitting posture with a thump that shook the room.

===Chinese===
“咳，住嘴吧，波普（Pope）！”说话的是陪审长；与此同时他伸出一只毛茸茸的大手，一把攥住鞋匠的衣摆，咚地一声把他按坐下去，震得满屋子直颤。

===Original===
But Mr. Pope, though seated, was not silenced. “I desire,” he said, “to have my protest put on record.”

===Chinese===
可是波普先生人虽坐下，嘴却不曾闭上。“我要求，”他说，“把我的抗议记录在案。”

===Original===
“I can’t do that,” said the coroner, “and I can’t allow you to interrupt the witnesses.”

===Chinese===
“这个我办不到，”验尸官说，“我也不能容许你打断证人。”

===Original===
“I am acting,” said Mr. Pope, “in the interests of my friend here and the members of a honorable﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我这是，”波普先生说，“在维护我这位朋友的利益，还有这个体面——”

===Original===
But here the butcher turned on him savagely, and, in a hoarse stage-whisper, exclaimed:

===Chinese===
可这时屠夫凶狠地朝他转过脸去，哑着嗓子、用舞台上的耳语声嚷道：

===Original===
“Look here, Pope; you’ve got too much of what the cat licks﻿—”

===Chinese===
“你听着，波普；你那猫儿舔的东西实在是太多了——”

===Original===
“Gentlemen! gentlemen!” the coroner protested sternly; “I cannot permit this unseemly conduct. You are forgetting the solemnity of the occasion and your own responsible positions. I must insist on more decent and decorous behavior.”

===Chinese===
“诸位！诸位！”验尸官严厉地申斥道，“我不能容许这种有失体统的举动。你们忘了这场合的庄严，也忘了你们自己身负的重责。我必须要求诸位举止得更像样、更规矩些。”

===Original===
There was profound silence, in the midst of which the butcher concluded in the same hoarse whisper:

===Chinese===
全场一片死寂；就在这一片寂静之中，屠夫用同样沙哑的耳语说完了那句话：

===Original===
“﻿—licks ’er paws with.”

===Chinese===
“——猫儿舔爪子用的那玩意儿。”

===Original===
The coroner cast a withering glance at him, and, turning to the witness, resumed the examination.

===Chinese===
验尸官朝他投去一记令人无地自容的眼色，随即转向证人，继续讯问。

===Original===
“Can you tell us, Doctor, how long a time has elapsed since the death of the deceased?”

===Chinese===
“医生，你能否告诉我们，死者死去至今已有多长时间了？”

===Original===
“I should say not less than eighteen months, but probably more. How much more it is impossible from inspection alone to say. The bones are perfectly clean﻿—that is, clean of all soft structures﻿—and will remain substantially in their present condition for many years.”

===Chinese===
“依我看，不少于十八个月，多半还不止。究竟多出多少，单凭检视是无从断言的。这些骨头干净得很——也就是说，一切软组织都已荡然无存——往后许多年里，大体上都将保持眼下的状态。”

===Original===
“The evidence of the man who found the remains in the watercress-bed suggests that they could not have been there for more than two years. Do the appearances in your opinion agree with that view?”

===Chinese===
“在水田芥田里发现遗骸的那位证人供称，遗骸在那儿的时间不可能超过两年。依你看，这些骨头的外观与这一说法相符吗？”

===Original===
“Yes; perfectly.”

===Chinese===
“相符；完全相符。”

===Original===
“There is one more point, Doctor; a very important one. Do you find anything in any of the bones, or all of them together, which would enable you to identify them as the bones of any particular individual?”

===Chinese===
“医生，还有一点；是非常要紧的一点。这些骨头之中——无论单独哪一块，还是合在一起——你可曾发现什么，能使你把它们认作某一个特定个人的骨头？”

===Original===
“No,” replied Dr. Summers; “I found no peculiarity that could furnish the means of personal identification.”

===Chinese===
“没有，”萨默斯医生答道，“我没有发现任何可供个人识别之用的特征。”

===Original===
“The description of a missing individual has been given to us,” said the coroner; “a man, fifty-nine years of age, five feet eight inches in height, healthy, well preserved, rather broad in build, and having an old Pott’s fracture of the left ankle. Do the remains that you have examined agree with that description?”

===Chinese===
“我们收到过一份失踪者的描述，”验尸官说，“是个男人，五十九岁，身高五英尺八英寸，健康，保养得宜，体格相当宽阔，左踝有一处陈旧的波特氏骨折。你所查验的这副遗骸，与那份描述相符么？”

===Original===
“Yes, so far as agreement is possible. There is no disagreement.”

===Chinese===
“相符，在可能相符的范围之内。没有任何不符之处。”

===Original===
“The remains might be those of that individual?”

===Chinese===
“那么这些遗骸可能就是那个人的？”

===Original===
“They might; but there is no positive evidence that they are. The description would apply to a large proportion of elderly men, except as to the fracture.”

===Chinese===
“可能；但是没有确凿的证据表明正是。这份描述可以套在很大一部分上了年纪的男人身上，只有骨折一项是例外。”

===Original===
“You found no signs of such a fracture?”

===Chinese===
“你没有发现这种骨折的痕迹？”

===Original===
“No. Pott’s fracture affects the bone called the fibula. That is one of the bones that has not yet been found, so there is no evidence on that point. The left foot was quite normal, but then it would be in any case, unless the fracture had resulted in great deformity.”

===Chinese===
“没有。波特氏骨折损及的那根骨头叫作腓骨。腓骨恰是至今尚未寻获的骨头之一，所以这一点上并无证据可考。左脚完全正常，不过话说回来，除非骨折造成了严重畸形，左脚无论如何都会是正常的。”

===Original===
“You estimated the height of the deceased as half an inch greater than that of the missing person. Does that constitute a disagreement?”

===Chinese===
“你对死者身高的估计，比失踪者高出了半英寸。这算不算一处不符？”

===Original===
“No; my estimate is only approximate. As the arms are complete and the legs are not, I have based my calculations on the width across the two arms. But measurement of the thighbones gives the same result. The length of the thighbones is one foot seven inches and five-eighths.”

===Chinese===
“不算；我的估计只是个约数。由于两臂完整而两腿不全，我的计算以两臂平张的宽度为据。但股骨的量度也得出了同一结果。股骨的长度为一英尺七英寸又八分之五。”

===Original===
“So the deceased might not have been taller than five feet eight?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，死者的身高也可能不超过五英尺八英寸？”

===Original===
“That is so; from five feet eight to five feet nine.”

===Chinese===
“正是；在五英尺八英寸到五英尺九英寸之间。”

===Original===
“Thank you. I think that is all we want to ask you, Doctor; unless the jury wish to put any questions.”

===Chinese===
“谢谢。我想我们要问你的就这么多，医生；除非陪审团诸位还想提问。”

===Original===
He glanced uneasily at that august body, and instantly the irrepressible Pope rose to the occasion.

===Chinese===
他惴惴不安地朝那个威严的团体瞥了一眼，那位按捺不住的波普立刻应声而起，抓住了这个机会。

===Original===
“About that finger that is missing,” said the cobbler. “You say that it was cut off after death?”

===Chinese===
“说说那根缺掉的手指，”鞋匠说，“你是说，它是死后砍掉的？”

===Original===
“That is my opinion.”

===Chinese===
“那是我的看法。”

===Original===
“Now can you tell us why it was cut off?”

===Chinese===
“那么你能不能告诉我们，它为什么被砍掉？”

===Original===
“No, I cannot.”

===Chinese===
“不能，我说不上。”

===Original===
“Oh, come now, Doctor Summers, you must have formed some opinion on the subject.”

===Chinese===
“嗳，得了吧，萨默斯医生，这件事上你总归是有些看法的。”

===Original===
Here the coroner interposed. “The Doctor is only concerned with the evidence arising out of the actual examination of the remains. Any personal opinions or conjectures that he may have formed are not evidence, and he must not be asked about them.”

===Chinese===
验尸官这时插话了。“医生只负责与遗骸实际查验相关的证据。他个人可能形成的任何意见或推测都不属于证据，诸位不得就此向他提问。”

===Original===
“But, sir,” objected Pope, “we want to know why that finger was cut off. It couldn’t have been took off for no reason. May I ask, sir, if the person who is missing had anything peculiar about that finger?”

===Chinese===
“可是，先生，”波普抗辩道，“我们想知道那根手指为什么被砍掉。它总不至于无缘无故就被剁掉吧。请问先生，那位失踪的人，那根手指上可曾有什么特别的地方？”

===Original===
“Nothing is stated to that effect in the written description,” replied the coroner.

===Chinese===
“书面的描述里没有这样的记载。”验尸官答道。

===Original===
“Perhaps,” suggested Pope, “Inspector Badger can tell us.”

===Chinese===
“要不然，”波普提议道，“巴杰探长兴许能告诉我们。”

===Original===
“I think,” said the coroner, “we had better not ask the police too many questions. They will tell us anything that they wish to be made public.”

===Chinese===
“我看，”验尸官说，“我们最好还是别向警方提太多的问题。凡他们愿意公之于众的事情，都会告诉我们的。”

===Original===
“Oh, very well,” snapped the cobbler. “If it’s a matter of hushing it up I’ve got no more to say; only I don’t see how we are to arrive at a verdict if we don’t have the facts put before us.”

===Chinese===
“噢，很好，”鞋匠抢白道，“既然是要捂盖子，我就没有别的可说了；只是我不明白，若不把事实摆在我们面前，这裁决叫我们怎么个下法。”

===Original===
All the witnesses having now been examined, the coroner proceeded to sum up and address the jury.

===Chinese===
证人至此已全部讯问完毕，验尸官便着手作总结陈词，向陪审团致辞。

===Original===
“You have heard the evidence, gentlemen, of the various witnesses, and you will have perceived that it does not enable us to answer either of the questions that form the subject of this inquiry. We now know that the deceased was an elderly man, about sixty years of age, and about five feet eight to nine in height; and that his death took place from eighteen months to two years ago. That is all we know. From the treatment to which the body has been subjected we may form conjectures as to the circumstances of his death. But we have no actual knowledge. We do not know who the deceased was or how he came by his death. Consequently, it will be necessary to adjourn this inquiry until fresh facts are available, and as soon as that is the case, you will receive due notice that your attendance is required.”

===Chinese===
“诸位，你们已经听了各位证人的证词，想必也看出来了：凭这些证词，我们回答不了构成本案的两个问题中的任何一个。如今我们知道的是：死者是个上了年纪的男人，约六十岁上下，身高约在五英尺八至九英寸之间；他的死亡发生在大约十八个月到两年之前。我们所知的尽在于此。从这具尸身所受的处置，我们未尝不能对其死亡的种种情由加以推测。但我们并没有确实的知识。我们不知道死者是谁，也不知道他是如何死的。因此，这场审讯必须延期，以待新的事实；一旦有了新的事实，你们将会及时接到通知，前来出席。

===Original===
The silence of the Court gave place to the confused noise of moving chairs and a general outbreak of eager talk amidst which I rose and made my way out into the street. At the door I encountered Dr. Summers, whose dogcart was waiting close by.

===Chinese===
法庭的寂静让位给搬动椅子的乱响和骤然四起的急切交谈；我趁乱起身，挤出人群来到街上。在门前撞见了萨默斯医生，他的双轮马车正候在近旁。

===Original===
“Are you going back to town now?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“你这就要回城里去？”他问。

===Original===
“Yes,” I answered; “as soon as I can catch a train.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我答道，“只要赶得上一班火车就走。”

===Original===
“If you jump into my cart I’ll run you down in time for the five-one. You’ll miss it if you walk.”

===Chinese===
“跳上我的车来吧，我送你下去，赶五点零一分那班还来得及。走着去可要误了。”

===Original===
I accepted his offer thankfully, and a minute later was spinning briskly down the road to the station.

===Chinese===
我感激地领了他的情，一分钟后，马车已顺着通往车站的大路轻快地疾驰而下。

===Original===
“Queer little devil, that man Pope,” Dr. Summers remarked. “Quite a character; a socialist, laborite, agitator, general crank; anything for a row.”

===Chinese===
“波普那个人，真是个古怪的小魔鬼，”萨默斯医生说道。“十足的一个角色：社会主义者、工党分子、煽动家、样样俱全的怪人；只要有热闹可闹，什么都干。”

===Original===
“Yes,” I answered; “that was what his appearance suggested. It must be trying for the coroner to get a truculent rascal like that on a jury.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我答道，“他的相貌本也透着这股劲儿。验尸官碰上这么一个凶横的刺儿头进了陪审团，想必很够受的。”

===Original===
Summers laughed. “I don’t know. He supplies the comic relief. And then, you know, those fellows have their uses. Some of his questions were pretty pertinent.”

===Chinese===
萨默斯笑了。“那倒未必。有他在，倒给这场面添些滑稽的调剂。再说，你知道，这号人物自有他们的用处。他的有些问题，问得还挺中肯。”

===Original===
“So Badger seemed to think.”

===Chinese===
“巴杰看来也是这么想的。”

===Original===
“Yes, by Jove,” chuckled Summers. “Badger didn’t like him a bit; and I suspect the worthy inspector was sailing pretty close to the wind in his answers.”

===Chinese===
“可不是么，”萨默斯呵呵笑道，“巴杰一点儿也不喜欢他；而且我疑心那位可敬的探长答起话来，简直是贴着谎话的边儿在走呢。”

===Original===
“You think he really has some private information?”

===Chinese===
“你认为他真有什么私下的消息？”

===Original===
“Depends upon what you mean by ‘information.’ The police are not a speculative body. They wouldn’t be taking all this trouble unless they had a pretty straight tip from somebody. How are Mr. and Miss Bellingham? I used to know them when they lived here.”

===Chinese===
“那要看你说的‘消息’作何解了。警方可不是凭空揣测的团体。若不是从什么人那里得了一条相当可靠的线报，他们才犯不上这么大费周章。贝林厄姆先生和贝林厄姆小姐都好吗？他们住在这儿的时候，我同他们是相熟的。”

===Original===
I was considering a discreet answer to this question when we swept into the station yard. At the same moment the train drew up at the platform, and, with a hurried handshake and hastily spoken thanks, I sprang from the dogcart and darted into the station.

===Chinese===
我正斟酌一个得体的回答，马车已一掠驶进了车站的院子。恰在同一刻，火车靠上了站台；我来不及多礼，匆匆一握、急急道谢，便跳下双轮马车，一头扎进了车站。

===Original===
During the rather slow journey homeward I read over my notes and endeavored to extract from the facts they set forth some significance other than that which lay on the surface, but without much success. Then I fell to speculating on what Thorndyke would think of the evidence at the inquest and whether he would be satisfied with the information that I had collected. These speculations lasted me, with occasional digressions, until I arrived at the Temple and ran up the stairs rather eagerly to my friends’ chambers.

===Chinese===
归途的车行相当迟缓；我把笔记从头温了一遍，想从其所载的事实里提炼出一点表面之下别的意涵，却收效不大。随后我又转而揣想：桑代克会如何看待验尸审讯上的这些证据？他对我收集到的情报是否满意？这些揣想伴了我一路，间或岔开几笔，直到我抵达内殿，颇为急切地跑上楼梯，奔向两位朋友的寓所。

===Original===
But here a disappointment awaited me. The nest was empty with the exception of Polton, who appeared at the laboratory door in his white apron, with a pair of flat-nosed pliers in his hands.

===Chinese===
然而这里有一场失望正等着我。窝里空空如也，只有波尔顿在；他系着白围裙出现在实验室门口，手里握着一把平嘴钳。

===Original===
“The Doctor had to go down to Bristol to consult over an urgent case,” he explained, “and Doctor Jervis has gone with him. They’ll be away a day or two, I expect, but the Doctor left this note for you.”

===Chinese===
“博士临时得赶往布里斯托尔（Bristol）会诊一桩急症，”他解释道，“杰维斯医生也跟他去了。我估摸着他们要离开一两天，不过博士给您留了这封短信。”

===Original===
He took a letter from the shelf, where it had been stood conspicuously on edge, and handed it to me. It was a short note from Thorndyke apologizing for his sudden departure and asking me to give Polton my notes with any comments that I had to make.

===Chinese===
他从架子上取下一封信——那信端端正正竖在那儿，摆在显眼处——递到我手上。这是桑代克的一封短简，为他的突然出行致歉，并嘱我把笔记，连同我有什么要提的意见，一概交给波尔顿。

===Original===
“You will be interested to learn,” he added, “that the application will be heard in the Probate Court the day after tomorrow. I shall not be present, of course, nor will Jervis, so I should like you to attend and keep your eyes open for anything that may happen during the hearing and that may not appear in the notes that Marchmont’s clerk will be instructed to take. I have retained Dr. Payne to stand by and help you with the practise, so that you can attend the Court with a clear conscience.”

===Chinese===
“你会有兴趣知道，”他接着写道，“那宗申请后日便将在遗嘱检验法院开庭听审。我自然不能到场，杰维斯也不能；所以我想请你去出席，睁大眼睛，留意听审过程中发生的、而受嘱记录的马奇蒙特书记员的笔录未必会载入的任何事情。我已延请佩恩（Payne）医生守着，替你照应诊所业务，这样你便可以问心无愧地出庭了。”

===Original===
This was highly flattering and quite atoned for the small disappointment; with deep gratification at the trust that Thorndyke had reposed in me, I pocketed the letter, handed my notes to Polton, wished him “Good evening,” and betook myself to Fetter Lane.

===Chinese===
这实在是一份莫大的荣宠，足以补偿方才那点小小的失望了；桑代克对我如此信托，我深感快慰，于是把信揣进衣袋，把笔记交与波尔顿，向他道了一声“晚上好”，便取路回菲特巷去了。

## XIV Which Carries the Reader Into the Probate Court / 十四 领读者进入遗嘱检验法院
===Original===
The Probate Court wore an air of studious repose when I entered with Miss Bellingham and her father. Apparently the great and inquisitive public had not become aware of the proceedings that were about to take place, or had not realized their connection with the sensational “Mutilation Case”; but barristers and Pressmen, better informed, had gathered in some strength, and the hum of their conversation filled the air like the droning of the voluntary that ushers in a cathedral service.

===Chinese===
我陪着贝林厄姆小姐和她的父亲走进遗嘱检验法院时，庭中一派潜心静穆的气象。看来，那人数众多、好事好奇的公众还不曾得知即将在此开庭的这桩案子，或者不曾想到它与轰动一时的“肢解案”之间的关联；倒是消息更为灵通的出庭律师和报界人士已经结伙而来，人数颇众，他们的谈话嗡嗡营营，弥漫全庭，宛如大教堂礼拜开场前大风琴序曲的低鸣。

===Original===
As we entered, a pleasant-faced, elderly gentleman rose and came forward to meet us, shaking Mr. Bellingham’s hand cordially and saluting Miss Bellingham with a courtly bow.

===Chinese===
我们一进门，一位面目和善的老绅士便起身迎上前来，热诚地与贝林厄姆先生握手，又向贝林厄姆小姐行了一个温文尔雅的鞠躬礼。

===Original===
“This is Mr. Marchmont, Doctor,” said the former, introducing me; and the solicitor, having thanked me for the trouble I had taken in attending at the inquest, led us to a bench, at the farther end of which was seated a gentleman whom I recognized as Mr. Hurst.

===Chinese===
“这位是马奇蒙特先生，医生。”前者介绍道。这位事务律师对我不辞辛劳出席验尸审讯表示了谢意，随即领我们到一条长凳前；凳子的另一头坐着一位绅士，我认出正是赫斯特先生。

===Original===
Mr. Bellingham recognized him at the same moment and glared at him wrathfully.

===Chinese===
贝林厄姆先生也在同一刻认出了他，怒冲冲地瞪着他。

===Original===
“I see that scoundrel is here!” he exclaimed in a distinctly audible voice, “pretending that he doesn’t see me, because he is ashamed to look me in the face, but﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我看见那个恶棍也在这儿！”他嗓门清清楚楚让人听得见地嚷道，“装作没瞧见我，因为他没脸正眼看我，可是——”

===Original===
“Hush! hush! my dear sir,” exclaimed the horrified solicitor; “we mustn’t talk like that, especially in this place. Let me beg you﻿—let me entreat you to control your feelings, to make no indiscreet remarks; in fact, to make no remarks at all,” he added, with the evident conviction that any remarks that Mr. Bellingham might make would be certain to be indiscreet.

===Chinese===
“嘘！嘘！亲爱的先生，”吓坏了的事务律师急忙叫道，“这种话可说不得，尤其是在这个地方。我求您——我恳求您克制自己的情绪，别说轻率的话；干脆，什么话都别说。”他又添了一句，显然深信贝林厄姆先生但凡开口，必定轻率无疑。

===Original===
“Forgive me, Marchmont,” Mr. Bellingham replied contritely. “I will control myself: I will really be quite discreet. I won’t even look at him again﻿—because, if I do, I shall probably go over and pull his nose.”

===Chinese===
“原谅我，马奇蒙特，”贝林厄姆先生悔愧地答道，“我会克制的：我当真会十分谨慎。我连再看他一眼都不看——因为，若是看了，我多半会走过去拧他的鼻子。”

===Original===
This form of discretion did not appear to be quite to Mr. Marchmont’s liking, for he took the precaution of insisting that Miss Bellingham and I should sit on the farther side of his client, and thus effectually separate him from his enemy.

===Chinese===
这一种谨慎看来不大合马奇蒙特先生的心意，因为他慎重其事，坚持要贝林厄姆小姐和我坐在他当事人的另一侧，如此便把他与他的仇人有效地隔开了。

===Original===
“Who’s the long-nosed fellow talking to Jellicoe?” Mr. Bellingham asked.

===Chinese===
“跟杰利科说话的那个长鼻子家伙是谁？”贝林厄姆先生问。

===Original===
“That is Mr. Loram, K.C., Mr. Hurst’s counsel; and the convivial-looking gentleman next to him is our counsel, Mr. Heath, a most able man and”﻿—here Mr. Marchmont whispered behind his hand﻿—“fully instructed by Doctor Thorndyke.”

===Chinese===
“那是御用大律师（K.C.）洛拉姆（Loram）先生，赫斯特先生的出庭律师；他旁边那位一脸宴乐相的绅士，是我们这边的出庭律师希思（Heath）先生，一位极其干练的人物，而且——”说到这里，马奇蒙特先生把手拢在嘴边悄悄说道——“得到了桑代克博士的周密指点。”

===Original===
At this juncture the judge entered and took his seat; the usher proceeded with great rapidity to swear in the jury, and the Court gradually settled down into that state of academic quiet which it maintained throughout the proceedings, excepting when the noisy swing-doors were set oscillating by some bustling clerk or reporter.

===Chinese===
正当此时，法官进庭落座；庭吏以极快的速度引陪审团宣誓，法庭便渐渐沉入一种学院式的静穆之中——整个庭审期间它始终保持着这份静穆，只是偶尔被哪个忙碌的书记官或记者撞得那两扇吵闹的弹簧门来回摇晃时，才被打破片刻。

===Original===
The judge was a somewhat singular-looking old gentleman, very short as to his face and very long as to his mouth; which peculiarities, together with a pair of large and bulging eyes (which he usually kept closed), suggested a certain resemblance to a frog. And he had a curious frog-like trick of flattening his eyelids﻿—as if in the act of swallowing a large beetle﻿—which was the only outward and visible sign of emotion that he ever displayed.

===Chinese===
这位法官是个模样颇为奇特的老绅士：脸极短，嘴极长；这份殊相，加上一双凸出的大眼睛（他通常闭而不睁），令人觉得他与青蛙有几分相像。他还有一种古怪的、青蛙式的习性——会把眼睑抹平，仿佛正在吞咽一只大甲虫——这是他唯一显露于外、肉眼可见的情绪表示。

===Original===
As soon as the swearing in of the jury was completed Mr. Loram rose to introduce the case; whereupon his lordship leaned back in his chair and closed his eyes, as if bracing himself for a painful operation.

===Chinese===
陪审团宣誓一毕，洛拉姆先生便起立介绍案情；法官大人当即往椅背上一靠，闭上了眼睛，仿佛在为一场痛苦的手术打定主意。

===Original===
“The present proceedings,” Mr. Loram explained, “are occasioned by the unaccountable disappearance of Mr. John Bellingham, of 141, Queen Square, Bloomsbury, which occurred about two years ago, or, to be more precise, on the twenty-third of November, nineteen hundred and two. Since that date nothing has been heard of Mr. Bellingham, and, as there are certain substantial reasons for believing him to be dead, the principal beneficiary under his will, Mr. George Hurst, is now applying to the Court for permission to presume the death of the testator and prove the will. As the time which has elapsed since the testator was last seen alive is only two years, the application is based upon the circumstances of the disappearance, which were, in many respects, very singular, the most remarkable feature of that disappearance being, perhaps, its suddenness and completeness.”

===Chinese===
“眼下的这起诉讼，”洛拉姆先生解释道，“是由布鲁姆斯伯里皇后广场 141 号的约翰·贝林厄姆先生那匪夷所思的失踪引起的。失踪发生在大约两年前，或者更确切地说，是一九〇二年十一月二十三日。自那日起，贝林厄姆先生音信全无；而既有若干实质理由相信他已身亡，其遗嘱的主要受益人乔治·赫斯特先生现在向本庭提出申请，请求推定立遗嘱人死亡并验证遗嘱。由于立遗嘱人最后一次被人亲眼见到至今只有两年，此项申请的依据便是失踪的种种情形——那些情形在许多方面都极为离奇，而其中最堪注目的一点，或许又是失踪的猝然与彻底。”

===Original===
Here the judge remarked in a still, small voice that “It would, perhaps, have been even more remarkable if the testator had disappeared gradually and incompletely.”

===Chinese===
这时法官用一种细弱而平静的声音评说道：“倘若立遗嘱人是渐渐地、不彻底地失踪的，那或许还要更加离奇些。”

===Original===
“No doubt, my lord,” agreed Mr. Loram; “but the point is that the testator, whose habits had always been regular and orderly, disappeared on the date mentioned without having made any of the usual provisions for the conduct of his affairs, and has not since then been seen or heard of.”

===Chinese===
“诚然，大人，”洛拉姆先生表示同意，“但问题在于：立遗嘱人素来习惯规律、行事有序，却在上述日期失踪，事先对其事务的处置未作任何惯常安排，此后便再没有人见过他、听过他的音讯。”

===Original===
With this preamble Mr. Loram proceeded to give a narrative of the events connected with the disappearance of John Bellingham, which was substantially identical with that which I had read in the newspapers; and having laid the actual facts before the jury, he went on to discuss their probable import.

===Chinese===
作过这段开场白，洛拉姆先生便开始叙述与约翰·贝林厄姆失踪相关的种种事件，内容与我曾在报纸上读到的大体一致；他把实际事实摆到陪审团面前之后，接着便讨论这些事实可能有的含义。

===Original===
“Now, what conclusion,” he asked, “will this strange, this most mysterious train of events suggest to an intelligent person who shall consider it impartially? Here is a man who steps forth from the house of his cousin or his brother, as the case may be, and forthwith, in the twinkling of an eye, vanishes from human ken. What is the explanation? Did he steal forth and, without notice or hint of his intention, take train to some seaport, thence to embark for some distant land, leaving his affairs to take care of themselves and his friends to speculate vainly as to his whereabouts? Is he now hiding abroad, or even at home, indifferent alike to the safety of his own considerable property and the peace of mind of his friends? Or is it that death has come upon him unawares by sickness, by accident, or, more probably, by the hand of some unknown criminal? Let us consider the probabilities.

===Chinese===
“那么，”他问道，“一个有头脑的人若不偏不倚地考量这一连串离奇至极、神秘至极的事件，会得出怎样的结论呢？有这么一个人，从他的表亲或兄弟——视情形而定——的宅子里跨出来，转瞬之间，眨眼工夫，便从人类的视界里消失了。作何解释呢？莫非他悄悄溜出，不曾知会一声、不曾暗示半点意图，便登车前往某个海港，再从那里搭船远赴异邦，任凭自己的事务自谋生计，任凭亲友对他的下落徒然揣测？他如今是藏身海外，甚或藏在国内，对自己那份可观财产的安危、对朋友们心境的安宁，一概漠不关心？抑或是死亡不期而至——因病，因意外，或者更有可能，是死在某个无名罪犯之手？且让我们来考量一下这些可能性。

===Original===
“Can he have disappeared by his own deliberate act? Why not? it may be asked. Men undoubtedly do disappear from time to time, to be discovered by chance or to reappear voluntarily after intervals of years and find their names almost forgotten and their places filled by newcomers. Yes; but there is always some reason for a disappearance of this kind, even though it be a bad one. Family discords that make life a weariness; pecuniary difficulties that make life a succession of anxieties; distaste for particular circumstances and surroundings from which there seems no escape; inherent restlessness and vagabond tendencies, and so on.

===Chinese===
“他会是蓄意自行失踪的吗？有人也许要问：有何不可？不错，人确实时有失踪，或日后偶然被人寻获，或隔了若干年自行现身，却发现姓名几乎被人忘却、位子早被新来者补上。是；但这一类失踪总归有个缘故，哪怕是个不成话的缘故。或是家庭龃龉，使生活成为苦役；或是钱财困厄，使生活成为接二连三的烦忧；或是对某种似乎无从脱身的境遇与环境心怀厌弃；或是生性躁动、带有流浪的癖性；诸如此类。

===Original===
“Do any of these explanations apply to the present case? No, they do not. Family discords﻿—at least those capable of producing chronic misery﻿—appertain exclusively to a married state. But the testator was a bachelor with no encumbrances whatever. Pecuniary anxieties can be equally excluded. The testator was in easy, in fact, in affluent circumstances. His mode of life was apparently agreeable and full of interest and activity, and he had full liberty of change if he wished. He had been accustomed to travel, and could do so again without absconding. He had reached an age when radical changes do not seem desirable. He was a man of fixed and regular habits, and his regularity was of his own choice and not due to compulsion or necessity. When last seen by his friends, as I shall prove, he was proceeding to a definite destination with the expressed intention of returning for purposes of his own appointing. He did return and then vanished, leaving those purposes unachieved.

===Chinese===
“这些解释可有哪一条适用于眼下的案子？没有，一条也没有。家庭龃龉——至少是那种足以造成绵长痛苦的重大家庭失和——唯独属于婚姻状态。而立遗嘱人是个单身汉，没有任何拖累。钱财上的烦忧同样可以排除：立遗嘱人境况宽裕，实可谓富足。他的生活方式显然称心适意，富于趣味与活动，倘若愿意，尽有随意变更的自由。他一向惯于旅行，想再度出行尽可光明正大，无须潜逃。他已到了一个不欲作剧烈变更的年纪。他是个习惯固定而有规律的人，他的规律出于自己的选择，并非迫于强制或需要。正如我将证明的，亲友最后一次见到他时，他正前往一个明确的去处，并明言要为着他自己定下的事由回来。他确实回来了，随即失踪，那些事由遂付诸东流。

===Original===
“If we conclude that he has voluntarily disappeared and is at present in hiding, we adopt an opinion that is entirely at variance with all these weighty facts. If, on the other hand, we conclude that he has died suddenly, or has been killed by an accident or otherwise, we are adopting a view that involves no inherent improbabilities and that is entirely congruous with the known facts; facts that will be proved by the testimony of the witnesses whom I shall call. The supposition that the testator is dead is not only more probable than that he is alive; I submit it is the only reasonable explanation of the circumstances of his disappearance.

===Chinese===
“倘若我们断定他是自行失踪、目前藏匿不出，我们所持的意见便与上述种种确凿的事实全然相悖。反之，倘若我们断定他是骤然身亡，或死于意外或其他变故，我们所取的看法便不含任何内在的不可能性，而且与已知的事实完全吻合——这些事实将由我传唤的证人以证词加以证明。立遗嘱人已死这一假设，不仅比他尚在人世更为可能；我敢说，这是对他的失踪情形唯一合理的解释。

===Original===
“But this is not all. The presumption of death which arises so inevitably out of the mysterious and abrupt manner in which the testator disappeared has recently received most conclusive and dreadful confirmation. On the fifteenth of July last there were discovered at Sidcup the remains of a human arm﻿—a left arm, gentlemen, from the hand of which the third, or ring, finger was missing. The doctor who has examined that arm will tell you that the finger was cut off either after death or immediately before; and his evidence will prove conclusively that that arm must have been deposited in the place where it was found just about the time when the testator disappeared. Since that first discovery, other portions of the same mutilated body have come to light; and it is a strange and significant fact that they have all been found in the immediate neighborhood of Eltham or Woodford. You will remember, gentlemen, that it was either at Eltham or Woodford that the testator was last seen alive.

===Chinese===
“然而还不止于此。立遗嘱人失踪方式的神秘与猝然，必然引出死亡推定；这一推定近来得到了最具定谳性质、也最可怖的证实。今年七月十五日，锡德卡普发现了一条人的手臂——诸位，是一条左臂，手上缺少第三指，也就是戴戒指的那根手指。检验过那条手臂的医生将告诉诸位：那根手指是在死后、或临死前夕被切下的；他的证词将确凿地证明，那条手臂被放置到发现地点的时间，恰在立遗嘱人失踪前后。自那第一次发现以来，同一具残缺尸身的其他部分相继出土；而所有这些残骸，竟都是在埃尔瑟姆或伍德福德的紧邻地带发现的，这是一个离奇而意味深长的事实。诸位当还记得，立遗嘱人最后一次活着被人见到，不是在埃尔瑟姆，便是在伍德福德。

===Original===
“And now observe the completeness of the coincidence. These human remains, as you will be told presently by the experienced and learned medical gentleman who has examined them most exhaustively, are those of a man of about sixty years of age, about five feet eight inches in height, fairly muscular and well preserved, apparently healthy, and rather stoutly built. Another witness will tell you that the missing man was about sixty years of age, about five feet eight inches in height, fairly muscular and well preserved, apparently healthy, and rather stoutly built. And﻿—another most significant and striking fact﻿—the testator was accustomed to wear upon the third finger of his left hand﻿—the very finger that is missing from the remains that were found﻿—a most peculiar ring, which fitted so tightly that he was unable to get it off after once putting it on; a ring, gentlemen, of so peculiar a pattern that had it been found on the body must have instantly established the identity of the remains. In a word, gentlemen, the remains which have been found are those of a man exactly like the testator; they differ from him in no respects whatever; they display a mutilation which suggests an attempt to conceal an identifying peculiarity which he undoubtedly presented; and they were deposited in their various hiding-places about the time of the testator’s disappearance. Accordingly, when you have heard these facts proved by the sworn testimony of competent witnesses, together with the facts relating to the disappearance, I shall ask you for a verdict in accordance with that evidence.”

===Chinese===
“再看这巧合何等完备。这几块人骨——对此，那位以最彻底的方式检验过它们、经验宏富而学问渊深的医学界绅士稍后将向诸位陈述——属于一个约莫六十岁的男人，身高约五英尺八英寸，肌肉相当发达，保存良好，外表健康，体格颇为壮硕。另有一位证人将告诉诸位：那位失踪的男子约莫六十岁，身高约五英尺八英寸，肌肉相当发达，保存良好，外表健康，体格颇为壮硕。而且——又是一个最意味深长、最触目的事实——立遗嘱人惯于在左手第三指上——恰恰是所发现遗骸上缺失的那根手指——戴一枚式样极为特殊的戒指，那戒指箍得极紧，一旦戴上便再也无法褪下；诸位，那戒指的式样如此特殊，倘若在尸身上被发现，必定能立刻确定遗骸的身份。总而言之，诸位，所发现的这些遗骸，属于一个与立遗嘱人一模一样的人；它们在任何方面都与他毫无二致；它们显示的残缺，提示有人企图掩盖他确曾具有的一个可资识别的特征；而它们被弃置于各处藏匿地点的时间，又恰在立遗嘱人失踪前后。因此，待诸位听罢这些事实经胜任的证人以宣誓证词证明，连同与失踪相关的种种事实，我便要请诸位作出与该项证据相一致的裁决。”

===Original===
Mr. Loram sat down, and adjusting a pair of pince-nez, rapidly glanced over his brief while the usher was administering the oath to the first witness.

===Chinese===
洛拉姆先生坐了下来，戴上一副夹鼻眼镜，趁庭吏给第一位证人宣誓的当口，飞快地翻阅案情摘要。

===Original===
This was Mr. Jellicoe, who stepped into the box and directed a stony gaze at the (apparently) unconscious judge. The usual preliminaries having been gone through, Mr. Loram proceeded to examine him.

===Chinese===
这第一位证人便是杰利科先生。他步入证人席，把一道石像般呆板的目光投向那位（看去）毫无知觉的法官。例行的开场程序过后，洛拉姆先生便开始讯问他。

===Original===
“You were the testator’s solicitor and confidential agent, I believe?”

===Chinese===
“我想，您是立遗嘱人的事务律师兼机密代理人吧？”

===Original===
“I was﻿—and am.”

===Chinese===
“曾经是——现在也是。”

===Original===
“How long have you known him?”

===Chinese===
“您认识他多久了？”

===Original===
“Twenty-seven years.”

===Chinese===
“二十七年。”

===Original===
“Judging from your experience of him, should you say that he was a person likely to disappear voluntarily and suddenly to cease to communicate with his friends?”

===Chinese===
“依您对他的了解，您认为他是那种可能自行失踪、骤然断绝与亲友音讯的人吗？”

===Original===
“No.”

===Chinese===
“不是。”

===Original===
“Kindly give your reasons for that opinion.”

===Chinese===
“请说明您持此见解的理由。”

===Original===
“Such conduct on the part of the testator would be entirely opposed to his habits and character as they are known to me. He was exceedingly regular and businesslike in his dealings with me. When traveling abroad he always kept me informed as to his whereabouts, or, if he was likely to be beyond reach of communication, he always advised me beforehand. One of my duties was to collect a pension which he drew from the Foreign Office, and on no occasion, previous to his disappearance, has he ever failed to furnish me punctually with the necessary documents.”

===Chinese===
“以我所知的立遗嘱人的习惯与品格而论，这样的行为与他全然相悖。他与我往来，极其守规律、极其有条理。赴国外旅行时，他总是让我知晓他的行踪；倘若可能去到通信不及之处，他也总是预先告知我。我的职责之一，是代领他从外交部支取的一笔年金；在失踪之前，他从无一次不按时把必需的证件文件交到我手上。”

===Original===
“Had he, so far as you know, any reasons for wishing to disappear?”

===Chinese===
“就您所知，他可有什么想要失踪的缘由？”

===Original===
“No.”

===Chinese===
“没有。”

===Original===
“When and where did you last see him alive?”

===Chinese===
“您最后一次见到活着的他，是在何时何地？”

===Original===
“At six o’clock in the evening, on the fourteenth of October, nineteen hundred and two, at 141, Queen Square, Bloomsbury.”

===Chinese===
“一九〇二年十月十四日傍晚六点，在布鲁姆斯伯里皇后广场 141 号。”

===Original===
“Kindly tell us what happened on that occasion.”

===Chinese===
“请告诉我们那天发生了什么。”

===Original===
“The testator had called for me at my office at a quarter past three, and asked me to come with him to his house to meet Doctor Norbury. I accompanied him to 141, Queen Square, and shortly after we arrived Doctor Norbury came to look at some antiquities that the testator proposed to give to the British Museum. The gift consisted of a mummy with four Canopic jars and other tomb-furniture which the testator stipulated should be exhibited together in a single case and in the state in which they were then presented. Of these objects, the mummy only was ready for inspection. The tomb-furniture had not yet arrived in England, but was expected within a week. Doctor Norbury accepted the gift on behalf of the Museum, but could not take possession of the objects until he had communicated with the Director and obtained his formal authority. The testator accordingly gave me certain instructions concerning the delivery of the gift, as he was leaving England that evening.”

===Chinese===
“当天三点一刻，立遗嘱人到我的事务所来接我，请我随他到他宅子里去见诺伯里博士。我随他到了皇后广场 141 号；我们到达后不久，诺伯里博士便前来看立遗嘱人打算赠给大英博物馆的一批古物。赠品包括一具木乃伊、四只卡诺皮克罐和其他墓葬随葬品；立遗嘱人言明，这批物件须同置一柜展出，并保持其当时呈现的原状。这些物件中，只有木乃伊可以当场查验。随葬品尚未运抵英国，预计一周之内到达。诺伯里博士代表博物馆接受了赠与，但在与馆长接洽并取得正式授权之前，尚不能提取这批物件。立遗嘱人当晚便要离开英国，于是就赠品的交付给了我若干指示。”

===Original===
“Are those instructions relevant to the subject of this inquiry?”

===Chinese===
“那些指示与本案讯问的题目有关吗？”

===Original===
“I think they are. The testator was going to Paris, and perhaps from thence to Vienna. He instructed me to receive and unpack the tomb-furniture on its arrival, and to store it, with the mummy, in a particular room, where it was to remain for three weeks. If he returned within that time he was to hand it over in person to the Museum authorities; if he had not returned within that time, he desired me to notify the Museum authorities that they were at liberty to take possession of and remove the collection at their convenience. From these instructions I gathered that the testator was uncertain as to the length of his absence from England and the extent of his journey.”

===Chinese===
“我想是有关的。立遗嘱人将赴巴黎，或许再从那里前往维也纳。他指示我，随葬品运到后由我接收、开箱，然后连同木乃伊一起存放在一个特定的房间里，为期三周。倘若他在此期间回来，便由他亲自把这批东西移交博物馆方面；倘若期满未归，他嘱咐我通知博物馆方面：他们可以随意前来接收并运走这批藏品。从这些指示我推知，立遗嘱人自己也不确定此番离英多久、行程多远。”

===Original===
“Did he state precisely where he was going?”

===Chinese===
“他可曾确切说过要去哪里？”

===Original===
“No. He said he was going to Paris and perhaps to Vienna, but he gave no particulars and I asked for none.”

===Chinese===
“没有。他说要去巴黎，或许还去维也纳，但没有细说，我也没有细问。”

===Original===
“Do you, in fact, know where he went?”

===Chinese===
“那么，您实际上知道他去了哪里吗？”

===Original===
“No. He left the house at six o’clock wearing a long, heavy overcoat and carrying a suitcase and an umbrella. I wished him ‘Goodbye’ at the door and watched him walk away as if going toward Southampton Row. I have no idea where he went, and I never saw him again.”

===Chinese===
“不知道。六点钟他离开宅子，身穿一件厚重的长外套，提着一只手提箱，带着一把伞。我在门口对他道了声‘再见’，望着他走远，方向像是往南安普敦巷（Southampton Row）那一带。他去了哪里，我毫无所知；此后我再也没有见过他。”

===Original===
“Had he no other luggage than the suitcase?”

===Chinese===
“除了那只手提箱，他没有别的行李吗？”

===Original===
“I do not know, but I believe not. He was accustomed to travel with the bare necessaries, and to buy anything further he wanted en route.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道，不过我想没有。他习惯只带最必需的物件出门，途中再添置所需之物。”

===Original===
“Did he say nothing to the servants as to the probable date of his return?”

===Chinese===
“他对仆人们可曾说起大概何时回来？”

===Original===
“There were no servants excepting the caretaker. The house was not used for residential purposes. The testator slept and took his meals at his club, though he kept his clothes at the house.”

===Chinese===
“家里没有仆人，只有一位看管人。那宅子不作居住之用。立遗嘱人歇宿用膳都在他的俱乐部，只是衣物存放在宅子里。”

===Original===
“Did you receive any communication from him after he left?”

===Chinese===
“他走后，您可曾收到他的任何音信？”

===Original===
“No. I never heard from him again in any way. I waited for three weeks as he had instructed me, and then notified the Museum authorities that the collection was ready for removal. Five days later Doctor Norbury came and took formal possession of it, and it was transferred to the Museum forthwith.”

===Chinese===
“没有。此后我以任何方式都没有再得到他的音讯。我遵照他的嘱咐等了三个星期，然后通知博物馆方面，藏品可以运走了。五天之后，诺伯里博士前来正式接收，藏品随即移交给了博物馆。”

===Original===
“When did you next hear of the testator?”

===Chinese===
“那之后，您何时再听到立遗嘱人的消息？”

===Original===
“On the twenty-third of November following at a quarter-past seven in the evening. Mr. George Hurst came to my rooms, which are over my office, and informed me that the testator had called at his house during his absence and had been shown into the study to wait for him. That on his﻿—Mr. Hurst’s﻿—arrival it was found that the testator had disappeared without acquainting the servants of his intended departure, and without being seen by anyone to leave the house. Mr. Hurst thought this so remarkable that he had hastened up to town to inform me. I also thought it a remarkable circumstance, especially as I had received no communication from the testator, and we both decided that it was advisable to inform the testator’s brother, Godfrey, of what had happened.

===Chinese===
“同年十一月二十三日晚上七点一刻。乔治·赫斯特先生来到我的寓所——就在我事务所的楼上——告诉我：他不在家的时候，立遗嘱人到他宅里来过，被引进书房等他；待到他——赫斯特先生——回家，却发现立遗嘱人已经不见，既未曾告知仆人们他要离去，也没有被任何人看见离开宅子。赫斯特先生觉得此事太过蹊跷，便急忙赶进城来告诉我。我也觉得此事非同小可，尤其我还一直没有得到立遗嘱人的任何音信；于是我们两人都认为，应当把发生的事情告知立遗嘱人的弟弟戈弗雷。

===Original===
“Accordingly Mr. Hurst and I proceeded as quickly as possible to Liverpool Street and took the first train available to Woodford, where Mr. Godfrey Bellingham then resided. We arrived at his house at five minutes to nine, and were informed by the servant that he was not at home, but that his daughter was in the library, which was a detached building situated in the grounds. The servant lighted a lantern and conducted us through the grounds to the library, where we found Mr. Godfrey Bellingham and Miss Bellingham. Mr. Godfrey had only just come in and had entered by the back gate, which had a bell that rang in the library. Mr. Hurst informed Mr. Godfrey of what had occurred, and then we left the library to walk up to the house. A few paces from the library I noticed by the light of the lantern, which Mr. Godfrey was carrying, a small object lying on the lawn. I pointed it out to him and he picked it up, and then we all recognized it as a scarab that the testator was accustomed to wear on his watch-chain. It was fitted with a gold wire passed through the suspension hole and a gold ring. Both the wire and the ring were in position, but the ring was broken. We went to the house and questioned the servants as to visitors; but none of them had seen the testator, and they all agreed that no visitor whatsoever had come to the house during the afternoon or evening. Mr. Godfrey and Miss Bellingham both declared that they had neither seen nor heard anything of the testator, and were both unaware that he had returned to England. As the circumstances were somewhat disquieting, I communicated, on the following morning, with the police and requested them to make inquiries; which they did, with the result that a suitcase bearing the initials ‘J. B.,’ was found to be lying unclaimed in the cloakroom at Charing Cross Station. I was able to identify the suitcase as that which I had seen the testator carry away from Queen Square. I was also able to identify some of the contents. I interviewed the cloakroom attendant, who informed me that the suitcase had been deposited on the twenty-third about 4:15 p.m. He had no recollection of the person who deposited it. It remained unclaimed in the possession of the railway company for three months, and was then surrendered to me.”

===Chinese===
“于是赫斯特先生和我尽快赶往利物浦街，搭上开往伍德福德的第一班火车——戈弗雷·贝林厄姆先生当时住在那里。九点差五分，我们到了他家；仆人告知说他不在家，但他女儿在藏书室里——那是坐落在宅园里的一座独立小屋。仆人点起一盏提灯，领我们穿过宅园到藏书室去，我们在那里见到了戈弗雷·贝林厄姆先生和贝林厄姆小姐。戈弗雷先生也是刚进家门，走的是后门——后门装有铃，铃声一响，藏书室里便听得见。赫斯特先生把事情的经过告诉了戈弗雷先生，随后我们便离开藏书室，往正宅走去。离藏书室几步路的地方，借着戈弗雷先生手里提灯的光，我注意到草地上躺着一个小物件。我指给他看，他把它拾起来，我们随即都认出，那是一枚圣甲虫，立遗嘱人惯于把它挂在表链上。甲虫上穿着一根金丝，从悬孔中通过，还连着一个小金环。金丝和金环都原样在位，只是金环断了。我们走进正宅，向仆人们查问来客的情形；可他们谁都没有见过立遗嘱人，而且众口一词：当天下午和晚上，宅子里根本没有来过任何客人。戈弗雷先生和贝林厄姆小姐都声明，立遗嘱人的影子他们既没有见过，声息也没有听过，而且都不知道他已经回到英国。这些情形颇为令人不安，于是第二天早晨，我便与警方接洽，请他们着手查访。他们查了，结果是：在查令十字车站的行李寄存处，发现一只无人认领的手提箱，箱上带有‘J. B.’两个缩写字母。我认出那只箱子，正是我亲眼见立遗嘱人从皇后广场提走的那只；箱中若干物件，我也认得出来。我讯问了寄存处的管理员，他告诉我，箱子是二十三日大约下午 4 点 15 分存入的；存箱的人长什么模样，他已毫无印象。那只箱子在铁路公司手里无人认领地存放了三个月，然后移交给了我。”

===Original===
“Were there any marks or labels on it showing the route by which it had traveled?”

===Chinese===
“箱子上可有什么标记或行李签，能显示它走过哪条路线？”

===Original===
“There were no labels on it and no marks other than the initials ‘J. B.’ ”

===Chinese===
“箱子上没有行李签，除了‘J. B.’两个缩写字母，也没有任何标记。”

===Original===
“Do you happen to know the testator’s age?”

===Chinese===
“您可知道立遗嘱人的年纪？”

===Original===
“Yes. He was fifty-nine on the eleventh of October, nineteen hundred and two.”

===Chinese===
“知道。一九〇二年十月十一日，他满了五十九岁。”

===Original===
“Can you tell us what his height was?”

===Chinese===
“能告诉我们他的身高吗？”

===Original===
“Yes. He was exactly five feet eight inches.”

===Chinese===
“能。他恰恰是五英尺八英寸。”

===Original===
“What sort of health had he?”

===Chinese===
“他的身体怎么样？”

===Original===
“So far as I know his health was good. I am not aware that he suffered from any disease. I am only judging by his appearance, which was that of a healthy man.”

===Chinese===
“据我所知，他身体很好。我不记得他患过什么疾病。我只是凭他的外表判断，而他的外表是一个健康人的外表。”

===Original===
“Should you describe him as well preserved or otherwise?”

===Chinese===
“依您之见，他可以说是保养得宜呢，还是并非如此？”

===Original===
“I should describe him as a well preserved man for his age.”

===Chinese===
“以他的年纪而论，我要说他是个保养得宜的人。”

===Original===
“How should you describe his figure?”

===Chinese===
“他的身量体格，您会怎么形容？”

===Original===
“I should describe him as rather broad and stout in build, and fairly muscular, though not exceptionally so.”

===Chinese===
“我要说，他骨架颇宽，体格壮硕，肌肉也相当发达，虽然算不上格外出众。”

===Original===
Mr. Loram made a rapid note of these answers and then said:

===Chinese===
洛拉姆先生把这些回答飞快地记下，然后说道：

===Original===
“You have told us, Mr. Jellicoe, that you have known the testator intimately for twenty-seven years. Now, did you ever notice whether he was accustomed to wear any rings upon his fingers?”

===Chinese===
“杰利科先生，您已经告诉我们，您与立遗嘱人亲密相知已有二十七年。那么请问，您可曾留意过，他手指上是否惯常戴着什么戒指？”

===Original===
“He wore upon the third finger of his left hand a copy of an antique ring which bore the device of the Eye of Osiris. That was the only ring he ever wore as far as I know.”

===Chinese===
“他在左手第三指上戴着一枚仿古戒指，戒面上錾着俄塞里斯之眼的纹样。据我所知，那是他戴过的唯一一枚戒指。”

===Original===
“Did he wear it constantly?”

===Chinese===
“他一直戴着它吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, necessarily; because it was too small for him, and having once squeezed it on he was never able to get it off again.”

===Chinese===
“是的，而且是不得不戴；因为那戒指对他来说太小了，一旦挤着戴上，便再也褪不下来。”

===Original===
This was the sum of Mr. Jellicoe’s evidence, and at its conclusion the witness glanced inquiringly at Mr. Bellingham’s counsel. But Mr. Heath remained seated, attentively considering the notes that he had just made, and finding that there was to be no cross-examination, Mr. Jellicoe stepped down from the box. I leaned back on my bench, and, turning my head, observed Miss Bellingham deep in thought.

===Chinese===
杰利科先生的证词到此为止。证词既毕，证人便征询似的朝贝林厄姆先生一方的出庭律师望了一眼。可是希思先生稳坐不动，正专心致志地琢磨他刚记下的笔记；杰利科先生见不会有交叉质询了，便走下证人席。我在长凳上往后一靠，侧过头去，见贝林厄姆小姐正自出神深思。

===Original===
“What do you think of it?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“你觉得这事怎样？”我问。

===Original===
“It seems very complete and conclusive,” she replied. And then, with a sigh, she murmured: “Poor old Uncle John! How horrid it sounds to talk of him in this cold-blooded, businesslike way, as ‘the testator,’ as if he were nothing but a sort of algebraical sign.”

===Chinese===
“看来十分完备，无可辩驳。”她答道。随即她叹了一口气，低声道：“可怜的约翰伯伯！用这种冷冰冰、公事公办的口吻谈论他，一口一个‘立遗嘱人’，仿佛他不过是一个代数符号似的，听着多么不是滋味。”

===Original===
“There isn’t much room for sentiment, I suppose, in the proceedings of the Probate Court,” I replied. To which she assented, and then asked: “Who is this lady?”

===Chinese===
“我猜，遗嘱检验法院的开庭程序里，怕是没有多少容纳感情的余地。”我答道。她表示同意，随即问道：“这位女士是谁？”

===Original===
“This lady” was a fashionably dressed young woman who had just bounced into the witness-box and was now being sworn. The preliminaries being finished, she answered Miss Bellingham’s question and Mr. Loram’s by stating that her name was Augustina Gwendoline Dobbs, and that she was housemaid to Mr. George Hurst, of “The Poplars,” Eltham.

===Chinese===
“这位女士”是个衣着时髦的年轻女子，刚一阵风似的闯进证人席，此刻正在宣誓。开场程序既毕，她回答了贝林厄姆小姐的疑问和洛拉姆先生的讯问，自陈姓名是奥古斯蒂娜·格温多琳·多布斯（Augustina Gwendoline Dobbs），身份是埃尔瑟姆“白杨居”乔治·赫斯特先生家的侍女。

===Original===
“Mr. Hurst lives alone, I believe?” said Mr. Loram.

===Chinese===
“我想，赫斯特先生是独自居住的吧？”洛拉姆先生说。

===Original===
“I don’t know what you mean by that,” Miss Dobbs began; but the barrister explained:

===Chinese===
“我不明白您这话是什么意思。”多布斯小姐开言道；出庭律师连忙解释：

===Original===
“I mean that I believe he is unmarried?”

===Chinese===
“我的意思是：我想他尚未娶亲吧？”

===Original===
“Well, and what about it?” the witness demanded tartly.

===Chinese===
“哟，那又怎么样？”证人尖酸地反问。

===Original===
“I am asking you a question.”

===Chinese===
“我是在向您提一个问题。”

===Original===
“I know that,” said the witness viciously; “and I say that you’ve no business to make any such insinuations to a respectable young lady when there’s a cook-housekeeper and a kitchenmaid living in the house, and him old enough to be my father﻿—”

===Chinese===
“这个我知道，”证人恶狠狠地说，“可我要说：宅子里明明住着一位掌厨的女管家和一个厨房丫头，他的年纪又大得够做我父亲——您对一位规规矩矩的年轻小姐说这种含沙射影的话，未免太不应该——”

===Original===
Here his lordship flattened his eyelids with startling effect, and Mr. Loram interrupted: “I make no insinuations. I merely ask, Is your employer, Mr. Hurst, an unmarried man, or is he not?”

===Chinese===
这时，法官大人把眼睑一抹，其效惊人；洛拉姆先生赶紧打断：“我并无影射之意。我只是问：您的东家赫斯特先生，究竟娶亲了，还是没有？”

===Original===
“I never asked him,” said the witness sulkily.

===Chinese===
“我从来没有问过他。”证人绷着脸说。

===Original===
“Please answer my question﻿—yes or no.”

===Chinese===
“请回答我的问题——是，还是不是。”

===Original===
“How can I answer your question? He may be married or he may not. How do I know? I’m no private detective.”

===Chinese===
“您叫我怎么回答？他也许娶了，也许没娶。我哪里知道？我又不是私家侦探。”

===Original===
Mr. Loram directed a stupefied gaze at the witness, and in the ensuing silence a plaintive voice came from the bench:

===Chinese===
洛拉姆先生目瞪口呆地望着证人；随之而来的静默之中，法官席上响起一个幽幽的声音：

===Original===
“Is that point material?”

===Chinese===
“这一点要紧吗？”

===Original===
“Certainly, my lord,” replied Mr. Loram.

===Chinese===
“当然要紧，大人。”洛拉姆先生答道。

===Original===
“Then, as I see that you are calling Mr. Hurst, perhaps you had better put the question to him. He will probably know.”

===Chinese===
“那么，我见你就要传赫斯特先生出庭，这个问题你不如问他本人。他多半是知道的。”

===Original===
Mr. Loram bowed, and as the Judge subsided into his normal state of coma he turned to the triumphant witness.

===Chinese===
洛拉姆先生躬身领命；法官大人重新沉入惯常的昏睡状态，他便转向那位得胜的证人。

===Original===
“Do you remember anything remarkable occurring on the twenty-third of November the year before last?”

===Chinese===
“前年十一月二十三日那天发生了什么不寻常的事，您还记得吗？”

===Original===
“Yes. Mr. John Bellingham called at our house.”

===Chinese===
“记得。约翰·贝林厄姆先生到我们宅里来过。”

===Original===
“How did you know he was Mr. John Bellingham?”

===Chinese===
“您怎么知道他就是约翰·贝林厄姆先生？”

===Original===
“I didn’t; but he said he was, and I supposed he knew.”

===Chinese===
“我原先并不知道；是他自己说的，我想他自己总清楚。”

===Original===
“At what time did he arrive?”

===Chinese===
“他几点钟到的？”

===Original===
“At twenty minutes past five in the evening.”

===Chinese===
“傍晚五点二十分。”

===Original===
“What happened then?”

===Chinese===
“随后怎样？”

===Original===
“I told him that Mr. Hurst had not come home yet, and he said he would wait for him in the study and write some letters; so I showed him into the study and shut the door.”

===Chinese===
“我告诉他赫斯特先生还没有回家，他说那他就在书房里等他，顺便写几封信；于是我把他领进书房，随手关上了门。”

===Original===
“What happened next?”

===Chinese===
“接下来呢？”

===Original===
“Nothing. Then Mr. Hurst came home at his usual time﻿—a quarter to six﻿—and let himself in with his key. He went straight into the study where I supposed Mr. Bellingham still was, so I took no notice, but laid the table for two. At six o’clock Mr. Hurst came into the dining-room﻿—he has tea in the City and dines at six﻿—and when he saw the table laid for two he asked the reason. I said I thought Mr. Bellingham was staying to dinner.

===Chinese===
“没什么。后来赫斯特先生按他平常的钟点——六点差一刻——回到家，用自己的钥匙开了门进去。他径直进了书房，我以为贝林厄姆先生还在里面，便没有在意，只管摆了两副餐具。六点钟，赫斯特先生走进饭厅——他在城里用茶点，六点开晚饭——看见桌上摆了两副餐具，便问缘故。我说，我以为贝林厄姆先生要留下用饭。

===Original===
“ ‘Mr. Bellingham!’ says he. ‘I didn’t know he was here. Why didn’t you tell me?’ he says. ‘I thought he was with you, sir,’ I said. ‘I showed him into the study,’ I said. ‘Well, he wasn’t there when I came in,’ he said, ‘and he isn’t there now,’ he said. ‘Perhaps he has gone to wait in the drawing-room,’ he said. So he went and looked in the drawing-room, but he wasn’t there. Then Mr. Hurst said he thought Mr. Bellingham must have got tired of waiting and gone away; but I told him I was quite sure he hadn’t, because I had been watching all the time. Then he asked me if Mr. Bellingham was alone or whether his daughter was with him, and I said that it wasn’t Mr. Bellingham at all, but Mr. John Bellingham, and then he was more surprised than ever. I said we had better search the house to make sure whether he was there or not, and Mr. Hurst said he would come with me; so we all went over the house and looked in all the rooms, but there was not a sign of Mr. Bellingham in any of them. Then Mr. Hurst got very nervous and upset, and when he had just snatched a little dinner he ran off to catch the six thirty-one train up to town.”

===Chinese===
“ ‘贝林厄姆先生！’他说。‘我不知道他在这儿。你怎么不告诉我？’他说。‘我以为他跟您在一块儿呢，先生，’我说。‘是我把他领进了书房，’我说。‘嗐，我进家门的时候他不在书房，’他说，‘现在也不在，’他说。‘也许他到客厅等着去了，’他说。于是他到客厅里看了看，人不在。这时赫斯特先生说，他猜贝林厄姆先生准是等得不耐烦、自己走了；可我对他说，我敢肯定他没有走，因为我一直盯着呢。他又问我，来的是贝林厄姆先生一个人呢，还是带着他女儿；我说来的根本不是贝林厄姆先生，是约翰·贝林厄姆先生，这一下他愈发吃惊了。我说，我们最好把宅子搜一遍，看看他到底在不在；赫斯特先生说他跟我一块儿去；于是我们把整座宅子走了一遍，每个房间都看了看，可哪里都没有贝林厄姆先生的一点影子。赫斯特先生这才慌了神，坐立不安，匆匆扒了几口晚饭，便跑去赶六点三十一分进城的火车了。”

===Original===
“You say that Mr. Bellingham could not have left the house because you were watching all the time. Where were you while you were watching?”

===Chinese===
“您说贝林厄姆先生不可能离宅，因为您一直盯着。您盯着的时候，人在哪里？”

===Original===
“I was in the kitchen. I could see the front gate from the kitchen window.”

===Chinese===
“我在厨房里。从厨房的窗子能望见前门。”

===Original===
“You say that you laid the table for two. Where did you lay it?”

===Chinese===
“您说您摆了两副餐具。摆在哪儿？”

===Original===
“In the dining-room, of course.”

===Chinese===
“当然是摆在饭厅里。”

===Original===
“Could you see the front gate from the dining-room?”

===Chinese===
“从饭厅里能望见前门吗？”

===Original===
“No, but I could see the study door. The study is opposite the dining-room.”

===Chinese===
“望不见，可我望得见书房的门。书房在饭厅对面。”

===Original===
“Do you have to come upstairs to get from the kitchen to the dining-room?”

===Chinese===
“从厨房到饭厅，是不是非得先上楼？”

===Original===
“Yes, of course you do!”

===Chinese===
“是呀，当然非得上楼！”

===Original===
“Then, might not Mr. Bellingham have left the house while you were coming up the stairs?”

===Chinese===
“那么，您上楼的时候，贝林厄姆先生岂不是有可能趁隙离宅？”

===Original===
“No, he couldn’t have done.”

===Chinese===
“不，不可能。”

===Original===
“Why not?”

===Chinese===
“为什么不可能？”

===Original===
“Because it would have been impossible.”

===Chinese===
“因为那是不可能的。”

===Original===
“But why would it have been impossible?”

===Chinese===
“可为什么那是不可能的？”

===Original===
“Because he couldn’t have done it.”

===Chinese===
“因为他不可能干出这种事来。”

===Original===
“I suggest that Mr. Bellingham left the house quietly while you were on the stairs?”

===Chinese===
“我提出这样一种可能：您在楼梯上的时候，贝林厄姆先生悄没声地离宅走了，对不对？”

===Original===
“No, he didn’t.”

===Chinese===
“不，他没走。”

===Original===
“How do you know he did not?”

===Chinese===
“您怎么知道他没走？”

===Original===
“I am quite sure he didn’t.”

===Chinese===
“我敢肯定他没走。”

===Original===
“But how can you be certain?”

===Chinese===
“可您怎么能确知？”

===Original===
“Because I should have seen him if he had.”

===Chinese===
“他要是走了，我自然会看见。”

===Original===
“But I mean when you were on the stairs.”

===Chinese===
“我说的是您在楼梯上的时候。”

===Original===
“He was in the study when I was on the stairs.”

===Chinese===
“我在楼梯上的时候，他在书房里。”

===Original===
“How do you know he was in the study?”

===Chinese===
“您怎么知道他在书房里？”

===Original===
“Because I showed him in there and he hadn’t come out.”

===Chinese===
“是我领他进去的，他又不曾出来。”

===Original===
Mr. Loram paused and took a deep breath, and his lordship flattened his eyelids.

===Chinese===
洛拉姆先生停下来，深深吸了一口气；法官大人把眼睑一抹。

===Original===
“Is there a side gate to the premises?” the barrister resumed wearily.

===Chinese===
“宅子可有边门？”出庭律师没精打采地重新开口。

===Original===
“Yes. It opens into a narrow lane at the side of the house.”

===Chinese===
“有。边门通到宅子旁边一条窄巷。”

===Original===
“And there is a French window in the study, is there not?”

===Chinese===
“书房里有一扇落地长窗，对不对？”

===Original===
“Yes. It opens on to the small grass plot opposite the side gate.”

===Chinese===
“有。长窗开向边门对面的一小块草地。”

===Original===
“The window and the gate both have catches on the inside. Could it have been possible for Mr. Bellingham to let himself out into the lane?”

===Chinese===
“长窗和边门的插销都装在里面。贝林厄姆先生有没有可能是自己开门出去，走到巷子里了？”

===Original===
“The window and gate both have catches on the inside. He could have got out that way, but, of course he didn’t.”

===Chinese===
“长窗和边门的插销是都装在里面。从那条道出去，倒是可以出去，不过他当然没有那么做。”

===Original===
“Why not?”

===Chinese===
“为什么当然没有？”

===Original===
“Well, no gentleman would go creeping out the back way like a thief.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，哪有绅士像贼似的从后道溜出去。”

===Original===
“Did you look to see if the French window was shut and fastened after you missed Mr. Bellingham?”

===Chinese===
“发觉贝林厄姆先生不见了之后，您可曾查看那扇落地长窗关了没有、闩了没有？”

===Original===
“I looked at it when we shut the house up for the night. It was then shut and fastened on the inside.”

===Chinese===
“晚上关宅门的时候，我看过。那时长窗关着，插销从里面闩着。”

===Original===
“And the side gate?”

===Chinese===
“那边门呢？”

===Original===
“That gate was shut and latched. You have to slam the gate to make the latch fasten, so no one could have gone out of the gate without being heard.”

===Chinese===
“边门关着，门闩扣着。那门闩非得把门摔上才能扣住，所以谁也别想从那门出去而不被人听见。”

===Original===
Here the examination-in-chief ended, and Mr. Loram sat down with an audible sigh of relief. Miss Dobbs was about to step down from the witness-box when Mr. Heath rose to cross-examine.

===Chinese===
主讯问至此结束，洛拉姆先生落了座，出了一声人人都听得见的宽心长叹。多布斯小姐正要步下证人席，希思先生起身进行交叉质询。

===Original===
“Did you see Mr. Bellingham in a good light?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“您看贝林厄姆先生的时候，光线好不好？”他问。

===Original===
“Pretty good. It was dark outside, but the hall-lamp was alight.”

===Chinese===
“挺好的。外头天黑了，可门厅的灯亮着。”

===Original===
“Kindly look at this”﻿—here a small object was passed across to the witness. “It is a trinket that Mr. Bellingham is stated to have carried suspended from his watch-guard. Can you remember if he was wearing it in that manner when he came to the house?”

===Chinese===
“请看看这个”——这时一个小物件递到了证人手里。“这是一件小饰物，据称贝林厄姆先生惯于把它吊在表链上随身带着。他到宅子里来的时候，是不是这样带着它，您记得吗？”

===Original===
“No, he was not.”

===Chinese===
“不是，没有那样带着。”

===Original===
“You are sure of that.”

===Chinese===
“这一点您能肯定。”

===Original===
“Quite sure.”

===Chinese===
“完全肯定。”

===Original===
“Thank you. And now I want to ask you about the search that you have mentioned. You say that you went all over the house. Did you go into the study?”

===Chinese===
“谢谢。现在我想问问您方才提到的那次搜查。您说你们把整座宅子都走了一遍。你们进书房了吗？”

===Original===
“No﻿—at least, not until Mr. Hurst had gone to London.”

===Chinese===
“没有——至少，在赫斯特先生动身去伦敦之前没有进过。”

===Original===
“When you did go in, was the window fastened?”

===Chinese===
“后来您进去的时候，长窗是闩着的吗？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“是。”

===Original===
“Could it have been fastened from the outside?”

===Chinese===
“那插销有没有可能是从外面闩上的？”

===Original===
“No; there is no handle outside.”

===Chinese===
“不可能；外面没有把手。”

===Original===
“What furniture is there in the study?”

===Chinese===
“书房里有些什么家具？”

===Original===
“There is a writing-table, a revolving-chair, two easy chairs, two large bookcases, and a wardrobe that Mr. Hurst keeps his overcoats and hats in.”

===Chinese===
“有一张写字台、一把转椅、两把安乐椅、两个大书柜，还有一口大衣柜，赫斯特先生用来放大衣和帽子。”

===Original===
“Does the wardrobe lock?”

===Chinese===
“那衣柜上有锁吗？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“有。”

===Original===
“Was it locked when you went in?”

===Chinese===
“您进去的时候，它锁着吗？”

===Original===
“I’m sure I don’t know. I don’t go about trying the cupboards and drawers.”

===Chinese===
“我实在说不上来。我又不会到处去拉人家那些柜子抽屉。”

===Original===
“What furniture is there in the drawing-room?”

===Chinese===
“客厅里有些什么家具？”

===Original===
“A cabinet, six or seven chairs, a Chesterfield sofa, a piano, a silver-table, and one or two occasional tables.”

===Chinese===
“一个陈设柜，六七把椅子，一张切斯特菲尔德长沙发，一架钢琴，一张银器桌，还有一两张杂用小桌。”

===Original===
“Is the piano a grand or upright?”

===Chinese===
“那钢琴是平台式的，还是立式的？”

===Original===
“It is an upright grand.”

===Chinese===
“是一架大立式钢琴。”

===Original===
“In what position is it placed?”

===Chinese===
“它摆在什么位置？”

===Original===
“It stands across a corner near the window.”

===Chinese===
“琴身横跨在靠窗的一个墙角上。”

===Original===
“Is there sufficient room behind it for a man to conceal himself?”

===Chinese===
“琴后头的空当，够不够一个男子藏身？”

===Original===
Miss Dobbs was amused and did not dissemble. “Oh, yes,” she sniggered, “there’s plenty of room for a man to hide behind it.”

===Chinese===
多布斯小姐乐了，也不掩饰。“哦，够，”她嗤嗤地笑道，“琴后头藏个男人，地方富余得很哩。”

===Original===
“When you searched the drawing-room, did you look behind the piano?”

===Chinese===
“你们搜客厅的时候，往钢琴后头看了吗？”

===Original===
“No, I didn’t,” Miss Dobbs replied scornfully.

===Chinese===
“没有，没看。”多布斯小姐不屑地答道。

===Original===
“Did you look under the sofa?”

===Chinese===
“沙发底下看了吗？”

===Original===
“Certainly not!”

===Chinese===
“当然没有！”

===Original===
“What did you do then?”

===Chinese===
“那你们做了什么？”

===Original===
“We opened the door and looked into the room. We were not looking for a cat or a monkey; we were looking for a middle-aged gentleman.”

===Chinese===
“我们开了门，朝屋里望了望。我们又不是找猫找猴儿；我们找的是一位中年绅士。”

===Original===
