“And am I to take it that your search over the rest of the house was conducted in a similar manner?”

===Chinese===
“那么我是否可以理解为：你们搜宅子其余各处时，也是照这个搜法？”

===Original===
“Certainly. We looked into the rooms, but we did not search under the beds or in the cupboards.”

===Chinese===
“当然。我们朝各个房间里望了望，可没有翻床底，也没有开柜子。”

===Original===
“Are all the rooms in the house in use as living or sleeping rooms?”

===Chinese===
“宅子里的房间，是不是间间都在使用，作起居室或卧室？”

===Original===
“No; there is one room on the second floor that is used as a store and lumber-room, and one on the first floor that Mr. Hurst uses to store trunks and things that he is not using.”

===Chinese===
“不是；三楼有一间屋子堆放杂物，作贮藏室用；二楼有一间，赫斯特先生用来存放不用的箱笼物件。”

===Original===
“Did you look in those rooms when you searched the house?”

===Chinese===
“你们搜宅子的时候，往那两间屋里看了吗？”

===Original===
“No.”

===Chinese===
“没有。”

===Original===
“Have you looked in them since?”

===Chinese===
“打那以后，可曾进去看过？”

===Original===
“I have been in the lumber-room since, but not in the other. It is always kept locked.”

===Chinese===
“杂物间后来进去过，另一间没有。那间屋子总是锁着。”

===Original===
At this point an ominous flattening became apparent in his lordship’s eyelids, but these symptoms passed when Mr. Heath sat down and indicated that he had no further questions to ask.

===Chinese===
到这时，法官大人的眼睑上眼看着泛起一阵不祥的抹动；不过希思先生落座、示意再没有别的话要问之后，这些症状便过去了。

===Original===
Miss Dobbs once more prepared to step down from the witness box, when Mr. Loram shot up like a jack-in-the-box.

===Chinese===
多布斯小姐又一次准备步下证人席，不料洛拉姆先生像玩具匣里弹出的玩偶一般，霍地立了起来。

===Original===
“You have made certain statements,” said he, “concerning the scarab which Mr. Bellingham was accustomed to wear suspended from his watch-guard. You say that he was not wearing it when he came to Mr. Hurst’s house on the twenty-third of November, nineteen hundred and two. Are you quite sure of that?”

===Chinese===
“您方才就贝林厄姆先生惯于吊在表链上的那枚圣甲虫，作了若干陈述。”他说，“您说，一九〇二年十一月二十三日他到赫斯特先生宅里来的时候，没有那样带着它。这一点，您完全肯定吗？”

===Original===
“Quite sure.”

===Chinese===
“完全肯定。”

===Original===
“I must ask you to be very careful in your statement on this point. The question is a highly important one. Do you swear that the scarab was not hanging from his watch-guard?”

===Chinese===
“我必须请您在这个问题上的陈述格外审慎。这个问题关系重大。您敢起誓，那枚圣甲虫当时没有吊在他的表链上吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, I do.”

===Chinese===
“敢，我起誓。”

===Original===
“Did you notice the watch-guard particularly?”

===Chinese===
“那条表链，您特别留意过吗？”

===Original===
“No; not particularly.”

===Chinese===
“没有；没有特别留意。”

===Original===
“Then what makes you sure that the scarab was not attached to it?”

===Chinese===
“那么，您凭什么肯定圣甲虫没有系在上面？”

===Original===
“It couldn’t have been.”

===Chinese===
“它不可能在上面。”

===Original===
“Why could it not?”

===Chinese===
“为什么不可能？”

===Original===
“Because if it had been there I should have seen it.”

===Chinese===
“因为要是在上面，我早就看见了。”

===Original===
“What kind of watch-guard was Mr. Bellingham wearing?”

===Chinese===
“贝林厄姆先生那时佩的是哪一种表链？”

===Original===
“Oh, an ordinary sort of watch-guard.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，就是普普通通的那种。”

===Original===
“I mean was it a chain or a ribbon or a strap?”

===Chinese===
“我是说，那是金属链子、缎带，还是皮条？”

===Original===
“A chain, I think﻿—or perhaps a ribbon﻿—or it might have been a strap.”

===Chinese===
“链子吧，我想——要不就是缎带——再不然，兴许是皮条。”

===Original===
His lordship flattened his eyelids, but made no further sign and Mr. Loram continued:

===Chinese===
法官大人把眼睑一抹，却再无别的表示；洛拉姆先生继续道：

===Original===
“Did you or did you not notice what kind of watch-guard Mr. Bellingham was wearing?”

===Chinese===
“您到底留意没留意过，贝林厄姆先生佩的是哪一种表链？”

===Original===
“I did not. Why should I? It was no business of mine.”

===Chinese===
“没留意。我留意它做什么？那与我什么相干。”

===Original===
“But yet you are quite sure about the scarab?”

===Chinese===
“可是对那枚圣甲虫，您倒完全肯定？”

===Original===
“Yes, quite sure.”

===Chinese===
“是，完全肯定。”

===Original===
“You noticed that, then?”

===Chinese===
“那么说，那个您倒是留意了？”

===Original===
“No, I didn’t. How could I when it wasn’t there?”

===Chinese===
“没有，我没留意。它又不在那儿，我怎么留意？”

===Original===
Mr. Loram paused and looked helplessly at the witness; a suppressed titter arose from the body of the Court, and a faint voice from the bench inquired:

===Chinese===
洛拉姆先生顿住了，一筹莫展地望着证人；旁听席上腾起一片压住的嗤笑，法官席上一个细弱的声音问道：

===Original===
“Are you *quite* incapable of giving a straightforward answer?”

===Chinese===
“您*当真*连一句直截了当的回答都给不出吗？”

===Original===
Miss Dobbs’ only reply was to burst into tears; whereupon Mr. Loram abruptly sat down and abandoned his reexamination.

===Chinese===
多布斯小姐的回应只有放声大哭；洛拉姆先生于是猛然落座，放弃了再讯问。

===Original===
The witness-box vacated by Miss Dobbs was occupied successively by Dr. Norbury, Mr. Hurst and the cloakroom attendant, none of whom contributed any new facts, but merely corroborated the statements made by Mr. Jellicoe and the housemaid. Then came the laborer who discovered the bones at Sidcup, and who repeated the evidence that he had given at the inquest, showing that the remains could not have been lying in the watercress-bed more than two years. Finally Dr. Summers was called, and, after he had given a brief description of the bones that he had examined, was asked by Mr. Loram:

===Chinese===
多布斯小姐空出来的证人席，先后由诺伯里博士、赫斯特先生和行李寄存处的管理员坐上；他们谁也没有提供什么新的事实，只是印证了杰利科先生和那位侍女所作的陈述。接着上来的是在锡德卡普发现骨殖的那个劳工，他把验尸审讯上作过的证词又复述了一遍，表明那些遗骸躺在这片水田芥田里的时间不可能超过两年。最后传的是萨默斯医生；他把检验过的骨殖简略描述一番之后，洛拉姆先生问他：

===Original===
“You have heard the description that Mr. Jellicoe has given of the testator?”

===Chinese===
“杰利科先生对立遗嘱人所作的那番描述，您听到了吧？”

===Original===
“I have.”

===Chinese===
“听到了。”

===Original===
“Does that description apply to the person whose remains you examined.”

===Chinese===
“那番描述，与您检验过其遗骸的那个人相符吗？”

===Original===
“In a general way it does.”

===Chinese===
“大体上相符。”

===Original===
“I must ask you for a direct answer﻿—yes or no. Does it apply?”

===Chinese===
“我必须请您给一个直接的回答——是，还是不是。相符吗？”

===Original===
“Yes. But I ought to say that my estimate of the height of the deceased is only approximate.”

===Chinese===
“相符。不过我应当说明，我对死者身高的估计只是个约数。”

===Original===
“Quite so. Judging from your examination of those remains and from Mr. Jellicoe’s description, might those remains be the remains of the testator, John Bellingham?”

===Chinese===
“正是如此。那么，依您对那些遗骸的检验，并参照杰利科先生的描述，那些遗骸有没有可能是立遗嘱人约翰·贝林厄姆的遗骸？”

===Original===
“Yes, they might.”

===Chinese===
“有，有可能。”

===Original===
On receiving this admission Mr. Loram sat down, and Mr. Heath immediately rose to cross-examine.

===Chinese===
得到了这番承认，洛拉姆先生便落了座；希思先生随即起立交叉质询。

===Original===
“When you examined these remains, Doctor Summers, did you discover any personal peculiarities which would enable you to identify them as the remains of any one individual rather than any other individual of similar size, age, and proportions?”

===Chinese===
“萨默斯医生，您检验这些遗骸的时候，可曾发现任何个人特征，足以使您把它们认定为某一个特定个人的遗骸，而不是另一个身材、年龄、比例相仿的个人？”

===Original===
“No. I found nothing that would identify the remains as those of any particular individual.”

===Chinese===
“没有。我没有发现任何东西，可以把这批遗骸认定为某个特定个人的遗骸。”

===Original===
As Mr. Heath asked no further questions, the witness received his dismissal, and Mr. Loram informed the Court that that was his case. The judge bowed somnolently, and then Mr. Heath rose to address the Court on behalf of the respondent. It was not a long speech, nor was it enriched by any displays of florid rhetoric; it concerned itself exclusively with a rebutment of the arguments of the counsel for the petitioner.

===Chinese===
希思先生不再多问，证人便获准退席；洛拉姆先生向法庭陈明，他所主张的案情至此已经陈述完毕。法官昏昏欲睡地点了点头，随后希思先生起立，代表被申请人向法庭致辞。那篇陈词不算长，也没有任何华丽辞藻为之增色；它所专力从事的，只有一件事——驳斥申请人一方的出庭律师提出的种种论证。

===Original===
Having briefly pointed out that the period of absence was too short to give rise of itself to the presumption of death, Mr. Heath continued:

===Chinese===
希思先生简短地指出，失踪的期限太短，其本身不足以产生死亡推定，然后继续说道：

===Original===
“The claim therefore rests upon evidence of a positive character. My learned friend asserts that the testator is presumably dead, and it is for him to prove what he has affirmed. Now, has he done this? I submit that he has not. He has argued with great force and ingenuity that the testator, being a bachelor, a solitary man without wife or child, dependant or master, public or private office of duty, or any bond, responsibility, or any other condition limiting his freedom of action, had no reason or inducement for absconding. This is my learned friend’s argument, and he has conducted it with so much skill and ingenuity that he has not only succeeded in proving his case; he has proved a great deal too much. For if it is true, as my learned friend so justly argues, that a man thus unfettered by obligations of any kind has no reason for disappearing, is it not even more true that he has no reason for not disappearing? My friend has urged that the testator was at liberty to go where he pleased, when he pleased, and how he pleased; and that therefore there was no need for him to abscond. I reply, if he was at liberty to go away, whither, when, and how he pleased, why do we express surprise that he has made use of his liberty? My learned friend points out that the testator notified nobody of his intention of going away and has acquainted no one with his whereabouts; but, I ask, whom should he have notified? He was responsible to nobody; there was no one dependent upon him; his presence or absence was the concern of nobody but himself. If circumstances suddenly arising made it desirable that he should go abroad, why should he not go? I say there was no reason whatever.

===Chinese===
“因此，这项请求所依据的，必须是积极性质的证据。我这位博学的朋友断言，立遗嘱人大概已死；那么，他肯定了什么，就该由他证明什么。他证明了吗？我敢说，他没有。他雄辩而巧妙地论证说：立遗嘱人身为单身汉，是个无妻无子、无亲随亦无东家、无公务亦无私职、更无任何约束、责任或其他限制其行动自由之境况的孤身男子，因此没有潜逃的理由，也没有潜逃的诱因。这就是我那位博学的朋友的论证；他施展的手段如此高明、如此巧妙，以致不但把他的案子证明成功了，还证明了太多、太多了。因为，倘若果真如我那位朋友所如此在理地论证的那样，一个这样不受任何义务束缚的人没有理由消失，那么，他连‘不消失’的理由也没有，岂不更是至理？我这位朋友力言：立遗嘱人想去哪里便去哪里、想何时去便何时去、想怎么去便怎么去，尽有自由，因此无须潜逃。我答曰：他既然想去哪里便去哪里、想何时去便何时去、想怎么去便怎么去，自由得很，那么他行使了这份自由，我们又何必大惊小怪？我那位博学的朋友指出，立遗嘱人临行不曾通知任何人，至今也未曾让任何人知晓他的下落；可是我要问：他该通知谁呢？他对任何人都不负责任；没有谁仰仗他生活；他在与不在，除他自己之外，与任何人无干。倘若突然出现的情况使他宜于出国，他为什么不该去？我说，无论如何都没有理由。

===Original===
“My learned friend has said that the testator went away leaving his affairs to take care of themselves. Now, gentlemen, I ask you if this can fairly be said of a man whose affairs are, as they have been for many years, in the hands of a highly capable, completely trustworthy agent who is better acquainted with them than the testator himself? Clearly it cannot.

===Chinese===
“我那位博学的朋友还说，立遗嘱人一走了之，任凭自己的事务自谋其是。那么，诸位，请你们说句公道话：一个人的事务多年来一向握在一位精明干练、完全可靠的代理人手里——此人对这些事务的了解，甚至比立遗嘱人本人还深——对这样一个人，‘听任事务自谋其是’这话能说得过去吗？显然说不过去。

===Original===
“To conclude this part of the argument: I submit that the circumstances of the so-called disappearance of the testator present nothing out of the ordinary. The testator is a man of ample means, without any responsibilities to fetter his movements, and has been in the constant habit of traveling, often into remote and distant regions. The mere fact that he has been absent somewhat longer than usual affords no ground whatever for the drastic proceeding of presumption of death and taking possession of his property.

===Chinese===
“归结到这部分论证：我敢说，立遗嘱人这桩所谓失踪的种种情形，并无任何异乎寻常之处。立遗嘱人家境殷实，没有任何牵累束缚他的行踪，又一贯惯于旅行，足迹常至僻远之域。仅仅因为此番外出比往常久了些，便断然推定他死亡、进而占据他的财产——这样严厉的举动，没有任何理由可言。

===Original===
“With reference to the human remains which have been mentioned in connection with the case I need say but little. The attempt to connect them with the testator has failed completely. You yourselves have heard Doctor Summers state on oath that they cannot be identified as the remains of any particular person. That would seem to dispose of them effectually. I must remark upon a very singular point that has been raised by the learned counsel for the petitioner, which is this:

===Chinese===
“至于那些与本案相提并论的人骨，我无须多费唇舌。把它们同立遗嘱人联系起来的企图，已彻底失败。诸位亲耳听到萨默斯医生宣誓陈述：这批遗骸无法认定为任何一个特定个人的遗骸。这一来，它们似乎便被有效地打发掉了。不过，申请人一方的出庭律师提出了一个非常离奇的论点，我必须说上一说，那就是：

===Original===
“My learned friend points out that these remains were discovered near Eltham and near Woodford and that the testator was last seen alive at one of these two places. This he considers for some reason to be a highly significant fact. But I cannot agree with him. If the testator had been last seen alive at Woodford and the remains had been found at Woodford, or if he had disappeared from Eltham, and the remains had been found at Eltham, that would have had some significance. But he can only have been last seen at one of the places, whereas the remains have been found at both places. Here again my learned friend seems to have proved too much.

===Chinese===
“我那位博学的朋友指出，这批遗骸是在埃尔瑟姆附近和伍德福德附近发现的，而立遗嘱人最后活人露面，是在这两地之一。不知出于什么缘故，他认为这是一个意义重大无比的事实。恕我不能同意。倘若立遗嘱人最后是在伍德福德被人看见，遗骸也是在伍德福德发现的；或者他是在埃尔瑟姆失踪的，遗骸也是在埃尔瑟姆发现的——那才有些意义。可他至多只能是在其中一地最后露面，而遗骸却在两地都有发现。在这一点上，我那位博学的朋友似乎又是证明得太多了。

===Original===
“But I need not occupy your time further. I repeat that, in order to justify us in presuming the death of the testator, clear and positive evidence would be necessary. That no such evidence has been brought forward. Accordingly, seeing that the testator may return at any time and is entitled to find his property intact, I shall ask you for a verdict that will secure to him this measure of ordinary justice.”

===Chinese===
“但我不必再占用诸位的时间了。我再说一遍：要有正当理由推定立遗嘱人死亡，必须有明确而积极的证据。这样的证据，一件也没有提出来。因此，鉴于立遗嘱人随时可能归来，而且有权发现自己的财产完好无损，我将请求诸位作出一项裁决，把这一份起码的普通公道保全给他。”

===Original===
At the conclusion of Mr. Heath’s speech the judge, as if awakening from a refreshing nap, opened his eyes; and uncommonly shrewd, intelligent eyes they were when the expressive eyelids were duly tucked up out of the way. He commenced by reading over a part of the will and certain notes﻿—which he appeared to have made in some miraculous fashion with his eyes shut﻿—and then proceeded to review the evidence and the counsels’ arguments for the instruction of the jury.

===Chinese===
希思先生的陈词一收尾，法官便睁开了眼睛，仿佛小睡一场、精神焕发地醒来；待那两片表情丰富的眼睑规规矩矩地收拢让路之后，那双眼实是无比犀利、无比聪慧。他先把遗嘱的一部分和若干笔记诵读了一遍——那些笔记似乎是他闭着眼睛以某种神乎其神的手段记下来的——然后开始为指点陪审团而检视证据与两位出庭律师的论证。

===Original===
“Before considering the evidence which you have heard, gentlemen,” he said, “it will be well for me to say a few words to you on the general aspects of the case which is occupying our attention.

===Chinese===
“诸位，在考量你们已经听到的证据之前，”他说，“且容我就眼下这桩案子的总体面貌，向你们讲几句话。

===Original===
“If a person goes abroad or disappears from his home and his ordinary places of resort and is absent for a long period of time, the presumption of death arises at the expiration of seven years from the date on which he was last heard of. That is to say, that the total disappearance of an individual for seven years constitutes presumptive evidence that the said individual is dead; and the presumption can be set aside only by the production of evidence that he was alive at some time within that period of seven years. But if, on the other hand, it is sought to presume the death of a person who has been absent for a shorter period than seven years, it is necessary to produce such evidence as shall make it highly probable that the said person is dead. Of course, presumption implies supposition as opposed to actual demonstration; but, nevertheless, the evidence in such a case must be of a kind that tends to create a very strong belief that death has occurred; and I need hardly say that the shorter the period of absence, the more convincing must be the evidence.

===Chinese===
“倘若有人出国去了，或者从自己的家宅和惯常的去处消失，长期杳无音信，那么，自最后一次得到他音讯之日起满七年，死亡推定便告成立。这就是说：一个人彻底消失满七年，即构成该人已经死亡的推定证据；除非提出证据，证明他在那七年之内的某个时候尚在人世，否则这一推定不能推翻。但反过来，倘若要推定一个失踪未满七年的人死亡，就必须提出证据，使该人已死的可能性显得极大。当然，‘推定’意味着假设，与实际的证明相对；尽管如此，这类案件中的证据，仍必须是属于那种足以使人产生强烈信念、确信死亡已经发生的证据；而且毋庸赘言：失踪的时间越短，证据就越要有说服力。

===Original===
“In the present case, the testator, John Bellingham, has been absent somewhat under two years. This is a relatively short period, and in itself gives rise to no presumption of death. Nevertheless, death has been presumed in a case where the period of absence was even shorter and the insurance recovered; but here the evidence supporting the belief in the occurrence of death was exceedingly weighty.

===Chinese===
“就本案而言，立遗嘱人约翰·贝林厄姆失踪尚不满两年。这算是比较短的期限，就其本身而言，不产生任何死亡推定。话虽如此，从前也有过一案：失踪的期限比这还短，死亡却得到推定，保险金也获得赔付；但在那桩案子里，支持死亡发生这一信念的证据，分量极重。

===Original===
“The testator in this case was a shipmaster, and his disappearance was accompanied by the disappearance of the ship and the entire ship’s company in the course of a voyage from London to Marseilles. The loss of the ship and her crew was the only reasonable explanation of the disappearance, and, short of actual demonstration, the facts offered convincing evidence of the death of all persons on board. I mention this case as an illustration. You are not dealing with speculative probabilities. You are contemplating a very momentous proceeding, and you must be very sure of your ground. Consider what it is that you are asked to do.

===Chinese===
“那桩案子的立遗嘱人是一位船长；在从伦敦驶往马赛的航程中，他与轮船及全船人员一同失踪。船与船员的覆没，是其失踪唯一合理的解释；而且虽无实际的证明，种种事实已为船上所有人尽皆身亡提供了令人信服的证据。我提这桩案子，不过举例为证。诸位此刻处理的，不是悬揣的概率。诸位正在掂量的是一桩极其重大的程序，诸位必须把脚跟站得极稳。请想一想：人家要求你们去做的，是什么事。

===Original===
“The petitioner asks permission to presume the death of the testator in order that the testator’s property may be distributed among the beneficiaries under the will. The granting of such permission involves us in the gravest responsibility. An ill-considered decision might be productive of a serious injustice to the testator, an injustice that could never be remedied. Hence it is incumbent upon you to weigh the evidence with the greatest care, to come to no decision without the profoundest consideration of all the facts.

===Chinese===
“申请人请求允许推定立遗嘱人死亡，以便立遗嘱人的财产得以在遗嘱的各受益人之间分配。这样的许可一经授予，我们便负起最重大的责任。一个欠考虑的决定，可能对立遗嘱人造成严重的不公——一种永远无从补救的不公。因此，诸位有责任以最大的审慎权衡证据；不把全部事实作最深切的考量，便不作出任何决定。

===Original===
“The evidence that you have heard divides itself into two parts﻿—that relating to the circumstances of the testator’s disappearance, and that relating to certain human remains. In connection with the latter I can only express my surprise and regret that the application was not postponed until the completion of the coroner’s inquest, and leave you to consider the evidence. You will bear in mind that Doctor Summers has stated explicitly that the remains cannot be identified as those of any particular individual, but that the testator and the unknown deceased had so many points of resemblance that they might possibly be one and the same person.

===Chinese===
“你们听到的证据可分为两部分：一部分涉及立遗嘱人失踪的情形，一部分涉及若干人骨。就后者而言，我只能表示我的诧异与遗憾：这项申请竟没有延期到验尸官的审讯完结之后再行提出；证据如何掂量，我便交给诸位自行斟酌。你们要记住：萨默斯医生已明确陈述，这批遗骸无法认定为任何特定个人的遗骸；但立遗嘱人与那位不知名的死者相似之处甚多，两人有可能是同一个人。

===Original===
“With reference to the circumstances of the disappearance, you have heard the evidence of Mr. Jellicoe to the effect that the testator has on no previous occasion gone abroad without informing him as to his proposed destination. But in considering what weight you are to give to this statement you will bear in mind that when the testator set out for Paris after his interview with Doctor Norbury he left Mr. Jellicoe without any information as to his specific destination, his address in Paris, or the precise date when he should return, and that Mr. Jellicoe was unable to tell us where the testator went or what was his business. Mr. Jellicoe was, in fact, for a time without any means of tracing the testator or ascertaining his whereabouts.

===Chinese===
“至于失踪的情形，你们已经听到杰利科先生的证词，大意是说：立遗嘱人以往出国，从无一次不把他拟去的去处告知杰利科先生。但在权衡这句话该有多重的分量时，你们要记住：立遗嘱人同诺伯里博士会面之后动身赴巴黎时，并没有把他的具体目的地、他在巴黎的住址、或他返程的确切日期告知杰利科先生；杰利科先生也说不清立遗嘱人去了哪里、所为何事。事实上，杰利科先生一度没有任何办法追寻立遗嘱人、查明他的下落。

===Original===
“The evidence of the housemaid, Dobbs, and of Mr. Hurst is rather confusing. It appears that the testator came to the house, and when looked for later was not to be found. A search of the premises showed that he was not in the house, whence it seems to follow that he must have left it; but since no one was informed of his intention to leave, and he had expressed the intention of staying to see Mr. Hurst, his conduct in thus going away surreptitiously must appear somewhat eccentric. The point that you have to consider, therefore, is whether a person who is capable of thus departing in a surreptitious and eccentric manner from a house, without giving notice to the servants, is capable also of departing in a surreptitious and eccentric manner from his usual places of resort without giving notice to his friends or thereafter informing them of his whereabouts.

===Chinese===
“侍女多布斯与赫斯特先生的证词，相当缠夹不清。情形看来是：立遗嘱人到过那座宅子，后来再找他时，人已不见。宅内一搜，表明他不在屋里；由此推论，他必定是离开了；可是，他离去的意图没有告知任何人，他又明言要留下同赫斯特先生见面，这样偷偷摸摸一走了之，行止未免有些古怪。因此，诸位须考量的一点是：一个能够这般鬼祟而古怪地离开一座宅子、不向仆人打一声招呼的人，是否也能同样鬼祟而古怪地离开他惯常的去处，不向朋友们辞行，此后也不将自己的下落告知他们。

===Original===
“The questions, then, gentlemen, that you have to ask yourselves before deciding on your verdict are two: first, Are the circumstances of the testator’s disappearance and his continued absence incongruous with his habits and personal peculiarities as they are known to you? and second, Are there any facts which indicate in a positive manner that the testator is dead? Ask yourselves these questions, gentlemen, and the answers to them, furnished by the evidence that you have heard, will guide you to your decision.”

===Chinese===
“因此，诸位在裁决定谳之前须自问的问题有两个：其一，立遗嘱人失踪及此后持续下落不明的种种情形，与你们所知的他的习惯和个人特性是否相悖？其二，是否存在任何事实，以积极的方式表明立遗嘱人已经死亡？诸位，把这两个问题问过自己，你们所听到的证据自会给出答案，指引你们作出决定。”

===Original===
Having delivered himself of the above instructions, the judge applied himself to the perusal of the will with professional gusto, in which occupation he was presently disturbed by the announcement of the foreman of the jury that a verdict had been agreed upon.

===Chinese===
法官发表完上述训示，便以行家的兴味研读起遗嘱来；这项消遣不久便被陪审长打断了——他宣布，陪审团已经议定裁决。

===Original===
The judge sat up and glanced at the jury-box, and when the foreman proceeded to state that “We find no sufficient reason for presuming the testator, John Bellingham, to be dead,” he nodded approvingly. Evidently that was his opinion, too, as he was careful to explain when he conveyed to Mr. Loram the refusal of the Court to grant the permission applied for.

===Chinese===
法官坐直身子，朝陪审席瞥了一眼；待陪审长陈明“我们认定，没有充分的理由推定立遗嘱人约翰·贝林厄姆已经死亡”，他赞许地点了点头。显而易见，那也正是他本人的意见——当他向洛拉姆先生转达本庭拒绝批准所请的许可时，还不忘把这层意思仔细说明。

===Original===
The decision was a great relief to me, and also, I think, to Miss Bellingham; but most of all to her father, who, with instinctive good manners, since he could not suppress a smile of triumph, rose and hastily stumped out of the Court, so that the discomfited Hurst should not see him. His daughter and I followed, and as we left the Court she remarked, with a smile:

===Chinese===
这一裁决使我不胜宽慰；我想，贝林厄姆小姐也是如此；而宽慰之最甚者，莫过于她的父亲——他抑制不住一脸得意的笑，出于天生的良好教养，便起身拖着腿匆匆走出法庭，免得让那个败下阵来的赫斯特瞧见。他女儿和我跟了出去；离庭之际，她微笑着说道：

===Original===
“So our pauperism is not, after all, made absolute. There is still a chance for us in the Chapter of Accidents﻿—and perhaps even for poor old Uncle John.”

===Chinese===
“这样看来，我们到底不至于沦为绝对的赤贫。在‘不测之祸’的一章里，我们仍有一线生机——说不定，连可怜的约翰伯伯也还有一线生机呢。”

## XV Circumstantial Evidence / 十五 间接证据
===Original===
The morning after the hearing saw me setting forth on my round in more than usually good spirits. The round itself was but a short one, for my list contained only a couple of “chronics,” and this, perhaps, contributed to my cheerful outlook on life. But there were other reasons. The decision of the Court had come as an unexpected reprieve and the ruin of my friends’ prospects was at least postponed. Then, I had learned that Thorndyke was back from Bristol and wished me to look in on him; and, finally, Miss Bellingham had agreed to spend this very afternoon with me, browsing round the galleries at the British Museum.

===Chinese===
开庭次日清晨，我出门巡诊，心绪比平日格外轻快。这趟巡诊本身不过短短一程，因为名单上只有两三名“老病号”，这或许也给我的人生观添了几分亮色。但还有别的缘故。法院的裁决竟成了一场出乎意料的缓刑，至少，朋友们前景的倾覆是被推迟了。再者，我已得知桑代克从布里斯托尔回来了，要我过去看他；最后，贝林厄姆小姐已经应允，就在今天下午陪我一同去大英博物馆的展厅里浏览盘桓。

===Original===
I had disposed of my two patients by a quarter to eleven, and three minutes later was striding down Mitre Court, all agog to hear what Thorndyke had to say with reference to my notes on the inquest. The “oak” was open when I arrived at his chambers, and a modest flourish on the little brass knocker of the inner door was answered by my quondam teacher himself.

===Chinese===
十点三刻之前，我已经把两三名病人打发停当；三分钟后，便大步流星地下了米特巷（Mitre Court），心急火燎地想听听桑代克对我的验尸记录有何见教。来到他的寓所，外层那道“橡木门”开着，我在内门那只小铜门环上斯斯文文地叩了一记，前来应门的，正是我昔日的老师本人。

===Original===
“How good of you, Berkeley,” he said, shaking hands genially, “to look me up so early. I am alone, just looking through the report of the evidence of yesterday’s proceedings.”

===Chinese===
“难为你了，伯克利，”他热诚地握着我的手说道，“这么早就来看我。眼下就我一个，正翻看昨天庭讯证词的笔录呢。”

===Original===
He placed an easy chair for me, and, gathering up a bundle of typewritten papers, laid them aside on the table.

===Chinese===
他替我摆好一把安乐椅，随即收拾起一叠打字稿，放到一旁的桌上。

===Original===
“Were you surprised at the decision?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“这裁决出乎您的意料么？”我问。

===Original===
“No,” he answered. “Two years is a short period of absence; but still, it might easily have gone the other way. I am greatly relieved. The respite gives us time to carry out our investigations without undue hurry.”

===Chinese===
“不，”他答道。“失踪两年，为期尚短；不过，话说回来，这事也完全可能走向另一头。我大大松了一口气。这段喘息，让我们可以从容不迫地展开调查。”

===Original===
“Did you find my notes of any use?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“我的记录派上用场了么？”我问。

===Original===
“Heath did. Polton handed them to him, and they were invaluable to him for his cross-examination. I haven’t seen them yet; in fact, I have only just got them back from him. Let us go through them together now.”

===Chinese===
“对希思有用。波尔顿把稿子转交给了他，对他盘问证人大有裨益。我自己倒还没看过；不瞒你说，我也是方才才从他手里取回来的。现在咱们就一起过一遍吧。”

===Original===
He opened a drawer and taking from it my notebook, seated himself, and began to read through my notes with grave attention, while I stood and looked shyly over his shoulder. On the page that contained my sketches of the Sidcup arm, showing the distribution of the snails’ eggs on the bones, he lingered with a faint smile that made me turn hot and red.

===Chinese===
他拉开抽屉，从里面取出我的笔记本，坐定下来，开始郑重其事地通读我的记录；我立在他身后，腼腆地越过他的肩头望着。翻到画有锡德卡普那条手臂、标明螺卵在骨上分布情形的那几页草图时，他流连片刻，唇边浮起一丝微笑，看得我脸上发烧，红透了。

===Original===
“Those sketches look rather footy,” I said; “but I had to put something in my notebook.”

===Chinese===
“那些草图看着怪寒碜的，”我说；“不过笔记本里总得记点什么。”

===Original===
“You did not attach any importance, then, to the facts that they illustrated?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你是没把草图所示的事实当一回事了？”

===Original===
“No. The egg-patches were there, so I noted the fact. That’s all.”

===Chinese===
“没有。螺卵斑就在那儿，我便记下这么个事实。仅此而已。”

===Original===
“I congratulate you, Berkeley. There is not one man in twenty who would have had the sense to make a careful note of what he considers an unimportant or irrelevant fact; and the investigator who notes only those things that appear significant is perfectly useless. He gives himself no material for reconsideration. But you don’t mean that these egg-patches and worm-tubes appeared to you to have no significance at all?”

===Chinese===
“我要向你道贺，伯克利。二十个人里也未必有一个，肯把自认无关紧要或不相干的事实细心记下来；而那种只记看似要紧之事的调查者，简直是全然无用。他不给自己留下一丝重新斟酌的余地。不过，你总不至于说，这些螺卵斑和虫管在你眼里竟毫无意义吧？”

===Original===
“Oh, of course, they show the position in which the bones were lying.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那当然，它们表明了骨殖原来躺卧的姿势。”

===Original===
“Exactly. The arm was lying, fully extended, with the dorsal side uppermost. But we also learn from these egg-patches that the hand had been separated from the arm before it was thrown into the pond; and there is something very remarkable in that.”

===Chinese===
“正是。那条手臂是完全伸展着躺卧的，背侧面朝上。但这些螺卵斑还告诉我们：手是在被抛入池中之前，就已同手臂分了家；这一点，大有可注意之处。”

===Original===
I leaned over his shoulder and gazed at my sketches, amazed at the rapidity with which he had reconstructed the limb from my rough drawings of the individual bones.

===Chinese===
我俯到他肩后，凝视着自己的草图，惊叹他竟从我这零散单骨的粗笔简图里，如此迅捷地重构出整条肢体。

===Original===
“I don’t quite see how you arrived at it, though,” I said.

===Chinese===
“不过，我还不大明白您是怎么推出来的。”我说。

===Original===
“Well, look at your drawings. The egg-patches are on the dorsal surface of the scapula, the humerus, and the bones of the forearm. But here you have shown six of the bones of the hand: two metacarpals, the os magnum, and three phalanges; and they all have egg-patches on the palmar surface. Therefore the hand was lying palm upward.”

===Chinese===
“喏，看你画的图。螺卵斑在肩胛骨、肱骨和前臂诸骨的背侧面。可你这里画了六块手骨：两块掌骨、一块头状骨，加三节指骨；它们上面的螺卵斑，却全在掌侧面。可见那只手当时是掌心朝上躺着的。”

===Original===
“But the hand may have been pronated.”

===Chinese===
“可那只手说不定原本是旋前的。”

===Original===
“If you mean pronated in relation to the arm, that is impossible, for the position of the egg-patches shows clearly that the bones of the arm were lying in the position of supination. Thus the dorsal surface of the arm and the palmar surface of the hand respectively were uppermost, which is an anatomical impossibility so long as the hand is attached to the arm.”

===Chinese===
“如果你说的是相对于手臂的旋前，那断无可能；因为螺卵斑的位置清清楚楚表明，手臂诸骨是取旋后位躺卧的。如此一来，臂的背侧面与手的掌侧面便各自朝天，而只要手还连在臂上，这在解剖学上是断然不可能的。”

===Original===
“But might not the hand have become detached after lying in the pond for some time?”

===Chinese===
“可是，手在池中泡了一阵之后，难道不会自行脱落吗？”

===Original===
“No. It could not have been detached until the ligaments had decayed, and if it had been separated after the decay of the soft parts, the bones would have been thrown into disorder. But the egg-patches are all on the palmar surface, showing that the bones were still in their normal relative positions. No, Berkeley, that hand was thrown into the pond separately from the arm.”

===Chinese===
“不会。非到韧带朽烂，它是脱不下来的；而倘若它是在软组织腐朽之后才分离的，诸骨势必乱作一团。可螺卵斑尽在掌侧面，说明诸骨仍保持寻常的相对位置。不会的，伯克利，那只手是同手臂分开抛进池子的。”

===Original===
“But why should it have been?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“可为什么要这样做呢？”我问。

===Original===
“Ah, there is a very pretty little problem for you to consider. And, meantime, let me tell you that your expedition has been a brilliant success. You are an excellent observer. Your only fault is that when you have noted certain facts you don’t seem fully to appreciate their significance﻿—which is merely a matter of inexperience. As to the facts that you have collected, several of them are of prime importance.”

===Chinese===
“啊，这可是个顶有趣的小问题，留给你去思量。这会儿，且让我告诉你：你这一趟出行，是一场漂亮的胜仗。你是个出色的观察者。唯一的短处是，记下某些事实之后，你似乎未能充分领会它们的意义——这不过是历练未到之故。至于你搜罗来的事实，其中几件是头等重要。”

===Original===
“I am glad you are satisfied,” said I, “though I don’t see that I have discovered much excepting those snail’s eggs; and they don’t seem to have advanced matters very much.”

===Chinese===
“您满意，我就高兴了，”我说，“不过照我看，除了那些螺卵，我实在没发现什么名堂；再说，它们似乎也没叫案情前进多少。”

===Original===
“A definite fact, Berkeley, is a definite asset. Perhaps we may presently find a little space in our Chinese puzzle which this fact of the detached hand will just drop into. But, tell me, did you find nothing unexpected or suggestive about those bones﻿—as to their number and condition, for instance?”

===Chinese===
“一个确凿的事实，伯克利，便是一笔确凿的资产。眼下这幅中国七巧板，说不定很快会缺出一小块空位，恰好把这‘断手’的事实嵌进去。不过你且说说，那些骨殖就没有让你看出一点出乎意料或耐人寻味的地方么——比方说，就其数目与状况而言？”

===Original===
“Well, I thought it a little queer that the scapula and clavicle should be there. I should have expected him to cut the arm off at the shoulder-joint.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，肩胛骨和锁骨居然也在其中，我觉得有点儿蹊跷。换了我，准以为他会在肩关节那儿把手臂卸下来。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Thorndyke; “so should I; and so it has been done in every case of dismemberment that I am acquainted with. To an ordinary person, the arm seems to join on to the trunk at the shoulder-joint, and that is where he would naturally sever it. What explanation do you suggest of this unusual mode of severing the arm?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”桑代克说；“我也这么想；就我所知的每一桩肢解案，都是这般行事的。在寻常人看来，手臂仿佛就是在肩关节处同躯干相连，那里自然也就是他下刀之处。对这种卸臂的反常方式，你能提出什么解释？”

===Original===
“Do you think the fellow could have been a butcher?” I asked, remembering Dr. Summers’ remark. “This is the way a shoulder of mutton is taken off.”

===Chinese===
“您看这家伙会不会是个屠夫？”我问，想起了萨默斯医生的话。“羊肩肉就是这么卸的。”

===Original===
“No,” replied Thorndyke. “A butcher includes the scapula in a shoulder of mutton for a specific purpose, namely, to take off a given quantity of meat. And also, as a sheep has no clavicle, it is the easiest way to detach the limb. But I imagine a butcher would find himself in difficulties if he attempted to take off a man’s arm in that way. The clavicle would be a new and perplexing feature. Then, too, a butcher does not deal very delicately with his subject; if he has to divide a joint, he just cuts through it and does not trouble himself to avoid marking the bones. But you note here that there is not a single scratch or score on any one of the bones, not even where the finger was removed. Now, if you have ever prepared bones for a museum, as I have, you will remember the extreme care that is necessary in disarticulating joints to avoid disfiguring the articular ends of the bones with cuts and scratches.”

===Chinese===
“不是，”桑代克答道。“屠夫把肩胛骨连在羊肩肉里，是有讲究的：为的是卸下足量的肉。再者，羊没有锁骨，那样卸肢最省事。可要是照那法子去卸一条人的手臂，我想屠夫就要抓瞎了。锁骨对他是个又新鲜又棘手的东西。况且，屠夫侍弄他的物件，并不怎么细腻；碰上要剖一个关节，他索性一刀剁穿，才不肯费神避着骨头。可你注意到没有：这里没有一块骨头上留有一丝刮痕或刀痕，连卸下手指的地方也没有。喏，你要是像我一样给博物馆制备过骨标本，就会记得，分解关节时得何等极端地小心，才不至于让切割和刮擦毁了骨头的关节端。”

===Original===
“Then you think that the person who dismembered this body must have had some anatomical knowledge and skill?”

===Chinese===
“那么您认为，肢解这具尸首的人，必定具备解剖学的知识和手上功夫了？”

===Original===
“That is what has been suggested. The suggestion is not mine.”

===Chinese===
“有人是这样推断的。这推断并非出自我口。”

===Original===
“Then I infer that you don’t agree?”

===Chinese===
“那我推断，您是不以为然了？”

===Original===
Thorndyke smiled. “I am sorry to be so cryptic, Berkeley, but you understand that I can’t make statements. Still, I am trying to lead you to make certain inferences from the facts that are in your possession.”

===Chinese===
桑代克微微一笑。“抱歉，我竟这样故弄玄虚，伯克利；不过你明白，我不能妄下断语。尽管如此，我正设法引你从自己掌握的事实里，作出几项推断来。”

===Original===
“If I make the right inference, will you tell me?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“倘若我推断对了，您肯告诉我么？”我问。

===Original===
“It won’t be necessary,” he answered, with the same quiet smile. “When you have fitted the puzzle together you don’t need to be told you have done it.”

===Chinese===
“那倒用不着，”他答道，脸上仍是那一抹安静的微笑。“等你把拼图拼拢了，自然晓得拼成了，用不着谁来告诉你。”

===Original===
It was most infernally tantalizing. I pondered on the problem with a scowl of such intense cogitation that Thorndyke laughed outright.

===Chinese===
这真是吊人胃口吊到了叫人光火的地步。我眉头紧锁，苦苦思索那道难题，一副冥思苦想到咬牙切齿的神情，惹得桑代克放声大笑。

===Original===
“It seems to me,” I said, at length, “that the identity of the remains is the primary question and that it is a question of fact. It doesn’t seem any use to speculate about it.”

===Chinese===
“依我看，”我终于开口道，“骨殖的身份才是头一个问题，而这是个事实问题。空自揣测，似乎全无益处。”

===Original===
“Exactly. Either these bones are the remains of John Bellingham or they are not. There will be no doubt on the subject when all the bones are assembled﻿—if ever they are. And the settlement of that question will probably throw light on the further question: Who deposited them in the places in which they were found? But to return to your observations: did you gather nothing from the other bones? From the complete state of the neck vertebrae for instance?”

===Chinese===
“正是。这些骨殖要么是约翰·贝林厄姆的遗骸，要么不是。待到诸骨凑齐——倘若真有凑齐的一天——此事便再无疑问了。而那个问题一了结，多半也能照见下一个问题：是谁把它们安置在各自的发现之地的？不过，且回到你的观察上来：从其余的骨殖里，你就毫无收获么？比如，从颈椎的齐全完好上？”

===Original===
“Well, it did strike me as rather odd that the fellow should have gone to the trouble of separating the atlas from the skull. He must have been pretty handy with the scalpel to have done it as cleanly as he seems to have done; but I don’t see why he should have gone about the business in the most inconvenient way.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，那家伙居然肯费事把寰椎从颅骨上分离下来，我确实觉得怪。瞧他做得那般干净利落，想必备刀功夫相当娴熟；可我不明白，他何苦偏挑最不便当的路数办事。”

===Original===
“You notice the uniformity of method. He has separated the head from the spine, instead of cutting through the spine lower down, as most persons would have done: he removed the arms with the entire shoulder-girdle, instead of simply cutting them off at the shoulder-joints. Even in the thighs the same peculiarity appears; for in neither case was the kneecap found with the thighbone, although it seems to have been searched for. Now the obvious way to divide the leg is to cut through the patellar ligament, leaving the kneecap attached to the thigh. But in this case, the kneecap appears to have been left attached to the shank. Can you explain why this person should have adopted this unusual and rather inconvenient method? Can you suggest a motive for this procedure, or can you think of any circumstances which might lead a person to adopt this method by preference?”

===Chinese===
“你注意到手法的一律了。他把头颅从脊柱上摘了下来，而不是像多数人那样从脊柱靠下处一斩了之：他连整个肩带一并卸下双臂，而不是简简单单从肩关节切下了事。连大腿上也现出同样的特异之处：两处发现的膝盖骨，都不与股骨相伴，尽管看来搜寻过。要知道，分断一条腿，最现成的法子是切断髌韧带，让膝盖骨连在大腿上。可这一案里，膝盖骨似乎被留给了小腿。你能解释，此人何以采用这种反常而颇不便当的方法么？你能为这套手法设想一个动机，或者想出什么情形，会叫人情愿独取此法么？”

===Original===
“It seems as if he wished, for some reason, to divide the body into definite anatomical regions.”

===Chinese===
“看样子，他出于某种缘故，想把尸体分作界限分明的各个解剖区域。”

===Original===
Thorndyke chuckled. “You are not offering that suggestion as an explanation, are you? Because it would require more explaining than the original problem. And it is not even true. Anatomically speaking, the kneecap appertains to the thigh rather than to the shank. It is a sesamoid bone belonging to the thigh muscles; yet in this case it has been left attached, apparently to the shank. No, Berkeley, that cat won’t jump. Our unknown operator was not preparing a skeleton as a museum specimen; he was dividing a body up into convenient sized portions for the purpose of conveying them to various ponds. Now what circumstances might have led him to divide it in this peculiar manner?”

===Chinese===
桑代克呵呵地笑了。“你该不是把这说法当作解释端出来的吧？因为要解释它，比原题还费唇舌。何况它压根儿站不住。就解剖学而论，膝盖骨归属于大腿，而非小腿。它是一块隶属大腿肌群的籽骨；可这一案里，看样子它却被留在了小腿上。不，伯克利，那可不成。我们这位无名的执刀人，并不是在制备一具供博物馆陈列的骨架；他是在把一具尸体分作大小合手的一块块，好搬运到各处池塘去。那么，是什么情形，可能引着他照这古怪的路数分尸呢？”

===Original===
“I am afraid I have no suggestion to offer. Have you?”

===Chinese===
“恐怕我提不出什么高见。您有么？”

===Original===
Thorndyke suddenly lapsed into ambiguity. “I think,” he said, “it is possible to conceive such circumstances, and so, probably, will you if you think it over.”

===Chinese===
桑代克忽然又含糊起来。“我想，”他说，“设想出这样的情形是可能的；而你若细细思量，多半也想得到。”

===Original===
“Did you gather anything of importance from the evidence at the inquest?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“从验尸审讯的证词里，您听出什么要紧的东西没有？”我问。

===Original===
“It is difficult to say,” he replied. “The whole of my conclusions in this case are based on what is virtually circumstantial evidence. I have not one single fact of which I can say that it admits only of a single interpretation. Still, it must be remembered that even the most inconclusive facts, if sufficiently multiplied, yield a highly conclusive total. And my little pile of evidence is growing, particle by particle; but we mustn’t sit here gossiping at this hour of the day; I have to consult with Marchmont and you say that you have an early afternoon engagement. We can walk together as far as Fleet Street.”

===Chinese===
“难说，”他答道。“我在这桩案子里的一切结论，实质上全建立在近乎间接证据的东西上。我拿不出一件事实，敢说它只容一种解释。然而别忘了：哪怕最不足为凭的事实，只要积得够多，也能汇成一个极具结论性的总和。我那一小堆证据，正一粒一粒地增高；不过，这个钟点，咱们可不能坐在这儿闲磕牙；我得去同马奇蒙特商量，而你呢，你也说过，午后早些时候有个约。咱们可以同路走到舰队街。”

===Original===
A minute or two later we went our respective ways, Thorndyke toward Lombard Street and I to Fetter Lane, not unmindful of those coming events that were casting so agreeable a shadow before them.

===Chinese===
一两分钟后，我们便分道而行：桑代克往朗伯德街（Lombard Street）去，我回菲特巷，心里还惦着那些渐渐临近的喜事，它们正把一道多么宜人的影子，投在身前。

===Original===
There was only one message awaiting me, and when Adolphus had delivered it (amidst mephitic fumes that rose from the basement, premonitory of fried plaice), I pocketed my stethoscope and betook myself to Gunpowder Alley, the aristocratic abode of my patient, joyfully threading the now familiar passages of Gough Square and Wine Office Court, and meditating pleasantly on the curious literary flavor that pervades these little-known regions. For the shade of the author of *Rasselas* still seems to haunt the scenes of his Titanic labors and his ponderous but homely and temperate rejoicings. Every court and alley whispers of books and of the making of books: forms of type, trundled noisily on trolleys by ink-smeared boys, salute the wayfarer at odd corners; piles of strawboard, rolls or bales of paper, drums of printing-ink or roller composition stand on the pavement outside dark entries; basement windows give glimpses into Hadean caverns tenanted by legions of printer’s devils; and the very air is charged with the hum of press and with odors of glue and paste and oil. The entire neighborhood is given up to the printer and binder; and even my patient turned out to be a guillotine-knife grinder﻿—a ferocious and revolutionary calling strangely at variance with his harmless appearance and meek bearing.

===Chinese===
只有一张留言条等着我。阿道弗斯把它交到我手里（其时地下室正腾起一股恶臭的烟气，预示着炸鲽鱼将上灶），我便揣起听诊器，往火药巷（Gunpowder Alley）——我那位高贵病人的府邸——去；一路欢欢喜喜，穿行高夫广场（Gough Square）和酒务巷（Wine Office Court）这些如今已走熟了的巷弄，悠然玩味着弥漫在这些僻静角落里的奇特书香。因为《拉塞拉斯》（*Rasselas*）作者的幽魂，似乎仍在这些旧址间流连——流连他那些泰坦式的浩大劳作，和他那笨重而家常、有节制的欢庆。每一条院落与巷子，都在悄声议论着书、议论着书的制作：浑身墨污的少年推着小车，把一版版活字轰隆隆地运过，在古怪的拐角向行人致意；草纸板的堆垛、成卷成捆的纸张、一桶桶的印刷油墨或墨辊胶，陈列在黑洞洞的门户旁的人行道上；地下室的窗口里，可以瞥见一座座冥府般的窟穴，穴中糜集着成群的印刷小鬼；连空气里都饱含着印刷机的嗡鸣，饱含着胶水、糨糊和油料的气味。整个街坊全交给了印刷匠与装订匠；连我这位病人，到头来也查出是一位闸刀磨刀匠——一门凶悍而革命的营生，同他那无害的相貌与温顺的举止奇怪地不相称。

===Original===
I was in good time at my tryst, despite the hindrances of fried plaice and invalid guillotinists; but, early as I was, Miss Bellingham was already waiting in the garden﻿—she had been filling a bowl with flowers﻿—ready to sally forth.

===Chinese===
尽管有炸鲽鱼和闹病号的闸刀匠们作梗，我还是准时赶到了约会之地；只是我到得再早，贝林厄姆小姐已经先在花园里候着了——她方才正往一只碗里插花——只待出发。

===Original===
“It is quite like old times,” she said, as we turned into Fetter Lane, “to be going to the Museum together. It brings back the Tell-el-Amarna tablets and all your kindness and unselfish labor. I suppose we shall walk there today?”

===Chinese===
“这真像旧日时光，”拐进菲特巷时她说道，“咱们又一道上博物馆去了。这让我想起泰尔-埃尔-阿马尔纳泥板，想起你的种种好心和无私的劳碌。我想，咱们今天是走着去？”

===Original===
“Certainly,” I replied; “I am not going to share your society with the common mortals who ride in omnibuses. That would be sheer, sinful waste. Besides, it is more companionable to walk.”

===Chinese===
“当然，”我答道；“我可舍不得把你我的相伴，分给马车上那些凡夫俗子。那简直是罪过的浪费。再说，走路也更亲近些。”

===Original===
“Yes, it is; and the bustle of the streets makes one more appreciative of the quiet of the Museum. What are we going to look at when we get there?”

===Chinese===
“是啊；再说，街市的喧闹，愈发衬出博物馆的清静。到了那儿，咱们看些什么呢？”

===Original===
“You must decide that,” I replied. “You know the collection much better than I do.”

===Chinese===
“这得由你来定夺，”我答道。“这批藏品你远比我熟。”

===Original===
“Well, now,” she mused, “I wonder what you would like to see; or, in other words, what I should like you to see. The old English pottery is rather fascinating, especially the Fulham ware. I rather think I shall take you to see that.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，我想想，”她沉吟道，“不知你喜欢看什么；或者换句话说，不知我乐意让你看什么。英国的旧陶器颇有迷人之处，尤其是富勒姆陶（Fulham ware）。我看就带你去瞧瞧那个吧。”

===Original===
She reflected a while, and then, just as we reached the gate of Staple Inn, she stopped and looked thoughtfully down the Gray’s Inn Road.

===Chinese===
她思忖了一小会儿，随后，恰在我们走到斯特普尔律师学院（Staple Inn）门口时，她停下脚步，若有所思地朝格雷律师学院路（Gray's Inn Road）的那头望去。

===Original===
“You have taken a great interest in our ‘case’ as Doctor Thorndyke calls it. Would you like to see the churchyard where Uncle John wished to be buried? It is a little out of our way, but we are not in a hurry, are we?”

===Chinese===
“你对咱们这桩‘案子’——桑代克博士是这么称呼它的——一向十分上心。你可愿意去看看约翰伯伯希望安葬的那座教堂墓园？稍微有些绕道，可咱们并不赶时间，不是么？”

===Original===
I, certainly, was not. Any deviation that might prolong our walk was welcome, and as to the place﻿—why, all places were alike to me if only she were by my side. Besides, the churchyard was really of some interest, since it was undoubtedly the “exciting cause” of the obnoxious paragraph two of the will. I accordingly expressed a desire to make its acquaintance, and we crossed to the entrance to Gray’s Inn Road.

===Chinese===
我自然不赶。凡能延长我们这趟散步的绕道，无不欢迎；至于那地方嘛——嘿，只要她在我身旁，普天之下哪里不是一样。何况那墓园也真有几分意味，因为遗嘱里那条可憎的第二款，无疑正是以它为“诱因”的。于是我表示很愿结识一番，我们便穿过马路，朝格雷律师学院路的入口走去。

===Original===
“Do you ever try,” she asked, as we turned down the dingy thoroughfare, “to picture familiar places as they looked a couple of hundred years ago?”

===Chinese===
“你可曾试过，”当我们拐下这条灰暗的大道时，她问道，“把熟悉的地方，想象成两百年前的模样？”

===Original===
“Yes,” I answered, “and very difficult I find it. One has to manufacture the materials for reconstruction, and then the present aspect of the place will keep obtruding itself. But some places are easier to reconstitute than others.”

===Chinese===
“试过，”我答道，“而且我发现这极难。重构的材料须得凭空造出来，而这地方眼下的面貌，又总要在眼前横插一杠。不过有些地方，总比别处容易复原些。”

===Original===
“That is what I find,” said she. “Now Holborn, for example, is quite easy to reconstruct, though I daresay the imaginary form isn’t a bit like the original. But there are fragments left, like Staple Inn and the front of Gray’s Inn; and then one has seen prints of the old Middle Row and some of the taverns, so that one has some material with which to help out one’s imagination. But this road we are walking in always baffles me. It looks so old and yet is, for the most part, so new that I find it impossible to make a satisfactory picture of its appearance, say, when Sir Roger de Coverley might have strolled in Gray’s Inn Walks, or farther back, when Francis Bacon had chambers in the Inn.”

===Chinese===
“我也是这般，”她说。“譬如霍尔本，重构起来便十分容易，虽然我敢说，想象出来的样子同原貌没有一分相像。可那儿到底剩着些残段，比如斯特普尔律师学院和格雷律师学院的门面；再说，人们也见过古老的中排楼（Middle Row）和几家老酒馆的版画，好歹有些材料来接济想象。可我们此刻走着的这条路，总叫我束手无策。它看着那样古老，实际上却又大半那样簇新，以致我实在无法为它的旧观构想出一幅称心的图景——比如，当罗杰·德·科弗利爵士（Sir Roger de Coverley）或许还在格雷律师学院步道里闲步的年代，或者更早，当弗朗西斯·培根（Francis Bacon）还在学院里设寓的时候。”

===Original===
“I imagine,” said I, “that part of the difficulty is in the mixed character of the neighborhood. Here on the one side, is old Gray’s Inn, not much changed since Bacon’s time﻿—his chambers are still to be seen, I think, over the gateway; and there, on the Clerkenwell side, is a dense and rather squalid neighborhood which has grown up over a region partly rural and wholly fugitive in character. Places like Bagnigge Wells and Hockley in the Hole would not have had many buildings that were likely to survive; and in the absence of surviving specimens the imagination hasn’t much to work from.”

===Chinese===
“依我想，”我说，“这难处，有一部分在于左近地带的驳杂。这边一侧，是古老的格雷律师学院，自培根时代以来变动不多——他的寓所如今还看得见，我想，就在门楼上方；那边一侧，往克勒肯维尔（Clerkenwell）方向，是一片稠密而相当鄙陋的街市，它是在一块半属村野、全然过眼云烟的地面上生长起来的。像巴尼格井（Bagnigge Wells）和霍克利谷地（Hockley in the Hole）那样的地方，当初谅必不会有几所指望留存下来的建筑；既没有存世的标本，想象力也就无从着手了。”

===Original===
“I daresay you are right,” said she. “Certainly, the purlieus of old Clerkenwell present a very confused picture to me; whereas, in the case of an old street like, say, Great Ormond Street, one has only to sweep away the modern buildings and replace them with glorious old houses like the few that remain, dig up the roadway and pavements and lay down cobblestones, plant a few wooden posts, hang up one or two oil-lamps, and the transformation is complete. And a very delightful transformation it is.”

===Chinese===
“我想你说得对，”她说。“老克勒肯维尔一带，在我心里呈现的图景确实乱作一团；而像大奥蒙德街（Great Ormond Street）那样一条老街，只消把现代的房屋一扫而空，换上同残存那几座一样辉煌的老宅，再掘开路面与人行道，铺上鹅卵石，栽几根木桩，挂起一两盏油灯，这场变身便大功告成。而且是一场多么惬意的变身。”

===Original===
“Very delightful; which, by the way, is a melancholy thought. For we ought to be doing better work than our forefathers; whereas what we actually do is to pull down the old buildings, clap the doorways, porticoes, paneling, and mantels in our museums, and then run up something inexpensive and useful and deadly uninteresting in their place.”

===Chinese===
“惬意得很；不过说起来，这倒是个可悲的念头。因为我们本该做出比祖先更好的活计；可我们实际上干的，却是拆掉老房子，把门道、柱廊、护壁板和壁炉架一股脑塞进博物馆，再在原处赶快竖起些省钱、耐用、又乏味得要死的玩意儿。”

===Original===
My companion looked at me and laughed softly. “For a naturally cheerful, and even gay young man,” said she, “you are most amazingly pessimistic. The mantle of Jeremiah﻿—if he ever wore one﻿—seems to have fallen on you, but without in the least impairing your good spirits excepting in regard to matters architectural.”

===Chinese===
我的同伴望着我，轻声笑了。“照说你是个生性快活、甚至嘻嘻哈哈的年轻人，”她说，“你这悲观却悲观得惊人。耶利米的衣钵——倘若他果真有过这么一件——看来落到你身上了，只是丝毫也不曾败坏你的好兴致，唯独在建筑问题上除外。”

===Original===
“I have much to be thankful for,” said I. “Am I not taken to the Museum by a fair lady? And does she not stay me with mummy cases and comfort me with crockery?”

===Chinese===
“我须得感恩的事可多着呢，”我说。“可不是有位佳人陪我上博物馆么？她岂不是用木乃伊匣将我款留，又用陶器将我宽慰么？”

===Original===
“Pottery,” she corrected; and then as we met a party of grave-looking women emerging from a side-street, she said: “I suppose those are lady medical students.”

===Chinese===
“是陶器，”她纠正道；随后，我们迎面遇上一小队神情庄重的女子从一条横街里出来，她便说：“我看那些是女医学生。”

===Original===
“Yes, on their way to the Royal Free Hospital. Note the gravity of their demeanor and contrast it with the levity of the male student.”

===Chinese===
“不错，正往皇家自由医院（Royal Free Hospital）去。留意她们举止的凝重，再对照一下男学生的轻浮。”

===Original===
“I was doing so,” she answered, “and wondering why professional women are usually so much more serious than men.”

===Chinese===
“我正这么对照着呢，”她答道，“同时在纳闷，为什么靠职业吃饭的女子，通常总比男人严肃得多。”

===Original===
“Perhaps,” I suggested, “it is a matter of selection. A peculiar type of woman is attracted to the professions, whereas every man has to earn his living as a matter of course.”

===Chinese===
“也许，”我揣测道，“这出在选择。职业吸引来的，是某一类特殊的女子；而男人谋生，却是天经地义、无一人能免的事。”

===Original===
“Yes, I daresay that is the explanation. This is our turning.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，想必就是这个缘故。咱们该拐弯了。”

===Original===
We passed into Heathcote Street, at the end of which was an open gate giving entrance to one of those disused and metamorphosed burial-grounds that are to be met with in the older districts of London; in which the dispossessed dead are jostled into corners to make room for the living. Many of the headstones were still standing, and others, displaced to make room for asphalted walks and seats, were ranged around by the walls exhibiting inscriptions made meaningless by their removal. It was a pleasant enough place on this summer afternoon, contrasted with the dingy streets whence we had come, though its grass was faded and yellow and the twitter of the birds in the trees mingled with the hideous Board-school drawls of the children who played around the seats and the few remaining tombs.

===Chinese===
我们转入希思科特街（Heathcote Street），街尾一扇敞开的大门，通向伦敦旧城区随处可见的那种废置而改作他用的墓园；在那儿，被夺去居所的死者被挤进角落，给活人腾出地方。许多墓碑仍立在原处，另一些则为给铺沥青的步道和座椅让位而挪开，靠着围墙四下里排开，碑上的铭文因迁移而变得文理断绝。在这个夏日的午后，比起我们方才走出的那些灰暗街巷，这地方倒也算宜人，尽管草色已经枯黄，树上雀鸟的啁啾，同围着座椅和残存几座坟玩耍的孩子们那教委会学校（Board-school）腔的难听拖音，混作一处。

===Original===
“So this is the last resting-place of the illustrious house of Bellingham,” said I.

===Chinese===
“如此说来，这里便是显赫的贝林厄姆门第的最后安息之所了，”我说。

===Original===
“Yes; and we are not the only distinguished people who repose in this place. The daughter of no less a person than Richard Cromwell is buried here; the tomb is still standing﻿—but perhaps you have been here before, and know it.”

===Chinese===
“是啊；而且长眠此地的名流，也不止我们这些贵胄。这里葬着一位姑娘，她的父亲不是别人，正是理查德·克伦威尔（Richard Cromwell）；那座墓至今还立着——不过，或许你从前来过，认得它。”

===Original===
“I don’t think I have ever been here before; and yet there is something about the place that seems familiar.” I looked around, cudgeling my brains for the key to the dimly reminiscent sensations that the place evoked; until, suddenly, I caught sight of a group of buildings away to the west, enclosed within a wall heightened by a wooden trellis.

===Chinese===
“我想我从前不曾来过；可这地方偏又有几分说不出的眼熟。”我环顾四周，绞尽脑汁，想寻出这地方唤起的那阵依稀怀旧之感的关键；直到蓦然间，我望见西边远处一带建筑，围在一堵墙里，墙头加了木格架增高。

===Original===
“Yes, of course!” I exclaimed. “I remember the place now. I have never been in this part before, but in that enclosure beyond, which opens at the end of Henrietta Street, there used to be and may be still, for all I know, a school of anatomy, at which I attended in my first year; in fact, I did my first dissection there.”

===Chinese===
“啊，想起来了！”我失声道。“我现在认得这地方了。这一带我确实没来过，可那边围场里，从亨利埃塔街（Henrietta Street）尽头进去的地方，从前有一所解剖学校——据我所知，如今兴许还在——我头一年就在那儿上的课；说实在的，我平生第一回解剖就是在那儿做的。”

===Original===
“There was a certain gruesome appropriateness in the position of the school,” remarked Miss Bellingham. “It would have been really convenient in the days of the resurrection men. Your material would have been delivered at your very door. Was it a large school?”

===Chinese===
“这学校的坐落处，倒有一种阴凄凄的相宜，”贝林厄姆小姐评论道。“在盗尸贼（resurrection men）当道的年月，那才真叫方便。你的材料保管送到你家门口。那是所大规模的学校么？”

===Original===
“The attendance varied according to the time of the year. Sometimes I worked there quite alone. I used to let myself in with a key and hoist my subject out of a sort of sepulchral tank by means of a chain tackle. It was a ghoulish business. You have no idea how awful the body used to look to my unaccustomed eyes, as it rose slowly out of the tank. It was like the resurrection scene that you see on some old tombstones, where the deceased is shown rising out of his coffin while the skeleton, Death, falls vanquished with his dart shattered and his crown toppling off.

===Chinese===
“上学的多寡，要看时节。有时我便独自一人在那儿操作。我惯常自己拿钥匙开门进去，用链滑车把我的解剖对象从一口坟墓般的大槽里吊上来。那是食尸鬼一般的营生。你无从想象，那具躯体打槽里缓缓升起时，在我这双不曾见惯的眼睛里，是何等一副怕人的模样。它就像你在某些旧墓碑上见到的复活图：死者正从棺中起身，而那具骷髅死神则败落倒地，飞镖折断，王冠摇摇欲坠。

===Original===
“I remember, too, that the demonstrator used to wear a blue apron, which created a sort of impression of a cannibal butcher’s shop. But I am afraid I am shocking you.”

===Chinese===
“我还记得，那位示教员总系一条蓝围裙，叫人恍然觉得走进了一间吃人生番的屠铺。不过，恐怕我把你吓着了吧。”

===Original===
“No you are not. Every profession has its unpresentable aspects, which ought not to be seen by outsiders. Think of the sculptor’s studio and of the sculptor himself when he is modeling a large figure or a group in clay. He might be a bricklayer or a roadsweeper if you judge by his appearance. This is the tomb I was telling you about.”

===Chinese===
“才没有呢。每一种行当都有它上不得台面的方面，本不该让外人瞧见。想想雕塑家的工作室，想想雕塑家本人捏塑一尊大像或一组群像时的模样。单凭那副外表，你尽可以拿他当砌砖匠，或者扫街的。这就是我方才说的那座墓。”

===Original===
We halted before the plain coffer of stone, weathered and wasted by age, but yet kept in decent repair by some pious hands, and read the inscription, setting forth with modest pride, that here reposed Anna, sixth daughter of Richard Cromwell, “The Protector.” It was a simple monument and commonplace enough, with the crude severity of the ascetic age to which it belonged. But still, it carried the mind back to those stirring times when the leafy shades of Gray’s Inn Lane must have resounded with the clank of weapons and the tramp of armed men; when this bald recreation-ground was a rustic churchyard, standing amidst green fields and hedgerows, and countrymen leading their packhorses into London through the Lane would stop to look in over the wooden gate.

===Chinese===
我们在那口朴素的石棺前停下。棺为岁月风雨所剥蚀消磨，却仍有虔诚的手把它保养得体面；我们读了铭文，那铭文以谦抑的矜夸昭示：此处安息着“护国公”理查德·克伦威尔的第六女安娜。这是一座简朴的纪念碑，平凡得很，带着它所属那个苦行时代的粗粝严冷。然而它仍能把人的心思带回那些动荡的岁月：那时，格雷律师学院巷的绿荫深处，想必回荡着兵器的铿锵与披甲者的踏步；那时，这片光秃秃的游乐场还是一座村野墓园，立在青青田野与树篱之间，赶着驮马取道此巷进城的乡人，会停步从木门上向里张望。

===Original===
Miss Bellingham looked at me critically as I stood thus reflecting, and presently remarked: “I think you and I have a good many mental habits in common.”

===Chinese===
我这般出神时，贝林厄姆小姐品评似的打量着我，半晌说道：“我想，你我两人有不少心思上的习惯是相同的。”

===Original===
I looked up inquiringly, and she continued: “I notice that an old tombstone seems to set you meditating. So it does me. When I look at an ancient monument, and especially an old headstone, I find myself almost unconsciously retracing the years to the date that is written on the stone. Why do you think that is? Why should a monument be so stimulating to the imagination? And why should a common headstone be more so than any other?”

===Chinese===
我探询地抬起头来，她接着说道：“我注意到，一方旧墓碑似乎总能引你沉思。于我也是一样。每当我望着一座古迹，尤其是一方旧墓碑，我发觉自己几乎是不知不觉地把岁月倒拨回去，一直数到碑上镌着的那个日期。你想这是为什么？为什么纪念物对想象力有这样大的激发？又为什么一座寻常的墓碑，比任何别的都激发得更甚？”

===Original===
“I suppose it is,” I answered reflectively, “that a churchyard monument is a peculiarly personal thing and appertains in a peculiar way to a particular time. And the circumstance that it has stood untouched by the passing years while everything around has changed, helps the imagination to span the interval. And the common headstone, the memorial of some dead and gone farmer or laborer who lived and died in the village hard by, is still more intimate and suggestive. The rustic, childish sculpture of the village mason and the artless doggerel of the village schoolmaster, bring back the time and place and the conditions of life more vividly than the more scholarly inscriptions and the more artistic enrichments of monuments of greater pretensions. But where are your own family tombstones?”

===Chinese===
“我想，”我沉思着答道，“这是因为教堂墓园的纪念物是一种格外切身的物件，又以一种独特的方式系属于一个特定的时代。它历经流逝的岁月而巍然未被触动，四周的一切却已变换了面目，这一层情由，正好帮想象力跨越那道间隔。至于寻常的墓碑，那近旁村中生生死死的某位农夫或劳工的纪念物，就更亲切、更能引人遐想了。村中石匠那质朴稚气的雕工，乡村塾师那朴拙无华的歪诗，比起学问更深的铭文、比起更气派的纪念碑上更具匠心的装点，能把那个时代、那个地方和那种生活境况，更鲜活地带回眼前。可是，你们自家的墓碑在哪里呢？”

===Original===
“They are over in that farther corner. There is an intelligent, but inopportune, person apparently copying the epitaphs. I wish he would go away. I want to show them to you.”

===Chinese===
“就在那边远处的角落里。有个聪明却来得不巧的人，看样子正在抄录那些碑文。我巴不得他走开才好。我要带你去看呢。”

===Original===
I now noticed, for the first time, an individual engaged, notebook in hand, in making a careful survey of a group of old headstones. Evidently he was making a copy of the inscriptions, for not only was he poring attentively over the writing on the face of the stone, but now and again he helped out his vision by running his fingers over the worn lettering.

===Chinese===
直到这时，我才头一回注意到一个人，手里拿着笔记本，正对一群旧墓碑作细致的查勘。他分明是在抄录碑文，因为他不但对着碑面的文字凝神细读，还不时用手指抚过磨损的刻痕，来接济自己的目力。

===Original===
“That is my grandfather’s tombstone that he is copying now,” said Miss Bellingham; and even as she spoke, the man turned and directed a searching glance at us with a pair of keen, spectacled eyes.

===Chinese===
“他此刻抄的正是我祖父的墓碑，”贝林厄姆小姐说；话犹未了，那人已转过身来，用一双锐利的、戴眼镜的眼睛，向我们投来探询的一瞥。

===Original===
Simultaneously we uttered an exclamation of surprise; for the investigator was Mr. Jellicoe.

===Chinese===
我们不约而同地发出一声惊呼；因为那位查勘者，竟是杰利科先生。

## XVI O Artemidorus, Farewell! / 十六 啊，阿耳忒弥多鲁斯，永别了！
===Original===
Whether or not Mr. Jellicoe was surprised to see us, it is impossible to say. His countenance (which served the ordinary purposes of a face, inasmuch as it contained the principal organs of special sense, with inlets to the alimentary and respiratory tracts) was, as an apparatus for the expression of the emotions, a total failure. To a thought-reader it would have been about as helpful as the face carved upon the handle of an umbrella; a comparison suggested, perhaps, by a certain resemblance to such an object. He advanced, holding open his notebook and pencil, and having saluted us with a stiff bow and an old-fashioned flourish of his hat, shook hands rheumatically and waited for us to speak.

===Chinese===
杰利科先生见到我们究竟惊不惊讶，无从断言。他那张脸（说它勉强算尽了一张脸的通常职分，是因为它毕竟容纳了主要的感觉器官，还有通向消化道和呼吸道的两口），作为一台表情达意的器械，则是彻头彻尾的失败。在善察人心的人看来，它的用处大约同伞柄上雕出的那张脸不相上下；这比方之所以浮上心头，或许正因为他与那种物件确有几分神似。他手里摊着笔记本和铅笔走上前来，僵硬地一躬身，又老派地一挥帽致意，带着一身风湿气同我们握过手，便等着我们开口。

===Original===
“This is an unexpected pleasure, Mr. Jellicoe,” said Miss Bellingham.

===Chinese===
“真是意想不到的乐事，杰利科先生，”贝林厄姆小姐说。

===Original===
“It is very good of you to say so,” he replied.

===Chinese===
“您这么说太客气了，”他答道。

===Original===
“And quite a coincidence﻿—that we should all happen to come here on the same day.”

===Chinese===
“而且也真是个巧合——我们几个偏偏都在同一天到了这里。”

===Original===
“A coincidence, certainly,” he admitted; “and if we had all happened not to come﻿—which must have occurred frequently﻿—that also would have been a coincidence.”

===Chinese===
“的确是巧合，”他承认；“不过，倘若我们几个碰巧都没来——这种情形想必也屡见——那也同样是个巧合。”

===Original===
“I suppose it would,” said she, “but I hope we are not interrupting you.”

===Chinese===
“我想也是，”她说，“不过但愿我们没有打搅您。”

===Original===
“Thank you, no. I had just finished when I had the pleasure of perceiving you.”

===Chinese===
“谢谢，不打搅。我刚做完事，就有幸望见二位了。”

===Original===
“You were making some notes in reference to the case, I imagine,” said I. It was an impertinent question, put with malice aforethought for the mere pleasure of hearing him evade it.

===Chinese===
“我猜，您方才是在做同本案有关的笔记吧。”我说。这是个唐突的问题，而且是蓄意提出的，单为听他闪避时的那一点乐趣。

===Original===
“The case?” he repeated. “You are referring, perhaps, to Stevens versus the Parish Council?”

===Chinese===
“本案？”他重复道。“您指的莫非是斯蒂文斯诉教区委员会案（Stevens versus the Parish Council）？”

===Original===
“I think Doctor Berkeley was referring to the case of my uncle’s will,” Miss Bellingham said quite gravely, though with a suspicious dimpling about the corners of her mouth.

===Chinese===
“我想伯克利医生指的是我伯父遗嘱的案子，”贝林厄姆小姐说得一本正经，嘴角却可疑地泛起了笑涡。

===Original===
“Indeed,” said Mr. Jellicoe. “There is a case, is there; a suit?”

===Chinese===
“哦，”杰利科先生说。“当真有这么个案子么？一场诉讼？”

===Original===
“I mean the proceedings instituted by Mr. Hurst.”

===Chinese===
“我是指赫斯特先生提起的那桩程序。”

===Original===
“Oh, but that was merely an application to the Court, and is, moreover, finished and done with. At least, so I understand. I speak, of course, subject to correction; I am not acting for Mr. Hurst, you will be pleased to remember. As a matter of fact,” he continued, after a brief pause, “I was just refreshing my memory as to the wording of the inscriptions on these stones, especially that of your grandfather, Francis Bellingham. It has occurred to me that if it should appear by the finding of the coroner’s jury that your uncle is deceased, it would be proper and decorous that some memorial should be placed here. But, as the burial ground is closed, there might be some difficulty about erecting a new monument, whereas there would probably be none in adding an inscription to one already existing. Hence these investigations. For if the inscriptions on your grandfather’s stone had set forth that ‘here rests the body of Francis Bellingham,’ it would have been manifestly improper to add ‘also that of John Bellingham, son of the above.’ Fortunately the inscription was more discreetly drafted, merely recording the fact that this monument is ‘sacred to the memory of the said Francis,’ and not committing itself as to the whereabouts of the remains. But perhaps I am interrupting you.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那不过是向法院提出的一项申请罢了，何况早已了结。至少，据我所知如此。当然，我说这话，尚祈指正；您当记得，我并不是赫斯特先生的代理律师。”他略停了停，又接着说，“方才我正在温习这几方碑石上的铭文措辞，尤其是令祖父弗朗西斯·贝林厄姆（Francis Bellingham）那一方。我想到，倘若验尸陪审团的裁定认定令伯父已经身故，那么在这里设一处纪念，便是合宜而得体的了。但这片墓园既已封闭，新立碑碣恐怕颇有为难，而在既有碑石上添刻铭文，则多半无碍。于是有了这番查考。因为，倘若令祖父碑上的铭文写的是‘此处安息着弗朗西斯·贝林厄姆的遗骸’，那么再添上‘亦有其子约翰·贝林厄姆’一句，便显然失当了。所幸原铭文的措辞较为审慎，只载明此碑‘虔敬纪念上述弗朗西斯’，并未牵涉遗骸的下落。不过，我恐怕已经打搅二位了。”

===Original===
“No, not at all,” replied Miss Bellingham (which was grossly untrue; he was interrupting *me* most intolerably); “we were going to the British Museum and just looked in here on our way.”

===Chinese===
“哪里，一点也不，”贝林厄姆小姐答道（这话纯属不实；他把我*打搅得简直忍无可忍）；“我们正要去大英博物馆，顺路进来看看。”

===Original===
“Ha,” said Mr. Jellicoe, “now, I happen to be going to the Museum too, to see Doctor Norbury. I suppose that is another coincidence?”

===Chinese===
“哈，”杰利科先生说，“巧了，我恰好也要到博物馆去，去看诺伯里博士。这想必又是一个巧合吧？”

===Original===
“Certainly it is,” Miss Bellingham replied; and then she asked: “Shall we walk together?” and the old curmudgeon actually said “yes”﻿—confound him!

===Chinese===
“当然是，”贝林厄姆小姐答道；接着她又问：“我们同路走，好么？”而那位老吝啬鬼居然说了声“好”——真可恶！

===Original===
We returned to the Gray’s Inn Road, where, as there was now room for us to walk abreast, I proceeded to indemnify myself for the lawyer’s unwelcome company by leading the conversation back to the subject of the missing man.

===Chinese===
我们回到格雷律师学院路上，这时既然有了并肩同行的余地，我便把话头引回失踪者一案，聊以补偿自己忍受这位律师作伴的不快。

===Original===
“Was there anything, Mr. Jellicoe, in Mr. John Bellingham’s state of health that would make it probable that he might die suddenly?”

===Chinese===
“杰利科先生，就约翰·贝林厄姆先生的健康状况而言，可有什么会使他有可能猝然死亡的因素么？”

===Original===
The lawyer looked at me suspiciously for a few moments and then remarked:

===Chinese===
律师狐疑地打量了我片刻，然后说道：

===Original===
“You seem to be greatly interested in John Bellingham and his affairs.”

===Chinese===
“您似乎对约翰·贝林厄姆和他的事务有着极浓的兴致。”

===Original===
“I am. My friends are deeply concerned in them, and the case itself is of more than common interest from a professional point of view.”

===Chinese===
“确是如此。我的朋友们同这些事务利害攸关，而单从职业角度看，这案子本身也有超乎寻常的兴味。”

===Original===
“And what is the bearing of this particular question?”

===Chinese===
“那么，方才那个问题又作何解？”

===Original===
“Surely it is obvious,” said I. “If a missing man is known to have suffered from some affection, such as heart disease, aneurism, or arterial degeneration, likely to produce sudden death, that fact will surely be highly material to the question as to whether he is probably dead or alive.”

===Chinese===
“这还不明白么，”我说。“倘若一个失踪者已知患有某种可能导致猝死的病症，比如心脏病、动脉瘤或动脉变性，那么，就他大概是生是死这一问题而言，这一事实必然关系重大。”

===Original===
“No doubt you are right,” said Mr. Jellicoe. “I have little knowledge of medical affairs, but doubtless you are right. As to the question itself, I am Mr. Bellingham’s lawyer, not his doctor. His health is a matter that lies outside my jurisdiction. But you heard my evidence in Court, to the effect that the testator appeared, to my untutored observation, to be a healthy man. I can say no more now.”

===Chinese===
“无疑您说得对，”杰利科先生说。“医学之事，我所知甚少，但您想必不错。至于问题本身，我是贝林厄姆先生的律师，不是他的医生。他的健康，不在我的职分之内。不过，您在法庭上听到过我的证词，大意是说，依我这未经训练的观察，立遗嘱人看来是个健康的人。我如今能说的，也不外乎此了。”

===Original===
“If the question is of any importance,” said Miss Bellingham, “I wonder they did not call his doctor and settle it definitely. My own impression is that he was﻿—or is﻿—rather a strong and sound man. He certainly recovered very quickly and completely after his accident.”

===Chinese===
“倘若这问题当真要紧，”贝林厄姆小姐说，“我就不明白，他们为什么不传他的医生出庭，把它彻底弄个明白。我自己的印象是，他从前——如今也还是——相当强健结实。那次意外之后，他好得又快又彻底，这是确实的。”

===Original===
“What accident was that?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“那是次什么意外？”我问。

===Original===
“Oh, hasn’t my father told you? It occurred while he was staying with us. He slipped from a curb and broke one of the bones of the left ankle﻿—somebody’s fracture﻿—”

===Chinese===
“哦，我父亲没跟您说过么？那是在他住到我们家的时候出的岔子。他在路边石上滑了一跤，摔断了左踝的一块骨头——某某氏骨折——”

===Original===
“Pott’s?”

===Chinese===
“波特氏？”

===Original===
“Yes; that was the name﻿—Pott’s fracture; and he broke both his kneecaps as well. Sir Morgan Bennett had to perform an operation, or he would have been a cripple for life. As it was, he was about again in a few weeks, apparently none the worse excepting for a slight weakness of the left ankle.”

===Chinese===
“对，就是这个名字——波特氏骨折；他还把两侧膝盖骨都摔断了。摩根·贝内特爵士（Sir Morgan Bennett）不得不给他动手术，不然他就终身残废了。虽说到头来，不出几个星期他又行动如常，除了左踝略有些发软，看上去毫发无伤。”

===Original===
“Could he walk upstairs?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“他还能上楼梯么？”我问。

===Original===
“Oh, yes; and play golf and ride a bicycle.”

===Chinese===
“哦，能；还能打高尔夫球、骑自行车呢。”

===Original===
“You are sure he broke both kneecaps?”

===Chinese===
“你确定他两块膝盖骨都断了？”

===Original===
“Quite sure. I remember that it was mentioned as an uncommon injury, and that Sir Morgan seemed quite pleased with him for doing it.”

===Chinese===
“十分确定。我记得人家说这是一种少见的伤，摩根爵士似乎还相当赞许他摔出了这个名堂。”

===Original===
“That sounds rather libelous; but I expect he was pleased with the result of the operation. He might well be.”

===Chinese===
“这话听上去颇有诽谤之嫌；不过我料想，他赞许的是手术的结果。他原也有理由满意。”

===Original===
Here there was a brief lull in the conversation, and, even as I was trying to think of a poser for Mr. Jellicoe, that gentleman took the opportunity to change the subject.

===Chinese===
谈话至此出现了短暂的冷场，我正搜肠刮肚，想给杰利科先生出个难题，那位先生却趁机转开了话头。

===Original===
“Are you going to the Egyptian rooms?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“你们要去埃及展厅么？”他问。

===Original===
“No,” replied Miss Bellingham; “we are going to look at the pottery.”

===Chinese===
“不去，”贝林厄姆小姐答道；“我们要去看陶器。”

===Original===
“Ancient or modern?”

===Chinese===
“古代的还是现代的？”

===Original===
“That old Fulham ware is what chiefly interests us at present; that of the seventeenth century. I don’t know whether you call that ancient or modern.”

===Chinese===
“我们眼下最感兴趣的是那种老富勒姆陶；十七世纪的东西。这算古还是算今，我就不知道了。”

===Original===
“Neither do I,” said Mr. Jellicoe. “Antiquity and modernity are terms that have no fixed connotation. They are purely relative and their application in a particular instance has to be determined by a sort of sliding scale. To a furniture collector, a Tudor chair or a Jacobean chest is ancient; to an architect, their period is modern, whereas an eleventh-century church is ancient; but to an Egyptologist, accustomed to remains of a vast antiquity, both are products of modern periods separated by an insignificant interval. And, I suppose,” he added reflectively, “that to a geologist, the traces of the very earliest dawn of human history appertain only to the recent period. Conceptions of time, like all other conceptions, are relative.”

===Chinese===
“我也说不上，”杰利科先生说。“古与今，本是含义不固定的字眼。它们纯然是相对的，用到具体事例上，须凭一种游移的标尺来判定。在家具藏家眼里，一把都铎坐椅或一口詹姆斯时代（Jacobean）的柜子是古物；在建筑师眼里，那个时期属于现代，倒是十一世纪的教堂才算古；可在看惯了太古遗物的埃及学家眼里，两者都是现代时期的产物，彼此只隔一段无足轻重的间歇。而且我想，”他若有所思地补了一句，“在地质学家看来，连人类历史最早熹微的遗迹，也只归入新近的时期。时间的概念，同其他一切概念一样，是相对的。”

===Original===
“You would appear to be a disciple of Herbert Spencer,” I remarked.

===Chinese===
“您这副做派，倒像是赫伯特·斯宾塞（Herbert Spencer）的门徒了，”我说。

===Original===
“I am a disciple of Arthur Jellicoe, sir,” he retorted. And I believed him.

===Chinese===
“先生，我是阿瑟·杰利科（Arthur Jellicoe）的门徒，”他反驳道。这话我信。

===Original===
By the time we had reached the Museum he had become almost genial; and, if less amusing in this frame, he was so much more instructive and entertaining that I refrained from baiting him, and permitted him to discuss his favorite topic unhindered, especially since my companion listened with lively interest. Nor, when we entered the great hall, did he relinquish possession of us, and we followed submissively, as he led the way past the winged bulls of Nineveh and the great seated statues, until we found ourselves, almost without the exercise of our volition, in the upper room amidst the glaring mummy cases that had witnessed the birth of my friendship with Ruth Bellingham.

===Chinese===
待走到博物馆，他已变得近乎和蔼可亲；这副形貌虽不及先前逗趣，却更有教益、更耐听，我便也打消了撩拨他的念头，任他不受拘束地畅谈心爱的话题，何况我这位同伴正听得津津有味。及至进了大厅，他也不肯把我们撒手，我们只得服服帖帖地跟着，由他领路，走过尼尼微（Nineveh）的带翅巨牛和那些高大的坐像，直到几乎身不由己地进了楼上那间展厅，置身于那些色彩刺目的木乃伊匣中间——正是在这里，我与露丝·贝林厄姆的友谊萌生。

===Original===
“Before I leave you,” said Mr. Jellicoe, “I should like to show you that mummy that we were discussing the other evening; the one, you remember, that my friend, John Bellingham, presented to the Museum a little time before his disappearance. The point that I mentioned is only a trivial one, but it may become of interest hereafter if any plausible explanation should be forthcoming.” He led us along the room until we arrived at the case containing John Bellingham’s gift, where he halted and gazed in at the mummy with the affectionate reflectiveness of the connoisseur.

===Chinese===
“在同二位分手之前，”杰利科先生说，“我想带你们看看那天晚上我们谈起过的那具木乃伊；就是我的朋友约翰·贝林厄姆在失踪前不久赠给博物馆的那一具。我提过的那一点原属小事，但日后若能有什么说得通的解释出现，它或许会变得有意思起来。”他领着我们沿展厅走去，到陈列约翰·贝林厄姆赠品的那具柜匣前站定，带着鉴赏家那种深情的专注，凝望柜中的木乃伊。

===Original===
“The bitumen coating was what we were discussing, Miss Bellingham,” said he. “You have seen it, of course.”

===Chinese===
“我们谈论的正是这层沥青涂层，贝林厄姆小姐，”他说。“您当然是见到过的。”

===Original===
“Yes,” she answered. “It is a dreadful disfigurement, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“见到了，”她答道。“这真是一处骇人的毁损，不是么？”

===Original===
“Esthetically it is to be deplored, but it adds a certain speculative interest to the specimen. You notice that the black coating leaves the principal decoration and the whole of the inscription untouched, which is precisely the part that one would expect to find covered up; whereas the feet and the back, which probably bore no writing, are quite thickly crusted. If you stoop down, you can see that the bitumen was daubed freely into the lacings of the back, where it served no purpose, so that even the strings are embedded.” He stooped as he spoke, and peered up inquisitively at the back of the mummy, where it was visible between the supports.

===Chinese===
“从审美上讲，诚然可叹；但它给这件标本添了一层思辨的趣味。您注意到没有，那层黑涂料避开了主要纹饰和整篇铭文，而那恰恰是按说最该涂抹的地方；反倒是不曾书写文字的脚部和背面，结着厚厚的一层。您俯下身去便能看到，沥青被大把大把抹进背面的系带缝里，在那儿毫无用处，连那些绳带都被嵌裹在内。”他一面说，一面俯下身去，从支架之间露出的木乃伊背面处，好奇地仰面窥看。

===Original===
“Has Doctor Norbury any explanation to offer?” asked Miss Bellingham.

===Chinese===
“诺伯里博士可有什么解释？”贝林厄姆小姐问。

===Original===
“None whatever,” replied Mr. Jellicoe. “He finds it as great a mystery as I do. But he thinks that we may get some suggestion from the Director when he comes back. He is a very great authority, as you know, and a practical excavator of great experience too. But I mustn’t stay here talking of these things, and keeping you from your pottery. Perhaps I have stayed too long already. If I have I ask your pardon, and I will now wish you a very good afternoon.” With a sudden return to his customary wooden impassivity, he shook hands with us, bowed stiffly, and took himself off toward the curator’s office.

===Chinese===
“一点儿也没有，”杰利科先生答道。“他同我一样，觉得这是一桩谜。不过他认为，等馆长回来，我们或许能从他那里得到一点提示。您知道，他是位极重大的权威，又是经验丰富的实地发掘者。但我不能站在这里尽谈这些，耽误你们看陶器了。也许我逗留得已经太久。若是这样，尚祈见谅，那我就此祝二位午安。”他骤然恢复了惯常的木然冷漠，同我们握了握手，僵硬地一躬身，便径自往主任办公室去了。

===Original===
“What a strange man that is,” said Miss Bellingham, as Mr. Jellicoe disappeared through the doorway at the end of the room, “or perhaps I should say, a strange being, for I can hardly think of him as a man. I have never met any other human creature at all like him.”

===Chinese===
“这人真是古怪，”杰利科先生的身影消失在展厅尽头的门口时，贝林厄姆小姐说，“或者我该说，是个古怪的存在，因为我几乎不能把他当作一个人。我还从来没有见过哪个人类像他。”

===Original===
“He is certainly a queer old fogey,” I agreed.

===Chinese===
“他确实是个怪老头，”我表示同意。

===Original===
“Yes, but there is something more than that. He is so emotionless, so remote and aloof from all mundane concerns. He moves among ordinary men and women, but as a mere presence, an unmoved spectator of their actions, quite dispassionate and impersonal.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，可还不止如此。他那样全无情感，对一切世俗事务那样疏远、隔膜。他行走于寻常男女之间，却只是一道存在，一个不为所动的旁观者，全然冷静，全然不带个人色彩。”

===Original===
“Yes; he is astonishingly self-contained; in fact, he seems, as you say, to go to and fro among men, enveloped in a sort of infernal atmosphere of his own, like Marley’s ghost. But he is lively and human enough as soon as the subject of Egyptian antiquities is broached.”

===Chinese===
“正是；他孤绝自守得惊人；事实上，正如你所说，他往还于世人之中，却裹在一团自家携来的地狱气息里，活像马利的鬼魂（Marley's ghost）。可只要一提起埃及古物这个话题，他立刻就有生气、有人味儿了。”

===Original===
“Lively, but not human. He is always, to me, quite unhuman. Even when he is most interested, and even enthusiastic, he is a mere personification of knowledge. Nature ought to have furnished him with an ibis’s head like Tahuti; then he would have looked his part.”

===Chinese===
“有生气，却不是有人味儿。在我眼里，他始终全然不似人。即便在他兴致最高、甚至热切的时候，他也不过是知识的化身。造化本该给他安一颗像塔胡提那样的鹮鸟头；那他才像足了本色。”

===Original===
“He would have made a rare sensation in Lincoln’s Inn if he had,” said I; and we both laughed heartily at the imaginary picture of Tahuti Jellicoe, slender-beaked and top-hatted, going about his business in Lincoln’s Inn and the Law Courts.

===Chinese===
“真要那样，他在林肯律师学院可要出足风头了，”我说；想到头戴大礼帽、生着细长鸟喙的塔胡提·杰利科穿行于林肯律师学院与皇家法院之间办公办事的情景，我们俩不由得开怀大笑。

===Original===
Insensibly, as we talked, we had drawn near to the mummy of Artemidorus, and now my companion halted before the case with her thoughtful gray eyes bent dreamily on the face that looked out at us. I watched her with reverent admiration. How charming she looked as she stood with her sweet, grave face turned so earnestly to the object of her mystical affection! How dainty and full of womanly dignity and grace! And then suddenly it was borne in upon me that a great change had come over her since the day of our first meeting. She had grown younger, more girlish, and more gentle. At first she had seemed much older than I; a sad-faced woman, weary, solemn, enigmatic, almost gloomy, with a bitter, ironic humor and a bearing distant and cold. Now she was only maidenly and sweet; tinged, it is true, with a certain seriousness, but frank and gracious and wholly lovable.

===Chinese===
不知不觉间，我们谈着谈着，已走近了阿耳忒弥多鲁斯的木乃伊；这时我的同伴在柜匣前驻足，一双含情的灰眼睛朦朦胧胧，凝望着那张正对着我俩的脸。我怀着近乎虔敬的倾慕注视她。她把她那张甜美、沉静的脸那样恳切地转向她那秘密倾慕的对象，这时她多么动人啊！多么俏丽，又多么蕴涵着女性的端庄与优雅！蓦地，我恍然明白：自我们初次相见那天起，她身上已发生了一场巨大的变化。她变得年轻了，更孩子气了，也更温柔了。起初她显得远比我年长：一个愁容的妇人，疲惫、庄重、像谜一样，近乎阴郁，带着辛酸的、讥诮的幽默，仪态疏远而冷淡。如今她只是清纯而甜美；固然染着一层郑重，却坦率、亲切，全然讨人喜欢。

===Original===
Could the change be due to our friendship? As I asked myself the question, my heart leaped with a new hope. I yearned to tell her all that she was to me﻿—all that I hoped we might be to one another in the years to come.

===Chinese===
这变化，莫非是我们的友谊所致？当我这样自问时，我的心因一线新的希望而悸动。我渴望告诉她，她于我是怎样的一切——渴望告诉我们，在未来的岁月里，彼此或许能成为怎样的一切。

===Original===
At length I ventured to break in upon her reverie.

===Chinese===
我终于大着胆子，闯入了她的沉思。

===Original===
“What are you thinking about so earnestly, fair lady?”

===Chinese===
“美丽的淑女，你在想什么，这样入神？”

===Original===
She turned quickly with a bright smile and sparkling eyes that looked frankly into mine. “I was wondering,” said she, “if he was jealous of my new friend. But what a baby I am to talk such nonsense!”

===Chinese===
她倏然转过脸来，粲然一笑，一双明亮的眼睛坦率地望进我的眼睛。“我在想，”她说，“他会不会嫉妒我的新朋友。可我多孩子气呀，竟说这样的傻话！”

===Original===
She laughed softly and happily with just an adorable hint of shyness.

===Chinese===
她轻轻地、快活地笑了，笑意里恰含一缕惹人怜爱的羞怯。

===Original===
“Why should he be jealous?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“他为什么要嫉妒呢？”我问。

===Original===
“Well, you see, before﻿—we were friends, he had me all to himself. I have never had a man friend before﻿—except my father﻿—and no really intimate friend at all. And I was very lonely in those days, after our troubles had befallen. I am naturally solitary, but still, I am only a girl; I am not a philosopher. So when I felt very lonely, I used to come here and look at Artemidorus and make believe that he knew all the sadness of my life and sympathized with me. It was very silly, I know, but yet, somehow it was a real comfort to me.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，你想，在从前——我们做朋友之前——他是完完全全独属于我的。我从来没有过一位男性的朋友——除了我父亲——也压根没有过真正知心的朋友。那些日子，自从我们的祸事临头，我非常孤单。我天性喜独处，可我到底只是个女孩子，不是哲学家。所以，每当我孤单得难受，我就到这儿来，望着阿耳忒弥多鲁斯，假装他知道我一生所有的愁苦，还同情着我。我知道这样很傻，可不知怎么，它对我竟是一种真实的安慰。”

===Original===
“It was not silly of you at all. He must have been a good man, a gentle, sweet-faced man who had won the love of those who knew him, as this beautiful memorial tells; and it was wise and good of you to sweeten the bitterness of your life with the fragrance of this human love that blossoms in the dust after the lapse of centuries. No, you were not silly, and Artemidorus is not jealous of your new friend.”

===Chinese===
“你一点儿也不傻。他生前必定是个好人，一个温文尔雅、面容和善的人，赢得了相识者的爱，正如这座美好的纪念碑所诉说的那样；而你以这份人类之爱的芬芳——它历经数百年仍在尘土中吐蕊——来化解生活中的苦涩，这于你既明智又美好。不，你不傻，阿耳忒弥多鲁斯也绝不嫉妒你的新朋友。”

===Original===
“Are you sure?” She still smiled as she asked the question, but her glance was soft﻿—almost tender﻿—and there was a note of whimsical anxiety in her voice.

===Chinese===
“你确定么？”她问时仍带着笑，但目光温柔——近乎缱绻——声音里含着一缕异想天开的不安。

===Original===
“Quite sure. I give you my confident assurance.”

===Chinese===
“十分确定。我向你郑重保证。”

===Original===
She laughed gaily. “Then,” said she, “I am satisfied, for I am sure you know. But here is a mighty telepathist who can read the thoughts even of a mummy. A most formidable companion. But tell me how you know.”

===Chinese===
她欢畅地笑了。“那么，”她说，“我满意了，因为我确信你是知道的。瞧，这里就站着一位了不起的传心术士，连木乃伊的心思都读得出来。一位何等厉害的同伴。不过，告诉我你是怎么知道的。”

===Original===
“I know because it is he who gave you to me to be my friend. Don’t you remember?”

===Chinese===
“我知道，因为正是他把你说给我、要你做我朋友的。你不记得了么？”

===Original===
“Yes, I remember,” she answered softly. “It was when you were so sympathetic with my foolish whim that I felt we were really friends.”

===Chinese===
“记得，”她柔声答道。“正是在你那样体谅我的痴念的时候，我感到我们真正成了朋友。”

===Original===
“And I, when you confided your pretty fancy to me, thanked you for the gift of your friendship, and treasured it, and do still treasure it, above everything on earth.”

===Chinese===
“而我呢，当你把那个可爱的幻想说给我听时，我便为这份友谊的馈赠谢过你，把它珍藏着，至今仍珍藏着，胜过世上的一切。”

===Original===
