She looked at me quickly with a sort of nervousness in her manner, and cast down her eyes. Then, after a few moments’ almost embarrassed silence, as if to bring back our talk to a less emotional plane, she said:

===Chinese===
她很快地看了我一眼，举止间带着几分不安，垂下了眼睛。随后，经过几近窘促的片刻沉默，她仿佛要把我们的话引回一个不那么动情的层面上来，说道：

===Original===
“Do you notice the curious way in which this memorial divides itself up into two parts?”

===Chinese===
“你注意到这座纪念物自行分成两部分的奇妙格局了么？”

===Original===
“How do you mean?” I asked a little disconcerted by the sudden descent.

===Chinese===
“此话怎讲？”我问，被这陡然降下的话题弄得有些发窘。

===Original===
“I mean that there is a part of it that is purely decorative and a part that is expressive or emotional. You notice that the general design and scheme of decoration, although really Greek in feeling, follows rigidly the Egyptian conventions. But the portrait is entirely in the Greek manner, and when they came to that pathetic farewell, it had to be spoken in their own tongue, written in their own familiar characters.”

===Chinese===
“我是说，它有一部分纯然是装饰，另有一部分则重在表达与抒情。你注意到，它的总体设计与装饰布局，虽然情致上确是希腊的，却严格遵循埃及的成规；可那幅画像却纯然是希腊作风；而临到那声凄婉的诀别，他们就不得不用自己的语言来说，用自己惯熟的字母来写。”

===Original===
“Yes. I have noticed that and admired the taste with which they have kept the inscription so inconspicuous as not to clash with the decoration. An obtrusive inscription in Greek characters would have spoiled the consistency of the whole scheme.”

===Chinese===
“是的。我注意到这一点，也叹赏他们的趣味，把铭文安排得如此不显眼，不致与装饰相冲突。一段招摇的希腊字母铭文，会败坏整个布局的浑然一致。”

===Original===
“Yes, it would.” She assented absently as if she were thinking of something else, and once more gazed thoughtfully at the mummy. I watched her with deep content: noted the lovely contour of her cheek, the soft masses of hair that strayed away so gracefully from her brow, and thought her the most wonderful creature that had ever trod the earth. Suddenly she looked at me reflectively.

===Chinese===
“是的，会的。”她心不在焉地应着，仿佛想着别的什么，又出神地凝望起那具木乃伊。我怀着深深的满足注视她：留意她面颊可爱的轮廓，那一头柔软的鬈发从她额边何等袅娜地披散开去，只觉得她是自有这个世界以来踏行其上的最奇妙的造物。忽然，她若有所思地望向了我。

===Original===
“I wonder,” she said, “what made me tell you about Artemidorus. It was a rather silly, childish sort of make-believe, and I wouldn’t have told anyone else for the world; not even my father. How did I know that you would sympathize and understand?”

===Chinese===
“我纳闷，”她说，“是什么让我把阿耳忒弥多鲁斯的事讲给了你听。那本是相当傻、相当孩子气的假想，我无论如何也不肯讲给别人的；连我父亲都不曾。我怎么就知道，你会体谅、会懂得呢？”

===Original===
She asked the question in all simplicity with her serious gray eyes looking inquiringly into mine. And the answer came to me in a flash, with the beating of my own heart.

===Chinese===
她全然天真地问出这话，一双严肃的灰眼睛探询地望进我的眼睛。而答案电光石火般地来到我心头，伴着我自己的心跳。

===Original===
“I will tell you how you know, Ruth,” I whispered passionately. “It was because I loved you more than anyone else in the world has ever loved you, and you felt my love in your heart and called it sympathy.”

===Chinese===
“我这就告诉你是怎么知道的，露丝，”我激动地低声说。“那是因为，我爱你胜过世上任何人曾经爱过你，而你在心里感到了这份爱，把它唤作了体谅。”

===Original===
I stopped short, for she had blushed scarlet and then turned deathly pale. And now she looked at me wildly, almost with terror.

===Chinese===
我蓦地顿住，因为她已涨得满脸绯红，随即又转为死一般的苍白。此刻她狂乱地望着我，几乎带着惊恐。

===Original===
“Have I shocked you, Ruth dearest?” I exclaimed penitently, “have I spoken too soon? If I have, forgive me. But I had to tell you. I have been eating my heart out for love of you for I don’t know how long. I think I have loved you from the first day we met. Perhaps I shouldn’t have spoken yet, but, Ruth dear, if you only knew what a sweet girl you are, you wouldn’t blame me.”

===Chinese===
“我把你吓着了么，最亲爱的露丝？”我悔愧地喊道，“我说得太早了么？若是，请原谅我。可我不得不告诉你。不知有多久了，我一直为爱你而心力交瘁。我想，从我们相遇的头一天起，我就爱上你了。也许我本不该这么早开口，可是，亲爱的露丝，你若知道自己是个多么甜美的姑娘，就不会怪我了。”

===Original===
“I don’t blame you,” she said, almost in a whisper; “I blame myself. I have been a bad friend to you, who have been so loyal and loving to me. I ought not to have let this happen. For it can’t be, Paul; I can’t say what you want me to say. We can never be anything more to one another than friends.”

===Chinese===
“我不怪你，”她说，声音低得近乎耳语；“我怪我自己。我一直是个亏欠你的朋友，而你待我这样忠心、这样深情。我不该让这种事发生。因为这是不可能的，保罗；你要我说的话，我说不出。我们之间，永远只能止于朋友，再不能多一分。”

===Original===
A cold hand seemed to grasp my heart﻿—a horrible fear that I had lost all that I cared for﻿—all that made life desirable.

===Chinese===
仿佛有一只冰冷的手攫住了我的心——一阵可怕的恐惧：怕我已失去了一切我所在乎的东西——一切使人生值得眷恋的东西。

===Original===
“Why can’t we?” I asked. “Do you mean that﻿—that the gods have been gracious to some other man?”

===Chinese===
“为什么不能呢？”我问。“你是说——莫非诸神已经垂青了另一个男人？”

===Original===
“No, no,” she answered hastily﻿—almost indignantly, “of course I don’t mean that.”

===Chinese===
“不，不，”她急急地答道——几乎是愤然地，“我当然不是那个意思。”

===Original===
“Then it is only that you don’t love me yet. Of course you don’t. Why should you? But you will, dear, some day. And I will wait patiently until that day comes and not trouble you with entreaties. I will wait for you as Jacob waited for Rachel; and as the long years seemed to him but as a few days because of the love he bore her, so it shall be with me, if only you will not send me away quite without hope.”

===Chinese===
“那么，只是你还不爱我。你自然还不爱。为什么要爱呢？可是你会爱的，亲爱的，总有一天。我会耐心等到那一天，不用恳求来烦扰你。我要像雅各等候拉结（Rachel）那样等候你；在他，漫长的岁月因他对她的爱而恍如数日，在我也将如此——只要你不把我打发走，不至于连一线指望都不留。”

===Original===
She was looking down, white-faced, with a hardening of the lips as if she were in bodily pain. “You don’t understand,” she whispered. “It can’t be﻿—it can never be. There is something that makes it impossible, now and always. I can’t tell you more than that.”

===Chinese===
她低着头，面色苍白，双唇紧抿，仿佛正忍受着身体的疼痛。“你不懂，”她低声说。“不可能的——永远不可能。有一件事使它无从谈起，如今如此，永远如此。我至多只能说到这里。”

===Original===
“But, Ruth dearest,” I pleaded despairingly, “may it not become possible some day? Can it not be made possible? I can wait, but I can’t give you up. Is there no chance whatever that this obstacle may be removed?”

===Chinese===
“可是，最亲爱的露丝，”我绝望地恳求道，“难道它不会有朝一日变为可能么？难道不能设法使它变为可能么？我可以等，可我不能放弃你。横在我们中间的这道障碍，难道就毫无除去的机会么？”

===Original===
“Very little, I fear. Hardly any. No, Paul; it is hopeless, and I can’t bear to talk about it. Let me go now. Let us say goodbye here and see one another no more for a while. Perhaps we may be friends again some day﻿—when you have forgiven me.”

===Chinese===
“机会很小，我怕。几乎没有。不，保罗；是没有指望了，我不忍再谈下去。现在让我走吧。让我们就在这里道别，彼此暂且不要再见面。也许有一天，我们还能重做朋友——等你原谅了我之后。”

===Original===
“Forgiven you, dearest!” I exclaimed. “There is nothing to forgive. And we are friends, Ruth. Whatever happens, you are the dearest friend I have on earth, or can ever have.”

===Chinese===
“原谅你，最亲爱的！”我喊道。“没有什么可原谅的。而且我们是朋友，露丝。无论发生什么，你都是我在人世间拥有的、或所能拥有的最亲爱的朋友。”

===Original===
“Thank you, Paul,” she said faintly. “You are very good to me. But let me go, please. I must be alone.”

===Chinese===
“谢谢你，保罗，”她微弱地说。“你待我太好了。可是请让我走吧。我必须独自待着。”

===Original===
She held out a trembling hand, and, as I took it, I was shocked to see how terribly agitated and ill she looked.

===Chinese===
她伸出一只颤抖的手，我握住时，看见她那副激动欲狂、形同病中的模样，不由得暗暗心惊。

===Original===
“May I not come with you, dear?” I pleaded.

===Chinese===
“我不能陪你一起去么，亲爱的？”我恳求道。

===Original===
“No, no!” she exclaimed breathlessly; “I must go away by myself. I want to be alone. Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
“不，不！”她气促地喊道；“我必须自己一个人走开。我想独自待着。再见。”

===Original===
“Before I let you go, Ruth﻿—if you must go﻿—I must have a most solemn promise from you.”

===Chinese===
“在我放你走之前，露丝——倘若你非走不可——我必须得到你最郑重的承诺。”

===Original===
Her sad gray eyes met mine and her lips quivered with an unspoken question.

===Chinese===
她那双哀愁的灰眼睛迎上我的目光，嘴唇因一个未出口的疑问而微微颤动。

===Original===
“You must promise me,” I went on, “that if ever this barrier that parts us should be removed, you will let me know instantly. Remember that I love you always, and that I am waiting for you always on this side of the grave.”

===Chinese===
“你必须答应我，”我接着说，“倘若这道隔开我们的屏障有朝一日得以除去，你要立刻让我知道。记住，我永远爱你，我在这坟墓的这一边永远等着你。”

===Original===
She caught her breath in a quick little sob, and pressed my hand.

===Chinese===
她猛地抽了一口气，像一声短短的呜咽，随即紧握了握我的手。

===Original===
“Yes,” she whispered: “I promise. Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
“好，”她低声说：“我答应。再见。”

===Original===
She pressed my hand again and was gone; and, as I gazed at the empty doorway through which she had passed, I caught a glimpse of her reflection in a glass on the landing, where she had paused for a moment to wipe her eyes. I felt it, in a manner, indelicate to have seen her, and turned away my head quickly; and yet I was conscious of a certain selfish satisfaction in the sweet sympathy that her grief bespoke.

===Chinese===
她又握了握我的手，便走了；我凝望着她穿过去的那扇空门，瞥见楼梯平台上的一面镜子里她的身影——她曾在那里停了片刻，拭眼睛。在某种意义上，我觉得自己窥见了她，未免不甚体贴，连忙把头掉了开去；然而她那悲戚所透露的甜美情意，又使我暗自怀着一种自私的满足。

===Original===
But now that she was gone a horrible sense of desolation descended on me. Only now, by the consciousness of irreparable loss, did I begin to realize the meaning of this passion of love that had stolen unawares into my life. How it had glorified the present and spread a glamor of delight over the dimly considered future: how all pleasures and desires, hopes and ambitions, had converged upon it as a focus; how it had stood out as the one great reality behind which the other circumstances of life were as a background, shimmering, half seen, immaterial and unreal. And now it was gone﻿—lost, as it seemed, beyond hope; and that which was left to me was but the empty frame from which the picture had vanished.

===Chinese===
可她一走，一阵可怕的荒凉便落到了我的身上。直到此刻，凭着这无可挽回的损失的自觉，我才开始懂得这场不识不觉潜入我生命的爱情意味着什么。它曾怎样给当下镀了金，又给那原本只朦胧设想的将来铺上一层欢悦的光晕；一切的欢愉与欲求、希望与抱负，曾怎样汇拢到它身上，如同聚向一个焦点；它曾怎样凸现为唯一伟大的真实，人生的其余境况都退到它背后，成了浮光闪烁、隐约可见、无关紧要也不真实的背景。而如今它去了——失去了，似乎已失去得无可挽回；留给我的，只是一具画已隐去的空画框。

===Original===
I have no idea how long I stood rooted to the spot where she had left me, wrapped in a dull consciousness of pain, immersed in a half-numb reverie. Recent events flitted, dreamlike, through my mind; our happy labors in the reading-room; our first visit to the Museum; and this present day that had opened so brightly and with such joyous promise. One by one these phantoms of a vanished happiness came and went. Occasional visitors sauntered into the room﻿—but the galleries were mostly empty that day﻿—gazed inquisitively at my motionless figure, and went their way. And still the dull, intolerable ache in my breast went on, the only vivid consciousness that was left to me.

===Chinese===
我在她撇下我的地方伫立了多久，自己也不知道，只觉裹在一层钝痛的意识里，沉浸在半麻木的出神之中。近来的诸般事件，梦一般掠过我的脑海：我们在阅览室里愉快的劳作；我们初访博物馆；还有今天这个日子，它开篇时那样明朗，那样满怀欢欣的指望。这些消逝了的幸福的幻影，一个个来了又去。偶有参观者踱进展厅——那天各展厅大半空着——好奇地望一望我那纹丝不动的身影，便各自走开。而我胸中那阵钝钝的、难堪的疼痛仍在继续，那是我仅存的一点鲜明的知觉。

===Original===
Presently I raised my eyes and met those of the portrait. The sweet, pensive face of the old Greek settler looked out at me wistfully as though he would offer comfort; as though he would tell me that he, too, had known sorrow when he lived his life in the sunny Fayyum. And a subtle consolation, like the faint scent of old rose leaves, seemed to exhale from that friendly face that had looked on the birth of my happiness and had seen it wither and fade. I turned away, at last, with a silent farewell; and when I looked back, he seemed to speed me on my way with gentle valediction.

===Chinese===
终于，我抬起眼睛，迎上了画像的那双眼睛。那位古希腊老移民的甜美而忧郁的脸，正怅然望着我，仿佛要给我安慰；仿佛要告诉我，当年他在阳光和煦的法尤姆过活时，也曾识得忧愁。一缕细微的慰藉，仿佛干玫瑰叶的一丝幽香，从那张友善的脸上散发出来——这张脸见过我的幸福的降生，也眼看着它凋萎、消逝。我终于转过身去，向他默然道别；待我回首再望，他仿佛正以一声温柔的诀别，送我上路。

## XVII The Accusing Finger / 十七 指控的手指
===Original===
Of my wanderings after I left the Museum on that black and dismal *dies irae*, I have but a dim recollection. But I must have traveled a quite considerable distance, since it wanted an hour or two to the time for returning to the surgery, and I spent the interval walking swiftly through streets and squares, unmindful of the happenings around, intent only on my present misfortune, and driven by a natural impulse to seek relief in bodily exertion. For mental distress sets up, as it were, a sort of induced current of physical unrest; a beneficent arrangement, by which a dangerous excess of emotional excitement may be transformed into motor energy, and so safely got rid of. The motor apparatus acts as a safety-valve to the psychical; and if the engine races for a while, with the onset of a bodily fatigue the emotional pressure-gauge returns to a normal reading.

===Chinese===
在那个阴沉晦暗的*dies irae*（震怒之日）离开博物馆之后，我一路如何游荡，如今只余模糊的记忆。但想必走了颇远的一段路，因为离回诊所应诊的时辰还差一两个钟头，我便把这段光阴消磨在疾行于街衢广场之间，对周遭发生的一切浑不在意，一心只念着眼下的不幸，又受着天性冲动的驱使，想借身体的操劳寻求排遣。原来精神上的痛苦，好比诱发出一股肉体不安的感应电流；这是一种仁慈的安排，凭着它，情绪亢奋那危险的超载得以转化为运动的能量，从而安然消解。运动器官之于心灵，恰似一只安全阀；引擎纵然轰鸣狂转一阵，待到肉体疲劳袭来，情绪的压力表也就回落到正常的读数上了。

===Original===
And so it was with me. At first I was conscious of nothing but a sense of utter bereavement, of the shipwreck of all my hopes. But, by degrees, as I threaded my way among the moving crowds, I came to a better and more worthy frame of mind. After all, I had lost nothing that I had ever had. Ruth was still all that she had ever been to me﻿—perhaps even more; and if that had been a rich endowment yesterday, why not today also? And how unfair it would be to her if I should mope and grieve over a disappointment that was no fault of hers and for which there was no remedy? Thus I reasoned with myself, and to such purpose that, by the time I reached Fetter Lane, my dejection had come to quite manageable proportions and I had formed the resolution to get back to the *status quo ante bellum* as soon as possible.

===Chinese===
我便是如此。起初我只觉万般失落，所有希望尽数倾覆。然而，在熙攘人流中穿行渐久，我的心境也渐渐好转，变得较为达观。毕竟，我并未失去任何曾经拥有的东西。露丝于我仍是她一向的那样——甚或更有过之；倘若这份丰厚的赐予昨天值得珍视，今天何尝不然？况且，这场失意既非她的过错，又无可挽回，我若为此郁郁寡欢、自伤自怜，对她岂非不公？我如此这般与自己讲理，竟颇见成效：待走到菲特巷时，我的沮丧已缩成相当可控的分量，并且拿定主意，要尽早恢复*status quo ante bellum*（战前原状）。

===Original===
About eight o’clock, as I was sitting alone in the consulting-room, gloomily persuading myself that I was now quite resigned to the inevitable, Adolphus brought me a registered packet, at the handwriting on which my heart gave such a bound that I had much ado to sign the receipt. As soon as Adolphus had retired (with undissembled contempt of the shaky signature) I tore open the packet, and as I drew out a letter a tiny box dropped on the table.

===Chinese===
八点钟左右，我正独自坐在诊室里，阴郁地劝说自己如今已甘心认命，阿道弗斯送进来一个挂号邮包，一瞥那上面的笔迹，我的心便猛地一跳，连回执都几乎签不成字。等阿道弗斯退下去（他对那发颤的签名毫不掩饰地表示鄙夷）之后，我扯开邮包，往外抽信时，一只小小的盒子落在了桌上。

===Original===
The letter was all too short, and I devoured it over and over again with the eagerness of a condemned man reading a reprieve:

===Chinese===
信写得未免太短，我读了一遍又一遍，那份急切，恰似死囚诵读缓刑的文书：

===Original===
> My Dear Paul,
> Forgive me for leaving you so abruptly this afternoon, and leaving you so unhappy, too. I am more sane and reasonable now, and so send you greeting and beg you not to grieve for that which can never be. It is quite impossible, dear friend, and I entreat you, as you care for me, never to speak of it again; never again to make me feel that I can give you so little when you have given so much. And do not try to see me for a little while. I shall miss your visits, and so will my father, who is very fond of you; but it is better that we should not meet, until we can take up our old relations﻿—if that can ever be.
> I am sending you a little keepsake in case we should drift apart on the eddies of life. It is the ring that I told you about﻿—the one that my uncle gave me. Perhaps you may be able to wear it as you have a small hand, but in any case keep it in remembrance of our friendship. The device on it is the Eye of Osiris, a mystic symbol for which I have a sentimentally superstitious affection, as also had my poor uncle, who actually bore it tattooed in scarlet on his breast. It signifies that the great judge of the dead looks down on men to see that justice is done and that truth prevails. So I commend you to the good Osiris; may his eye be upon you, ever watchful over your welfare in the absence of
> Your affectionate friend, **Ruth**.

===Chinese===
> 我亲爱的保罗：
> 原谅我今天下午那样突然地离你而去，还让你那样难过。我此刻清醒明理多了，因此向你致意，并求你不要为那永远不能之事悲伤。那是全然不可能的，亲爱的朋友；我恳求你，看在你对我的情分上，永远不要再提起它；永远不要再让我觉得，你给了我那么多，而我所能回报的却这样少。这一阵子也不要设法来看我。我会惦念你的来访，我父亲也会——他是很喜欢你的；不过在我们能重续旧谊之前——如果终究能够的话——还是不相见的好。
> 随信寄上一件小小的纪念物，以防我们会在人生的洄流中各自漂散。就是我跟你说起过的那枚戒指——我伯父给我的那枚。你的手生得小，兴许戴得进去；但无论如何，请留着它，纪念我们的友谊。戒面上的图纹是俄塞里斯之眼，那是个神秘的象征，我对它怀有一种近乎迷信的深情，我那可怜的伯父亦复如此——他竟真的让它以朱砂纹在自己的胸前。它意味着：亡者的伟大审判者俯视世人，务教正义得伸、真理得胜。所以，我将你托付给善良的俄塞里斯；愿他的眼睛看顾着你，在你那
> 挚爱你的朋友**露丝**不在跟前的时日里，永远守护着你的安康。

===Original===
It was a sweet letter, I thought, even if it carried little comfort; quiet and reticent like its writer, but with an undertone of affection. I laid it down at length, and, taking the ring from its box, examined it fondly. Though but a copy, it had all the quaintness and feeling of the antique original, and, above all, it was fragrant with the spirit of the giver. Dainty and delicate, wrought of silver and gold, with an inlay of copper, I would not have exchanged it for the Koh-i-noor; and when I had slipped it on my finger its tiny eye of blue enamel looked up at me so friendly and companionable that I felt the glamour of the old-world superstition stealing over me too.

===Chinese===
这是一封亲切可爱的信，我想，尽管其中安慰的成分寥寥；它安静而含蓄，一如写信的人，字里行间却透着深情底韵。我终于把信放下，从盒中取出戒指，深情地端详。它虽只是件仿制品，却有古物原型的全部雅趣与情韵，而尤其可贵的是，它散发着赠予人的气质。精致纤巧，以银与金制成，又嵌着铜饰，拿光山钻石（Koh-i-noor）来换我都不肯；等我把它套上手指，那小小的蓝珐琅眼睛友善而亲昵地仰望着我，我不觉也感到那古老迷信的魅力悄悄袭上身来。

===Original===
Not a single patient came in this evening, which was well for me (and also for the patient), as I was able forthwith to write in reply a long letter; but this I shall spare the long-suffering reader excepting its concluding paragraph:

===Chinese===
这天晚上没有一个病人上门，这于我（也于病人）倒是好事，因为我得以立刻写一封长长的回信；不过除了末段之外，其余部分我就不拿去折磨宽厚的读者了：

===Original===
> And now, dearest, I have said my say; once for all I have said it, and I will not open my mouth on the subject again (I am not actually opening it now) “until the times do alter.” And if the times do never alter﻿—if it shall come to pass, in due course, that we two shall sit side by side, white-haired, and crinkly-nosed, and lean our poor old chins upon our sticks and mumble and gibber amicably over the things that might have been if the good Osiris had come up to the scratch﻿—I will still be content, because your friendship, Ruth, is better than another woman’s love. So you see, I have taken my gruel and come up to time smiling﻿—if you will pardon the pugilistic metaphor﻿—and I promise you loyally to do your bidding and never again to distress you.
> Your faithful and loving friend, **Paul**.

===Chinese===
> 如今，最亲爱的，我要说的都已说尽；一次说了，便永远说了，这个话题我绝不再开口（此刻其实也并不在开口）“直到时势改变”。倘若时势永不改变——倘若当真有那么一天，你我白发苍苍、皱着鼻子并肩而坐，把可怜的老下巴拄在手杖上，絮絮叨叨、和和气气地念叨那些“本来或许可以”——只要善良的俄塞里斯当初肯出场应战——我也依然心满意足，因为露丝，你的友谊胜过另一个女人的爱情。你看，我已领受了我那碗麦片粥，仍按时含笑上场——请恕我用拳击的比方——我忠实地向你保证，一切听你的吩咐，再不让你难过。
> 你忠实而深情的朋友，**保罗**。

===Original===
This letter I addressed and stamped, and then, with a wry grimace which I palmed off on myself (but not on Adolphus) as a cheerful smile, I went out and dropped it into the postbox; after which I further deluded myself by murmuring *Nunc dimittis* and assuring myself that the incident was now absolutely closed.

===Chinese===
我在信封上写好地址、贴上邮票，随即扮出一个扭曲的怪相——我骗自己说（却骗不过阿道弗斯）那是一副愉快的笑容——出门把信投进了邮筒；之后我又低声念着*Nunc dimittis*（西面颂），哄骗自己说这段公案如今已彻底了结。

===Original===
But despite this comfortable assurance I was, in the days that followed, an exceedingly miserable young man. It is all very well to write down troubles of this kind as trivial and sentimental. They are nothing of the kind. When a man of essentially serious nature has found the one woman of all the world who fulfils his highest ideals of womanhood, who is, in fact, a woman in ten thousand, to whom he has given all that he has to give of love and worship, the sudden wreck of all his hopes is no small calamity. And so I found it. Resign myself as I would to the bitter reality, the ghost of the might-have-been haunted me night and day, so that I spent my leisure wandering abstractedly about the streets, always trying to banish thought and never for an instant succeeding. A great unrest was upon me; and when I received a letter from Dick Barnard announcing his arrival at Madeira, homeward bound, I breathed a sigh of relief. I had no plans for the future, but I longed to be rid of the now irksome, routine of the practise﻿—to be free to come and go when and how I pleased.

===Chinese===
尽管有这句宽心话垫底，接下来的那些日子里，我仍是个愁苦万状的年轻人。把这类烦恼一笔勾销为琐屑多感，倒也轻巧；它们其实绝非如此。一个本质上严肃的人，既已在普天之下寻到了那唯一一个符合他女性理想之极致的女子——事实上是万里挑一的女子——并已把他在爱与崇敬上所有能给的一切都给了她，那么全部希望的骤然倾覆便绝非小小的灾难。我也确实领教了。任凭我怎样向这苦涩的现实低头认命，那“本可如何”的幽灵仍日夜缠着我不放，害得我把闲暇都耗在心不在焉的街头漫游里，一味想驱散思绪，却一刻也不曾成功。一股大大的不安压在我身上；等到收到迪克·巴纳德的信，说他已抵达马德拉（Madeira）、踏上归程，我才长出了一口气。我对前途并无筹划，只渴望摆脱如今已变得烦人的诊所例行公事——渴望来去自由，何时走、怎么走，全凭自家高兴。

===Original===
One evening, as I sat consuming with little appetite my solitary supper, there fell on me a sudden sense of loneliness. The desire that I had hitherto felt to be alone with my own miserable reflections gave place to a yearning for human companionship. That, indeed, which I craved for most was forbidden, and I must abide by my lady’s wishes; but there were my friends in the Temple. It was more than a week since I had seen them; in fact, we had not met since the morning of that unhappiest day of my life. They would be wondering what had become of me. I rose from the table, and having filled my pouch from a tin of tobacco, set forth for King’s Bench Walk.

===Chinese===
一天晚上，我正坐着吃那食不知味的独自晚餐，一阵孤独之感突然袭上心头。此前我一直渴望独处，与自己的愁思作伴，此刻这渴望却让位给了对人世伴侣的渴求。我最渴望的那一位固然是禁地，我必须遵从我的小姐的心愿；可内殿里还有我的朋友们。我已有一个多星期没见他们了；事实上，自我此生最不幸的那天早晨之后，我们就不曾见过面。他们多半在纳闷我出了什么事。我离了餐桌，从烟草罐里装满烟袋，动身往王座步道去。

===Original===
As I approached the entry of No. 5A in the gathering darkness I met Thorndyke himself emerging encumbered with two deck-chairs, a reading-lantern, and a book.

===Chinese===
暮色渐浓中，我走近五号A的门洞，迎面碰上桑代克本人正往外走，手里大包大揽地拿着两把帆布躺椅、一盏读书提灯和一本书。

===Original===
“Why, Berkeley!” he exclaimed, “is it indeed thou? We have been wondering what had become of you.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，伯克利！”他叫道，“当真是你么？我们正纳闷你上哪儿去了呢。”

===Original===
“It *is* a long time since I looked you up,” I admitted.

===Chinese===
“我*确实*好久没来看你们了，”我承认。

===Original===
He scrutinized me attentively by the light of the entry lamp, and then remarked: “Fetter Lane doesn’t seem to be agreeing with you very well, my son. You are looking quite thin and peaky.”

===Chinese===
他就着门洞的灯光仔细端详我，然后说道：“菲特巷的水土跟你好像不大合呢，小伙子。你看上去清瘦憔悴了不少。”

===Original===
“Well, I’ve nearly done with it. Barnard will be back in about ten days. His ship is putting in at Madeira to coal and take in some cargo, and then he is coming home. Where are you going with those chairs?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，我也快熬到头了。巴纳德大约十天后就回来。他的船要在马德拉停靠加煤、装些货，然后就动身回家。你抱这些椅子到哪儿去？”

===Original===
“I am going to sit down at the end of the Walk by the railings. It’s cooler there than indoors. If you will wait a moment I will go and fetch another chair for Jervis, though he won’t be back for a little while.” He ran up the stairs, and presently returned with a third chair, and we carried our impedimenta down to the quiet corner of the Walk.

===Chinese===
“我打算到步道尽头栏杆边去坐坐。那儿比屋里凉快。请你稍等片刻，我去给杰维斯再搬一把椅子来，虽然他一时半会儿还回不来。”他跑上楼去，不一会儿就搬了第三把椅子下来，我们便把这些行装搬到步道那个安静的角落。

===Original===
“So your term of servitude is coming to an end,” said he, when we had placed the chairs and hung the lantern on the railings. “Any other news?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你的苦役期限快满了，”等椅子放好、提灯挂上栏杆之后，他说道。“还有别的消息吗？”

===Original===
“No. Have you any?”

===Chinese===
“没有。你们有吗？”

===Original===
“I am afraid I have not. All my inquiries have yielded negative results. There is, of course, a considerable body of evidence, and it all seems to point one way. But I am unwilling to make a decisive move without something more definite. I am really waiting for confirmation or otherwise of my ideas on the subject; for some new item of evidence.”

===Chinese===
“恐怕也没有。我的一切查访都毫无结果。当然，证据已颇有几件，而且似乎都指向同一个方向。但没有更确凿的东西，我不愿走决定性的一步。实际上，我在等待自己对此案的见解得到证实或推翻；等待某项新的证据出现。”

===Original===
“I didn’t know there was any evidence.”

===Chinese===
“我原来还不知道有什么证据。”

===Original===
“Didn’t you?” said Thorndyke. “But you know as much as I know. You have all the essential facts; but apparently you haven’t collated them and extracted their meaning. If you had, you would have found them curiously significant.”

===Chinese===
“是吗？”桑代克说。“可是你知道的和我知道的一样多。全部关键事实你都掌握了；只是你显然没有把它们加以排比、提炼出其中的含义。要是做了，你就会发现它们意味深长得蹊跷。”

===Original===
“I suppose I mustn’t ask what their significance is?”

===Chinese===
“我想，它们的含义我是不该问的吧？”

===Original===
“No, I think not. When I am conducting a case I mention my surmises to nobody﻿—not even to Jervis. Then I can say confidently that there has been no leakage. Don’t think I distrust you. Remember that my thoughts are my client’s property, and that the essence of strategy is to keep the enemy in the dark.”

===Chinese===
“不错，不该问。我承办一件案子的时候，我的揣测对谁都不说——连杰维斯也不例外。这样我才能满有把握地说，机密从未外泄。别以为我信不过你。记住，我的思路是委托人的财产，而战略的要义就在于让敌手蒙在鼓里。”

===Original===
“Yes, I see that. Of course I ought not to have asked.”

===Chinese===
“是，这我明白。我本来就不该问的。”

===Original===
“You ought not to need to ask,” Thorndyke replied, with a smile; “you should put the facts together and reason from them yourself.”

===Chinese===
“你应当根本用不着问，”桑代克含笑答道；“你该自己把事实归拢到一起，从中推出道理来。”

===Original===
While we had been talking I had noticed Thorndyke glance at me inquisitively from time to time. Now after an interval of silence, he asked suddenly:

===Chinese===
方才谈话之际，我已注意到桑代克不时探究地瞟我一眼。这时沉默了片刻之后，他突然问道：

===Original===
“Is anything amiss, Berkeley? Are you worrying about your friends’ affairs?”

===Chinese===
“有什么不顺心的事么，伯克利？是在为你朋友们的案子发愁吗？”

===Original===
“No, not particularly; though their prospects don’t look very rosy.”

===Chinese===
“没有，算不上；虽说他们的前景看起来不很乐观。”

===Original===
“Perhaps they are not quite so bad as they look,” said he. “But I am afraid something is troubling you. All your gay spirits seem to have evaporated.” He paused for a few moments, and then added: “I don’t want to intrude on your private affairs, but if I can help you by advice or otherwise, remember that we are old friends and that you are my academic offspring.”

===Chinese===
“也许并不像看上去那么糟，”他说。“不过我怕是有什么事在烦扰你。你那股快活劲儿似乎都蒸发光了。”他顿了片刻，又接着说：“我并不想打探你的私事，但倘若我能出个主意或帮上什么忙，请记住，我们是老朋友，而且你是我门下出身的学生。”

===Original===
Instinctively, with a man’s natural reticence, I began to mumble a half-articulate disclaimer; and then I stopped. After all, why should I not confide in him? He was a good man and a wise man, full of human sympathy, as I knew, though so cryptic and secretive in his professional capacity. And I wanted a friend badly just now.

===Chinese===
出于男人天生的缄默，我本能地咕哝起半吞半吐的否认来；随即又打住了。毕竟，我为什么不能向他倾吐呢？他是好人，也是智者，富于人类的同情心，这个我知道，尽管他在职业上那样讳莫如深、守口如瓶。而此时此刻，我正迫切需要一个朋友。

===Original===
“I’m afraid,” I began shyly, “it is not a matter that admits of much help, and it’s hardly the sort of thing that I ought to worry you by talking about﻿—”

===Chinese===
“恐怕，”我怯生生地开口，“这件事不是外人帮得上忙的，而且也多半不是我该拿来说了、平白烦扰你的那一类事——”

===Original===
“If it is enough to make you unhappy, my dear fellow, it is enough to merit serious consideration by your friend; so if you don’t mind telling me﻿—”

===Chinese===
“既然它足以使你不快，亲爱的伙计，它也就足以值得你的朋友认真看待；所以，要是你不介意告诉我——”

===Original===
“Of course I don’t, sir!” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“我当然不介意，先生！”我叫道。

===Original===
“Then fire away; and don’t call me ‘sir.’ We are brother practitioners now.”

===Chinese===
“那就尽管说吧；别叫我‘先生’。如今咱们是同行了。”

===Original===
Thus encouraged, I poured out the story of my little romance; bashfully at first and with halting phrases, but later, with more freedom and confidence. He listened with grave attention, and once or twice put a question when my narrative became a little disconnected. When I had finished he laid his hand softly on my arm.

===Chinese===
受到这样的鼓励，我便把那段小小罗曼史的始末倾吐出来；起初怯生生的，语句迟滞，后来说得便较自由、较有底气。他神情郑重地倾听，在我叙述得有些前后不接的时候，插问过一两次。等我说完，他把手轻轻放在我的臂上。

===Original===
“You have had rough luck, Berkeley. I don’t wonder that you are miserable. I am more sorry than I can tell you.”

===Chinese===
“你的运气太坏了，伯克利。你心里苦，我不奇怪。我心里难过得很，非言语所能告诉你。”

===Original===
“Thank you,” I said. “It’s exceedingly good of you to listen so patiently, but it’s a shame for me to pester you with my sentimental troubles.”

===Chinese===
“谢谢你，”我说。“你这样耐心听我讲，真是太难为你了；只是拿我这多愁善感的烦恼来搅扰你，实在惭愧。”

===Original===
“Now, Berkeley, you don’t think that, and I hope you don’t think that I do. We should be bad biologists and worse physicians if we should underestimate the importance of that which is nature’s chiefest care. The one salient biological truth is the paramount importance of sex; and we are deaf and blind if we do not hear and see it in everything that lives when we look abroad upon the world; when we listen to the spring song of the birds, or when we consider the lilies of the field. And as is man to the lower organisms, so is human love to their merely reflex manifestations of sex. I will maintain, and you will agree with me, I know, that the love of a serious and honorable man for a woman who is worthy of him is the most momentous of all human affairs. It is the foundation of social life, and its failure is a serious calamity, not only to those whose lives may be thereby spoilt, but to society at large.”

===Chinese===
“得了，伯克利，你自己并不这么想，我也希望你不会以为我是这么想的。倘若我们低估了天性最着意经营之事的重要性，我们就是不称职的生物学家，更不称职的医生。生物学的第一条显著真理，便是性的至高无上；当我们放眼世界，若在一切生灵身上听不出、看不出这一点，我们便是聋子瞎子；当我们听百鸟的春歌，或者思量野地里的百合花的时候，也是如此。而人对低等生物是什么关系，人类的爱对它们那种纯属反射的性表现就是什么关系。我要主张——而且我知道你会赞成我——一个严肃而可敬的男人对一个配得上他的女人的爱，是人间万事中最重大的一件。它是社会生活的根基；它的失败是一场严重的灾祸，不仅对于可能因此被葬送一生的人，也对于整个社会。”

===Original===
“It’s a serious enough matter for the parties concerned,” I agreed; “but that is no reason why they should bore their friends.”

===Chinese===
“对当事人来说，这确实够严重的了，”我表示同意；“可这并不能成为他们拿去烦扰朋友的理由。”

===Original===
“But they don’t. Friends should help one another and think it a privilege.”

===Chinese===
“可他们并没有烦扰。朋友本该互相扶助，并且引以为荣。”

===Original===
“Oh, I shouldn’t mind coming to you for help, knowing you as I do. But no one can help a poor devil in a case like this﻿—and certainly not a medical jurist.”

===Chinese===
“哦，像你这样的人，来向你求助我倒不介意。可是这样一宗案子里的可怜虫，谁也帮不上忙——法医学家么，就更不必提了。”

===Original===
“Oh, come, Berkeley!” he protested, “don’t rate us too low. The humblest of creatures has its uses﻿—‘even the little pismire,’ you know, as Izaak Walton tells us. Why, I have got substantial help from a stamp-collector. And then reflect upon the motor-scorcher and the earthworm and the blowfly. All these lowly creatures play their parts in the scheme of nature; and shall we cast out the medical jurist as nothing worth?”

===Chinese===
“哦，得了，伯克利！”他抗议道，“别把咱们估得太低。最卑微的生物也有它的用处——“就连小小的蚂蚁”，你知道，伊萨克·沃尔顿就这样告诉我们。嘿，我还从一位集邮家那里得到过实实在在的帮助呢。再想想飙车的人、蚯蚓和绿头苍蝇吧。这些低微的角色都在大自然的蓝图中各尽其职；难道倒要把法医学家当作一钱不值的东西扫地出门么？”

===Original===
I laughed dejectedly at my teacher’s genial irony.

===Chinese===
我黯然一笑，领受了我老师这善意的调侃。

===Original===
“What I meant,” said I, “was that there is nothing to be done but wait﻿—perhaps forever. I don’t know why she isn’t able to marry me, and I mustn’t ask her. She can’t be married already.”

===Chinese===
“我的意思是，”我说，“如今除了等待无事可做——也许要永远等下去。我不知道她为什么不能嫁给我，又不许我问她。她总不会是已经嫁了人。”

===Original===
“Certainly not. She told you explicitly that there was no man in the case.”

===Chinese===
“当然不是。她明明白白告诉过你，这事里头并没有别的男人。”

===Original===
“Exactly. And I can think of no other valid reason, excepting that she doesn’t care enough for me. That would be a perfectly sound reason, but then it would only be a temporary one, not the insuperable obstacle that she assumes to exist, especially as we really got on excellently together. I hope it isn’t some confounded perverse feminine scruple. I don’t see how it could be; but women are most frightfully tortuous and wrongheaded at times.”

===Chinese===
“正是。除此以外，我想不出别的站得住的理由，除非是她对我的情意还不够深。那倒是个完全说得通的理由；可那样的话，它便只是一时的，而不是她所认定存在的那种无法逾越的障碍，何况我们相处得实在非常融洽。但愿不是女人家那种可恶的别扭顾忌。我看不出怎么会是；可女人有时候那弯弯肠子和拧巴劲儿，怕人得很。”

===Original===
“I don’t see,” said Thorndyke, “why we should cast about for perversely abnormal motives when there is a perfectly reasonable explanation staring us in the face.”

===Chinese===
“我看不出，”桑代克说，“明明有一个完全合乎情理的解释摆在眼前，咱们何必还去搜寻什么反常乖张的动机。”

===Original===
“Is there?” I exclaimed. “I see none.”

===Chinese===
“有吗？”我叫道。“我看不出来。”

===Original===
“You are, not unnaturally, overlooking some of the circumstances that affect Miss Bellingham; but I don’t suppose she has failed to grasp their meaning. Do you realize what her position really is? I mean with regard to her uncle’s disappearance?”

===Chinese===
“你忽略了几件牵涉贝林厄姆小姐的情节，这也不足为怪；但我想她不会没有领会它们的含义。你可意识到她的处境实际上是什么样子？我是指关于她伯父失踪一事而言。”

===Original===
“I don’t think I quite understand you.”

===Chinese===
“我想我不大明白你的意思。”

===Original===
“Well, there is no use in blinking the facts,” said Thorndyke. “The position is this: if John Bellingham ever went to his brother’s house at Woodford, it is nearly certain that he went there after his visit to Hurst. Mind, I say ‘*if* he went’; I don’t say that I believe he did. But it is stated that he appears to have gone there; and if he did go, he was never seen alive afterward. Now, he did not go in at the front door. No one saw him enter the house. But there was a back gate, which John Bellingham knew, and which had a bell which rang in the library. And you will remember that, when Hurst and Jellicoe called, Mr. Bellingham had only just come in. Previous to that time Miss Bellingham had been alone in the library; that is to say, she was alone in the library at the very time when John Bellingham is said to have made his visit. That is the position, Berkeley. Nothing pointed has been said up to the present. But, sooner or later, if John Bellingham is not found, dead or alive, the question will be opened. Then it is certain that Hurst, in self-defense, will make the most of any facts that may transfer suspicion from him to someone else. And that someone else will be Miss Bellingham.”

===Chinese===
“呃，回避事实是没有用的，”桑代克说。“情形是这样：倘若约翰·贝林厄姆那天当真去了他弟弟在伍德福德的宅子，那么几乎可以断定，他是去过赫斯特那儿之后才去的。注意，我说的是‘*倘若*他去了’；我并不是说我相信他去了。但据称他似乎是到过那里；而倘若他去了，此后就再没有人见过活着的他。喏，他不是从前门进去的。没有人看见他进宅。可那宅子有一扇后门，约翰·贝林厄姆是知道它的，那门上有个铃，直通书房响。你总还记得，赫斯特和杰利科登门的时候，贝林厄姆先生也是刚进门不久。在那之前，贝林厄姆小姐一直独自在书房里；这就是说，据称约翰·贝林厄姆来访的当儿，她正独自待在书房里。形势便是如此，伯克利。到目前为止，还没有人把话说到这样露骨的程度。可是，倘若约翰·贝林厄姆一直生死不明、寻不着下落，这个问题迟早会被重新翻出来。到那时，赫斯特为求自保，必定要尽量利用任何能把嫌疑从他身上转到别人头上的事实。而那个别人，就会是贝林厄姆小姐。”

===Original===
I sat for some moments literally paralyzed with horror. Then my dismay gave place to indignation. “But damn it!” I exclaimed, starting up﻿—“I beg your pardon﻿—but could anyone have the infernal audacity to insinuate that that gentle, refined lady murdered her uncle?”

===Chinese===
我一时间竟吓得整个人僵在了那里。随即惊愕化作义愤。“可是，该死的！”我霍地跳起，嚷道——“恕我口出恶言——难道竟有人能这样丧心病狂，含沙射影地说那位温婉高贵的小姐谋害了自己的伯父？”

===Original===
“That is what will be hinted, if not plainly asserted; and she knows it. And that being so, is it difficult to understand why she should refuse to allow you to be publicly associated with her? To run the risk of dragging your honorable name into the sordid transactions of the police-court or the Old Bailey? To invest it, perhaps, with a dreadful notoriety?”

===Chinese===
“人家要暗示的正是这个，说不定还要明说；而她也明白。既然如此，她为什么不愿让你在众目睽睽之下与她牵连在一起，这还难理解吗？她岂肯去冒那样的风险，把你可敬的名声拖进治安法庭或老贝利那些乌七八糟的勾当里？岂肯让它也许就此罩上一层可怕的恶名？”

===Original===
“Oh, don’t! for God’s sake! It is too horrible! Not that I would care for myself. I would be proud to share her martyrdom of ignominy, if it had to be; but it is the sacrilege, the blasphemy of even thinking of her in such terms that enrages me.”

===Chinese===
“哦，别说了！看在上帝份上！这太可怕了！倒不是我自己在乎。倘若真有那么一天，我与她同受那场耻辱的殉难，我只会引以为荣；可是一想到竟有人用这等字眼去想她，那简直是亵渎、是渎神，这才叫我怒不可遏。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Thorndyke; “I understand and sympathize with you. Indeed, I share your righteous indignation at this dastardly affair. So you mustn’t think me brutal for putting the case so plainly.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”桑代克说；“我理解你，也同情你。说实在的，对这桩卑鄙的勾当，我同你一样义愤填膺。所以，我把话讲得这样直白，你可别以为我狠心。”

===Original===
“I don’t. You have only shown me the danger that I was fool enough not to see. But you seem to imply that this hideous position has been brought about deliberately.”

===Chinese===
“我没有那样想。你不过是让我看清了我蠢得竟没有看见的危险。不过，你的话里似乎在暗示：这个可怖的局面是有人处心积虑造成的。”

===Original===
“Certainly I do! This is no chance affair. Either the appearances indicate the real events﻿—which I am sure they do not﻿—or they have been created of a set purpose to lead to false conclusions. But the circumstances convince me that there has been a deliberate plot; and I am waiting﻿—in no spirit of Christian patience, I can tell you﻿—to lay my hand on the wretch who has done this.”

===Chinese===
“当然是这样！这绝不是偶然的事。这些表象要么反映了真实经过——我确信并非如此——要么就是有人蓄意制造出来、引人得出错误结论的。而种种情节使我相信：这里头有一场处心积虑的阴谋；我正在等着——我告诉你，可没有一点儿基督徒的耐心——等着一把揪住那个干出这事的恶棍。”

===Original===
“What are you waiting for?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“你等什么呢？”我问。

===Original===
“I am waiting for the inevitable,” he replied; “for the false move that the most artful criminal invariably makes. At present he is lying low; but presently he will make a move, and then I shall have him.”

===Chinese===
“我等那不可避免的一刻，”他答道；“等最狡黠的罪犯必然要走的那步错棋。眼下他按兵不动；可他不久就会有所动作，到那时我就能逮住他。”

===Original===
“But he may go on lying low. What will you do then?”

===Chinese===
“可他也许一直潜伏下去。到那时你怎么办？”

===Original===
“Yes, that is the danger. We may have to deal with the perfect villain who knows when to leave well alone. I have never met him, but he may exist, nevertheless.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，危险正在这里。咱们碰上的也许是个登峰造极的恶棍，深谙见好就收之道。这种人我从未遇到过，不过他未必就不存在。”

===Original===
“And then we should have to stand by and see our friends go under.”

===Chinese===
“到那时咱们就只能眼睁睁站着，瞧咱们的朋友们沉下去。”

===Original===
“Perhaps,” said Thorndyke; and we both subsided into gloomy and silent reflection.

===Chinese===
“也许吧，”桑代克说；于是我们俩都堕入阴郁而沉默的思索之中。

===Original===
The place was peaceful and quiet, as only a backwater of London can be. Occasional hoots from faraway tugs and steamers told of the busy life down below in the crowded Pool. A faint hum of traffic was borne in from the streets outside the precincts, and the shrill voices of newspaper boys came in unceasing chorus from the direction of Carmelite Street. They were too far away to be physically disturbing, but the excited yells, toned down as they were by distance, nevertheless stirred the very marrow in my bones, so dreadfully suggestive were they of those possibilities of the future at which Thorndyke had hinted. They seemed like the sinister shadows of coming misfortunes.

===Chinese===
这地方安谧宁静，唯有伦敦的一处世外静隅才能这样。远处拖轮与汽船不时传来的鸣笛，诉说着下方船只麇集的泊船河段里那一派忙碌生涯。一阵低微含糊的车马之声从界墙外的街市飘进来，报童尖利的嗓音又从卡迈利特街（Carmelite Street）的方向连声叠唱、不绝于耳。这些声音离得太远，还不足以真切地搅扰人心；然而那一片亢奋的叫喊，虽经距离磨钝了棱角，仍搅动了我的骨髓——它们令人毛骨悚然地联想到桑代克方才暗示过的那些可能的变故。它们像是将临灾祸投下的阴森暗影。

===Original===
Perhaps they called up the same association of ideas in Thorndyke’s mind, for he remarked presently:

===Chinese===
这些声音在桑代克心里也许唤起了同样的联想，因为过了一会儿他说道：

===Original===
“The newsvendor is abroad tonight like a bird of ill-omen. Something unusual has happened; some public or private calamity, most likely, and these yelling ghouls are out to feast on the remains. The newspaper men have a good deal in common with the carrion-birds that hover over a battlefield.”

===Chinese===
“卖报的今夜四出乱飞，活像不祥之鸟。出事了，出了不寻常的事；多半是什么公共或私人的灾祸，这些嚎叫的食尸鬼正出来大啖残骸。办报的人同战场上空盘旋的食腐之鸟，原有许多相通之处。”

===Original===
Again we subsided into silence and reflection. Then, after an interval, I asked:

===Chinese===
我们又堕入沉默与思索。隔了一阵，我问道：

===Original===
“Would it be possible for me to help in any way in this investigation of yours?”

===Chinese===
“你这桩查访，我能不能在什么地方帮上一手？”

===Original===
“That is exactly what I have been asking myself,” replied Thorndyke. “It would be right and proper that you should, and I think you might.”

===Chinese===
“我恰恰也一直在这样自问，”桑代克答道。“你来帮忙是合情合理的，而且我想你也帮得上。”

===Original===
“How?” I asked eagerly.

===Chinese===
“怎么帮？”我急切地问。

===Original===
“I can’t say offhand; but Jervis will be going away for his holiday almost at once﻿—in fact, he will go off actual duty tonight. There is very little doing; the long vacation is close upon us, and I can do without him. But if you would care to come down here and take his place, you would be very useful to me; and if there should be anything to be done in the Bellinghams’ case, I am sure you would make up in enthusiasm for any deficiency in experience.”

===Chinese===
“一时还说不死；不过杰维斯马上要去休假了——事实上，今晚他就交卸实务。眼下没什么案子；司法界的暑期大假转眼就到，没有他我也应付得来。可要是你愿意搬到这里来，顶替他的位置，你对我就大有用处；而且倘若贝林厄姆家的案子有什么要办的，我相信你热情有余，尽可弥补经验的不足。”

===Original===
“I couldn’t really take Jervis’s place,” said I, “but if you would let me help you in any way it would be a great kindness. I would rather clean your boots than be out of it altogether.”

===Chinese===
“杰维斯的位置我实在顶不了，”我说，“可要是你肯让我以任何方式帮你，那就是天大的恩惠了。我宁可替你擦靴子，也不愿整个儿置身事外。”

===Original===
“Very well. Let us leave it that you come here as soon as Barnard has done with you. You can have Jervis’s room, which he doesn’t often use nowadays, and you will be more happy here than elsewhere, I know. I may as well give you my latchkey now. I have a duplicate upstairs, and you understand that my chambers are yours too from this moment.”

===Chinese===
“很好。就这么说定：巴纳德那边一放你走，你就搬过来。你可以住杰维斯的房间，如今他不常用；而且我知道，你在这儿会比在别处快活。我不妨现在就把我的门锁钥匙给你。楼上还有一把备用的；你也明白，从这一刻起，我的寓所也就是你的了。”

===Original===
He handed me the latchkey and I thanked him warmly from my heart, for I felt sure that the suggestion was made, not for any use that I should be to him, but for my own peace of mind. I had hardly finished speaking when a quick step on the paved walk caught my ear.

===Chinese===
他把钥匙递给我，我发自心底地热忱道谢；因为我确信，他提这个建议，并不是看我于他有多大用处，而是为了让我心境安宁。我的话音刚落，铺石步道上一阵急促的脚步声便传入耳中。

===Original===
“Here is Jervis,” said Thorndyke. “We will let him know that there is a locum tenens ready to step into his shoes when he wants to be off.” He flashed the lantern across the path, and a few moments later his junior stepped up briskly with a bundle of newspapers tucked under his arm.

===Chinese===
“杰维斯来了，”桑代克说。“咱们正好让他知道，已有一位临时代理医生整装待命，等他想脱身时便来接他的班。”他把提灯朝小径那边一晃，片刻之后，他的这位副手便胳肢窝里夹着一捆报纸，快步走了上来。

===Original===
It struck me that Jervis looked at me a little queerly when he recognized me in the dim light; also he was a trifle constrained in his manner, as if my presence were an embarrassment. He listened to Thorndyke’s announcement of our newly made arrangement without much enthusiasm and with none of his customary facetious comments. And again I noticed a quick glance at me, half curious, half uneasy, and wholly puzzling to me.

===Chinese===
我察觉，杰维斯在昏暗的光线里认出我时，看我的神色有点异样；他的举止也略显拘束，仿佛我在场使他颇为尴尬。他听桑代克讲说我们新定的安排，不甚起劲，平日那些插科打诨的话一句也没有。我又一次注意到他向我飞快地瞥了一眼，那眼神半是好奇，半是不安，而全然叫我摸不着头脑。

===Original===
“That’s all right,” he said when Thorndyke had explained the situation. “I daresay you’ll find Berkeley as useful as me, and, in any case, he’ll be better here than staying on with Barnard.” He spoke with unwonted gravity, and there was in his tone a solicitude for me that attracted my notice and that of Thorndyke as well, for the latter looked at him curiously, though he made no comment. After a short silence, however, he asked: “And what news does my learned brother bring? There is a mighty shouting among the outer barbarians and I see a bundle of newspapers under my learned friend’s arm. Has anything in particular happened?”

===Chinese===
“那敢情好，”等桑代克说明情形之后，他说。“我料想你们会发现伯克利不亚于我这么有用；而且无论如何，他在这里总比在巴纳德那儿强。”他说得异乎寻常地郑重，语气里对我的一番关切，引起了我的注意，也引起了桑代克的注意，因为后者好奇地望着他，却没有作声。不过，沉默了片刻之后，他问道：“我这位博学的同仁带来了什么消息？外邦野人之间喊声震天，我又看见我博学的朋友臂下夹着一捆报纸。可是发生了什么特别的事么？”

===Original===
Jervis looked more uncomfortable than ever. “Well﻿—yes,” he replied hesitatingly, “something has happened﻿—there! It’s no use beating about the bush; Berkeley may as well learn it from me as from those yelling devils outside.” He took a couple of papers from his bundle and silently handed one to me and the other to Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
杰维斯的神色比先前更不自在了。“呃——是，”他迟疑着答道，“是出事了——唉！拐弯抹角也没用了；这事让伯克利从我嘴里听到，总比从外头那些嚎叫的魔鬼嘴里听到强。”他从那捆报纸里抽出两份，默默递给我一份，递给桑代克一份。

===Original===
Jervis’s ominous manner, naturally enough, alarmed me not a little. I opened the paper with a nameless dread. But whatever my vague fears, they fell far short of the occasion; and when I saw those yells from without crystallized into scare headlines and flaming capitals I turned for a moment sick and dizzy with fear.

===Chinese===
杰维斯这凶多吉少的神气，自然叫我不免惊心。我怀着一种说不出的恐惧打开报纸。然而无论我那些模糊的预感是什么，都远远够不上眼前这一场合；待到看见外面那些叫喊凝成了骇人的标题和火红的大字，我一时吓得头晕目眩，几乎作呕。

===Original===
The paragraph was only a short one, and I read it through in less than a minute.

===Chinese===
那段文字不长，不到一分钟我便读完了。

===Original===
> The mystery that has surrounded the remains of a mutilated human body, portions of which have been found in various places in Kent and Essex, has received a partial and very sinister solution. The police have, all along, suspected that those remains were those of a Mr. John Bellingham who disappeared under circumstances of some suspicion about two years ago. There is now no doubt upon the subject, for the finger which was missing from the hand that was found at Sidcup has been discovered at the bottom of a disused well *together with a ring*, which has been identified as one habitually worn by Mr. John Bellingham.
> The house in the garden of which the well is situated was the property of the murdered man, and was occupied at the time of the disappearance by his brother, Mr. Godfrey Bellingham. But the latter left it very soon after, and it has been empty ever since. Just lately it has been put in repair, and it was in this way that the well came to be emptied and cleaned out. It seems that Detective-Inspector Badger, who was searching the neighborhood for further remains, heard of the emptying of the well and went down in the bucket to examine the bottom, where he found the three bones and the ring.
> Thus the identity of the body is established beyond all doubt, and the question that remains is, Who killed John Bellingham? It may be remembered that a trinket, apparently broken from his watch-chain, was found in the grounds of this house on the day that he disappeared, and that he was never again seen alive. What may be the import of these facts time will show.

===Chinese===
> 那具残缺人体遗骸的一部分分别在肯特郡与埃塞克斯郡多处发现，围绕它们的谜团，如今得到了一个局部的、而且极为不祥的解答。警方一直疑心，那些遗骸便是约翰·贝林厄姆先生的——此人大约两年前在颇有几分可疑的情形中失踪。如今这一问题已无可置疑，因为锡德卡普发现的那只手上原先缺失的手指，已在一眼废井的井底寻获，*并且与一枚戒指同在*；该戒指经辨认证实为约翰·贝林厄姆先生素常佩戴之物。
> 井所在园子的那座宅子，属被害者所有，失踪案发生时由其胞弟戈弗雷·贝林厄姆先生租住。但后者此后不久即行迁出，房子从此空置。近来这宅子开工修缮，那口井也因此得以抽干淘净。据说侦探督察巴杰正在那一带搜寻更多遗骸，听闻淘井之事，便坐着吊桶下到井底查验，在那儿找到了那三根骨头与那枚戒指。
> 如此，尸身的身份已确凿无疑地认定；剩下的问题便是：是谁杀了约翰·贝林厄姆？人们当还记得，就在他失踪当天，这座宅子的园子里发现过一件小饰物，显系从他的表链上崩落；此后他在人间便再无踪影。这些事实究竟意味着什么，时间自会昭示。

===Original===
That was all; but it was enough. I dropped the paper to the ground and glanced round furtively at Jervis, who sat gazing gloomily at the toes of his boots. It was horrible! It was incredible! The blow was so crushing that it left my faculties numb, and for a while I seemed unable even to think intelligibly.

===Chinese===
报道至此为止；可是已经够了。我把报纸撂在地上，偷偷瞟了一眼杰维斯，他正坐着，郁郁地盯着自己的靴尖。可怕呀！这简直不可思议！这一击如此沉重，把我的心智都砸麻了，有一阵子我仿佛连明白地思考都不能了。

===Original===
I was aroused by Thorndyke’s voice﻿—calm, businesslike, composed:

===Chinese===
唤醒我的是桑代克的声音——平静、利落、沉着：

===Original===
“Time will show, indeed! But meanwhile we must go warily. And don’t be unduly alarmed, Berkeley. Go home, take a good dose of bromide with a little stimulant, and turn in. I am afraid this has been rather a shock to you.”

===Chinese===
“时间自会昭示，一点不假！不过眼下咱们务必谨慎行事。还有，不要慌得失了分寸，伯克利。回家去，服一剂足量的溴化物，兑一点提神的酒，然后睡下。我怕这对你不啻是一记闷棍。”

===Original===
I rose from my chair like one in a dream and held out my hand to Thorndyke; and even in the dim light and in my dazed condition I noticed that his face bore a look that I had never seen before; the look of a granite mask of Fate﻿—grim, stern, inexorable.

===Chinese===
我如入梦境般从椅子上站起来，向桑代克伸出手去；即便在昏暗的光线里、在我神思恍惚之中，我也注意到他脸上带着一种我从未见过的神情；那是命运的花岗岩面具——冷酷、严峻、决不容情。

===Original===
My two friends walked with me as far as the gateway at the top of Inner Temple Lane, and as we reached the entry a stranger, coming quickly up the Lane, overtook and passed us. In the glare of the lamp outside the porter’s lodge he looked at us quickly over his shoulder, and though he passed on without halt or greeting, I recognized him with a certain dull surprise which I did not understand then and do not understand now. It was Mr. Jellicoe.

===Chinese===
两位朋友陪我走到内殿巷（Inner Temple Lane）上端的巷门口，我们刚到门洞，一个陌生人正沿着巷子快步走来，赶过并越过了我们。在门房外那盏灯的强光里，他回头迅速地看了我们一眼；他虽未停步、也未打招呼便走了过去，我却认出了他，心里泛起一种我当时不明白、如今也不明白的迟钝的惊讶。那是杰利科先生。

===Original===
I shook hands once more with my friends and strode out into Fleet Street, but as soon as I was outside the gate I made direct for Nevill’s Court. What was in my mind I do not know; only that some instinct of protection led me there, where my lady lay unconscious of the hideous menace that hung over her. At the entrance to the Court a tall, powerful man was lounging against the wall, and he seemed to look at me curiously as I passed; but I hardly noticed him and strode forward into the narrow passage. By the shabby gateway of the house I halted and looked up at such of the windows as I could see over the wall. They were all dark. All the inmates, then, were in bed. Vaguely comforted by this, I walked on to the New Street end of the Court and looked out. Here, too, a man﻿—a tall, thickset man﻿—was loitering; and as he looked inquisitively into my face I turned and reentered the Court, slowly retracing my steps. As I again reached the gate of the house I stopped to look once more at the windows, and turning I found the man whom I had last noticed close behind me. Then, in a flash of dreadful comprehension, I understood. These two were plainclothes policemen.

===Chinese===
我又同两位朋友握了握手，大步走进舰队街；可一出大门，我便径直奔内维尔巷去。我心里想的是什么，我自己也不知道；只知道某种保护的本能把我引到那里——我的小姐正躺在那里，对悬在她头顶的那可怕威胁一无所知。在巷口，一个高大壮实的男人正倚墙闲立，我经过时他似乎好奇地打量我；但我几乎没有留意他，只顾迈步走进那条窄巷。在那宅子破败的门前，我停住脚，仰头张望高墙内看得见的几扇窗。窗子全都黑着。这么说，宅里的人都已就寝。这隐约使我得到一点安慰；我又走到巷子通新街（New Street）的那一头，向外望去。这里也有一个男人——一个高大敦实的男人——在徘徊游荡；当他探究地细看我的脸时，我便转身重新进入巷中，缓缓往回走。当我再次走到那宅子的门前时，我又停下来想再看一眼窗户，一回头，却发现我刚才留意到的那个男人紧跟在我身后。于是，在一阵可怕的恍然之中，我明白了。这两个人是便衣警察。

===Original===
For a moment a blind fury possessed me. An insane impulse urged me to give battle to this intruder; to avenge upon this person the insult of his presence. Fortunately the impulse was but momentary, and I recovered myself without making any demonstration. But the appearance of those two policemen brought the peril into the immediate present, imparted to it a horrible actuality. A chilly sweat of terror stood on my forehead, and my ears were ringing when I walked with faltering steps out into Fetter Lane.

===Chinese===
一时之间，一股盲目的狂怒攫住了我。一种疯狂的冲动怂恿我向这个闯来的人开战；在这家伙身上，为他人到此出现的侮辱复仇。幸而这冲动只是转瞬即逝，我未及有任何举动便自持住了。可是那两个警察的出现，把危险径直拉到了眼前，赋予它一种可怕的实在。一阵恐怖的冷汗沁出我的额头，两耳嗡嗡作响，我脚步踉跄地走出巷口，到了菲特巷。

## XVIII John Bellingham / 十八 约翰·贝林厄姆
===Original===
The next few days were a very nightmare of horror and gloom. Of course, I repudiated my acceptance of the decree of banishment that Ruth had passed upon me. I was her friend, at least, and in time of peril my place was at her side. Tacitly﻿—though thankfully enough, poor girl!﻿—she had recognized the fact and made me once more free of the house.

===Chinese===
此后那几天，简直是一场恐怖与阴郁织成的噩梦。当然，露丝对我下了逐客令，而我当初的俯首认命，如今一概不作数了。至少，我是她的朋友，危难当头，我的位置就该在她身边。她默认了这一事实——也真是谢天谢地，可怜的姑娘！——于是重新让我在这座宅子里自由出入。

===Original===
For there was no disguising the situation. Newspaper boys yelled the news up and down Fleet Street from morning to night; soul-shaking posters grinned on gaping crowds; and the newspapers fairly wallowed in the “Shocking details.”

===Chinese===
局势已无从掩饰。报童们从早到晚在舰队街上来回叫卖这条新闻；撼人心魄的海报对着张口呆望的人群龇牙而笑；各家报章更是撒开了欢，在那些“骇人听闻的细节”里打滚。

===Original===
It is true that no direct accusations were made; but the original reports of the disappearance were reprinted with such comments as made me gnash my teeth with fury.

===Chinese===
诚然，报上并没有指名道姓的直接指控；但当初关于失踪案的报道被重新刊出，配上那样的评论，直气得我咬牙切齿。

===Original===
The wretchedness of those days will live in my memory until my dying day. Never can I forget the dread that weighed me down, the horrible suspense, the fear that clutched at my heart as I furtively scanned the posters in the streets. Even the wretched detectives who prowled about the entrances to Nevill’s Court became grateful to my eyes, for, embodying as they did the hideous menace that hung over my dear lady, their presence at least told me that the blow had not yet fallen. Indeed, we came, after a time, to exchange glances of mutual recognition, and I thought that they seemed to be sorry for her and for me, and had no great liking for their task. Of course, I spent most of my leisure at the old house, though my heart ached more there than elsewhere; and I tried, with but poor success, I fear, to maintain a cheerful, confident manner, cracking my little jokes as of old, and even essaying to skirmish with Miss Oman. But this last experiment was a dead failure; and when she had suddenly broken down in a stream of brilliant repartee to weep hysterically on my breast, I abandoned the attempt and did not repeat it.

===Chinese===
那些日子的凄惶，将留在我记忆里，直到我咽气的那一天。我永远忘不了压得我直不起腰的恐惧，忘不了那可怕的悬望，忘不了我在街头偷觑海报时攫住我心的惊惶。甚至连那些在内维尔巷各个入口逡巡窥伺的倒霉侦探，也渐渐看得顺眼了；因为他们虽然体现着悬在我心上人头顶的狰狞威胁，但他们还在那里，至少说明那一击尚未落下。真的，过了一些时候，我们竟互相交换起心照不宣的眼色来；我觉得他们似乎也在为她、为我难过，对自己这份差事也没多大兴致。自然，我的闲暇大半都消磨在那所老宅里，虽然我在那里比在别处更加心痛；我竭力维持一副欢快自信的神气，照旧说些小小的笑话，甚至还想同奥曼小姐斗斗嘴皮。只是我恐怕收效甚微。这最后一项试验更是一败涂地——她正妙语连珠地斗嘴斗得起劲，忽然一下子撑不住，伏在我胸口歇斯底里地痛哭起来；我从此死了这条心，再不重演。

===Original===
A dreadful gloom had settled down upon the old house. Poor Miss Oman crept silently but restlessly up and down the ancient stairs with dim eyes and a tremulous chin, or moped in her room with a parliamentary petition (demanding, if I remember rightly, the appointment of a female judge to deal with divorce and matrimonial causes) which lay on her table languidly awaiting signatures that never came. Mr. Bellingham, whose mental condition at first alternated between furious anger and absolute panic, was fast sinking into a state of nervous prostration that I viewed with no little alarm. In fact the only really self-possessed person in the entire household was Ruth herself, and even she could not conceal the ravages of sorrow and suspense and overshadowing peril. Her manner was almost unchanged; or rather, I should say, she had gone back to that which I had first known﻿—quiet, reserved, taciturn, with a certain bitter humor showing through her unvarying amiability. When she and I were alone, indeed, her reserve melted away and she was all sweetness and gentleness. But it wrung my heart to look at her, to see how, day by day, she grew ever more thin and haggard; to watch the growing pallor of her cheek; to look into her solemn gray eyes, so sad and tragic and yet so brave and defiant of fate.

===Chinese===
一层可怕的阴霾罩住了这所老宅。可怜的奥曼小姐眼色黯淡、下巴发颤，默不作声却又心神不宁地在古旧的楼梯间走上走下；再不就闷在自己房里，守着桌上那份议会请愿书（如果我记得不错，是要求任命一位女法官审理离婚与婚姻诉讼的）无精打采地等着永远不来的签名。贝林厄姆先生呢，起初心境还在狂怒与极度惊惶之间来回打摆子，如今正迅速陷入一种神经衰竭的状态，我看了忧惧不已。事实上，全家上下唯一真正镇定自若的，只有露丝自己；可就连她，也掩不住悲伤、悬望和迫近的危难留下的摧残。她的举止几乎没有改变；或者不如说，她退回到了我初识她时的模样——安静、矜持、寡言，在一成不变的温婉之下透出一丝苦涩的幽默。的确，只有她和我两人单独相处时，她的矜持才消融了，满心都是甜美温柔。可是看着她，我的心便如刀绞：眼看她一天天消瘦憔悴；看着她面颊上日甚一日的苍白；望进她那双庄重的灰眼睛——那么哀伤、那么悲怆，却又那么勇敢、那么不向命运低头。

===Original===
It was a terrible time; and through it all the dreadful questions haunted me continually: When will the blow fall? What is it that the police are waiting for? And when they do strike, what will Thorndyke have to say?

===Chinese===
那是一段可怕的时光；自始至终，几个可怕的问题不住地缠着我：那一击何时落下？警方在等什么？待到他们当真出手时，桑代克又将有何话说？

===Original===
So things went on for four dreadful days. But on the fourth day, just as the evening consultations were beginning and the surgery was filled with waiting patients, Polton appeared with a note, which he insisted, to the indignation of Adolphus, on delivering into my own hands. It was from Thorndyke, and was to the following effect:

===Chinese===
就这样熬过了可怕的四天。到第四天傍晚，晚间应诊刚刚开始，诊室里坐满了候诊的病人，波尔顿拿着一张便条来了；他不顾阿道弗斯的愤慨，坚持要亲手交到我本人手里。那是桑代克写来的，大意如下：

===Original===
> I learn from Dr. Norbury that he has recently heard from Herr Lederbogen, of Berlin﻿—a learned authority on Oriental antiquities﻿—who makes some reference to an English Egyptologist whom he met in Vienna about a year ago. He cannot recall the Englishman’s name, but there are certain expressions in the letter which make Dr. Norbury suspect that he is referring to John Bellingham.
> I want you to bring Mr. and Miss Bellingham to my chambers this evening at 8:30, to meet Dr. Norbury and talk over his letter; and in view of the importance of the matter, I look to you not to fail me.

===Chinese===
> 我从诺伯里博士处得知，他近日接到柏林的莱德博根先生（Herr Lederbogen）——一位东方古物的权威学者——来信，信中提到一位约一年前他在维也纳结识的英国埃及学家。他想不起那位英国人的名字，但信中有某些措辞，使诺伯里博士疑心所指正是约翰·贝林厄姆。
> 我要你今晚8时30分带贝林厄姆先生和贝林厄姆小姐到我的寓所来，会见诺伯里博士并商谈那封信；此事关系重大，望你切勿误我。

===Original===
A wave of hope and relief swept over me. It was still possible that this Gordian knot might be cut; that the deliverance might come before it was too late. I wrote a hasty note to Thorndyke and another to Ruth, making the appointment; and having given them both to the trusty Polton, returned somewhat feverishly to my professional duties. To my profound relief, the influx of patients ceased, and the practise sank into its accustomed torpor; whereby I was able without base and mendacious subterfuge to escape in good time to my tryst.

===Chinese===
一阵希望与宽慰的浪潮涌遍我全身。这个戈耳迪之结仍有希望快刀斩断；解救或许还来得及，不至于太迟。我匆匆给桑代克和露丝各写一张便条约定时间，把两张条子都托付给可靠的波尔顿，便带着几分亢奋回到诊务上去。令我深感宽慰的是，病人潮退了，诊所恢复了惯常的委顿；我于是不必使出卑劣而虚诳的托词，便得以及时脱身赴约。

===Original===
It was near upon eight o’clock when I passed through the archway into Nevill’s Court. The warm afternoon light had died away, for the summer was running out apace. The last red glow of the setting sun had faded from the ancient roofs and chimney stacks, and down in the narrow court the shades of evening had begun to gather in nooks and corners. I was due at eight, and, as it still wanted some minutes to the hour, I sauntered slowly down the court, looking reflectively on the familiar scene and the well-known friendly faces.

===Chinese===
将近八点钟，我穿过拱门走进内维尔巷。午后温暖的日光已经消歇，因为夏天正匆匆奔向尽头。落日最后的红光已从古老的屋顶和高高低低的烟囱上褪尽，窄巷深处，暮色开始在各个角落里聚拢。我约定的是八点，离那时辰还差几分钟，我便沿着巷子缓缓踱去，若有所思地望着这熟悉的景致和那些熟识的亲切面孔。

===Original===
The day’s work was drawing to a close. The little shops were putting up their shutters; lights were beginning to twinkle in parlor windows; a solemn hymn arose in the old Moravian chapel, and its echoes stole out through the dark entry that opens into the court under the archway.

===Chinese===
一天的营生正接近尾声。小店家纷纷上着门板；客厅的窗里开始眨起点点灯火；古老的摩拉维亚礼拜堂里响起一支庄重的圣诗，余音悄悄渗出黑洞洞的门道——那门道就在拱门底下，通着这条巷子。

===Original===
Here was Mr. Finneymore (a man of versatile gifts, with a leaning toward paint and varnish) sitting, white-aproned and shirt-sleeved, on a chair in his garden, smoking his pipe with a complacent eye on his dahlias. There at an open window a young man, with a brush in his hand and another behind his ear, stood up and stretched himself while an older lady deftly rolled up a large map. The barber was turning out the gas in his little saloon; the greengrocer was emerging with a cigarette in his mouth and an aster in his buttonhole, and a group of children were escorting the lamplighter on his rounds.

===Chinese===
这边是芬尼莫尔先生（Finneymore，一位多才多艺的人物，偏爱油漆上光的行当）系着白围裙、挽着衬衫袖子，坐在自家花园的椅子上，叼着烟斗，心满意足地端详他的大丽花。那边一扇敞开的窗前，一个小伙子手里一支画笔、耳后还别着一支，站起来伸了个懒腰；一位年长的妇人正麻利地卷起一大幅地图。理发师在他小小的店堂里关掉煤气灯；蔬菜水果店老板叼着烟卷、纽孔里插一枝紫菀走了出来；一群孩子正跟着点灯人一路巡行。

===Original===
All these good, homely folk were Nevill’s Courtiers of the genuine breed; born in the court, as had been their fathers before them for generations. And of such to a great extent was the population of the place. Miss Oman herself claimed aboriginal descent and so did the sweet-faced Moravian lady next door﻿—a connection of the famous La Trobes of the old Conventicle, whose history went back to the Gordon Riots; and as to the gentleman who lived in the ancient timber-and-plaster house at the bottom of the court, it was reported that his ancestors had dwelt in that very house since the days of James the First.

===Chinese===
这些善良淳朴的人，都是血统纯正的内维尔巷“朝臣”；他们生在这巷子里，他们的父祖也一样，世代如此。这地方的人烟，大半便是这般出身。奥曼小姐自己便自称巷中土著的后代，隔壁那位面目慈祥的摩拉维亚太太也是如此——她是旧日非国教聚会所那位有名的拉特罗布家族（La Trobes）的亲戚，家史可以上溯到戈登暴乱年代；至于住在巷底那所古旧木骨泥墙宅子里的先生，据说他的祖先自詹姆斯一世的时代起，就一直住在那所宅子里。

===Original===
On these facts I reflected as I sauntered down the court, on the strange phenomenon of an old-world hamlet with its ancient population lingering in the very heart of the noisy city; an island of peace set in an ocean of unrest, an oasis in a desert of change and ferment.

===Chinese===
我一路踱下巷去，一面想着这些，想着这桩奇观：一个旧世界的小村落，连同它古老的居民，竟滞留在喧嚣闹市的正中心；那是一片动荡之海中的安宁孤岛，是变化与骚动之荒漠里的一片绿洲。

===Original===
My meditations brought me to the shabby gate in the high wall, and as I raised the latch and pushed it open, I saw Ruth standing at the door of the house talking to Miss Oman. She was evidently waiting for me, for she wore her somber black coat and hat and a black veil, and when she saw me she came out, closing the door after her, and holding out her hand.

===Chinese===
这一番冥想把我带到了高墙下那扇破旧的门前；我掀起门闩推门进去，看见露丝正站在宅门口和奥曼小姐说话。她显然是在等我，因为她已穿上那件暗色的黑外套，戴好帽子和黑色面纱；她一见我，便随手带上门走出来，向我伸出手。

===Original===
“You are punctual,” said she. “St. Dunstan’s clock is striking now.”

===Chinese===
“你倒守时，”她说。“圣邓斯坦教堂（St. Dunstan's）的钟此刻正敲着呢。”

===Original===
“Yes,” I answered. “But where is your father?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我答道。“可你父亲呢？”

===Original===
“He has gone to bed, poor old dear. He didn’t feel well enough to come, and I did not urge him. He is really very ill. This dreadful suspense will kill him if it goes on much longer.”

===Chinese===
“他睡下了，可怜的老爸爸。他觉得身子撑不住，没有来，我也没有劝他。他实在病得很重。这可怕的悬望再拖下去，会要了他的命的。”

===Original===
“Let us hope it won’t,” I said, but with little conviction, I fear, in my tone.

===Chinese===
“但愿不至于，”我说；只是我话音里恐怕没什么底气。

===Original===
It was harrowing to see her torn by anxiety for her father, and I yearned to comfort her. But what was there to say? Mr. Bellingham was breaking up visibly under the stress of the terrible menace that hung over his daughter, and no words of mine could make the fact less manifest.

===Chinese===
眼看她为父亲忧心如焚，实在令人心痛；我渴望安慰她。可又有什么可说的呢？贝林厄姆先生在悬于女儿头顶的可怕威胁的重压之下，眼见着一天天垮下去，我说什么话，也不能叫这个事实减轻分毫。

===Original===
We walked silently up the court. The lady at the window greeted us with a smiling salutation, Mr. Finneymore removed his pipe and raised his cap, receiving a gracious bow from Ruth in return, and then we passed through the covered way into Fetter Lane, where my companion paused and looked about her.

===Chinese===
我们默默走上巷去。窗前那位太太笑吟吟地向我们招呼致意，芬尼莫尔先生取下烟斗、举了举帽，露丝雍容地欠身还礼；随后我们穿过带顶的过道进入菲特巷，我的同伴在这里停住脚，四下张望。

===Original===
“What are you looking for?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“你在找什么？”我问。

===Original===
“The detective,” she answered quietly. “It would be a pity if the poor man should miss me after waiting so long. However, I don’t see him.” And she turned away toward Fleet Street. It was an unpleasant surprise to me that her sharp eyes detected the secret spy upon her movements; and the dry, sardonic tone of her remark pained me too, recalling, as it did, the frigid self-possession that had so repelled me in the early days of our acquaintance. And yet I could not but admire the cool unconcern with which she faced her horrible peril.

===Chinese===
“找那位侦探，”她平静地答道。“这可怜的人等了这么久，要是把我跟丢了，岂不可惜。不过，我没瞧见他。”说着，她转身朝舰队街走去。她那双锐利的眼睛竟察觉了暗中监视她行动的人，这使我吃了一惊，心里很不是滋味；她那句干巴巴的讥诮也刺痛了我，因为它令我想起初识时那般令我生畏的冷漠自持。然而她面对可怕危难时的那份镇定淡然，又不能不令我叹服。

===Original===
“Tell me a little more about this conference,” she said, as we walked down Fetter Lane. “Your note was rather more concise than lucid; but I suppose you wrote it in a hurry.”

===Chinese===
“再跟我讲讲这次会面吧，”我们沿菲特巷走去时她说。“你那张便条未免简而不明；不过我想你是匆匆写就的。”

===Original===
“Yes, I did. And I can’t give you any details now. All I know is that Doctor Norbury has had a letter from a friend of his in Berlin, an Egyptologist, as I understand, named Lederbogen, who refers to an English acquaintance of his and Norbury’s whom he saw in Vienna about a year ago. He cannot remember the Englishman’s name, but from some of the circumstances Norbury seems to think that he is referring to your Uncle John. Of course, if this should turn out to be really the case, it would set everything straight; so Thorndyke was anxious that you and your father should meet Norbury and talk it over.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，是匆匆写的。眼下我也说不出什么细节。我只知道诺伯里医生接到他柏林一位朋友的来信，据我理解是位埃及学家，名叫莱德博根，信中提到一位他和诺伯里都认识的英国朋友，说是约一年前在维也纳见过他。他想不起那位英国人的名字，但从某些情形看，诺伯里似乎认为他指的就是你伯父约翰。当然，倘若果真如此，一切便都迎刃而解了；所以桑代克急欲让你和你父亲见见诺伯里，当面商谈。”

===Original===
“I see,” said Ruth. Her tone was thoughtful but by no means enthusiastic.

===Chinese===
“我明白了，”露丝说。她语气若有所思，却谈不上热切。

===Original===
“You don’t seem to attach much importance to the matter,” I remarked.

===Chinese===
“你好像并不把这件事放在心上，”我说。

===Original===
“No. It doesn’t seem to fit the circumstances. What is the use of suggesting that poor Uncle John is alive﻿—and behaving like an imbecile, which he certainly was not﻿—when his dead body has actually been found?”

===Chinese===
“是谈不上。这事与眼前的情况对不上。他的尸身都已经找到了，再来暗示可怜的伯父约翰还活着——而且行事像个白痴，他分明不是那样的人——又有什么用呢？”

===Original===
“But,” I suggested lamely, “there may be some mistake. It may not be his body after all.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”我讪讪地说，“说不定出了什么差错。那具尸体也许终究不是他的。”

===Original===
“And the ring?” she asked, with a bitter smile.

===Chinese===
“那么戒指呢？”她问，带着一丝苦笑。

===Original===
“That may be just a coincidence. It was a copy of a well-known form of antique ring. Other people may have had copies made as well as your uncle. Besides,” I added with more conviction, “we haven’t seen the ring. It may not be his at all.”

===Chinese===
“那或许只是巧合。那是仿的一种有名的古董戒指，别人也能像你伯父一样订做仿品。再说，”我添上一句，这回多了几分底气，“咱们并没有亲眼见过那枚戒指。它也许根本不是他的。”

===Original===
She shook her head. “My dear Paul,” she said quietly, “it is useless to delude ourselves. Every known fact points to the certainty that it is his body. John Bellingham is dead: there can be no doubt of that. And to everyone except his unknown murderer and one or two of my own loyal friends, it must seem that his death lies at my door. I realized from the beginning that the suspicion lay between George Hurst and me; and the finding of the ring fixes it definitely on me. I am only surprised that the police have made no move yet.”

===Chinese===
她摇了摇头。“我亲爱的保罗，”她平静地说，“自己骗自己是没用的。每一件已知的事实都指明，那就是他的尸体。约翰·贝林厄姆死了：这一点无可怀疑。在除那不知名的凶手和我自己一两位忠实朋友之外的所有人看来，他的死必定要算在我头上。我从一开始就明白，嫌疑不出乔治·赫斯特和我两人之间；戒指一找到，嫌疑便落定在我身上了。我只是奇怪，警方至今还没有动手。

===Original===
The quiet conviction of her tone left me for a while speechless with horror and despair. Then I recalled Thorndyke’s calm, even confident, attitude, and I hastened to remind her of it.

===Chinese===
她语气里那份沉静的确信，让我一时惊骇绝望得说不出话来。随后我想起桑代克那镇定、甚至可说自信的态度，便连忙向她提起。

===Original===
“There is one of your friends,” I said, “who is still undismayed. Thorndyke seems to anticipate no difficulties.”

===Chinese===
“你有一位朋友，”我说，“至今毫不气馁。桑代克好像预料不会有什么难关。”

===Original===
“And yet,” she replied, “he is ready to consider a forlorn hope like this. However, we shall see.”

===Chinese===
“然而，”她答道，“连这样一线渺茫的希望他也肯认真考虑。且看下文吧。”

===Original===
I could think of nothing more to say, and it was in gloomy silence that we pursued our way down Inner Temple Lane and through the dark entries and tunnel-like passages that brought us out, at length, by the Treasury.

===Chinese===
我再想不出别的话可说，便在阴郁的沉默中一路走去，下内殿巷，穿过黑洞洞的门道和隧道似的走廊，最后从财务楼旁边走了出来。

===Original===
“I don’t see any light in Thorndyke’s chambers,” I said, as we crossed King’s Bench Walk; and I pointed out the row of windows all dark and blank.

===Chinese===
“桑代克寓所里看不见灯光，”我们穿过王座步道时我说；我指给她看那一排黑沉沉、暗漠漠的窗户。

===Original===
“No; and yet the shutters are not closed. He must be out.”

===Chinese===
“是没有；可百叶窗还没关上。他准是出去了。”

===Original===
“He can’t be after making an appointment with you and your father. It is most mysterious. Thorndyke is so very punctilious about his engagements.”

===Chinese===
“约了你们父女俩，他不可能出去。这事真是蹊跷透了。桑代克对自己的约会向来一丝不苟。”

===Original===
The mystery was solved, when we reached the landing, by a slip of paper fixed by a tack on the iron-bound “oak.”

===Chinese===
到了楼梯口，谜底揭晓了：那张铁皮包镶的“橡木门”上，用图钉钉着一张纸条。

===Original===
“A note for P. B. is on the table,” was the laconic message: on reading which I inserted my key, swung the heavy door outward, and opened the lighter inner door. The note was lying on the table and I brought it out to the landing to read by the light of the staircase lamp.

===Chinese===
“给P. B.的便条在桌上，”留言简短如此；我读罢便插入钥匙，把沉重的门外推开，又打开较轻的内门。便条果然在桌上，我拿到楼梯口，就着楼梯灯的光读了起来。

===Original===
> “Apologize to our friends,” it ran, “for the slight change of programme. Norbury is anxious that I should get my experiments over before the Director returns, so as to save discussion. He has asked me to begin tonight and says he will see Mr. and Miss Bellingham here, at the Museum. Please bring them along at once. I think some matters of importance may transpire at the interview﻿—J. E. T.”

===Chinese===
> “谨向我们的朋友致歉，”便条上写道，“因计划略有变更。诺伯里急欲我趁馆长回来之前把实验做完，省得一番讨论。他嘱我今晚就开始，并说他将在博物馆这里会见贝林厄姆先生和小姐。请即刻带他们前来。我想这次会面或有要事发生——J. E. T.”

===Original===
“I hope you don’t mind,” I said apologetically, when I had read the note to Ruth.

===Chinese===
我把便条念给露丝听过之后，抱歉地说：“希望你不见怪。”

===Original===
“Of course I don’t,” she replied. “I am rather pleased. We have so many associations with the dear old Museum, haven’t we?” She looked at me for a moment with a strange and touching wistfulness and then turned to descend the stone stairs.

===Chinese===
“当然不怪，”她答道。“我倒挺高兴。那座亲爱的老博物馆，跟咱们有多少旧情分啊，是不是？”她带着一种奇特而动人的怅惘望了我片刻，便转身走下石阶。

===Original===
At the Temple gate I hailed a hansom, and we were soon speeding westward and north to the soft twinkle of the horse’s bell.

===Chinese===
在坦普尔大门口我唤了一辆双轮马车，不多时我们便向西再折北疾驰而去，一路伴着马铃柔和的叮当声。

===Original===
“What are these experiments that Doctor Thorndyke refers to?” she asked presently.

===Chinese===
“桑代克医生提起的那些实验是怎么回事？”过了一会儿她问。

===Original===
“I can only answer you vaguely,” I replied. “Their object, I believe, is to ascertain whether the penetrability of organic substances by the X-rays becomes altered by age; whether, for instance, an ancient block of wood is more or less transparent to the rays than a new block of the same size.”

===Chinese===
“我也只能说个大概，”我答道。“据我理解，实验的目的是要查明：有机物质对X射线的可透性，是否会随年代而改变；譬如说，一块古木头对射线的透明程度，比起同样大小的新木头来，究竟是更高还是更低。”

===Original===
“And of what use would the knowledge be, if it were obtained?”

===Chinese===
“这知识即便到手了，又有什么用处呢？”

===Original===
“I can’t say. Experiments are made to obtain knowledge without regard to its utility. The use appears when the knowledge has been acquired. But in this case, if it should be possible to determine the age of any organic substance by its reaction to X-rays, the discovery might be found of some value in legal practise﻿—as in demonstrating a new seal on an old document, for instance. But I don’t know whether Thorndyke has anything definite in view; I only know that the preparations have been on a most portentous scale.”

===Chinese===
“这我说不好。做实验是为了求得知识，并不计较它有什么用处；用处要等知识到手之后才会显露出来。不过就眼下这件事而言，倘若真能凭有机物对X射线的反应测出它的年代，这一发现在法律实务上或许不无价值——譬如，可用以证明旧文书上新盖的印。但桑代克心中是否已有确定的目标，我不知道；我只知道，那些筹备工作的规模实在大得吓人。”

===Original===
“How do you mean?”

===Chinese===
“怎么讲？”

===Original===
“In regard to size. When I went into the workshop yesterday morning, I found Polton erecting a kind of portable gallows about nine feet high, and he had just finished varnishing a pair of enormous wooden trays each over six feet long. It looked as if he and Thorndyke were contemplating a few private executions with subsequent postmortems on the victims.”

===Chinese===
“就规模而言。昨天早晨我走进工作间，看见波尔顿正在竖一台便携式的绞架，约有九英尺高；他刚给一对巨大的木槽上完清漆，每只都有六英尺多长。那情形，仿佛他和桑代克正打算私行几场绞刑，还要给受刑者验尸似的。”

===Original===
“What a horrible suggestion!”

===Chinese===
“这念头多可怕！”

===Original===
“So Polton said, with his quaint, crinkly smile. But he was mighty close about the use of the apparatus all the same. I wonder if we shall see anything of the experiments, when we get there. This is Museum Street, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“波尔顿也这么说，脸上带着他那种古里古气的皱笑。不过说起那套装置的用途，他嘴可紧得很。不知到了那儿，咱们能不能见识见识那些实验。这是博物馆街（Museum Street）了，是不是？”

===Original===
“Yes.” As she spoke, she lifted the flap of one of the little windows in the back of the cab and peered out. Then, closing it with a quiet, ironic smile, she said:

===Chinese===
“是。”她一面说，一面掀起车厢后壁一个小窗的挡板朝外窥看。然后她放下挡板，带着一丝沉静而讥诮的微笑，说道：

===Original===
“It is all right; he hasn’t missed us. It will be quite a nice little change for him.”

===Chinese===
“没事；他没有把咱们跟丢。对他来说，这番小小的改换路线，想必别有风味。”

===Original===
The cab swung round into Great Russell Street, and, glancing out as it turned, I saw another hansom following; but before I had time to inspect its solitary passenger, we drew up at the Museum gates.

===Chinese===
马车一拐弯上了大拉塞尔街（Great Russell Street）；转弯时我往外一瞥，看见后面跟着另一辆双轮马车；还不等我看清那车上孤单的乘客，我们已在博物馆大门前停住。

===Original===
The gate porter, who seemed to expect us, ushered us up the drive to the great portico and into the Central Hall, where he handed us over to another official.

===Chinese===
门房看来早等着我们，引我们沿车道走上宏大的门廊，进了中央大厅，把我们交托给另一位馆员。

===Original===
“Doctor Norbury is in one of the rooms adjoining the Fourth Egyptian Room,” the latter stated in answer to our inquiries: and, providing himself with a wire-guarded lantern, he prepared to escort us thither.

===Chinese===
“诺伯里博士在埃及第四陈列室（Fourth Egyptian Room）隔壁的一间屋子里，”后者回答我们的问询说；他取来一盏罩着铁丝网的提灯，准备领我们前去。

===Original===
Up the great staircase, now wrapped in mysterious gloom, we passed in silence with bittersweet memories of that day of days when we had first trodden its steps together; through the Central Saloon, the Medieval Room and the Asiatic Saloon, and so into the long range of the Ethnographical Galleries.

===Chinese===
我们默默走上大楼梯——它此刻笼罩在神秘的幽暗里——心中怀着苦乐参半的回忆，想起当初我们第一次一同踏过这些台阶的那个难忘的日子；穿过中央陈列厅、中世纪室和亚洲厅，便进入了民族学陈列廊长长的廊道。

===Original===
It was a weird journey. The swaying lantern shot its beams abroad into the darkness of the great, dim galleries, casting instantaneous flashes on the objects in the cases, so that they leaped into being and vanished in the twinkling of an eye. Hideous idols with round, staring eyes started forth from the darkness, glared at us for an instant and were gone. Grotesque masks, suddenly revealed by the shimmering light, took on the semblance of demon faces that seemed to mow and gibber at us as we passed. As for the life-sized models﻿—realistic enough by daylight﻿—their aspect was positively alarming; for the moving light and shadow endowed them with life and movement, so that they seemed to watch us furtively, to lie in wait and to hold themselves in readiness to steal out and follow us. The illusion evidently affected Ruth as well as me, for she drew nearer to me and whispered:

===Chinese===
那是一段诡异的旅程。摇晃的提灯把光柱射进阴暗的大陈列廊深处的黑暗里，瞬息间照亮橱窗中的陈列品，教它们倏然现形，又转眼即逝。狰狞的神像瞪着一双圆眼从黑暗里跳将出来，对我们怒视一瞬，随即消隐。怪诞的面具被闪烁的灯光骤然照亮，化作一张张鬼脸，仿佛在我们经过时挤眉弄眼、喋喋怪笑。至于那些真人大小的人像模型——白日里看已经够逼真了——此刻的模样简直骇人；因为移动的光与影赋予了它们生命和动作，教人觉得它们正在暗中窥伺我们，正埋伏待机，正蓄势欲出，要悄悄溜出来尾随我们。这幻觉显然也感染了露丝，因为她向我偎近了些，低声道：

===Original===
“These figures are quite startling. Did you see that Polynesian? I really felt as if he were going to spring out on us.”

===Chinese===
“这些人像真吓人。你看见那个波利尼西亚人了吗？我真觉得他就要朝咱们扑出来了。”

===Original===
“They are rather uncanny,” I admitted, “but the danger is over now. We are passing out of their sphere of influence.”

===Chinese===
“是有点邪门，”我承认道，“不过眼下危险已经过去了。咱们正在走出它们的势力范围。”

===Original===
We came out on a landing as I spoke and then turned sharply to the left along the North Gallery, from the center of which we entered the Fourth Egyptian Room.

===Chinese===
说话间我们来到一处平台，随即向左急转，沿北廊走去，从北廊中段进了埃及第四陈列室。

===Original===
Almost immediately, a door in the opposite wall opened; a peculiar, high-pitched humming sound became audible, and Jervis came out on tiptoe with his hand raised.

===Chinese===
几乎立刻，对面墙上一扇门开了；一阵尖细的古怪嗡鸣隐约可闻，杰维斯踮着脚走出来，一手举起示意。

===Original===
“Tread as lightly as you can,” he said. “We are just making an exposure.”

===Chinese===
“脚步越轻越好，”他说。“我们正在曝光。”

===Original===
The attendant turned back with his lantern, and we followed Jervis into the room from whence he had come. It was a large room, and little lighter than the galleries, for the single glow-lamp that burned at the end where we entered left the rest of the apartment in almost complete obscurity. We seated ourselves at once on the chairs that had been placed for us, and, when the mutual salutations had been exchanged, I looked about me. There were three people in the room besides Jervis: Thorndyke, who sat with his watch in his hand, a gray-headed gentleman whom I took to be Dr. Norbury, and a smaller person at the dim farther end﻿—undistinguishable, but probably Polton. At our end of the room were the two large trays that I had seen in the workshop, now mounted on trestles and each fitted with a rubber drain-tube leading down to a bucket. At the farther end of the room the sinister shape of the gallows reared itself aloft in the gloom; only now I could see that it was not a gallows at all. For affixed to the top crossbar was a large, bottomless glass basin, inside which was a glass bulb that glowed with a strange green light; and in the heart of the bulb a bright spot of red.

===Chinese===
那馆员提着灯转身回去，我们跟着杰维斯走进他出来的那间屋子。那是间大屋子，比陈列廊亮不了多少；我们进来这一端点着一盏孤零零的辉光灯，屋子的其余部分便几乎完全没在昏暗里。我们立刻在为我们备好的椅子上落座；寒暄既毕，我便环顾四周。屋里除杰维斯外还有三个人：桑代克，手里握着怀表；一位灰白头发的绅士，我猜便是诺伯里博士；还有昏暗的远端一个小些的身影——看不真切，多半是波尔顿。屋子我们这一端摆着我在工作间见过的那两只大木槽，如今架在支架上，各接一条橡皮排水管，直通下面一只桶。屋子远端，那绞架狰狞的形体在幽暗中高高耸起；只是此刻我已看明白，那根本不是绞架。因为顶上那根横梁上装着一只大的无底玻璃盆，盆里是一只玻璃球泡，散发着奇异的绿光；球泡正中，还有一点明亮的红。

===Original===
It was all clear enough so far. The peculiar sound that filled the air was the hum of the interrupter; the bulb was, of course, a Crookes tube, and the red spot inside it, the glowing red-hot disc of the anti-cathode. Clearly an X-ray photograph was being made; but of what? I strained my eyes, peering into the gloom at the foot of the gallows, but though I could make out an elongated object lying on the floor directly under the bulb, I could not resolve the dimly seen shape into anything recognizable. Presently, however, Dr. Norbury supplied the clue.

===Chinese===
到此为止，一切都清清楚楚。满屋那阵古怪的声音，是断续器的嗡鸣；那球泡自然是一支克鲁克斯管（Crookes tube），管内那点红，便是烧得红热的对阴极圆盘。显然正在拍一张X光照片；可是拍的是什么呢？我睁大眼睛，细看绞架脚下昏暗处的情形；虽然能辨出球泡正下方的地上躺着一件细长的东西，却怎么也认不出那依稀的轮廓是什么。不过，诺伯里博士随即给了线索。

===Original===
“I am rather surprised,” said he, “that you chose so composite an object as a mummy to begin on. I should have thought that a simpler object, such as a coffin or a wooden figure, would have been more instructive.”

===Chinese===
“我倒有点奇怪，”他说，“你们头一炮就挑了木乃伊这么个成分复杂的对象。我本以为，挑个简单些的东西——比如一口棺材或一尊木雕像——更有教益。”

===Original===
“In some ways it would,” replied Thorndyke, “but the variety of materials that the mummy gives us has its advantages. I hope your father is not ill, Miss Bellingham.”

===Chinese===
“在某些方面确实如此，”桑代克答道，“但木乃伊提供给我们的材料形形色色，自有它的好处。希望你父亲没有贵恙吧，贝林厄姆小姐。”

===Original===
“He is not at all well,” said Ruth, “and we agreed that it was better for me to come alone. I knew Herr Lederbogen quite well. He stayed with us for a time when he was in England.”

===Chinese===
“他身子很不好，”露丝说，“我们商量下来，还是我一个人来好。莱德博根先生我是很熟的。他在英国那阵子，还在我们家住过些日子呢。”

===Original===
“I trust,” said Dr. Norbury, “that I have not troubled you for nothing. Herr Lederbogen speaks of ‘our erratic English friend with the long name that I can never remember,’ and it seemed to me that he might be referring to your uncle.”

===Chinese===
“但愿我没有让你白跑一趟，”诺伯里博士说。“莱德博根先生信里提到‘我们那位性情古怪、名字长得叫我怎么也记不住的英国朋友’，我寻思，他说的兴许就是你伯父。”

===Original===
“I should hardly have called my uncle erratic,” said Ruth.

===Chinese===
“我倒很难说我伯父性情古怪，”露丝说。

===Original===
“No, no. Certainly not,” Dr. Norbury agreed hastily. “However, you shall see the letter presently and judge for yourself. We mustn’t introduce irrelevant topics while the experiment is in progress, must we, Doctor?”

===Chinese===
“不，不，当然不怪，”诺伯里博士连忙附和。“不过，回头你就能见到那封信，自己判断。实验进行的时候，我们可不该扯不相干的话题，是不是，博士？”

===Original===
“You had better wait until we have finished,” said Thorndyke, “because I am going to turn out the light. Switch off the current, Polton.”

===Chinese===
“你们最好等我们做完再说，”桑代克道，“因为我要把灯关掉了。波尔顿，把电流切断。”

===Original===
The green light vanished from the bulb, the hum of the interrupter swept down an octave or two and died away.
