Then Thorndyke and Dr. Norbury rose from their chairs and went toward the mummy, which they lifted tenderly while Polton drew from beneath it what presently turned out to be a huge black paper envelope. The single glow-lamp was switched off, leaving the room in total darkness until there burst out suddenly a bright orange red light immediately above one of the trays.

===Chinese===
绿光从球泡里消失了，断续器的嗡鸣滑落一两个八度，渐至无声。随后桑代克和诺伯里博士起身走向那具木乃伊，轻轻地把它抬起来；波尔顿趁机从它底下抽出一物，不久便看清是一只巨大的黑纸封套。那盏孤零零的辉光灯被关掉，全屋陷入一片漆黑，直到一只木槽上方骤然迸出一团橙红色的亮光。

===Original===
We all gathered round to watch, as Polton﻿—the high priest of these mysteries﻿—drew from the black envelope a colossal sheet of bromide paper, laid it carefully in the tray and proceeded to wet it with a large brush which he had dipped in a pail of water.

===Chinese===
我们全都围拢来看：波尔顿——这些秘仪的大祭司——从黑纸封套里抽出一大张溴化银相纸，小心平放进木槽，又拿一支在大水桶里蘸过的软刷，把相纸缓缓浸湿。

===Original===
“I thought you always used plates for this kind of work,” said Dr. Norbury.

===Chinese===
“我以为你们做这类工作，一向都用干版呢，”诺伯里博士说。

===Original===
“We do, by preference; but a six-foot plate would be impossible, so I had a special paper made to the size.”

===Chinese===
“能用的都宁用干版；可六英尺的干版根本办不到，所以我照这尺寸特别定做了相纸。”

===Original===
There is something singularly fascinating in the appearance of a developing photograph; in the gradual, mysterious emergence of the picture from the blank, white surface of plate or paper. But a skiagraph, or X-ray photograph, has a fascination all its own. Unlike the ordinary photograph, which yields a picture of things already seen, it gives a presentment of objects hitherto invisible; and hence, when Polton poured the developer on the already wet paper, we all craned over the tray with the keenest curiosity.

===Chinese===
显影中的照片，其浮现自有一种奇异的魅力：影像从干版或相纸空白的表面上渐渐地、神秘地现出。而X光照片（skiagraph）更有它独具的魔力。寻常照片摄下的本是眼见之物，X光照片呈现的却是迄今不可见的对象；因此，当波尔顿把显影液浇上浸湿的相纸时，我们个个伸长脖子俯向木槽，好奇心炽到了极点。

===Original===
The developer was evidently a very slow one. For fully half a minute no change could be seen in the uniform surface. Then, gradually, almost insensibly, the marginal portion began to darken, leaving the outline of the mummy in pale relief. The change, once started, proceeded apace. Darker and darker grew the margin of the paper until from slaty gray it had turned to black; and still the shape of the mummy, now in strong relief, remained an enlongated patch of bald white. But not for long. Presently the white shape began to be tinged with gray, and, as the color deepened, there grew out of it a paler form that seemed to steal out of the enshrouding gray like an apparition, spectral, awesome, mysterious. The skeleton was coming into view.

===Chinese===
这显影液显然极慢。足足半分钟，那均匀的表面上不见丝毫变化。随后，渐渐地、几乎不知不觉地，边缘部分开始转暗，衬出木乃伊的轮廓，泛着淡白。变化一经开始，便进展神速。相纸边缘越来越暗，从板岩灰直变成黑色；而木乃伊的形体虽已轮廓鲜明，仍是一片光秃秃的白。但这局面为时不久。转眼间，那白色的形体开始染上灰意；随着颜色加深，灰底里又生出一个更淡的形影，仿佛幽灵一般，悄然渗出那笼罩的灰霭——飘忽、骇人、神秘。骷髅现形了。

===Original===
“It is rather uncanny,” said Dr. Norbury. “I feel as if I were assisting at some unholy rite. Just look at it now!”

===Chinese===
“真是有点邪门，”诺伯里博士说。“我觉得自己像在旁观什么亵渎的仪式。快看现在这模样！”

===Original===
The gray shadow of the cartonnage, the wrappings and the flesh was fading away into the background and the white skeleton stood out in sharp contrast. And it certainly was rather a weird spectacle.

===Chinese===
棺匣、裹尸布和皮肉的灰色阴影渐渐隐入背景，雪白的骷髅则对比鲜明地凸显出来。那景象确实堪称诡异。

===Original===
“You’ll lose the bones if you develop much farther,” said Dr. Norbury.

===Chinese===
“再显下去，骨头可就要看不出来了，”诺伯里博士说。

===Original===
“I must let the bones darken,” Thorndyke replied, “in case there are any metallic objects. I have three more papers in the envelope.”

===Chinese===
“我必须让骨头也转暗，”桑代克答道，“以防还有金属物件。封套里我还有三张相纸。”

===Original===
The white shape of the skeleton now began to gray over and, as Dr. Norbury had said, its distinctness became less and yet less. Thorndyke leaned over the tray with his eyes fixed on a point in the middle of the breast and we all watched him in silence. Suddenly he rose. “Now, Polton,” he said sharply; “get the hypo on as quickly as you can.”

===Chinese===
骷髅的白色形体如今开始泛灰，而且正如诺伯里博士所言，它的清晰度愈来愈低。桑代克俯身向木槽，双眼紧盯胸口正中的一点；我们都默不作声地望着他。他霍然起身。“波尔顿，”他厉声道；“快，赶快把定影液倒上去。”

===Original===
Polton, who had been waiting with his hand on the stopcock of the drain-tube, rapidly ran off the developer into the bucket and flooded the paper with the fixing solution.

===Chinese===
波尔顿早已一手扶着排水管的活塞守候多时，闻声迅速把显影液放进桶里，随即用定影液浸满整张相纸。

===Original===
“Now we can look at it at our leisure,” said Thorndyke. After waiting a few seconds, he switched on one of the glow-lamps, and as the flood of light fell on the photograph, he added: “You see we haven’t quite lost the skeleton.”

===Chinese===
“现在可以从容观看了，”桑代克说。他稍等了几秒钟，便拧亮一盏辉光灯；灯光洒落在相片上，他又道：“你们看，骷髅并没有完全消失。”

===Original===
“No.” Dr. Norbury put on a pair of spectacles and bent down over the tray; and at this moment I felt Ruth’s hand touch my arm, lightly at first, and then with a strong nervous grasp; and I could feel that her hand was trembling. I looked round at her anxiously and saw that she had turned deathly pale.

===Chinese===
“是啊，没全丢。”诺伯里博士戴上一副眼镜，俯身向木槽；也就在这一刻，我觉得露丝的手碰了碰我的手臂，起初轻轻的，随即紧紧攥住，紧张有力；我感觉得出她的手在发抖。我焦急地回头看，只见她已面无人色。

===Original===
“Would you rather go out into the gallery?” I asked; for the room with its tightly shut windows was close and hot.

===Chinese===
“你要不要到陈列廊去透透气？”我问；因为这屋子窗子关得严严实实，又闷又热。

===Original===
“No,” she replied quietly. “I will stay here. I am quite well.” But still she kept hold of my arm.

===Chinese===
“不用，”她平静地答道。“我留在这儿。我很好。”可她的手仍攥着我的手臂不放。

===Original===
Thorndyke glanced at her keenly and then looked away as Dr. Norbury turned to ask him a question.

===Chinese===
桑代克锐利地瞥了她一眼，随即移开目光，因为诺伯里博士转过身来，要问他一个问题。

===Original===
“Why is it, think you, that some of the teeth show so much whiter than others?”

===Chinese===
“依你看，为什么有几颗牙齿比别的白得多呢？”

===Original===
“I think the whiteness of the shadows is due to the presence of metal,” Thorndyke replied.

===Chinese===
“我想，影子之所以发白，是因为有金属的缘故，”桑代克答道。

===Original===
“Do you mean that the teeth have metal fillings?” asked Dr. Norbury.

===Chinese===
“你是说，这些牙齿里有金属填补？”诺伯里博士问。

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“是的。”

===Original===
“Really! This is very interesting. The use of gold stoppings﻿—and artificial teeth, too﻿—by the ancient Egyptians is well known, but we have no examples in this Museum. This mummy ought to be unrolled. Do you think all those teeth are filled with the same metal? They are not equally white.”

===Chinese===
“真的吗！这太有意思了。古埃及人使用黄金补牙——还镶假牙——这是世人熟知的事，可我们馆里没有实物例证。这具木乃伊真该解开来看看。你认为那些牙齿里补的都是同一种金属吗？它们的白度可并不一致。”

===Original===
“No,” replied Thorndyke. “Those teeth that are perfectly white are undoubtedly filled with gold, but that grayish one is probably filled with tin.”

===Chinese===
“不是，”桑代克答道。“那些纯白的牙齿，无疑补的是黄金；而那颗发灰的，多半补的是锡。”

===Original===
“Very interesting,” said Dr. Norbury. “*Very* interesting! And what do you make of that faint mark across the chest, near the top of the sternum?”

===Chinese===
“有意思，”诺伯里博士说。“*非常*有意思！还有，胸口那道浅浅的痕迹——就在胸骨上端近旁——你怎么解释？”

===Original===
It was Ruth who answered his question.

===Chinese===
回答他这一问的，是露丝。

===Original===
“It is the Eye of Osiris!” she exclaimed in a hushed voice.

===Chinese===
“那是俄塞里斯之眼！”她低声惊呼。

===Original===
“Dear me!” exclaimed Dr. Norbury, “so it is. You are quite right. It is the Utchat﻿—the Eye of Horus﻿—or Osiris, if you prefer to call it so. That, I presume, will be a gilded device on some of the wrappings.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀！”诺伯里博士惊叹道，“可不是嘛。你说得对。那是‘乌查特’（Utchat）——荷鲁斯之眼——你若愿意，管它叫俄塞里斯之眼也行。依我看，那想必是某些裹尸布上的描金纹饰。”

===Original===
“No; I should say it is a tattoo mark. It is too indefinite for a gilded device. And I should say further that the tattooing is done in vermilion, as carbon tattooing could cast no visible shadow.”

===Chinese===
“不；依我看，那是一个纹身印记。若是描金纹饰，轮廓不会这样模糊。而且我还想进一步说：这纹身是用朱砂刺的，因为炭黑纹身投不出这样看得见的影子。”

===Original===
“I think you must be mistaken about that,” said Dr. Norbury, “but we shall see, if the Director allows us to unroll the mummy. By the way, those little objects in front of the knees are metallic, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
“这一点，恐怕是你弄错了，”诺伯里博士说，“不过我们往后看便知，只要馆长答应我们解开这具木乃伊。对了，膝盖前面那些小物件，想必也是金属的吧？”

===Original===
“Yes, they are metallic. But they are not in front of the knees; they are in the knees. They are pieces of silver wire which have been used to repair fractured kneecaps.”

===Chinese===
“是的，是金属。但它们不在膝盖前面，而在膝盖里面。那是几截银丝，是修复碎裂的膝盖骨时用上去的。”

===Original===
“Are you sure of that?” exclaimed Dr. Norbury, peering at the little white marks with ecstasy; “because if you are, and if these objects are what you say they are, the mummy of Sebek-hotep is an absolutely unique specimen.”

===Chinese===
“这话当真？”诺伯里博士狂喜地细看那些小白点，大声道；“因为倘若当真，倘若这些物件确如你所说，那么塞贝克霍特普的这具木乃伊，便是绝无仅有的孤例。”

===Original===
“I am quite certain of it,” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“我完全确定，”桑代克说。

===Original===
“Then,” said Dr. Norbury, “we have made a discovery, thanks to your inquiring spirit. Poor John Bellingham! He little knew what a treasure he was giving us! How I wish he could have known! How I wish he could have been here with us tonight!”

===Chinese===
“那么，”诺伯里博士说，“多亏你这份穷究的精神，我们有了一个发现。可怜的约翰·贝林厄姆！他哪里知道，他送给我们的是怎样一件珍宝！我多希望他能知道啊！我多希望他今晚能同我们在一起，在这里啊！”

===Original===
He paused once more to gaze in rapture at the photograph. And then Thorndyke, in his quiet, impassive way, said:

===Chinese===
他又一次停下，心醉神迷地凝望那张照片。随后，桑代克以他那一贯平静、不动声色的方式说道：

===Original===
“John Bellingham is here, Doctor Norbury. This is John Bellingham.”

===Chinese===
“约翰·贝林厄姆就在这里，诺伯里博士。这便是约翰·贝林厄姆。”

===Original===
Dr. Norbury started back and stared at Thorndyke in speechless amazement.

===Chinese===
诺伯里博士猛地向后一缩，哑然无语地死盯着桑代克。

===Original===
“You don’t mean,” he exclaimed, after a long pause, “that this mummy is the body of John Bellingham!”

===Chinese===
“你该不是说，”半晌，他才惊呼出声，“这具木乃伊就是约翰·贝林厄姆的遗体吧！”

===Original===
“I do indeed. There is no doubt of it.”

===Chinese===
“我正是此意。毫无疑义。”

===Original===
“But it is impossible! The mummy was here in the gallery a full three weeks before he disappeared.”

===Chinese===
“可这不可能！他失踪之前整整三个星期，这具木乃伊就已经在陈列室里了。”

===Original===
“Not so,” said Thorndyke. “John Bellingham was last seen alive by you and Mr. Jellicoe on the fourteenth of October, more than three weeks before the mummy left Queen Square. After that date he was never seen alive or dead by any person who knew him and could identify him.”

===Chinese===
“不然，”桑代克说。“你和杰利科先生最后一次见到活着的约翰·贝林厄姆，是十月十四日；那比木乃伊运出皇后广场，还早三个多星期。自那日期之后，无论生死，再没有任何一个认识他、能够辨认他的人见过他。”

===Original===
Dr. Norbury reflected a while in silence. Then, in a faint voice, he asked:

===Chinese===
诺伯里博士默然寻思了片刻，然后才声音微弱地问：

===Original===
“How do you suggest that John Bellingham’s body came to be inside that cartonnage?”

===Chinese===
“照你看来，约翰·贝林厄姆的遗体，是怎么进到那棺匣里去的呢？”

===Original===
“I think Mr. Jellicoe is the most likely person to be able to answer that question,” Thorndyke replied dryly.

===Chinese===
“我想，最有可能答得上这个问题的，是杰利科先生，”桑代克干巴巴地答道。

===Original===
There was another interval of silence, and then Dr. Norbury asked suddenly:

===Chinese===
又是一阵沉默，随后诺伯里博士蓦地问道：

===Original===
“But what do you suppose has become of Sebek-hotep? The real Sebek-hotep, I mean?”

===Chinese===
“可你想过没有，塞贝克霍特普怎么样了？我是说，真正的塞贝克霍特普？”

===Original===
“I take it,” said Thorndyke, “that the remains of Sebek-hotep, or at least a portion of them, are at present lying in the Woodford mortuary awaiting an adjourned inquest.”

===Chinese===
“依我之见，”桑代克说，“塞贝克霍特普的遗骸，或者至少其中一部分，此刻正躺在伍德福德停尸房里，等着延期续审的验尸呢。”

===Original===
As Thorndyke made this statement a flash of belated intelligence, mingled with self-contempt, fell on me. Now that the explanation was given, how obvious it was! And yet I, a competent anatomist and physiologist and actually a pupil of Thorndyke’s, had mistaken those ancient bones for the remains of a recent body!

===Chinese===
桑代克这话一出，一道迟来的灵光，掺着自惭，蓦地照上我的心头。解释既出，事情多么明白！然而我，一个合格的解剖学家兼生理学家，还是桑代克的及门弟子，竟把那些千年的老骨，错当成了一具新近死者的遗骸！

===Original===
Dr. Norbury considered the last statement for some time in evident perplexity. “It is all consistent enough, I must admit,” said he, at length, “and yet﻿—are you quite sure there is no mistake? It seems so incredible.”

===Chinese===
诺伯里博士显然大为困惑，把桑代克最后这话咀嚼了半天。“我得承认，这一切都前后贯通，”他终于说道，“可是——你能担保没有差错吗？这事似乎太不可思议了。”

===Original===
“There is no mistake, I assure you,” Thorndyke answered. “To convince you, I will give you the facts in detail. First, as to the teeth. I have seen John Bellingham’s dentist and obtained particulars from his casebook. There were in all five teeth that had been filled. The right upper wisdom tooth, the molar next to it, and the second lower molar on the left side, had all extensive gold fillings. You can see them all quite plainly in the skiagraph. The lower left lateral incisor had a very small gold filling, which you can see as a nearly circular white dot. In addition to these, a filling of tin amalgam had been inserted while the deceased was abroad, in the second left upper bicuspid, the rather gray spot that we have already noticed. These would, by themselves, furnish ample means of identification. But in addition, there is the tattooed device of the Eye of Osiris﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我向你担保，绝无差错，”桑代克答道。“为使你信服，我把事实一一详陈。先说牙齿。我见过约翰·贝林厄姆的牙医，从他的病历册里取得了详情。他前后共有五颗牙补过。右上智齿、紧挨它的那颗臼齿，以及左侧下第二臼齿，都做过大面积的黄金填补；这几处在X光片上你们都看得清清楚楚。左下侧切牙有一处很小的金补，你们看得出那是个近乎圆形的小白点。除此之外，死者旅居国外期间，左上第二前臼齿还补过一处锡汞齐——就是我们已经注意到的那处浅灰斑点。单凭这些，已足以提供充分的辨认手段。此外，还有俄塞里斯之眼的纹身图纹——”

===Original===
“Horus,” murmured Dr. Norbury.

===Chinese===
“荷鲁斯，”诺伯里博士喃喃道。

===Original===
“Horus, then﻿—in the exact locality in which it was borne by the deceased and tattooed, apparently, with the same pigment. There are, further, the suture wires in the kneecaps; Sir Morgan Bennett, having looked up the notes of the operation, informs me that he introduced three suture wires into the left patella and two into the right; which is what the skiagraph shows. Lastly, the deceased had an old Pott’s fracture on the left side. It is not very apparent now, but I saw it quite distinctly just now when the shadows of the bones were whiter. I think that you may take it that the identification is beyond all doubt or question.”

===Chinese===
“那么，荷鲁斯——它所在的位置，恰是死者生前所刺的位置，而且所用的颜料，看来也同出一辙。此外，还有膝盖骨里的缝线：摩根·贝内特爵士查过当年的手术记录，告诉我他在左侧膝盖骨里放进过三根缝线，右侧放进过两根；X光片所显示的正与此相符。最后，死者左侧有一处陈旧的波特氏骨折。此刻它不太明显，但方才骨影较白的时候，我看得分明。我想你们可以认定：这具遗体的身份，已无一丝可疑之处。”

===Original===
“Yes,” agreed Dr. Norbury, with gloomy resignation, “it sounds, as you say, quite conclusive. Well, well, it is a most horrible affair. Poor old John Bellingham! it looks uncommonly as if he had met with foul play. Don’t you think so?”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”诺伯里博士忧郁而听天由命地表示同意，“正如你所说，听上去确凿无疑。唉，唉，这是一桩顶可怕的事。可怜的老约翰·贝林厄姆！瞧这光景，他多半是遭了毒手。你不这么想吗？”

===Original===
“I do,” replied Thorndyke. “There was a mark on the right side of the skull that looked rather like a fracture. It was not very clear, being at the side, but we must develop the negative to show it.”

===Chinese===
“我是这么想的，”桑代克答道。“颅骨右侧有一道痕迹，颇像骨折。因为位置偏在侧面，看得不大真切，我们必须把那张底片显出来给大家看。”

===Original===
Dr. Norbury drew his breath in sharply through his teeth. “This is a gruesome business, Doctor,” said he. “A terrible business. Awkward for our people, too. By the way, what is our position in the matter? What steps ought we to take?”

===Chinese===
诺伯里博士从齿缝里猛吸了一口气。“这事可真是骇人，博士，”他说。“可怕之极。对我们馆里也不好交代。对了，在这件事上，我们持什么立场？该采取些什么步骤？”

===Original===
“You should give notice to the coroner﻿—I will manage the police﻿—and you should communicate with one of the executors of the will.”

===Chinese===
“你们应当报知验尸官——警方由我来打招呼——你们还应当与遗嘱执行人之一取得联系。”

===Original===
“Mr. Jellicoe?”

===Chinese===
“杰利科先生？”

===Original===
“No, not Mr. Jellicoe, under the peculiar circumstances. You had better write to Mr. Godfrey Bellingham.”

===Chinese===
“不，事出这般特殊情况，不宜找杰利科先生。你最好写信给戈弗雷·贝林厄姆先生。”

===Original===
“But I rather understood that Mr. Hurst was the co-executor,” said Dr. Norbury.

===Chinese===
“可我原先还当赫斯特先生是共同执行人呢，”诺伯里博士说。

===Original===
“He is, surely, as matters stand,” said Jervis.

===Chinese===
“就目前情形而论，他确实是，”杰维斯说。

===Original===
“Not at all,” replied Thorndyke. “He *was* as matters *stood*; but he is not now. You are forgetting the condition of clause two. That clause sets forth the conditions under which Godfrey Bellingham shall inherit the bulk of the estate and become the co-executor and those conditions are: ‘that the body of the testator shall be deposited in some authorized place for the reception of the bodies of the dead, situate within the boundaries of, or appertaining to some place of worship within, the parish of St. George, Bloomsbury, and St. Giles in the Fields, or St. Andrew above the Bars and St. George the Martyr.’ Now Egyptian mummies are bodies of the dead, and this Museum is an authorized place for their reception; and this building is situate within the boundaries of the parish of St. George, Bloomsbury. Therefore the provisions of clause two have been duly carried out and therefore Godfrey Bellingham is the principal beneficiary under the will, and the co-executor, in accordance with the wishes of the testator. Is that quite clear?”

===Chinese===
“完全不是，”桑代克答道。“事态*如彼*之时，他*曾*是；如今已不是了。你忘了第二条的条件。那一条开列了戈弗雷·贝林厄姆继承遗产大宗、并成为共同执行人所须满足的条件，即：‘立遗嘱人之遗体，应安放于布卢姆斯伯里圣乔治与田野圣吉尔斯两堂区、或栏上圣安德鲁与殉道者圣乔治两堂区境内、或其所属礼拜堂之内、经核准收殓死者遗体之场所。’而埃及木乃伊正是死者之遗体，本馆正是经核准收殓它们的场所；本馆又坐落于布卢姆斯伯里圣乔治堂区境内。因此，第二条的规定业已正当履行；因此，戈弗雷·贝林厄姆便是本遗嘱之下的主要受益人，并循立遗嘱人之意愿，成为共同执行人。这样说，清楚了吗？”

===Original===
“Perfectly,” said Dr. Norbury; “and a most astonishing coincidence﻿—but, my dear young lady, had you not better sit down? You are looking very ill.”

===Chinese===
“十分清楚，”诺伯里博士说；“这巧合也真够惊人的——不过，亲爱的小姐，你还是坐下吧？你的脸色很不好。”

===Original===
He glanced anxiously at Ruth, who was pale to the lips and was now leaning heavily on my arm.

===Chinese===
他焦急地望了露丝一眼；她连嘴唇都没了血色，此刻正沉沉地倚在我的手臂上。

===Original===
“I think, Berkeley,” said Thorndyke, “you had better take Miss Bellingham out into the gallery, where there is more air. This has been a tremendous climax to all the trials she has borne so bravely. Go out with Berkeley,” he added gently, laying his hand on her shoulder, “and sit down while we develop the other negatives. You mustn’t break down now, you know, when the storm has passed and the sun is beginning to shine.” He held the door open and as we passed out his face softened into a smile of infinite kindness. “You won’t mind my locking you out,” said he; “this is a photographic darkroom at present.”

===Chinese===
“伯克利，”桑代克说，“你最好带贝林厄姆小姐到陈列廊去，那边空气好些。她如此勇敢地熬过了种种磨难，这结局未免来得太惊心动魄。跟伯克利出去吧，”他手搭在她肩上，温和地添了一句，“坐下歇歇，我们把其余几张底片显出来。你可不能在风暴已过、太阳初升的当口倒下啊。”他拉开门；我们走出去时，他的面容化作一个无限慈和的微笑。“我把你们锁在外头，你们别见怪，”他说；“此地眼下是间摄影暗房。”

===Original===
The key grated in the lock and we turned away into the dim gallery. It was not quite dark, for a beam of moonlight filtered in here and there through the blinds that covered the skylights. We walked on slowly, her arm linked in mine, and for a while neither of us spoke. The great rooms were very silent and peaceful and solemn. The hush, the stillness, the mystery of the half-seen forms in the cases around, were all in harmony with the deeply-felt sense of a great deliverance that filled our hearts.

===Chinese===
钥匙在锁孔里刺啦一转，我们转身走进幽暗的陈列廊。廊里不算全黑，几道月光透过天窗上的遮帘，星星点点滤了进来。我们缓缓走着，她的手臂挽着我的手臂，好一阵子谁都没有说话。这些大屋子静极了，安宁而肃穆。四下的静谧、沉寂，以及橱窗里那些半隐半现的形影所含的神秘，都与充溢我们心头的那份深沉的大解脱之感，融融相合。

===Original===
We had passed through into the next room before either of us broke the silence. Insensibly our hands had crept together, and as they met and clasped with mutual pressure, Ruth exclaimed: “How dreadful and tragic it is! Poor, poor Uncle John! It seems as if he had come back from the world of shadows to tell us of this awful thing. But, O God! what a relief it is!”

===Chinese===
走进隔壁一间陈列室，我们中才有一个打破沉默。不知不觉间，我们的手已悄悄凑到一处；两手相遇、紧握相扣的一刻，露丝脱口喊道：“这事多么可怕、多么悲惨啊！可怜、可怜的伯父约翰！他仿佛是从冥影之国回来，把这桩惨事亲口告诉了我们。可是，上帝啊！这又是多大的解脱！”

===Original===
She caught her breath in one or two quick sobs and pressed my hand passionately.

===Chinese===
她一两声急促的抽噎，喘过气来，又激动地紧握我的手。

===Original===
“It is over, dearest,” I said. “It is gone forever. Nothing remains but the memory of your sorrow and your noble courage and patience.”

===Chinese===
“都过去了，最亲爱的，”我说。“一去不复返了。留下来的，只有对你的悲伤的记忆，和你那高贵的勇气与坚忍。”

===Original===
“I can’t realize it yet,” she murmured. “It has been like a frightful, interminable dream.”

===Chinese===
“我还不敢信这是真的，”她喃喃道。“这一切就像个可怕的、无穷无尽的长梦。”

===Original===
“Let us put it away,” said I, “and think only of the happy life that is opening.”

===Chinese===
“把它抛开吧，”我说，“只想着那正在开启的幸福生活。”

===Original===
She made no reply, and only a quick catch in her breath, now and again, told of the long agony that she had endured with such heroic calm.

===Chinese===
她没有作声；只有呼吸间不时一声短促的哽咽，透露出她以何等英勇的平静，熬过了这场漫长的苦难。

===Original===
We walked on slowly, scarcely disturbing the silence with our soft footfalls, through the wide doorway into the second room. The vague shapes of mummy-cases standing erect in the wall-cases, loomed out dim and gigantic, silent watchers keeping their vigil with the memories of untold centuries locked in their shadowy breasts. They were an awesome company. Reverend survivors from a vanished world, they looked out from the gloom of their abiding-place, but with no shade of menace or of malice in their silent presence; rather with a solemn benison on the fleeting creatures of today.

===Chinese===
我们继续缓步前行，轻软的脚步几乎惊不动这片寂静，穿过宽大的门洞，走进第二间陈列室。壁橱里直立着的木乃伊匣轮廓模糊，昏昏而巨大地隐现着，都是沉默的守望者，幽暗的胸怀中锁着说不清多少世纪的记忆，尽着自己的长更。它们是一列令人敬畏的同伴。这些从消逝世界里幸存的可敬元老，从栖居之地的幽暗中向外凝望，然而它们静默的临在，毫无威胁或恶意可言；反倒带着庄严的祝福，降及今日这些倏忽而过的众生。

===Original===
Halfway along the room a ghostly figure, somewhat aloof from its companions, showed a dim, pallid blotch where its face would have been. With one accord we halted before it.

===Chinese===
陈列室中段，有一个鬼影似的身形，与同伴们略略隔开，在脸孔应在之处现出一团朦胧的苍白。我们不约而同地在它面前停住了脚。

===Original===
“Do you know who this is, Ruth?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“你知道这是谁吗，露丝？”我问。

===Original===
“Of course I do,” she answered. “It is Artemidorus.”

===Chinese===
“我当然知道，”她答道。“这是阿耳忒弥多鲁斯。”

===Original===
We stood, hand in hand, facing the mummy, letting our memories fill in the vague silhouette with its well-remembered details. Presently I drew her nearer to me and whispered:

===Chinese===
我们手牵着手，面对这具木乃伊伫立，由记忆把那些难忘的细节，一一填进这模糊的剪影。过了一会儿，我把她拉近身旁，低声说：

===Original===
“Ruth! do you remember when we last stood here?”

===Chinese===
“露丝！你可记得，我们上一次站在这里的时候？”

===Original===
“As if I could ever forget!” she answered passionately. “Oh, Paul! The sorrow of it! The misery! How it wrung my heart to tell you! Were you very unhappy when I left you?”

===Chinese===
“我怎么会忘得掉！”她激动地答道。“啊，保罗！那滋味！那种煎熬！向你开口的时候，我的心是怎样地绞痛！我离你而去之后，你很痛苦吗？”

===Original===
“Unhappy! I never knew, until then, what real heartbreaking sorrow was. It seemed as if the light had gone out of my life forever. But there was just one little spot of brightness left.”

===Chinese===
“痛苦！直到那时我才知道，什么叫真正肝肠寸断的悲伤。仿佛我生命里的光，从此永远熄灭了。只是，还留下小小的一星亮。”

===Original===
“What was that?”

===Chinese===
“哪一星？”

===Original===
“You made me a promise, dear﻿—a solemn promise; and I felt﻿—at least I hoped﻿—that the day would come, if I only waited patiently, when you would be able to redeem it.”

===Chinese===
“你给过我一个许诺，亲爱的——一个庄重的许诺；我感到——至少我希望——只要我耐心等待，那一天总会到来，你必能践约。”

===Original===
She crept closer to me and yet closer, until her head nestled on my shoulder and her soft cheek lay against mine.

===Chinese===
她向我偎来，越偎越近，直到她的头安靠在我肩上，她那柔软的面颊贴上了我的面颊。

===Original===
“Dear heart,” I whispered, “is it now? Is the time fulfilled?”

===Chinese===
“心爱的人儿，”我悄声道，“是现在吗？时候到了吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, dearest,” she murmured softly. “It is now﻿—and forever.”

===Chinese===
“是的，最亲爱的，”她温柔地低语。“是现在——也是永远。”

===Original===
Reverently I folded her in my arms; gathered her to the heart that worshiped her utterly. Henceforth no sorrows could hurt us, no misfortune vex; for we should walk hand in hand on our earthly pilgrimage and find the way all too short.

===Chinese===
我满怀虔敬把她搂进臂弯，把她拥进这颗将她奉若神明的心。从此再没有悲伤能伤害我们，再没有不幸能搅扰我们；因为我们将在尘世的朝圣路上携手同行，只嫌这条路太短。

===Original===
Time, whose sands run out with such unequal swiftness for the just and the unjust, the happy and the wretched, lagged, no doubt, with the toilers in the room that we had left. But for us its golden grains trickled out apace, and left the glass empty before we had begun to mark their passage. The turning of a key and the opening of a door aroused us from our dream of perfect happiness. Ruth raised her head to listen, and our lips met for one brief moment. Then, with a silent greeting to the friend who had looked on our grief and witnessed our final happiness, we turned and retraced our steps quickly, filling the great empty rooms with chattering echoes.

===Chinese===
时间的流沙，为义人与不义的人、幸福者与不幸者，流逝得何等参差不齐；它在那间屋子里的劳作者身边，无疑走得慢吞吞的。而在我们这里，金色的沙粒却奔流而下，我们还没开始计较它们的流逝，沙漏已经空了。钥匙一转，门扉一开，把我们从至福的梦境中唤醒。露丝抬头谛听，我们的唇短暂地一触。随后，我们向那位曾看着我们悲伤、也见证了这最终幸福的朋友默默致意，转身快步循原路而回，空旷的大屋里顿时充满了叽叽喳喳的回声。

===Original===
“We won’t go back into the darkroom﻿—which isn’t dark now,” said Ruth.

===Chinese===
“我们不回那暗房去了——如今它也不暗了，”露丝说。

===Original===
“Why not?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“为什么不？”我问。

===Original===
“Because﻿—when I came out I was very pale; and I’m﻿—well, I don’t think I am very pale now. Besides, poor Uncle John is in there﻿—and﻿—I should be ashamed to look at him with my selfish heart overflowing with happiness.”

===Chinese===
“因为——我出来的时候，脸色白得很；而我这会儿——嗯，我想我现在已经不很苍白了。再说，可怜的伯父约翰还在里面——我——满心自私地涨溢着幸福，再去见他，我是要惭愧的。”

===Original===
“You needn’t be,” said I. “It is the day of our lives and we have a right to be happy. But you shan’t go in, if you don’t wish to,” and I accordingly steered her adroitly past the beam of light that streamed from the open door.

===Chinese===
“用不着惭愧，”我说。“这是我们一生的大日子，我们有权利快乐。不过你若不想进去，就不进去好了。”于是我灵巧地领着她，绕开那道从敞开的门里流泻出来的光。

===Original===
“We have developed four negatives,” said Thorndyke, as he emerged with the others, “and I am leaving them in the custody of Doctor Norbury, who will sign each when they are dry, as they may have to be put in evidence. What are you going to do?”

===Chinese===
“我们显了四张底片，”桑代克同其他人一道走出来时说，“我把它们交托诺伯里博士保管，待干透后由他逐张签署，因为日后可能要作为证物。你们有何打算？”

===Original===
I looked at Ruth to see what she wished.

===Chinese===
我看露丝的意思如何。

===Original===
“If you won’t think me ungrateful,” said she, “I should rather be alone with my father tonight. He is very weak, and﻿—”

===Chinese===
“倘若你不怪我不识好歹，”她说，“今晚我倒想单独陪着我父亲。他很虚弱，而且——”

===Original===
“Yes, I understand,” I said hastily. And I did. Mr. Bellingham was a man of strong emotions and would probably be somewhat overcome by the sudden change of fortune and the news of his brother’s tragic death.

===Chinese===
“是，我明白，”我连忙说。我也确实明白。贝林厄姆先生情感深厚，骤然时来运转、又闻弟弟惨死的噩耗，只怕一时承受不住。

===Original===
“In that case,” said Thorndyke, “I will bespeak your services. Will you go on and wait for me at my chambers, when you have seen Miss Bellingham home?”

===Chinese===
“既然如此，”桑代克说，“我就预约你的效劳了。送贝林厄姆小姐到家之后，请你先到我家去等我，如何？”

===Original===
I agreed to this, and we set forth under the guidance of Dr. Norbury (who carried an electric lamp) to return by the way we had come; two of us, as least, in a vastly different frame of mind. The party broke up at the entrance gates, and as Thorndyke wished my companion “Good night,” she held his hand and looked up in his face with swimming eyes.

===Chinese===
我应承了；于是我们在诺伯里博士（他提着一盏电灯）的引导下，循来路折返；至少我们两个，此刻的心境与来时已判若云泥。一行人在大门口分手；桑代克向我的同伴道“晚安”时，她握住他的手，泪光盈盈地仰望着他的脸。

===Original===
“I haven’t thanked you, Doctor Thorndyke,” she said, “and I don’t feel that I ever can. What you have done for me and my father is beyond all thanks. You have saved his life and you have rescued me from the most horrible ignominy. Goodbye! and God bless you!”

===Chinese===
“我还没有谢您，桑代克博士，”她说，“而且我觉得，我永远也谢不完。您为我和我父亲所做的一切，非言语所能报答。您救了他的性命，又把我从最可怕的污名中救拔了出来。再见了！愿上帝保佑您！”

===Original===
The hansom that bowled along eastward﻿—at most unnecessary speed﻿—bore two of the happiest human beings within the wide boundaries of the town. I looked at my companion as the lights of the street shone into the cab, and was astonished at the transformation. The pallor of her cheek had given place to a rosy pink; the hardness, the tension, the haggard self-repression that had aged her face, were all gone, and the girlish sweetness that had so bewitched me in the early days of our love had stolen back. Even the dimple was there when the sweeping lashes lifted and her eyes met mine in a smile of infinite tenderness.

===Chinese===
那辆向东疾驰的双轮马车——速度快得全无必要——载着这座广大城邑之内两个最幸福的人。街灯的光射进车厢，我端详着我的同伴，为她的变化之巨而惊讶。她颊上的苍白已让位给玫瑰般的红润；那曾使她容颜见老的冷硬、紧张和憔悴的自我压抑，都已烟消云散；在我们初恋时节令我如此倾倒的那份少女的甜美，又悄悄回来了。当低垂的长睫扬起，她的眼睛迎着我的目光，绽出一缕无限温柔的微笑时，连那对笑靥也回来了。

===Original===
Little was said on that brief journey. It was happiness enough to sit, hand clasped in hand, and know that our time of trial was past; that no cross of Fate could ever part us now.

===Chinese===
这段短短的旅程上，我们几乎没有说什么。手握着手坐着，知道我们受磨难的时日已经过去；知道如今无论命运摆下什么十字架，再也不能把我们拆散——这已是足够的幸福。

===Original===
The astonished cabman set us down, according to instructions, at the entrance to Nevill’s Court, and watched us with open mouth as we vanished into the narrow passage. The court had settled down for the night, and no one marked our return; no curious eye looked down on us from the dark house-front as we said “Goodbye,” just inside the gate.

===Chinese===
那诧异不已的马车夫遵照嘱咐，在内维尔巷口让我们下了车，张着嘴巴目送我们隐入那条窄巷。巷子已经安歇了，没有人留意我们归来；我们在门里道“再见”时，也没有好奇的眼睛从黑黢黢的临街房上往下瞧。

===Original===
“You will come and see us tomorrow, dear, won’t you?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“你明天会来看我们的，亲爱的，是不是？”她问。

===Original===
“Do you think it possible that I could stay away, then?”

===Chinese===
“你想，到那时我还离得开吗？”

===Original===
“I hope not, but come as early as you can. My father will be positively frantic to see you; because I shall have told him, you know. And, remember, that it is you who have brought us this great deliverance. Good night, Paul.”

===Chinese===
“我希望你离不开。不过，要尽早来。我父亲见着你，准会欢喜得发狂；因为到那时，我已经把一切都告诉他了，你知道。还有，记住：给我们带来这场大解救的，正是你。晚安，保罗。”

===Original===
“Good night, sweetheart.”

===Chinese===
“晚安，亲爱的。”

===Original===
She put up her face frankly to be kissed and then ran up to the ancient door; whence she waved me a last goodbye. The shabby gate in the wall closed behind me and hid her from my sight; but the light of her love went with me and turned the dull street into a path of glory.

===Chinese===
她大大方方地仰起脸来领受一吻，随后便奔向那扇古老的门；站在那儿向我挥手，作最后的道别。高墙下那扇破旧的门在我身后合上，把她掩没了；但她爱情的光一路伴着我，把那条晦暗的街道，化作了一条荣耀之路。

## XIX A Strange Symposium / 十九 奇异的座谈会
===Original===
It came upon me with something of a shock of surprise to find the scrap of paper still tacked to the oak of Thorndyke’s chambers. So much had happened since I had last looked on it that it seemed to belong to another epoch of my life. I removed it thoughtfully and picked out the tack before entering, and then, closing the inner door, but leaving the oak open, I lit the gas and fell to pacing the room.

===Chinese===
重又看见那张小纸片仍钉在桑代克寓所的橡木外门上，我不由得微微一怔。自上次见到它以来，世上发生了那么多事，它仿佛已属于我生命中另一个纪元。我若有所思地取下纸片，拔去图钉，这才进门；随后关上内门，敞着橡木外门，点起煤气灯，便在屋里踱起步来。

===Original===
What a wonderful episode it had been! How the whole aspect of the world had been changed in a moment by Thorndyke’s revelation! At another time, curiosity would have led me to endeavor to trace back the train of reasoning by which the subtle brain of my teacher had attained this astonishing conclusion. But now my own happiness held exclusive possession of my thoughts. The image of Ruth filled the field of my mental vision. I saw her again as I had seen her in the cab with her sweet, pensive face and downcast eyes; I felt again the touch of her soft cheek and the parting kiss by the gate, so frank and simple, so intimate and final.

===Chinese===
这是一段何等奇妙的经历！桑代克的揭示，顷刻之间便改变了整个世界的面貌。若在旁的时候，好奇心定会驱使我追本溯源，看看我这位老师那颗细密的头脑，究竟循着怎样的思路达到了这个惊人的结论。可是此刻，我自己的幸福独占了全部心神。露丝的形影充盈着我心灵的视野。我又看见她坐在马车里的模样，那张甜美而脉脉含愁的脸，那双低垂的眼；我又感到她柔软脸颊的贴触，和大门旁那个道别的吻——那么坦率，那么单纯，那么亲昵，又那么地一锤定音。

===Original===
I must have waited quite a long time, though the golden minutes sped unreckoned, for when my two colleagues arrived they tendered needless apologies.

===Chinese===
想必我等了颇久，只是那黄金般的分分秒秒飞逝而过，无从计数；因为等到两位同仁到时，竟都毫无必要地道起歉来。

===Original===
“And I suppose,” said Thorndyke, “you have been wondering what I wanted you for.”

===Chinese===
“我猜，”桑代克说，“你一直在纳闷，我叫你来是要做什么吧。”

===Original===
I had not, as a matter of fact, given the matter a moment’s consideration.

===Chinese===
其实，此事我片刻也不曾放在心上。

===Original===
“We are going to call on Mr. Jellicoe,” Thorndyke explained. “There is something behind this affair, and until I have ascertained what it is, the case is not complete from my point of view.”

===Chinese===
“我们这就去拜访杰利科先生，”桑代克解释道。“这桩案子背后另有文章，在查明那文章是什么之前，依我看，这案子便不算了结。”

===Original===
“Wouldn’t it have done as well tomorrow?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“放到明天再去，不也一样吗？”我问。

===Original===
“It might; and then it might not. There is an old saying as to catching a weasel asleep. Mr. Jellicoe is a somewhat wide-awake person, and I think it best to introduce him to Inspector Badger at the earliest possible moment.”

===Chinese===
“也许一样，也许就不一样了。有句老话，讲的是趁黄鼠狼打盹好捉它。杰利科先生是个警醒不过的人物，所以我想，还是尽早让他认识认识巴杰探长为妙。”

===Original===
“The meeting of a weasel and a badger suggests a sporting interview,” remarked Jervis. “But you don’t expect Jellicoe to give himself away, do you?”

===Chinese===
“黄鼠狼跟獾相会，”杰维斯评道，“这场会面想必有声有色。不过，你总不至于指望杰利科不打自招吧？”

===Original===
“He can hardly do that, seeing that there is nothing to give away. But I think he may make a statement. There were some exceptional circumstances, I feel sure.”

===Chinese===
“他谈不上什么招不招，因为根本没有什么可供招认的。不过我想，他或许会作一番陈述。当初必有某种非常的情形，这一点我有把握。”

===Original===
“How long have you known that the body was in the Museum?” I asked.

===Chinese===
“尸体在大英博物馆里，这你知道多久了？”我问。

===Original===
“About thirty or forty seconds longer than you have, I should say.”

===Chinese===
“依我看，也就比你知道的早个三四十秒钟罢了。”

===Original===
“Do you mean,” I exclaimed, “that you did not know until the negative was developed?”

===Chinese===
“你的意思是说，”我失声道，“直到底片显影，你才知道？”

===Original===
“My dear fellow,” he replied, “do you suppose that, if I had had certain knowledge where the body was, I should have allowed that noble girl to go on dragging out a lingering agony of suspense that I could have cut short in a moment? Or that I should have made these humbugging pretenses of scientific experiments if a more dignified course had been open to me?”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的伙计，”他答道，“你想，倘若我确知尸体下落，我岂能听任那位高贵的姑娘，在悬望的煎熬里一天天捱下去——那煎熬我本可以顷刻了断？倘若还有一条更体面的路可走，我岂会装模作样，搞那些所谓科学实验的骗人把戏？”

===Original===
“As to the experiments,” said Jervis, “Norbury could hardly have refused if you had taken him into your confidence.”

===Chinese===
“说到实验，”杰维斯说，“你要是把实情吐露给诺伯里，他总不至于拒绝吧。”

===Original===
“Indeed he could, and probably would. My ‘confidence’ would have involved a charge of murder against a highly respectable gentleman who was well known to him. He would probably have referred me to the police, and then what could I have done? I had plenty of suspicions, but not a single solid fact.”

===Chinese===
“他完全可能拒绝，而且多半会拒绝。我那点‘信任’，等于指控一位极有名望的绅士犯了谋杀罪，而此人偏偏是他的至交。他多半会把这件事推给警察，那时我还有什么办法？怀疑我有一大把，实打实的事实却一件也没有。”

===Original===
Our discussion was here interrupted by hurried footsteps on the stairs and a thundering rat-tat on our knocker.

===Chinese===
谈话至此，被楼梯上一阵急促的脚步声和门环上雷鸣般的敲击打断了。

===Original===
As Jervis opened the door, Inspector Badger burst into the room in a highly excited state.

===Chinese===
杰维斯开门处，巴杰探长一头闯进屋来，激动得非同小可。

===Original===
“What is all this, Doctor Thorndyke?” he asked. “I see you’ve sworn an information against Mr. Jellicoe, and I have a warrant to arrest him; but before anything else is done I think it right to tell you that we have more evidence than is generally known pointing to quite a different quarter.”

===Chinese===
“这究竟是怎么回事，桑代克博士？”他问。“我见你已经具状控告了杰利科先生，我也拿到了逮捕他的拘票；可是趁一切还没动手，我觉得应当告诉你一声，我们手上还有些外人不知道的证据，指向的完全是另一个方向。”

===Original===
“Derived from Mr. Jellicoe’s information,” said Thorndyke. “But the fact is that I have just examined and identified the body at the British Museum, where it was deposited by Mr. Jellicoe. I don’t say that he murdered John Bellingham﻿—though that is what appearances suggest﻿—but I do say that he will have to account for his secret disposal of the body.”

===Chinese===
“那都是从杰利科先生提供的消息里来的。”桑代克道。“不过事实是，我方才在大英博物馆检验并认明了那具尸体，正是杰利科先生把它存放到那里去的。我没有说他谋杀了约翰·贝林厄姆——虽然种种表象给人的印象正是如此——但我要说，他私自处理尸体的行为，总得有个交代。”

===Original===
Inspector Badger was thunderstruck. Also he was visibly annoyed. The salt which Mr. Jellicoe had so adroitly sprinkled on the constabulary tail appeared to develop irritating properties, for when Thorndyke had given him a brief outline of the facts he stuck his hands in his pockets and exclaimed gloomily:

===Chinese===
巴杰探长如遭雷击，同时也明显地老大不痛快。杰利科先生往警方尾巴上那么机灵地撒的一把盐，此刻显出了刺激的药性：桑代克把事实梗概约略一说，他便双手插进裤兜，悻悻地嚷道：

===Original===
“Well, I’m hanged! And to think of all the time and trouble I’ve spent on those damned bones! I suppose they were just a plant?”

===Chinese===
“好啊，我真是见了鬼了！想想我在那些该死的骨头上费掉的时间和工夫！敢情那全是设好的圈套？”

===Original===
“Don’t let us disparage them,” said Thorndyke. “They have played a useful part. They represent the inevitable mistake that every criminal makes sooner or later. The murderer will always do a little too much. If he would only lie low and let well alone, the detectives might whistle for a clue. But it is time we are starting.”

===Chinese===
“咱们可别小看它们，”桑代克说。“它们是出过力的。它们体现了那条规律：凡是罪犯，早晚必犯那个不可避免的错误。杀人犯总是要多此一举。他若是肯蛰伏不动、见好就收，侦探们怕就只好朝风里吹口哨，一条线索也别想捞着。不过，咱们该动身了。”

===Original===
“Are we all going?” asked the inspector, looking at me in particular with no very gracious recognition.

===Chinese===
“我们都去吗？”探长问，特别朝我看了一眼，算不上什么客气的相识。

===Original===
“We will all come with you,” said Thorndyke; “but you will, naturally, make the arrest in the way that seems best to you.”

===Chinese===
“我们都随你去，”桑代克道，“不过逮捕自然要按你认为最妥当的方式来执行。”

===Original===
“It’s a regular procession,” grumbled the inspector; but he made no more definite objection, and we started forth on our quest.

===Chinese===
“这简直成了出巡的仪仗了。”探长咕哝道；但他没有再明确提出异议，于是我们便出发办案去了。

===Original===
The distance from the Temple to Lincoln’s Inn is not great. In five minutes we were at the gateway in Chancery Lane, and a couple of minutes later saw us gathered round the threshold of the stately old house in New Square.

===Chinese===
从坦普尔到林肯律师学院路途不远。五分钟工夫，我们已到大法官巷（Chancery Lane）的院门口；再过两分钟，一行人便齐集在新广场那座气派老宅的门阶前。

===Original===
“Seems to be a light in the first-floor front,” said Badger. “You’d better move away before I ring the bell.”

===Chinese===
“二楼临街的房间好像有灯光。”巴杰说。“按铃之前，你们最好先避避开。”

===Original===
But the precaution was unnecessary. As the inspector advanced to the bellpull a head was thrust out of the open window immediately above the street door.

===Chinese===
但这一谨慎是多余的。探长刚要上前去拉门铃索，临街大门正上方那扇敞开的窗子里，一颗脑袋探了出来。

===Original===
“Who are you?” inquired the owner of the head in a voice which I recognized as that of Mr. Jellicoe.

===Chinese===
“你们是什么人？”那颗脑袋的主人问道。那嗓音我一听便认出是杰利科先生的。

===Original===
“I am Inspector Badger of the Criminal Investigation Department. I wish to see Mr. Arthur Jellicoe.”

===Chinese===
“我是刑事调查部的巴杰探长。我要见阿瑟·杰利科先生。”

===Original===
“Then look at me. I am Mr. Arthur Jellicoe.”

===Chinese===
“那你就瞧着我吧。我就是阿瑟·杰利科先生。”

===Original===
“I hold a warrant for your arrest, Mr. Jellicoe. You are charged with the murder of Mr. John Bellingham, whose body has been discovered in the British Museum.”

===Chinese===
“我手里有逮捕你的拘票，杰利科先生。你被控谋杀了约翰·贝林厄姆先生，他的尸体已在大英博物馆中被发现。”

===Original===
“By whom?”

===Chinese===
“谁控告的？”

===Original===
“By Doctor Thorndyke.”

===Chinese===
“桑代克博士。”

===Original===
“Indeed,” said Mr. Jellicoe. “Is he here?”

===Chinese===
“是嘛。”杰利科先生说。“他来了吗？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“来了。”

===Original===
“Ha! and you wish to arrest me, I presume?”

===Chinese===
“哈！那么，你们是要逮捕我了，我猜？”

===Original===
“Yes. That is what I am here for.”

===Chinese===
“不错。我就是为此而来的。”

===Original===
“Well, I will agree to surrender myself subject to certain conditions.”

===Chinese===
“那么，我同意自行投案，但须附带几个条件。”

===Original===
“I can’t make any conditions, Mr. Jellicoe.”

===Chinese===
“条件我可不能答应，杰利科先生。”

===Original===
“No, I will make them, and you will accept them. Otherwise you will not arrest me.”

===Chinese===
“不，条件由我来开，而你们必须接受。不然，你们就逮捕不成我。”

===Original===
“It’s no use for you to talk like that,” said Badger. “If you don’t let me in I shall have to break in. And I may as well tell you,” he added mendaciously, “that the house is surrounded.”

===Chinese===
“你这么说毫无用处。”巴杰道。“你要是不放我进来，我就只好破门而入了。不妨顺便告诉你，”他又昧着良心添了一句，“这宅子已被团团围住了。”

===Original===
“You may accept my assurance,” Mr. Jellicoe replied calmly, “that you will not arrest me if you do not accept my conditions.”

===Chinese===
“这一点我可以向你保证，”杰利科先生从容不迫地答道，“你不接受我的条件，就逮捕不了我。”

===Original===
“Well, what are you conditions?” demanded Badger.

===Chinese===
“那么，你的条件是什么？”巴杰质问。

===Original===
“I desire to make a statement,” said Mr. Jellicoe.

===Chinese===
“我要求作一项陈述。”杰利科先生说。

===Original===
“You can do that, but I must caution you that anything you say may be used in evidence against you.”

===Chinese===
“陈述尽可以作，但我必须提醒你：你说的任何话，都可能被用作对你不利的证据。”

===Original===
“Naturally. But I wish to make the statement in the presence of Doctor Thorndyke, and I desire to hear a statement from him of the method of investigation by which he discovered the whereabouts of the body. That is to say, if he is willing.”

===Chinese===
“那是自然。不过，我要当着桑代克博士的面作这项陈述，并且想请他也陈述一遍，讲讲他用什么侦查方法查明了尸体的下落。当然，前提是他愿意。”

===Original===
“If you mean that we should mutually enlighten one another, I am very willing indeed,” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“如果你的意思是让我们彼此开导开导，那我是求之不得。”桑代克说。

===Original===
“Very well. Then my conditions, Inspector, are that I shall hear Doctor Thorndyke’s statement and that I shall be permitted to make a statement myself, and that until those statements are completed, with any necessary interrogation and discussion, I shall remain at liberty and shall suffer no molestation or interference of any kind. And I agree that, on the conclusion of the said proceedings, I will submit without resistance to any course that you may adopt.”

===Chinese===
“很好。那么我的条件如下，探长：第一，我要听取桑代克博士的陈述；第二，我要获准自己也作一项陈述；第三，在这些陈述，连同必要的讯问和讨论全部完毕之前，我保持自由之身，不受任何形式的滋扰和干预。我并且同意：上述程序一结束，无论你采取何种措施，我都将不作抵抗，听凭发落。”

===Original===
“I can’t agree to that,” said Badger.

===Chinese===
“这个我答应不了。”巴杰说。

===Original===
“Can’t you?” said Mr. Jellicoe coldly; and after a pause he added: “Don’t be hasty. I have given you warning.”

===Chinese===
“答应不了吗？”杰利科先生冷冷地说；顿了一顿，又道：“莫要急。我可提醒过你了。”

===Original===
There was something in Mr. Jellicoe’s passionless tone that disturbed the inspector exceedingly, for he turned to Thorndyke and said in a low tone:

===Chinese===
杰利科先生那不动声色的语气里有一种东西，令探长大为不安，只见他转向桑代克，低声说：

===Original===
“I wonder what his game is? He can’t get away, you know.”

===Chinese===
“他葫芦里卖的什么药？他是跑不掉的，这你知道。”

===Original===
“There are several possibilities,” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“可能性有好几种。”桑代克说。

===Original===
“M’yes,” said Badger, stroking his chin perplexedly.

===Chinese===
“呣，是啊。”巴杰摸着下巴，一脸踌躇。

===Original===
“After all, is there any objection? His statement might save trouble, and you’d be on the safe side. It would take you some time to break in.”

===Chinese===
“说到底，有什么可反对的呢？他的陈述也许能省却许多麻烦，你也落个稳当。真要破门而入，还得费你不少工夫。”

===Original===
“Well,” said Mr. Jellicoe, with his hand on the window, “do you agree﻿—yes or no?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样，”杰利科先生手扶着窗框说道，“你到底答应——还是不答应？”

===Original===
“All right,” said Badger sulkily. “I agree.”

===Chinese===
“行吧。”巴杰没好气地说。“我答应。”

===Original===
“You promise not to molest me in any way until I have quite finished?”

===Chinese===
“你保证，在我全部讲完之前，绝不来打扰我？”

===Original===
“I promise.”

===Chinese===
“我保证。”

===Original===
Mr. Jellicoe’s head disappeared and the window closed. After a short pause we heard the jar of massive bolts and the clank of a chain, and, as the heavy door swung open, Mr. Jellicoe stood revealed, calm and impassive, with an old-fashioned office candlestick in his hand.

===Chinese===
杰利科先生的头缩了回去，窗子随即关上。稍过片刻，只听得粗大门闩的涩响和门链的哗啦声；沉重的宅门豁然而开，杰利科先生现身门内，神色安详，无动于衷，手里端着一盏老式的托盘烛台。

===Original===
“Who are the others?” he inquired, peering out sharply through his spectacles.

===Chinese===
“另外几位是谁？”他问道，一面从眼镜后面犀利地往外打量。

===Original===
“Oh, they are nothing to do with me,” replied Badger.

===Chinese===
“哦，他们与我不相干。”巴杰答道。

===Original===
“They are Doctor Berkeley and Doctor Jervis,” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“这两位是伯克利博士和杰维斯博士。”桑代克说。

===Original===
“Ha!” said Mr. Jellicoe; “very kind and attentive of them to call. Pray, come in, gentlemen. I am sure you will be interested to hear our little discussion.”

===Chinese===
“哈！”杰利科先生道。“二位枉驾光临，真是又殷勤又体贴。诸位请进吧。我担保，听听我们这场小小的座谈，诸位会觉得有趣的。”

===Original===
He held the door open with a certain stiff courtesy, and we all entered the hall led by Inspector Badger. He closed the door softly and preceded us up the stairs and into the apartment from the window of which he had dictated the terms of surrender. It was a fine old room, spacious, lofty, and dignified, with paneled walls and a carved mantelpiece, the central escutcheon of which bore the initials “J. W. P.” with the date “1671.” A large writing-table stood at the farther end, and behind it was an iron safe.

===Chinese===
他以一种略见僵硬的客气扶着门，我们便随巴杰探长之后鱼贯进入门厅。他轻轻关上大门，领头上楼，走进那间屋子——方才在窗口发号施令、开出投降条件的正是这间屋的窗口。这是一间华贵的旧屋，宽敞、高朗、庄重，四壁镶着护墙板，壁炉架精雕细琢，正中的纹章盾上镌着姓名缩写“J. W. P.”和年份“1671”。屋子尽头立着一张大写字台，台后是一座铁保险柜。

===Original===
“I have been expecting this visit,” Mr. Jellicoe remarked tranquilly as he placed four chairs opposite the table.

===Chinese===
“我一直在恭候这次来访。”杰利科先生一边在桌子对面摆下四把椅子，一边从容地说。

===Original===
“Since when?” asked Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“从什么时候起？”桑代克问。

===Original===
“Since last Monday evening, when I had the pleasure of seeing you conversing with my friend Doctor Berkeley at the Inner Temple gate, and then inferred that you were retained in the case. That was a circumstance that had not been fully provided for. May I offer you gentlemen a glass of sherry?”

===Chinese===
“从上礼拜一晚上起。那晚我有幸看见您在内殿大门口与敝友伯克利博士交谈，随即推断您已受聘承办此案。这一层情形，原是未曾完全料到的。几位先生可要来一杯雪利酒？”

===Original===
As he spoke he placed on the table a decanter and a tray of glasses, and looked at us interrogatively with his hand on the stopper.

===Chinese===
说话间，他把一只酒樽和一盘酒杯摆上桌来，一只手按着瓶塞，用征询的目光望了我们一眼。

===Original===
“Well, I don’t mind if I do, Mr. Jellicoe,” said Badger, on whom the lawyer’s glance had finally settled. Mr. Jellicoe filled a glass and handed it to him with a stiff bow; then, with the decanter still in his hand, he said persuasively: “Doctor Thorndyke, pray allow me to fill you a glass?”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，既蒙盛情，我就来一杯吧，杰利科先生。”巴杰说——律师的目光最终落在了他身上。杰利科先生斟满一杯，僵硬地一躬身，递了过去；随后，手里仍握着酒樽，用劝诱的口吻说：“桑代克博士，容我也给您斟上一杯？”

===Original===
“No, thank you,” said Thorndyke, in a tone so decided that the inspector looked round at him quickly. And as Badger caught his eye, the glass which he was about to raise to his lips became suddenly arrested and was slowly returned to the table untasted.

===Chinese===
“不，谢谢。”桑代克说。那语气斩钉截铁，探长闻声霍然回头看他。巴杰与他的目光一接，那杯正要送到唇边的酒蓦地停住，又被慢慢放回桌上，一口未沾。

===Original===
“I don’t want to hurry you, Mr. Jellicoe,” said the inspector, “but it’s rather late, and I should like to get this business settled. What is it that you wish to do?”

===Chinese===
“我不是要催您，杰利科先生，”探长说，“只是时候不早了，我想把这件事了结掉。您打算怎么办呢？”

===Original===
“I desire,” replied Mr. Jellicoe, “to make a detailed statement of the events that have happened, and I wish to hear from Doctor Thorndyke precisely how he arrived at his very remarkable conclusion. When this has been done I shall be entirely at your service; and I suggest that it would be more interesting if Doctor Thorndyke would give us his statement before I furnish you with the actual facts.”

===Chinese===
“我的愿望是，”杰利科先生答道，“把已往的种种事件作一番详尽的陈述；同时我也想听听桑代克博士亲口讲讲，他究竟如何得出了那个非同寻常的结论。这两件事办完，我便全凭诸位发落。另外我提议：由桑代克博士先作陈述，然后我再把实际情形奉告诸位，这样的次序会更有趣味。”

===Original===
“I am entirely of your opinion,” said Thorndyke.

===Chinese===
“我完全赞成。”桑代克说。

===Original===
“Then in that case,” said Mr. Jellicoe, “I suggest that you disregard me, and address your remarks to your friends as if I were not present.”

===Chinese===
“既然这样，”杰利科先生说，“我建议您权当我这个人并不在场，只管向您的朋友们陈述便是。”

===Original===
Thorndyke acquiesced with a bow, and Mr. Jellicoe, having seated himself in his elbow-chair behind the table, poured himself out a glass of water, selected a cigarette from a neat silver case, lighted it deliberately, and leaned back to listen at his ease.

===Chinese===
桑代克躬身应允。杰利科先生在桌后的扶手椅里落座，给自己倒了一杯清水，从一只精致的银烟盒里拣出一支香烟，不慌不忙地点上，往椅背上一靠，舒舒服服地听着。

===Original===
“My first acquaintance with this case,” Thorndyke began without preamble, “was made through the medium of the daily papers about two years ago; and I may say that, although I had no interest in it beyond the purely academic interest of a specialist in a case that lies in his particular specialty, I considered it with deep attention. The newspaper reports contained no particulars of the relations of the parties that could furnish any hints as to motives on the part of any of them, but merely a bare statement of the events. And this was a distinct advantage, inasmuch as it left one to consider the facts of the case without regard to motive﻿—to balance the prima facie probabilities with an open mind. And it may surprise you to learn that those prima facie probabilities pointed from the very first to that solution which has been put to the test of experiment this evening. Hence it will be well for me to begin by giving the conclusions that I reached by reasoning from the facts set forth in the newspapers before any of the further facts came to my knowledge.

===Chinese===
“我与此案的初次相识，”桑代克开门见山地说，“是在大约两年前，经由日报的报道。可以说，此案于我，不过是本行专家遇上一起正属本行的案子时那种纯学理的兴趣；但我仍以极深的注意研究了它。报纸的报道对各当事人之间的关系未著一字，因而提不出任何有关动机的线索，只是干巴巴地陈述了事件。这反倒是一种明显的便利，因为它使人能够撇开动机，专就案情事实来考量——以不偏不倚之心去权衡表面概率。而说来也许令诸位吃惊：这些表面概率，从一开始便指向了今晚那经受实验检验的答案。因此，我不妨先讲结论——在我获知后来的种种事实之前，仅凭报纸所载事实推演而得的那些结论。

===Original===
“From the facts as stated in the newspaper reports it is obvious that there were four possible explanations of the disappearance.

===Chinese===
“从报纸报道所载的事实看，此失踪案显然可能有四种解释。

===Original===
“1. The man might be alive and in hiding. This was highly improbable, for the reasons that were stated by Mr. Loram at the late hearing of the application, and for a further reason that I shall mention presently.

===Chinese===
“一、此人可能尚在人世，匿而不出。这极不可能，理由即洛拉姆先生在上次申请案听证时申述的那些；另有一个理由，我稍后再提。

===Original===
“2. He might have died by accident or disease, and his body failed to be identified. This was even more improbable, seeing that he carried on his person abundant means of identification, including visiting cards.

===Chinese===
“二、他可能死于意外或疾病，而尸体未能验明身份。这就更不可能了，因为他身上带着大量可供验明身份之物，连名片都在。

===Original===
“3. He might have been murdered by some stranger for the sake of his portable property. This was highly improbable for the same reason: his body could hardly have failed to be identified.

===Chinese===
“三、他可能被某个陌生人为了劫夺随身财物而杀害。出于同样的理由，这也极不可能：他的尸体几乎不可能验不出身份。

===Original===
“These three explanations are what we may call the outside explanations. They touched none of the parties mentioned; they were all obviously improbable on general grounds; and to all of them there was one conclusive answer﻿—the scarab which was found in Godfrey Bellingham’s garden. Hence I put them aside and gave my attention to the fourth explanation. This was that the missing man had been made away with by one of the parties mentioned in the report. But, since the reports mentioned three parties, it was evident that there was a choice of three hypotheses, namely:

===Chinese===
“这三种解释，不妨称之为局外的解释。它们都不牵涉报道中提到的任何当事人；以常情而论，都显然不甚可靠；而且对这三种解释，有一个一锤定音的答复——在戈弗雷·贝林厄姆花园里发现的那枚圣甲虫。因此，我把它们搁置一旁，专心考虑第四种解释，即：失踪者已被报道中提到的某一方谋害。但报道提到了三方，可见要在三个假说中作一选择，即：

===Original===
“(a) That John Bellingham had been made away with by Hurst; or (b) by the Bellinghams; or (c) by Mr. Jellicoe.

===Chinese===
“（甲）约翰·贝林厄姆系遭赫斯特谋害；或（乙）遭贝林厄姆父女谋害；或（丙）遭杰利科先生谋害。

===Original===
“Now, I have constantly impressed on my pupils that the indispensable question that must be asked at the outset of such an inquiry as this is, ‘When was the missing person last undoubtedly seen or known to be alive?’ That is the question that I asked myself after reading the newspaper report; and the answer was, that he was last certainly seen alive on the fourteenth of October, nineteen hundred and two, at 141, Queen Square, Bloomsbury. Of the fact that he was alive at that time and place there could be no doubt whatever; for he was seen at the same moment by two persons, both of whom were intimately acquainted with him, and one of whom, Doctor Norbury, was apparently a disinterested witness. After that date he was never seen, alive or dead, by any person who knew him and was able to identify him. It was stated that he had been seen on the twenty-third of November following by the housemaid of Mr. Hurst; but as this person was unacquainted with him, it was uncertain whether the person whom she saw was or was not John Bellingham.

===Chinese===
“我一向谆谆告诫我的学生们：这类侦查一开始，有一个必不可少的问题必须先提出来——‘失踪者最后一次确凿无疑地被人见到或确知活着，是在什么时候？’我读完报纸报道后，问自己的正是这个问题；答案是：一九〇二年十月十四日，在布鲁姆斯伯里皇后广场 141 号，人们最后一次确凿看见他活着。至于他当时当地确在人间，这一点毫无疑义，因为同一时刻有两人见到他，两人都与他过从甚密，其中一位诺伯里博士，显然是个无利害瓜葛的证人。自那日期之后，无论死活，再没有任何认识他、能辨认他的人见过他。据报道，同年十一月二十三日，赫斯特先生的女仆曾见过他；但此人素不相识，她所见者究竟是不是约翰·贝林厄姆，无从断定。

===Original===
“Hence the disappearance dated, not from the twenty-third of November, as everyone seems to have assumed, but from the fourteenth of October; and the question was not, ‘What became of John Bellingham after he entered Mr. Hurst’s house?’ but, ‘What became of him after his interview in Queen Square?’

===Chinese===
“如此说来，失踪的起算之日，并不是人人仿佛认定的十一月二十三日，而是十月十四日；问题也就不是‘约翰·贝林厄姆进了赫斯特先生的宅子之后怎么样了’，而是‘他在皇后广场会谈之后怎么样了’。

===Original===
“But as soon as I had decided that that interview must form the real starting point of the inquiry, a most striking set of circumstances came into view. It became obvious that if Mr. Jellicoe had had any reason for wishing to make away with John Bellingham, he had such an opportunity as seldom falls to the lot of an intending murderer.

===Chinese===
“可是一旦认定那次会谈才是侦查真正的起点，一组极其触目的情形便浮现眼前。事情很清楚：倘若杰利科先生有什么理由想除掉约翰·贝林厄姆，那么他得到的机会，实为蓄意杀人者罕逢难遇的机会。

===Original===
“Just consider the conditions. John Bellingham was known to be setting out alone upon a journey beyond the sea. His exact destination was not stated. He was to be absent for an undetermined period, but at least three weeks. His disappearance would occasion no comment; his absence would lead to no inquiries, at least for several weeks, during which the murderer would have leisure quietly to dispose of the body and conceal all traces of the crime. The conditions were, from a murderer’s point of view, ideal.

===Chinese===
“只需想想当时的种种条件。众人皆知，约翰·贝林厄姆将独自出洋远行。确切目的地未作宣布。行期不定，但至少三个星期。他的失踪不会引人议论；他的不在不会引来查问——至少好几个星期之内不会；在这段时间里，凶手尽可从容处理尸体，湮灭罪迹。站在凶手的立场上看，这些条件堪称理想。

===Original===
“But that was not all. During that very period of John Bellingham’s absence Mr. Jellicoe was engaged to deliver to the British Museum what was admittedly a dead human body; and that body was to be enclosed in a sealed case. Could any more perfect or secure method of disposing of a body be devised by the most ingenious murderer? The plan would have had only one weak point: the mummy would be known to have left Queen Square *after* the disappearance of John Bellingham, and suspicion might in the end have arisen. To this point I shall return presently; meanwhile we will consider the second hypothesis﻿—that the missing man was made away with by Mr. Hurst.

===Chinese===
“这还不算完。恰在约翰·贝林厄姆外出的那段时间，杰利科先生正受托要向大英博物馆交付一具公认的人类尸体；而且那尸体将封存在一只密封的箱匣里。再精明的杀人犯，还能想出比这更完美、更稳妥的尸体处理法吗？这计划只有一处软肋：人们会知道，木乃伊离开皇后广场，是在约翰·贝林厄姆失踪*之后*，疑心终究可能由此而生。这一点我稍后再谈；眼下先考虑第二个假说——失踪者系遭赫斯特先生谋害。

===Original===
“Now, there seemed to be no doubt that some person, purporting to be John Bellingham, did actually visit Mr. Hurst’s house; and he must either have left the house or remained in it. If he left, he did so surreptitiously; if he remained, there could be no reasonable doubt that he had been murdered and that his body had been concealed. Let us consider the probabilities in each case.

===Chinese===
“那么，看来确曾有人自称约翰·贝林厄姆，造访赫斯特先生的宅子，这一点殆无可疑；此人要么离开了那宅子，要么留在了里面。若是离开，必是偷偷溜走的；若是留下，那么他已遭杀害、尸体已被藏匿，便几乎没有怀疑的余地了。两种情形的或然性，我们分别掂量掂量。

===Original===
“Assuming﻿—as everyone seems to have done﻿—that the visitor was really John Bellingham, we are dealing with a responsible, middle-aged gentleman, and the idea that such a person would enter a house, announce his intention of staying, and then steal away unobserved is very difficult to accept. Moreover, he would appear to have come down to Eltham by rail immediately on landing in England, leaving his luggage in the cloakroom at Charing Cross. This pointed to a definiteness of purpose quite inconsistent with his casual disappearance from the house.

===Chinese===
“姑且照人人仿佛默认的那样，假定来客真是约翰·贝林厄姆，那么我们所面对的，是一位持重的中年绅士。这样一个人进了宅子，声言要住下，随后又趁人不备悄悄溜走——这种设想很难令人接受。何况他似乎刚在英国登岸，便径自乘火车下了埃尔瑟姆，行李则寄存在查令十字的行李房里。这说明他的来意明确坚定，与他从宅中飘然自失的说法全然相悖。

===Original===
“On the other hand, the idea that he might have been murdered by Hurst was not inconceivable. The thing was physically possible. If Bellingham had really been in the study when Hurst came home, the murder could have been committed﻿—by appropriate means﻿—and the body temporarily concealed in the cupboard or elsewhere. But although possible it was not at all probable. There was no real opportunity. The risk and the subsequent difficulties would be very great; there was not a particle of positive evidence that a murder had occurred; and the conduct of Hurst in immediately leaving the house in possession of the servants is quite inconsistent with the supposition that there was a body concealed in it. So that, while it is almost impossible to believe that John Bellingham left the house of his own accord, it is equally difficult to believe that he did not leave it.

===Chinese===
“另一方面，说他可能被赫斯特所杀，也并非不可想象。此事在物理上并非不可能。倘若赫斯特回家时贝林厄姆确在书房里，那么行凶未尝不可——用适当的手段——尸体也可暂时藏进柜橱或别的什么地方。但虽属可能，却全然不甚可信。并没有真正的机会。风险和善后的困难都极大；没有一丝一毫的正面证据表明发生过凶案；而赫斯特随即把宅子丢给仆人们，自己径自离去，这一行径与宅中藏有尸体的假定全然不合。于是乎，几乎无法相信约翰·贝林厄姆是自己走出那宅子的，却也同样难以相信他没有走出去。

===Original===
“But there is a third possibility, which, strange to say, no one seems to have suggested. Supposing that the visitor was not John Bellingham at all, but someone who was impersonating him? That would dispose of the difficulties completely. The strange disappearance ceases to be strange, for a personator would necessarily make off before Mr. Hurst should arrive and discover the imposture. But if we accept this supposition, we raise two further questions: ‘Who was the personator?’ and ‘What was the object of the personation?’

===Chinese===
“可是还有第三种可能——说来奇怪，似乎无一人提起过。假如来客根本不是约翰·贝林厄姆，而是某个冒充他的人呢？那样一来，一切疑难便涣然冰释。这场离奇的失踪不再离奇，因为冒充者必然要在赫斯特先生到家、骗局败露之前逃之夭夭。可是一旦接受这个假设，又生出两个新的问题：‘冒充者是谁？’‘冒充的目的何在？’

===Original===
“Now, the personator was clearly not Hurst himself, for he would have been recognized by his housemaid; he was therefore either Godfrey Bellingham or Mr. Jellicoe or some other person; and as no other person was mentioned in the newspaper reports I confined my speculations to these two.

===Chinese===
“首先，冒充者显然不会是赫斯特本人，因为他的女仆会认出他来；那么此人要么是戈弗雷·贝林厄姆，要么是杰利科先生，要么另有其人；而报纸报道中并未提到别人，我便把推想限于这两人。

===Original===
“And, first, as to Godfrey Bellingham. It did not appear whether he was or was not known to the housemaid, so I assumed﻿—wrongly, as it turns out﻿—that he was not. Then he might have been the personator. But why should he have personated his brother? He could not have already committed the murder. There had not been time enough. He would have had to leave Woodford before John Bellingham had set out for Charing Cross. And even if he had committed the murder, he would have no object in raising this commotion. His cue would have been to remain quiet and know nothing. The probabilities were all against the personator being Godfrey Bellingham.

===Chinese===
“先说戈弗雷·贝林厄姆。女仆认不认得他，报道中未见分晓，于是我当时假定她不认得——后来证明这一假定是错的。这样，他便有可能是那冒充者。可是，他为什么要冒充自己的哥哥？他不可能此前已犯下凶案，时间根本不够：他必须赶在约翰·贝林厄姆动身去查令十字之前便离开伍德福德才行。退一步说，纵然他已行凶，他也没有道理掀起这场风波。他的上策是缄默不言、佯作不知。种种或然性都表明，冒充者不会是戈弗雷·贝林厄姆。

===Original===
“Then could it be Mr. Jellicoe? The answer to this question is contained in the answer to the further question: ‘What could have been the object of the personation?’

===Chinese===
“那么，会不会是杰利科先生？这个问题的答案，包含在另一个问题的答案里：‘冒充的目的可能是什么？’

===Original===
“What motive could this unknown person have had in appearing, announcing himself as John Bellingham, and forthwith vanishing? There could only have been one motive: that, namely, of fixing the date of John Bellingham’s disappearance﻿—of furnishing a definite moment at which he was last seen alive.

===Chinese===
“这个身份不明的人现身出来，自称约翰·贝林厄姆，随即消遁无踪，图的是什么？动机只可能有一个：坐实约翰·贝林厄姆失踪的日期——制造出一个确切的时刻，作为他最后一次被人看见还活着的时间。

===Original===
“But who was likely to have had such a motive? Let us see.

===Chinese===
“那么，谁最可能怀有这样的动机？我们来看看。

===Original===
“I said just now that if Mr. Jellicoe had murdered John Bellingham and disposed of the body in the mummy-case, he would have been absolutely safe for the time being. But there would be a weak spot in his armor. For a month or more the disappearance of his client would occasion no remark. But presently, when he failed to return, inquiries would be set on foot; and then it would appear that no one had seen him since he left Queen Square. Then it would be noted that the last person with whom he was seen was Mr. Jellicoe. It might, further, be remembered that the mummy had been delivered to the Museum some time *after* the missing man was last seen alive. And so suspicion might arise and be followed by disastrous investigations. But supposing it should be made to appear that John Bellingham had been seen alive more than a month after his interview with Mr. Jellicoe and some weeks after the mummy had been deposited in the Museum? Then Mr. Jellicoe would cease to be in any way connected with the disappearance and henceforth would be absolutely safe.

===Chinese===
“我方才说过，倘若杰利科先生谋杀了约翰·贝林厄姆，把尸体藏进木乃伊棺匣，那么他一时可保万全。但他的甲胄上终有一处破绽。一个月或更久，当事人失踪一事不会引人注目。可是等他迟迟不归，查问就会渐渐而起；那时人们便会发现，他离开皇后广场之后，再无人见过他。接着又会注意到，最后与他一同为人所见者，正是杰利科先生。再往后，人们或许还会想起，木乃伊送进博物馆，是在失踪者最后露面*之后*又过了些时日。于是疑心便会滋生，招来祸事一般的调查。可是，倘若安排得天衣无缝，让人以为约翰·贝林厄姆在与杰利科先生会谈一个多月之后、在木乃伊入藏博物馆数周之后，还被人见过活着呢？那么，杰利科先生与失踪案便再无干系，从此万无一失了。

===Original===
“Hence, after carefully considering this part of the newspaper report, I came to the conclusion that the mysterious occurrence at Mr. Hurst’s house had only one reasonable explanation, namely, that the visitor was not John Bellingham, but someone personating him; and that that someone was Mr. Jellicoe.

===Chinese===
“因此，反复掂量报纸报道的这一部分之后，我得出结论：赫斯特先生宅中那桩神秘事件，只有一种合理的解释——来客并非约翰·贝林厄姆，而是冒充他的某个人；而那个人，就是杰利科先生。

===Original===
“It remains to consider the case of Godfrey Bellingham and his daughter, though I cannot understand how any sane person can have seriously suspected either” (here Inspector Badger smiled a sour smile). “The evidence against them was negligible, for there was nothing to connect them with the affair save the finding of the scarab on their premises; and that event which might have been highly suspicious under other circumstances, was robbed of any significance by the fact that the scarab was found on a spot which had been passed a few minutes previously by the other suspected party, Hurst. The finding of the scarab did, however, establish two important conclusions: namely, that John Bellingham had probably met with foul play, and that of the four persons present when it was found, one at least had had possession of the body. As to which of the four was the one, the circumstances furnished only a hint, which was this: If the scarab had been purposely dropped, the most likely person to find it was the one who dropped it. And the person who discovered it was Mr. Jellicoe.

===Chinese===
“剩下该考虑的，是戈弗雷·贝林厄姆父女的情形了——尽管我不能理解，任何一个神志清明的人，怎么会当真怀疑他们两位”（说到这里，巴杰探长苦笑了一下）。“对他们不利的证据微不足道，因为除了圣甲虫在他们宅地上被发现之外，再无一事将他们与此案相连；而这件事——若换在别的情形之下，本可启人重大疑窦——却因圣甲虫的落点恰是另一位涉嫌者赫斯特几分钟前走过的地方，而失掉了全部意义。不过，圣甲虫的发现倒也坐实了两个重要结论：其一，约翰·贝林厄姆多半已遭毒手；其二，发现圣甲虫时在场的四人之中，至少有一人曾占有过尸体。至于那人是四人中的哪一个，种种情形只提供了一条暗示，即：倘若圣甲虫是有人有意丢下的，那么最可能拾到它的，正是丢下它的那个人。而发现圣甲虫的，是杰利科先生。

===Original===
“Following up this hint, if we ask ourselves what motive Mr. Jellicoe could have had for dropping it﻿—assuming him to be the murderer﻿—the answer is obvious. It would not be his policy to fix the crime on any particular person, but rather to set up a complication of conflicting evidence which would occupy the attention of investigators and divert it from himself.

===Chinese===
“顺着这条暗示推究下去，倘若我们自问：假定杰利科先生是凶手，他丢下圣甲虫所为何来？答案显而易见。把罪名钉死在某一个特定的人身上，并非他的策略；他的策略，是制造出互相抵牾、纠缠不清的一大团证据，叫侦查人员的心思全耗在里面，无暇旁顾他自己。

===Original===
“Of course, if Hurst had been the murderer, he would have had a sufficient motive for dropping the scarab, so that the case against Mr. Jellicoe was not conclusive; but the fact that it was he who found it was highly significant.

===Chinese===
“当然，倘若凶手是赫斯特，他丢下圣甲虫的动机便也充分，所以对杰利科先生的指认并非铁案；但圣甲虫偏偏是他发现的，这一事实意味极深。

===Original===
“This completes the analysis of the evidence contained in the original newspaper report describing the circumstances of the disappearance. The conclusions that followed from it were, as you will have seen:

===Chinese===
“以上便是对最初那份失踪报道所载证据的剖析。由此推出的结论，诸位想已了然：

===Original===
“1. That the missing man was almost certainly dead, as proved by the finding of the scarab after his disappearance.

===Chinese===
“一、失踪者几乎可以断定已死——他失踪之后圣甲虫被发现，便是明证。

===Original===
“2. That he had probably been murdered by one or more of four persons, as proved by the finding of the scarab on the premises occupied by two of them and accessible to the others.

===Chinese===
“二、他多半是被那四人中的一人或数人所害——圣甲虫发现于其中两人占用的宅地之上，而其余两人也可出入其间，便是明证。

===Original===
“3. That, of those four persons, one﻿—Mr. Jellicoe﻿—was the last person who was known to have been in the company of the missing man; had had an exceptional opportunity for committing the murder; and was known to have delivered a dead body to the Museum subsequently to the disappearance.

===Chinese===
“三、四人之中，有一位——杰利科先生——是已知与失踪者最后同处的人；犯下凶案的机会又得天独厚；且经查明，失踪之后向博物馆交付了一具尸体的，正是他。

===Original===
“4. That the supposition that Mr. Jellicoe had committed the murder rendered all the other circumstances of the disappearance clearly intelligible, whereas on any other supposition they were quite inexplicable.

===Chinese===
“四、只要假定凶手是杰利科先生，失踪案其余的一切情形便都豁然可解；而换作任何别的假定，它们便全然无从索解。

===Original===
“The evidence of the newspaper report, therefore, clearly pointed to the probability that John Bellingham had been murdered by Mr. Jellicoe and his body concealed in the mummy-case.

===Chinese===
“因此，报纸报道的证据清楚地指向这样一种或然性：约翰·贝林厄姆已被杰利科先生杀害，尸体藏于木乃伊棺匣之中。

===Original===
“I do not wish to give you the impression that I, then and there, believed that Mr. Jellicoe was the murderer. I did not. There was no reason to suppose that the report contained all the essential facts, and I merely considered it speculatively as a study in probabilities. But I did decide that that was the only probable conclusion from the facts that were given.

===Chinese===
“我并不想给诸位留下这样的印象，仿佛我当时当地便已认定杰利科先生是凶手。我没有。没有任何理由假定那篇报道已载明全部关键事实，我只是把它当作一道或然性的研习题，推演了一番。但我确实断定：就已给出的这些事实而论，那是唯一可信的结论。

===Original===
“Nearly two years had passed before I heard anything more of the case. Then it was brought to my notice by my friend, Doctor Berkeley, and I became acquainted with certain new facts, which I will consider in the order in which they became known to me.

===Chinese===
“此后近两年，此案再无音讯。后来，我的朋友伯克利博士把它引到了我的眼前，我这才得知若干新的事实。下面我便按它们为我所知的先后，逐一考量。

===Original===
“The first new light on the case came from the will. As soon as I had read the document I felt convinced that there was something wrong. The testator’s evident intention was that his brother should inherit the property, whereas the construction of the will was such as almost certainly to defeat that intention. The devolution of the property depended on the burial clause﻿—clause two; but the burial arrangement would ordinarily be decided by the executor, who happened to be Mr. Jellicoe. Thus the will left the disposition of the property under the control of Mr. Jellicoe, though his action could have been contested.

===Chinese===
“此案的第一缕新光，来自那份遗嘱。文件一读完，我便确信其中有诈。立遗嘱人的用意，显然是要弟弟承继产业；而遗嘱的行文构造，却几乎注定要挫败这一用意。产业的归属，系于埋葬条款——第二条；而丧葬如何安排，寻常要由遗嘱执行人定夺，这位执行人偏偏是杰利科先生。于是，产业的处置便留在了杰利科先生的掌控之下——尽管对他的作为，并非无从抗争。

===Original===
“Now, this will, although drawn up by John Bellingham, was executed in Mr. Jellicoe’s office as is proved by the fact that it was witnessed by two of his clerks. He was the testator’s lawyer, and it was his duty to insist on the will being properly drawn. Evidently he did nothing of the kind, and this fact strongly suggested some kind of collusion on his part with Hurst, who stood to benefit by the miscarriage of the will. And this was the odd feature in the case, for whereas the party responsible for the defective provisions was Mr. Jellicoe, the party who benefited was Hurst.

===Chinese===
“再者，这份遗嘱虽出自约翰·贝林厄姆的手笔，其签署生效却是在杰利科先生的事务所里——两名见证人皆是他手下的文书，便是明证。他是立遗嘱人的律师，职责所在，本应坚持把遗嘱拟写得法。显然，他全无此等作为。这一事实强烈暗示：他与赫斯特之间有着某种勾结——遗嘱落空，得利的正是赫斯特。而蹊跷也正在这里：应对条款疏漏负责的一方是杰利科先生，从疏漏中得利的一方却是赫斯特。

===Original===
“But the most startling peculiarity of the will was the way in which it fitted the circumstances of the disappearance. It looked as if clause two had been drawn up with those very circumstances in view. Since, however, the will was ten years old, this was impossible. But if clause two could not have been devised to fit the disappearance, could the disappearance not have been devised to fit clause two? That was by no means impossible: under the circumstances it looked rather probable. And if it had been so contrived, who was the agent in that contrivance? Hurst stood to benefit, but there was no evidence that he even knew the contents of the will. There only remained Mr. Jellicoe, who had certainly connived at the misdrawing of the will for some purpose of his own﻿—some dishonest purpose.

===Chinese===
“但遗嘱最令人骇异之处，在于它与失踪情形丝丝入扣的贴合。第二条仿佛正是照着那些情形拟出来的。然而遗嘱已有十年之龄，这便不可能了。可是，倘若不是第二条为失踪案量身而设，那么，会不会是失踪案为第二条量身而设呢？这绝非不可能：在此情形之下，反倒颇有可能。倘若真是这样筹划的，那筹划之人又是谁？赫斯特可以得利，却无证据表明他连遗嘱的内容都不甚了了。剩下的便只有杰利科先生——他确曾对遗嘱的错拟听之任之，为他自己的某种目的——某种见不得人的目的。

===Original===
“The evidence of the will, then, pointed to Mr. Jellicoe as the agent in the disappearance, and, after reading it, I definitely suspected him of the crime.

===Chinese===
“于是，遗嘱的证据把失踪案的幕后之手指向了杰利科先生；读完遗嘱之后，我已明确把他列为嫌疑人。

===Original===
“Suspicion, however, is one thing and proof is another; I had not nearly enough evidence to justify me in laying an information, and I could not approach the Museum officials without making a definite accusation. The great difficulty of the case was that I could discover no motive. I could not see any way in which Mr. Jellicoe would benefit by the disappearance. His own legacy was secure, whenever and however the testator died. The murder and concealment apparently benefited Hurst alone; and, in the absence of any plausible motive, the facts required to be much more conclusive than they were.”

===Chinese===
“然而，疑心是一回事，实证是另一回事。我掌握的证据，远不足以支撑一纸控状；而不提出明确的指控，我又无法去惊动博物馆的官员们。此案最大的难处，在于我寻不出动机。我看不出杰利科先生能从失踪案中得到任何好处。他自己那笔遗赠，无论立遗嘱人何时身故、如何身故，都稳如泰山。谋杀与藏尸，明摆着只便宜了赫斯特一人；而在拿不出任何说得通的动机的情况下，事实本身就必须远比现有的确凿得多才行。”

===Original===
“Did you form absolutely no opinion as to motive?” asked Mr. Jellicoe.

===Chinese===
“关于动机，您当时竟连一点看法也没有形成吗？”杰利科先生问。

===Original===
He put the question in a quiet, passionless tone, as if he were discussing some cause célèbre in which he had nothing more than a professional interest. Indeed, the calm, impersonal interest that he displayed in Thorndyke’s analysis, his unmoved attention, punctuated by little nods of approval at each telling point in the argument, were the most surprising features of this astounding interview.

===Chinese===
他发问的语气平静无波，仿佛正在谈论一桩名案（cause célèbre），自己不过抱着一层职业上的关切。的确，他对桑代克的剖析所表现出的那种冷静的、与己无关的兴味，那种不动声色的专注——论证每中一处肯綮，他便微微颔首称是——正是这场惊人晤谈中最令人称奇的地方。

===Original===
“I did form an opinion,” replied Thorndyke, “but it was merely speculative, and I was never able to confirm it. I discovered that about ten years ago Mr. Hurst had been in difficulties and that he had suddenly raised a considerable sum of money, no one knew how or on what security. I observed that this even coincided with the execution of the will, and I surmised that there might be some connection between them. But that was only a surmise; and, as the proverb has it, ‘He discovers who proves.’ I could prove nothing, so that I never discovered Mr. Jellicoe’s motive, and I don’t know it now.”

===Chinese===
“看法是形成了的，”桑代克答道，“但纯属推测，始终无从证实。我查出，大约十年前，赫斯特先生手头拮据，却忽然筹得一大笔钱，无人知其来路，也无人知其凭据。我又注意到，此事竟与遗嘱的签署恰在同一时日，于是猜想两者之间或有牵连。但那不过是猜想；而正如谚语所说，‘能证明者，方为发现者。’我什么也证明不了，所以始终未曾发现杰利科先生的动机，如今也仍然不知。”

===Original===
“Don’t you really?” said Mr. Jellicoe, in something approaching a tone of animation. He laid down the end of his cigarette, and, as he selected another from the silver case, he continued: “I think that is the most interesting feature of your really remarkable analysis. It does you great credit. The absence of motive would have appeared to most persons a fatal objection to the theory, of what I may call, the prosecution. Permit me to congratulate you on the consistency and tenacity with which you have pursued the actual, visible facts.”

===Chinese===
“当真不知道吗？”杰利科先生说，语气里透出一丝近乎生动的意味。他放下吸剩的烟头，一面从银烟盒里另拣一支，一面续道：“我以为，这是您这次精彩之至的剖析中最有趣的一点。它足以为您增光。在多数人看来，动机的缺席，足以否决那个理论——恕我杜撰个名目——那个‘控方’的理论。请容我向您道贺：您对实际的、可见的事实，追索得如此始终如一，如此锲而不舍。”

===Original===
He bowed stiffly to Thorndyke (who returned his bow with equal stiffness), lighted a fresh cigarette, and once more leaned back in his chair with the calm, attentive manner of a man who is listening to a lecture or a musical performance.

===Chinese===
他向桑代克僵硬地一躬（桑代克以同样僵硬的一躬回敬），点起新烟，重又靠回椅背，那副安然专注的神态，宛如一个听讲演或听音乐会的人。

===Original===
“The evidence, then, being insufficient to act upon,” Thorndyke resumed, “there was nothing for it but to wait for some new facts. Now, the study of a large series of carefully conducted murders brings into view an almost invariable phenomenon. The cautious murderer, in his anxiety to make himself secure, does too much; and it is this excess of precaution that leads to detection. It happens constantly; indeed, I may say that it always happens﻿—in those murders that are detected; of those that are not we say nothing﻿—and I had strong hopes that it would happen in this case. And it did.

===Chinese===
“证据既不足以付诸行动，”桑代克接着说，“便只有坐等新的事实。而研究过一大批办理细密的凶案之后，有一个几乎恒定的现象会浮现眼前：小心的凶手，因为求稳心切，总是做得过了头；而正是这过头的防范，招来了败露。这种事屡见不鲜；不，我简直可以说每案皆然——当然是指那些被侦破的凶案；至于没破的，我们无从谈起——所以我有充分的指望，此案也会如此。而它果然如此。

===Original===
“At the very moment when my client’s case seemed almost hopeless, some human remains were discovered at Sidcup. I read the account of the discovery in the evening paper, and scanty as the report was, it recorded enough facts to convince me that the inevitable mistake had been made.”

===Chinese===
“正在我当事人的案子几乎陷入绝境之时，锡德卡普发现了一些人的残骸。我在晚报上读到发现的报道；报道虽简略，所载的事实已足以令我确信：那个不可避免的错误，已经犯下了。”

===Original===
“Did it, indeed?” said Mr. Jellicoe. “A mere, inexpert, hearsay report! I should have supposed it to be quite valueless from a scientific point of view.”

===Chinese===
“是吗，当真？”杰利科先生说。“一份出自外行、道听途说的简报！我本当以为，从科学的观点看，它全无价值。”

===Original===
“So it was,” said Thorndyke. “But it gave the date of the discovery and the locality, and it also mentioned what bones had been found. Which were all vital facts. Take the question of time. These remains, after lying perdu for two years, suddenly come to light just as the parties﻿—who have also been lying perdu﻿—have begun to take action in respect of the will; in fact, within a week or two of the hearing of the application. It was certainly a remarkable coincidence. And when the circumstances that occasioned the discovery were considered, the coincidence became more remarkable still. For these remains were found on land actually belonging to John Bellingham, and their discovery resulted from certain operations (the clearing of the watercress-beds) carried out on behalf of the absent landlord. But by whose orders were those works undertaken? Clearly by the orders of the landlord’s agent. But the landlord’s agent was known to be Mr. Jellicoe. Therefore these remains were brought to light at this peculiarly opportune moment by the action of Mr. Jellicoe. The coincidence, I say again, was very remarkable.

===Chinese===
“全无价值，确实。”桑代克说。“但它给出了发现的日期和地点，还提到了发现的是哪些骨头。而这些都是性命攸关的事实。单说时间这一项。这些残骸蛰伏了两载，忽然重见天日，恰在各方当事人——他们也蛰伏了两载——开始为遗嘱兴讼之时；实际上，离申请案的听证不过一两个星期。这可真是桩了不得的巧合。再考量一下促成发现的种种情形，这巧合便愈发可惊了。这些残骸，恰恰发现于原属约翰·贝林厄姆名下的土地上；而发现之所以发生，又缘于若干工程（清理水田芥田）——它们正是替这位缺席的地主实施的。可这些工程奉谁的命令兴办？分明是奉地主代理人的命令。而地主的代理人，据查明正是杰利科先生。可见，残骸恰在这样一个妙至毫巅的时机重见天日，全拜杰利科先生之举所赐。我再说一遍：这巧合，实在非同小可。

===Original===
“But what instantly arrested my attention on reading the newspaper report was the unusual manner in which the arm had been separated; for, besides the bones of the arm proper, there were those of what anatomists call the ‘shoulder-girdle’﻿—the shoulder-blade and collarbone. This was very remarkable. It seemed to suggest a knowledge of anatomy, and yet no murderer, even if he possessed such knowledge, would make a display of it on such an occasion. It seemed to me that there must be some other explanation. Accordingly, when other remains had come to light and all had been collected at Woodford, I asked my friend Berkeley to go down there and inspect them. He did so, and this is what he found:

===Chinese===
“但读报时立刻攫住我注意力的，是那条手臂被分离的不寻常方式：除了手臂本身的骨头，还有解剖学家称作‘肩带’（shoulder-girdle）的那几块——肩胛骨与锁骨。这一点非常可观。它似乎暗示着某种解剖学知识；然而任何凶手，纵然身怀此等学识，也断不会在这种场合炫学卖弄。依我看，必有另一种解释。于是，待到其余残骸相继出土、全部汇集到伍德福德之后，我便请我的朋友伯克利前去查验。他去了，查验所得如下：

===Original===
“Both arms had been detached in the same peculiar manner; both were complete, and all the bones were from the same body. The bones were quite clean﻿—of soft structures, I mean. There were no cuts, scratches or marks on them. There was not a trace of adipocere﻿—the peculiar waxy soap that forms in bodies that decay in water or in a damp situation. The right hand had been detached at the time the arm was thrown into the pond, and the left ring finger had been separated and had vanished. This latter fact had attracted my attention from the first, but I will leave its consideration for the moment and return to it later.”

===Chinese===
“两条手臂以同一种特殊的方式离断；两条都完整无缺，全部骨头出自同一具身体。骨头相当干净——我是说，软组织已荡然无存。骨上没有切口、擦痕或任何印记。尸蜡——那种在水中或潮湿处腐败的尸体内生成的蜡状皂质——则一丝痕迹也没有。右手是在手臂被抛入池塘之时离断的；左手无名指则已被分离，不知去向。后一事实我从一开始便深为注意，但暂且按下，容后再谈。”

===Original===
“How did you discover that the hand had been detached?” Mr. Jellicoe asked.

===Chinese===
“手是离断的，这个您是怎么发现的？”杰利科先生问。

===Original===
“By the submersion marks,” replied Thorndyke. “It was lying on the bottom of the pond in a position which would have been impossible if it had been attached to the arm.”

===Chinese===
“凭水浸的痕迹。”桑代克答道。“那只手躺在塘底的姿势，倘若仍连在臂上，是绝无可能的。”

===Original===
“You interest me exceedingly,” said Mr. Jellicoe. “It appears that a medico-legal expert finds ‘books in the running brooks, sermons in bones, and evidence in everything.’ But don’t let me interrupt you.”

===Chinese===
“您真是让我大感兴趣。”杰利科先生说。“看来，法医学专家能从奔流的溪涧里读出书卷，从白骨中听出布道，从万物之中见出证据。不过，我不打搅您了。”

===Original===
“Doctor Berkeley’s observations,” Thorndyke resumed, “together with the medical evidence at the inquest, led me to certain conclusions.

===Chinese===
“伯克利博士的查验所得，”桑代克续道，“连同验尸庭审讯中的医学证词，使我得出了若干结论。

===Original===
“Let me state the facts which were disclosed.

===Chinese===
“且让我把查明的事实一一摆出来。

===Original===
“The remains which had been assembled formed a complete human skeleton with the exception of the skull, one finger, and the legs from the knee to the ankle, including both kneecaps. This was a very impressive fact; for the bones that were missing included all those which could have been identified as belonging or not belonging to John Bellingham; and the bones that were present were the unidentifiable remainder.

===Chinese===
